# Bone Infarct

*Pathology · 5 / 66*

## 1 Basics

### Pathophysiology

**Spectrum** — same disease spectrum as **osteonecrosis** · medullary lesion

**Mechanism** — **interruption of blood supply** by intrinsic/extrinsic factors (atherosclerosis, arteritis, thrombosis)

**Site** — **metaphysis or diaphysis** of long bones (femur, tibia)

**Healing** — progressive but **incomplete resorptive replacement** → thick **calcified fibro-osseous wall**

### Risk factors

| Risk factor | Bone infarct? |
|---|---|
| **Sickle cell disease** | ✓ |
| **Thalassemia** | ✓ |
| **Gaucher disease** | ✓ |
| **Steroid use** (chronic prednisone) | ✓ |
| Connective tissue disorders | ✓ |
| Trauma | ✓ |
| **Infection** | **not a risk factor** |

## 3 Presentation

**Usually asymptomatic** — **incidental** on films taken for other reasons (twisted knee, fall)

**Pain** — if painful → **seek another aetiology**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — medullary **sheet-like central lucency + surrounding sclerosis with serpiginous border** → **"smoke up the chimney"**

![Smoke up the chimney — medullary infarct](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8078-bone-infarct-xray.jpg)

**MRI** — key: **central signal = normal marrow** · T1 **peripheral low-signal rim** (granulation tissue, sclerosis), may enhance · T2 **double line**: inner bright granulation ring + outer dark sclerotic ring · acute infarct ill-defined ↑ T2

![Bone infarct MRI — central marrow signal preserved](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8078-bone-infarct-mri.jpg)

**Bone scan** — **cold early** · **hot** in late resorptive / revascularisation phase

**Biopsy** — usually unnecessary · mineralised necrotic marrow

## 5 Management

**Observation** — asymptomatic, **no treatment** required

## 6 Complications

**Malignant transformation** — rare · **MFH / undifferentiated pleomorphic sarcoma** at infarct site

## X vs Y

| | **Bone infarct** | **Enchondroma** |
|---|---|---|
| Matrix | Serpiginous peripheral sclerotic rim, "smoke up the chimney" | **Rings and arcs** (chondroid) throughout |
| MRI | Central normal marrow fat | Lobulated T2-bright cartilage |
| Risk factors | Sickle, Gaucher, steroids | None |

## How it's asked

**"Incidental medullary metaphyseal lesion, serpiginous sclerotic border — diagnosis?"** → **bone infarct**\
✗ Wrong options: enchondroma · metastasis · osteosarcoma

**"NOT a risk factor for this lesion?"** → **infection** (✓ sickle cell, thalassemia, Gaucher, chronic steroids)

**"Treatment?"** → **observation**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Bone Tumor Staging Systems

*Pathology · 6 / 66*

## 1 Basics

### Prognostic variables

**Prognosis depends on** — overall **stage** · **metastasis** (incl. **skip / discontinuous** lesions in same bone) · **histologic grade** · **tumour size** · tumour type

**Two systems** — **MSTS (Enneking)** — most useful to orthopaedic surgeons · **AJCC** — adds size, depth, nodes

**Enneking limitation** — only for tumours of **mesenchymal origin**

### Compartment & grade

**Intracompartmental (T1)** — bone tumour **confined within the cortex**

**Extracompartmental (T2)** — extends **beyond the cortex**

**Grade** — by **% cellular atypia** · low grade = low metastatic potential (**parosteal OS**) · high grade = **intramedullary OS, Ewing, dedifferentiated chondrosarcoma**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### MSTS (Enneking) — malignant (Roman numerals)

| Stage | Grade | Site | Metastasis |
|---|---|---|---|
| **IA** | Low | T1 intracompartmental | M0 |
| **IB** | Low | T2 extracompartmental | M0 |
| **IIA** | High | T1 intracompartmental | M0 |
| **IIB** | High | **T2 extracompartmental** | M0 |
| **III** | Any | T1 or T2 | **M1 (regional or distant)** |

**Commonest presenting stage** — **IIB** (e.g. high-grade OS with soft-tissue extension, no mets)

![Enneking staging](../../MCQ/attachments/q7490-illa-enneking.jpg)

### MSTS (Enneking) — benign (Arabic numerals)

| Stage | Behaviour | Examples |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | **Latent** | **NOF**, **enchondroma** |
| **2** | **Active** | **ABC, UBC**, chondromyxoid fibroma, chondroblastoma · **GCT without cortical breakthrough** |
| **3** | **Aggressive** | **GCT** (cortical breakthrough, soft-tissue extension) |

### AJCC — bone sarcoma

| Stage | Grade | Size / feature | Metastasis | 5-yr survival (Orthobullets) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **IA** | Low | **≤8 cm** | None | 98% |
| **IB** | Low | **>8 cm** | None | — |
| **IIA** | High | ≤8 cm | None | 82% |
| **IIB** | High | >8 cm | None | 82% |
| **III** | Any | **Skip (discontinuous) lesions in same bone** | None | 52% |
| **IVA** | Any | Any | **Lung** | — |
| **IVB** | Any | Any | **Non-pulmonary** (other bone) or **nodes** | 30% |

![AJCC staging](../../MCQ/attachments/q7490-illb-ajcc.jpg)

### AJCC — soft-tissue prognosis factors

**Grade** — low G1–G2 · high G3–G4

**Size** — T1 <8 cm · T2 >8 cm (as listed in Orthobullets)

**Nodes** — N0 / N1 · **distant mets → automatically stage IV**

## 5 Management

### Limb salvage principles & contraindications

**Three strikes rule** — 4 components of a viable limb: **bone, nerves, vessels, soft-tissue envelope** · 1–2 resected → salvageable · **3 involved → salvage not worth considering** **(TO)**

**Barriers (relative, not absolute)** — **poorly placed biopsy** · **major vascular involvement** · **encasement of major motor nerve** · **pathological fracture** **(TO)**

**Two of four components involved** — **not** a contraindication **(TO)**

**Nerves sacrificed during proximal humerus resection** (axillary + radial) — **continue with endoprosthesis** **(TO)** · axillary usually sacrificed anyway · radial → **later tendon transfers** (or repair same sitting) · amputation indicated for **intra-op tumour spillage**, not nerve loss

## How it's asked

**"High-grade distal femur OS with soft-tissue extension, no mets — MSTS stage?"** → **IIB**

**"Enneking IIB means?"** → **high grade, extracompartmental, no metastases**

**"Most common presenting stage — which patient?"** → **high-grade OS extending into soft tissue, no mets**\
✗ Wrong options: confined chondrosarcoma = IIA · lung mets = III

**"OS distal femur + biopsy-proven contralateral femur lesion — AJCC?"** → **IVB** (non-pulmonary metastasis)\
✗ Wrong options: III = skip lesion in same bone

**"GCT without cortical breakthrough — Enneking?"** → **2 (benign active)**\
✗ Wrong options: Roman numerals = malignant

**"Limb salvage contraindication — all EXCEPT?"** → **two of four components involved** (✓ bad biopsy, ✓ path fracture, ✓ motor nerve encasement) **(TO)**

**"Axillary + radial nerve sacrificed during humeral OS resection — next?"** → **proceed with planned endoprosthesis** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: amputation

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Chemotherapy

*Pathology · 7 / 66*

## 1 Basics

### Apoptosis mechanism

**Primary mechanism** — conventional chemo kills cancer cells by **apoptosis** (programmed cell death) · targets **rapidly dividing cells**

**Side effects** — gut lining, **bone marrow**, hair, skin (also rapidly dividing)

**Targeted agents** — tyrosine kinase inhibitors: **imatinib** (CML) · **gefitinib** (EGFR; lung, breast) · **erlotinib** (EGFR; NSCLC, pancreas)

**Role** — eliminates **lung micrometastases**

| Therapy | Mechanism |
|---|---|
| **Doxorubicin neoadjuvant chemo** | ✓ **Apoptosis** (blocks topoisomerase II, DNA/RNA synthesis) |
| Phenol in GCT bed | **Lysis** of residual cells (not apoptosis) |
| TNF-α inhibitor (RA) | Immune modulation |
| Colchicine (gout) | Immune / crystal inhibition |
| Vancomycin | Cell-wall synthesis inhibition |

### Genes & lab markers

**MDR (multi-drug resistance) gene** — pumps drug out of cell → **very poor prognosis** · in **25% primary**, **50% metastatic** lesions

**Necrosis >98%** after chemo — **good prognostic sign** (osteosarcoma)

## 5 Management

### Indications

**Chemo is integral (with surgery)** — **osteosarcoma** (intramedullary + periosteal) · **Ewing / PNET** · **MFH (UPS)** · **dedifferentiated chondrosarcoma**

**Controversial** — soft-tissue sarcoma

**Schedule** — **preop 8–12 weeks** → surgery → **maintenance 6–12 months**

### Agents, toxicities & antidotes

| Agent | Dose-limiting / classic toxicity |
|---|---|
| **Doxorubicin (Adriamycin)** | ✓ **Cardiomyopathy / CHF** (cumulative dose) · ~7% |
| Ifosfamide | **Encephalopathy** (neurotoxicity) |
| Cyclophosphamide | **Myelosuppression, urotoxicity** |
| Bleomycin | **Pulmonary fibrosis** |

**Doxorubicin** — **anthracycline** antibiotic, cytostatic · delayed LV wall thinning → dilated cardiomyopathy · **dexrazoxane** protective

| Extravasated agent | Antidote |
|---|---|
| Mechlorethamine / cisplatin | **Sodium thiosulfate** |
| **Doxorubicin / epirubicin** | **Dexrazoxane** |
| Vinca alkaloids (vincristine, vinblastine) | **Hot compress + hyaluronidase** |
| Other vesicants | Cold compress |

![Chemotherapy agents and antidotes](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8003-antidotes.jpg)

## How it's asked

**"Major (dose-limiting) side effect of doxorubicin?"** → **cardiomyopathy / cardiac toxicity**\
✗ Wrong options: pulmonary fibrosis (bleomycin) · myelosuppression/urotoxicity (cyclophosphamide) · encephalopathy (ifosfamide) · hearing loss · neuropathy

**"Pharmacologically induced apoptosis occurs with?"** → **doxorubicin neoadjuvant chemotherapy**\
✗ Wrong options: phenol in GCT · anti-TNF · colchicine · vancomycin

**"True of apoptosis?"** → **primary mechanism by which chemotherapy kills cancer cells**

**"Drug protecting against doxorubicin cardiotoxicity?"** → **dexrazoxane**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Chondroblastoma

*Pathology · 8 / 66*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Rare** — ~**1%** of osseous neoplasms · **M:F 2:1**

**Age** — **80% <25 yr** · 10–25 typical (2nd decade) · average diagnosis 19–23 · rare after physeal closure

**Synonym** — **"Codman tumour"** **(TO)**

**Site** — **epiphysis of immature long bones** · may **cross the physis into the metaphysis** · **knee** (distal femur, proximal tibia) commonest → **proximal humerus** distant third · proximal femur\
✗ Wrong options: metaphysis **(TO)**

**Epiphyseal equivalents** — **apophysis** (greater tuberosity, greater trochanter) · **patella** · **talus, calcaneus** · flat bones

**Mnemonic "ECG"** — **E**piphysis: **C**hondroblastoma, **G**iant cell tumour (+ clear cell chondrosarcoma) **(TO)**

### Pathophysiology

**Benign, locally destructive** proliferation of **immature cartilage cells (chondroblasts)** · from epiphyseal plate / secondary ossification centre (controversial)

**Matrix** — chondroid matrix · **hyaline cartilage rarely** produced

**Natural history** — progressive bone destruction · **does not heal spontaneously**

**Secondary ABC** — up to **1/3** (15–32% on imaging)

### Genes & lab markers

**Genetics** — **histone H3.3** mutations · **chromosome 5 and 8** abnormalities

**IHC** — **S-100 +** · **SOX9 +** · vimentin, neuron-specific enolase + (GCT, ABC: no S-100)

**Bloods** — CBC, ESR, LDH normal (vs osteomyelitis, Ewing)

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — **progressive pain** (often night pain, not relieved by NSAIDs) · **joint stiffness** · **limp**

**Signs** — local muscle atrophy · tenderness · ↓ ROM · **effusion**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray views** — AP, lateral, oblique · **chest radiograph** (lung mets) at diagnosis

**X-ray** — **geographic, well-circumscribed round/oval lytic epiphyseal lesion** with **thin sclerotic rim** · may cross physis · 1–4 cm, lobular · expansile cortical remodelling · **metaphyseal periosteal reaction** (no periosteum over epiphysis) · effusion

![Lytic epiphyseal lesion with sclerotic rim — proximal tibia](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8021-tibia-classic-lesion.jpg)

**Calcification** — stippled matrix calcification, often **only on CT (25–45%)**

**MRI** — **extensive bone-marrow + soft-tissue oedema around the lesion** — distinguishing feature **(TO)** · T1 intermediate · T2 heterogeneous · fluid levels = secondary ABC · oedema within treated lesion → recurrence

![Peritumoral oedema on MRI](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8021-peritumoral-edema.jpg)

**Bone scan** — not required · ↑ uptake, especially adjacent uninvolved bone

**CT chest** — if chest film suspicious

**Soap bubble** — classically GCT, but chondroblastoma also in DD **(TO)** · DD: ABC, GCT, chondroblastoma, enchondroma, CMF, fibrous dysplasia, ameloblastoma, plasmacytoma, chordoma

**Benign lesions with marrow oedema (TO)** — **osteoid osteoma, osteoblastoma, chondroblastoma, LCH**\
✗ Wrong options: osteochondroma · enchondroma · GCT

### Histology

**Cells** — sheets of **polygonal mononuclear chondroblasts**, well-defined borders · **"cobblestone"** arrangement

**Nuclei** — large, central, **longitudinal groove → "coffee-bean"** · scarce mitoses

**Calcification** — fine pericellular **"chicken-wire"** dystrophic calcification

**Also** — scattered **multinucleated osteoclast-type giant cells** (almost always) · islands of amorphous **chondroid matrix**

![Chondroblasts with chicken-wire calcification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8021-histology-b-parsons-moved.png)

**Recurrence** — may show atypia → not malignant transformation

### Differential of an epiphyseal lesion

| Lesion | Key differentiating finding |
|---|---|
| **Chondroblastoma** | **Skeletally immature**, progressive pain, matrix mineralization, **extensive peritumoral oedema** |
| **Giant cell tumour** | **Skeletally mature, 20–40**, closed physes · starts in metaphysis → epiphysis · pathologic fracture common · giant-cell nuclei = stromal nuclei · S-100 negative |
| **Clear cell chondrosarcoma** | **3rd–5th decade**, **>4 cm**, **less oedema**, malignant · clear cells |
| Brodie abscess | Immature, often metaphyseal · **penumbra** sign on MRI · neutrophils |
| Intraosseous ganglion | Middle age · no periosteal reaction · cystic, less oedema |
| Eosinophilic granuloma | Variable appearance |
| Brown tumour | >50, CKD / hyperparathyroidism |
| ABC | <20, multicystic, fluid levels · not typically epiphyseal |
| **Osteoblastoma** | 2nd–3rd decade, rarely epiphyseal, mineralization, oedema |
| Osteoid osteoma | Diaphyseal, NSAID-relieved night pain, nidus on CT |
| TB arthritis | Epiphyseal possible · **granulomas** |

## 5 Management

### Treatment algorithm

```
Chondroblastoma (does not heal spontaneously)
├─ Most lesions → EXTENDED INTRALESIONAL CURETTAGE + ADJUVANT + GRAFT
├─ Small (~1 cm) lesion → RADIOFREQUENCY ABLATION
├─ Rib / aggressive pelvic lesion with soft-tissue extension → EN BLOC RESECTION
├─ Destructive aggressive recurrence (rare) → ENDOPROSTHESIS / AMPUTATION
└─ Lung metastases
       ├─ Solitary / oligometastases → RESECTION
       └─ Numerous → DENOSUMAB
```

### Curettage & bone grafting

**Standard of care** — **intralesional curettage + local adjuvant + bone graft** (or PMMA)

**Approach** — through **cortical window** · extend with **high-speed burr** · **spare the physis** if possible

**Adjuvants** — **phenol**, **hydrogen peroxide**, **argon beam**, **cryotherapy / liquid nitrogen**

**Graft** — fill cavity · **structural osteochondral graft** for weight-bearing surfaces

**RFA** — first-line option for small lesions (~**1 cm**) **(TO)** · recurrence ≈ curettage · success on MRI = necrosis beyond margin + resolution of oedema

**Not indicated** — observation (no medical therapy, no spontaneous healing) · **denosumab** (GCT) · chemo · radiation · wide resection (non-expendable bone) · steroid injection · TKA

## 6 Complications

**Local recurrence** — **10–15%** (OB review 14–18%) · surgery and RFA alike · **purely epiphyseal lesions recur more** · due to inadequate resection · **surveillance X-rays 5 yr**

**Limb length discrepancy** — physeal damage by tumour or treatment → contralateral epiphysiodesis vs lengthening

**Degenerative joint disease** — subchondral/cartilage damage → THA/TKA in adulthood

### Lung metastasis — chest radiograph

**"Benign" pulmonary metastasis** — **<1%** (Orthobullets) · **2%** (one OB explanation) · like GCT · indolent → resect

**Chest X-ray** — the benign lesion that **needs a chest film at screening**\
✗ Wrong options: enchondroma · NOF · osteochondroma · UBC

**Malignant transformation** — exceedingly rare · median age **52** · permeative growth, atypia, **no osteoid** · wide resection

## X vs Y

| | **Chondroblastoma** | **GCT** | **Clear cell chondrosarcoma** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Age | **10–25, open physis** | **20–40, closed physis** | 20–40 / 3rd–5th decade |
| Site | Epiphysis (± crosses physis) | Metaphysis → epiphysis, subchondral | Epi-metaphysis (proximal femur/humerus) |
| MRI oedema | **Extensive** | Less | **Less** |
| Histology | Chondroblasts, coffee-bean nuclei, chicken-wire Ca | Giant-cell nuclei = stromal nuclei | **Clear cells**, malignant |
| Marker | **S-100, SOX9** | **H3F3A**, RANKL | — |
| Treatment | Curettage + adjuvant + graft / RFA | Curettage + adjuvant · denosumab | **Wide excision** |

| | **Chondroblastoma** | **Chondromyxoid fibroma** |
|---|---|---|
| Site | **Epiphysis** | **Metaphysis** (anterior proximal tibia) |
| Histology | Polygonal chondroblasts, chicken-wire Ca | **Stellate cells** in chondroid/myxoid/fibrous lobules |

## How it's asked

**"Statement NOT true of chondroblastoma?"** → **"commonly involves metaphysis"** (✓ Codman tumour, ✓ RFA for small lesions, ✓ chicken-wire Ca) **(TO)**

**"15-yr-old, epiphyseal proximal tibial soap-bubble lesion?"** → **chondroblastoma** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: GCT (20–40) · CMF (metaphyseal) · osteoid osteoma (diaphyseal)

**"Benign tumour with extensive surrounding marrow/soft-tissue oedema on MRI?"** → **chondroblastoma** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: GCT · enchondroma · osteochondroma

**"Adolescent, epiphyseal lytic lesion, S-100 +, normal bloods — histology?"** → **chondroblasts with chicken-wire calcification**\
✗ Wrong options: GCT · ABC · Ewing · osteomyelitis

**"16-yr-old, medial heel / calcaneal epiphyseal-equivalent lesion?"** → **chondroblastoma**\
✗ Wrong options: GCT · CMF (stellate cells) · osteoblastoma

**"18-yr-old, greater tuberosity lesion?"** → **apophyseal chondroblastoma**

**"Best treatment?"** → **extended intralesional curettage + bone graft ± adjuvant**\
✗ Wrong options: denosumab · chemo · observation · radiation · wide resection · TKA

**"Benign lesion requiring chest radiograph at initial work-up?"** → **chondroblastoma**\
✗ Wrong options: enchondroma · NOF · osteochondroma · UBC

**"Recurrence rate after curettage?"** → **10–15%** · higher if purely epiphyseal

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Chondromyxoid Fibroma

*Pathology · 9 / 66*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Rare** — **<1%** of bone tumours · benign but painful, **locally aggressive**

**Demographics** — **males** · **2nd–3rd decades** (10–30) · up to 75 yr

**Site** — **metaphysis** of long bones · **proximal tibia** commonest (around knee: proximal tibia, proximal fibula, distal femur) · **pelvis** (ilium) · **foot/ankle**, hands, wrist (distal radius)

**Origin** — may arise from **physeal remnants**

### Genes & lab markers

**Chromosome 6 (q13)** — genetic rearrangement

## 3 Presentation

**History** — **long-standing pain, months–years** · may be **incidental**

**Symptoms** — pain + mild swelling · activity pain → rest pain

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — **lytic, eccentric, metaphyseal** · sharply demarcated, **narrow zone of transition** · **scalloped sclerotic rim** · lobulated / bubbly · cortical expansion · **calcification rare** · 2–10 cm

![Eccentric lytic metaphyseal lesion with sclerotic rim](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8022-case-d-tibia-xray-b-parsons-moved.png)

**vs NOF** — radiographically often indistinguishable · CMF **larger** and **active past skeletal maturity**

**MRI** — **T1 low**, **T2 high**, lobular growth · distinct margin

**CT** — low attenuation, peripheral bone rim, subtle matrix

**Bone scan** — ↑ uptake

### Histology (diagnostic criteria)

**Histology mandatory** for diagnosis

**Low power — biphasic** — **hypercellular** lobules of fibromyxoid tissue (spindle/stellate cells, hyperchromatic nuclei; giant cells + fibrovascular tissue between lobules) + **hypocellular chondroid** areas

**High power** — myxoid stroma with **stellate cells** · may have pleomorphic bizarre nuclei

![Stellate cells in myxoid stroma](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8022-stellate-cells-moved.jpg)

### Differential

| Radiographic DD | Histologic DD |
|---|---|
| ABC · chondroblastoma · **NOF** | Chondroblastoma · enchondroma · **chondrosarcoma** |

| Lesion | Distinguishing feature |
|---|---|
| **CMF** | **Stellate cells**, spindle cells, myxoid-cartilaginous matrix |
| NOF | **Storiform** bland fibroblasts, scattered giant cells |
| Enchondroma | Calcifications · bland hyaline cartilage, pyknotic nuclei |
| GCT | Radiolucent, not bubbly · giant-cell nuclei = stromal nuclei |
| Chondrosarcoma (conventional) | **Older (50+)** · uncommon in hands/feet · cortical erosion, soft-tissue mass |
| Atypical cartilaginous tumour | (= grade 1 extremity chondrosarcoma, WHO) · like large enchondroma + endosteal scalloping |

## 5 Management

**Intralesional curettage + bone grafting (or PMMA)** — **mainstay**

**Not indicated** — observation (painful) · chemo + wide excision · radiotherapy · radical resection

## 6 Complications

**Local recurrence** — **~25%** (Orthobullets) · 20–30% · 20–25% (OB)

**Negative prognostic factors** — **children** · **more lobulated, abundant myxoid** tumour

**Metastasis** — **not reported**

## X vs Y

| | **CMF** | **Chondroblastoma** |
|---|---|---|
| Site | **Metaphysis** (proximal tibia) | **Epiphysis** |
| Histology | **Stellate cells**, lobules of fibromyxoid tissue | Polygonal chondroblasts, **chicken-wire** Ca |
| Calcification | Rare | Common (CT) |
| Lung mets | **None reported** | <1% |
| Treatment | Curettage + graft / PMMA | Curettage + adjuvant + graft / RFA |

## How it's asked

**"Adolescent wrist/foot pain, eccentric lytic metaphyseal lesion with sclerotic rim, spindle/stellate cells in myxoid matrix?"** → **chondromyxoid fibroma**\
✗ Wrong options: NOF (storiform) · enchondroma · chondrosarcoma · osteosarcoma

**"21-yr-old, large lobulated eccentric metaphyseal lesion with sclerotic rim?"** → **CMF**\
✗ Wrong options: NOF · ABC · atypical cartilaginous tumour

**"Ilium lesion, biphasic histology with stellate cells — treatment?"** → **curettage + bone grafting**\
✗ Wrong options: chemo + wide excision · radiotherapy

**"Recurrence rate?"** → **~25%** · higher in children

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Chondrosarcoma

*Pathology · 10 / 66*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Rank** — **2nd commonest malignant primary bone tumour** (after OS) · commonest primary bone sarcoma in the US (OB) · commonest primary bone malignancy **of the hand** though rare there **(TO)**

**Age** — conventional **40–75**, most **>50** · peak primary **40–60**, secondary **25–45** **(TO)** · slight **male** predominance

**Site** — **pelvis (25%)**, proximal femur, distal femur, proximal humerus, scapula, ribs · **proximal/axial** · metaphyseal or diaphyseal (epiphyseal only = **clear cell**)

**Location rule** — cartilage tumours of **hands/feet mostly benign** · **flat bones (pelvis, scapula, ribs, sternum) malignant until proven otherwise** · subperiosteal or joint-lining cartilage almost always benign **(TO)**

### Primary vs secondary

**Primary** — sporadic · **conventional >90%** (central intramedullary 99%, periosteal ~1%) · dedifferentiated <10% · clear cell <5% · mesenchymal <1%

**Secondary** — from pre-existing benign cartilage lesion · **osteochondroma >80%** of secondary · younger (3rd–4th decade) · usually **low grade** · ~40% of MHE secondaries in **ilium** · histologically = conventional

### Malignant transformation risk

| Precursor | Orthobullets | OB explanation | TO |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Solitary osteochondroma** | **<1%** | 1% | <1% (~1% lifetime) |
| **Solitary enchondroma** | 1–9% | 1% | 1% |
| **MHE** | **5–10%** | ~10% | 1–10% · ~5% lifetime |
| **Ollier disease** | **25–30%** | 30% | 5–50% · ~25% by age 40 |
| **Maffucci syndrome** | **>50%** | **~100%** (highest) | 25–100% |

**Maffucci** — multiple enchondromas + **soft-tissue haemangiomas** (and lymphangiomas) · also pancreatic/hepatic adenocarcinoma, ovarian, glioma/astrocytoma

**Axial/pelvic osteochondroma** — more likely to transform than peripheral

**Other precursors (TO)** — **synovial chondromatosis**, CMF, periosteal chondroma, chondroblastoma, **prior radiation**, fibrous dysplasia, **Paget** (OS + chondrosarcoma) · not **chronic osteomyelitis** → **squamous cell carcinoma** (Marjolin)

### Genes & lab markers

**IDH1 / IDH2** mutations — primary + secondary conventional chondrosarcoma **and enchondromas**

**HEY1–NCOA2** fusion — **mesenchymal** chondrosarcoma

**t(12;22)** — listed for **clear cell** (Orthobullets) · clear cell: **S-100 +, PAS +** (glycogen → clear cytoplasm)

**Telomerase activity ↑** (RT-PCR) — **directly correlates with recurrence**\
✗ Wrong options: MHC II · gadolinium uptake · apoptosis · technetium uptake

## 3 Presentation

**Pain** — commonest · **pain attributable to the lesion** (night pain, not activity related) separates low-grade CS from enchondroma (vs pain from cuff tear / meniscus)

**Pelvis** — slowly growing mass · **bowel/bladder obstruction** from mass effect

**Secondary** — **new pain or growth of an osteochondroma after skeletal maturity**

**Dedifferentiated** — **pathologic fracture ~50%**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Work-up & referral

**Incidental chondroid lesion on MRI** (meniscus work-up) — **first get orthogonal radiographs** → often simple enchondroma\
✗ Wrong options: CT CAP · whole-limb MRI · core biopsy · curettage

**Suspicious lesion at community hospital** — **refer to orthopaedic oncologist**\
✗ Wrong options: observe · curettage · chemo/RT

**Staging** — **CT chest** (lungs = main site) + MRI whole bone + bone scan\
✗ Wrong options: CT abdomen/pelvis (bone sarcomas rarely go to viscera) · SPEP/UPEP (myeloma)

**Unknown lytic lesion >40 yr** — CT chest/abdomen/pelvis (carcinoma work-up)

**Biopsy** — core needle or open (gold standard) · target **area of ↓ T2 signal** (focus of dedifferentiation) · TO: needle biopsy not indicated for cartilage tumours (heterogeneous) · diagnosis = **clinical + radiographic + histologic correlation**

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — orthogonal views mandatory · usually **>4 cm** · lytic with **"rings and arcs" / "popcorn"** calcification (punctate, comma-shaped) · seen on X-ray ~70%

![Popcorn calcification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8023-popcorn-calcifications.jpg)

**Aggressive signs** — **endosteal scalloping >2/3 cortex** · cortical destruction · periosteal reaction · bone expansion · soft-tissue mass · permeative borders · higher grade → less mineral, more lytic

**CT** — **best for low-grade cartilage lesions** (cortical involvement) · matrix seen more easily · **deep endosteal scalloping** → CS · **90% cortical breach**

**MRI** — **lobulated**, T1 low–intermediate, **T2 high** (cartilage water) with low-signal septa · **"rings and arcs" septal enhancement** · defines soft-tissue extent

**Bone scan** — very hot in all grades · **uptake > ASIS** characteristic (enchondroma < ASIS)

![Bone scan uptake greater than ASIS](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8023-bone-scan-chondrosarc.jpg)

**Secondary from osteochondroma** — **cartilage cap >2 cm** (≥2 cm) on MRI/CT in a mature patient · lysis of calcification · cortical destruction · cap **14 mm, no growth, mild symptoms → serial imaging + exam**\
✗ Wrong options: FNA · chest CT · bone scan · wide excision

### Enchondroma vs low-grade chondrosarcoma (diagnostic criteria)

| | **Enchondroma** | **Chondrosarcoma** |
|---|---|---|
| Age | **<50** | **>50** |
| Size | **<5 cm** | **>5 cm** |
| Site | **Hands/feet common** · axial/pelvis rare | **Axial/pelvis common** · hands/feet rare |
| Pain | No (or other pathology) | **Yes, attributable to lesion** |
| Soft-tissue mass | No | Yes |
| Cortical destruction | No | Yes |
| Periosteal reaction | No | Yes |
| **Endosteal scalloping** | **<2/3 cortex** | **>2/3 cortex** |
| Expansion | No | Yes |
| **Bone scan** | **< ASIS** | **> ASIS** |

### Histology & grading (conventional)

**Graded by** — **cellularity + cytological atypia** (nuclear size, hyperchromasia), myxoid stroma · mitoses rare, not used **(TO)**

**Diagnostic of malignancy** — **permeation and entrapment of pre-existing bone trabeculae** · hypercellularity, plump nuclei, >occasional **binucleate** cells

**Any malignant osteoid** — → **chondroblastic osteosarcoma**, not CS **(TO)**

![Entrapment of host trabeculae](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8023-trabeculae-entrapped.jpg)

| Grade | Share | Histology | Metastasis | 5-yr survival |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | 60% | Like enchondroma · bland hyaline, low cellularity, binucleate · needs clinico-radiological correlation | Almost never | **90%** |
| **2** | 25% | Plump **bi-/tri-nucleated** cells, ↑ cellularity, atypia, myxoid change | **10–15%** | **60–70%** |
| **3** | 5% | Hypercellular permeative stroma, little hyaline, **myxoid**, pleomorphic, mitoses · "blue balls" | **>50%** | **30–50%** |
| **Dedifferentiated ("4")** | ~10% | Bimorphic | High | **<20%** (10% at 10 yr) |

**WHO 2020** — **grade 1 extremity CS = atypical cartilaginous tumour (ACT)** · axial cartilage tumours = separate, more aggressive category

### Subtypes

| Subtype | Age | Site | Imaging | Histology | Treatment / prognosis |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Conventional** | 40–75 | Pelvis, prox femur, humerus | Rings & arcs, scalloping | Hyaline, graded 1–3 | **Wide resection alone** |
| **Dedifferentiated** | ~60 | As conventional | **Bimorphic**: aggressive **unmineralized lytic** mass next to mineralized chondroid · path fracture | Low-grade cartilage **abruptly** next to **high-grade spindle sarcoma** (OS > fibrosarcoma > MFH) · not admixed | Wide resection ± **chemo** · **<20% 5-yr**, **10% 10-yr** |
| **Clear cell** | **20–40** | **Epiphysis** · 2/3 **humeral/femoral head** | Well-defined lytic epi-metaphyseal, thin sclerotic rim, mixed lucent/sclerotic, no periosteal reaction · mimics **chondroblastoma** | **Large vacuolated clear cells**, giant cells · ~50% low-grade CS areas · S-100, PAS + | **Wide resection** (no chemo) · 10-yr ~80% · mets ~15% |
| **Mesenchymal** | **20–40** | **Flat bones** (ilium, ribs, skull) · **30% soft tissue** | Moth-eaten / permeative, extraosseous | **Small round blue cells** + hyaline islands · **staghorn (haemangiopericytomatous) vessels** · HEY1–NCOA2 | Wide resection + **chemo ± RT** · <50% 5-yr |
| **Periosteal (juxtacortical)** | 2nd–4th decade | Surface, metaphysis femur/humerus | Juxtacortical mass, rings & arcs · **cortex eroded/saucerized** (vs periosteal chondroma) | Low grade | Wide resection · indolent |
| **Secondary** | 3rd–4th decade | Ilium (MHE) | Cap >2 cm | As conventional | As primary · usually low grade |

![Dedifferentiated chondrosarcoma — bimorphic histology](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8023-chondrosarcoma-dedifferentiated-moved.jpg)

### Differential of chondrosarcoma

| | Malignant, older patient | Similar chondroid histology | Sacral lesion, older | Wide resection alone |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Chondrosarcoma** | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |
| Metastasis · lymphoma · myeloma · MFH | ✓ | — | ✓ | — |
| Enchondroma / Ollier / Maffucci · periosteal chondroma · MHE | — | ✓ | — | — |
| Parosteal OS · adamantinoma | — | — | — | ✓ |
| **Chordoma** | — | — | ✓ | ✓ |

**Imaging distractors** — **bone infarct** (smoke up the chimney, cold, asymptomatic) · **fibrous dysplasia** (ground glass) · **osteosarcoma** (younger, cloud-like bone) · **plasmacytoma** (uniformly T2 bright, **cold** scan) · Ewing (lytic, no mineral)

## 5 Management

### Treatment algorithm

```
Chondrosarcoma (chemo- and radio-resistant)
├─ Grade 1 / ACT, extremity, confined to bone → EXTENDED CURETTAGE + ADJUVANT + GRAFT ± FIXATION
│      └─ Asymptomatic, no aggressive features → OBSERVE
├─ Grade 2–3, or ANY pelvic/axial lesion → WIDE RESECTION ALONE
├─ Mesenchymal → WIDE RESECTION + CHEMO (± RT)
├─ Dedifferentiated → WIDE RESECTION ± CHEMO
└─ Surgically inaccessible → RADIOTHERAPY
```

### Treatment — wide resection (chemo/radioresistant)

**Why resistant** — **low mitotic rate**, **poor perfusion / low blood supply**, chondroid/**myxoid matrix** limits drug diffusion, few cells in growth phase

**Wide excision** — better survival than marginal or intralesional · grade 2 distal femur → **wide excision**\
✗ Wrong options: curettage · marginal · chemo first · hip disarticulation

**Pelvic CS** — **internal hemipelvectomy or hindquarter amputation** (✗ Wrong options: + chemo · + RT) · **wide resection alone** (✗ Wrong options: brachytherapy)

**Proximal fibula CS** — **wide resection + reconstruct LCL and biceps femoris** (transfer to lateral tibia, staple) → prevents varus instability + flexion weakness\
✗ Wrong options: curettage + liquid N₂ + cement (GCT) · denosumab (GCT) · + chemo (OS)

**Ollier patient with CS** — **wide resection only**

**Clear cell after "benign" curettage** — **wide resection alone**\
✗ Wrong options: chemo · curettage · forequarter

**Lung metastases** — resect if possible **(TO)**

### Low-grade lesion — extended curettage

**ACT / grade 1 extremity** — **extended intralesional curettage + high-speed burr + adjuvant + bone-void graft ± prophylactic fixation** · same recurrence as wide resection, better function

**Adjuvants** — cryotherapy / **liquid nitrogen**, argon beam, phenol, ethanol, hydrogen peroxide

**Pelvis / axial grade 1** — **wide excision** (higher recurrence + metastasis, poor biopsy concordance)

**Not for ACT** — neoadjuvant RT 50–60 Gy · proximal humeral resection + megaprosthesis (old standard) · forequarter amputation

### Dedifferentiated chondrosarcoma

**Treatment** — **wide resection + endoprosthetic reconstruction** · chemo possible role (controversial) · RT palliative/adjuvant only

**IM nail through dedifferentiated CS** — seeds entire femur → **amputation**\
✗ Wrong options: proximal femoral replacement · chemo · RT

**Curettage + cement + THA, histology dedifferentiated** — resect whole bed → **hemipelvectomy**

**Pathologic fracture + high-grade CS** — next test **CT chest**

## 6 Complications

**Local recurrence** — grade 1: rare after wide resection, **5–15% after curettage + adjuvant** · grade 2: margin dependent · grade 3: **25%** recurrence, **>30%** mets · high-grade recurrence → mets + ↓ survival

### Prognostic factors

**Most important** — **histological grade** **(TO — FNB 2016)**\
✗ Wrong options: site · matrix calcification · age

**Poor prognosis** — **high grade** · **axial / proximal** (pelvis) · **advanced age** · **inadequate margins** · **↑ telomerase** (recurrence)

**Not prognostic** — gender · negative margins, younger age, tumour-centre biopsy = **favourable**

**Spine/sacrum CS** — worse local control/survival with **increasing age**

## X vs Y

| | **Chondrosarcoma (rings & arcs)** | **Osteosarcoma (cloud-like)** | **Bone infarct** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Age | >50 | Adolescent | Any (risk factors) |
| Mineral | Rings, arcs, popcorn | Fluffy/cloud-like osteoid | Serpiginous rim, smoke up chimney |
| Bone scan | Hot (> ASIS) | Hot | Not hot |
| Symptoms | Night pain | Pain, mass | Asymptomatic |

| | **Chondrosarcoma** | **Mesenchymal CS** | **Ewing** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Round blue cells | No | ✓ + hyaline islands, staghorn vessels | ✓ sheets, CD99 |
| Chemo | No | Yes | Yes |

## How it's asked

**"52-yr-old, night shoulder pain, rings-and-arcs, hot bone scan, lobular T2-bright lesion?"** → **chondrosarcoma**\
✗ Wrong options: infarct · OS · Ewing · FD · plasmacytoma

**"Tumour resistant to chemo AND radiation?"** → **classic intramedullary chondrosarcoma**\
✗ Wrong options: Ewing · synovial sarcoma · chordoma (RT) · DFSP

**"Pelvic CS — treatment?"** → **wide resection alone** (internal hemipelvectomy or hindquarter amputation)

**"Grade 2 distal femoral CS?"** → **wide excision**

**"Symptomatic grade 1 ACT proximal humerus?"** → **extended intralesional curettage + graft ± prophylactic fixation**

**"Proximal fibula CS — best function?"** → **wide resection + LCL + biceps reconstruction**

**"Correct about dedifferentiated CS?"** → **~10% 10-yr survival**\
✗ Wrong options: de novo · homogeneous · curettage · adolescents

**"73-yr-old, pathologic fracture, high-grade spindle cells next to benign cartilage?"** → **dedifferentiated CS**\
✗ Wrong options: synovial sarcoma (keratin glands) · metastatic prostate · periosteal OS

**"Dedifferentiated CS with path fracture — staging test?"** → **CT chest**\
✗ Wrong options: CT CAP · SPEP/UPEP

**"IM nail placed through dedifferentiated CS?"** → **amputation**

**"38-yr-old, subchondral femoral head lesion, clear vacuolated cells?"** → **clear cell chondrosarcoma**\
✗ Wrong options: chondroblastoma · GCT

**"Highest malignant risk?"** → **Maffucci** > Ollier > MHE > solitary lesions

**"MHE — malignant histology?"** → **low-grade chondrosarcoma** (EXT1 worst)

**"Secondary CS NOT seen in?"** → **chronic osteomyelitis** (→ SCC) (✓ Paget, ✓ synovial chondromatosis, ✓ osteochondroma) **(TO)**

**"FALSE about CS (FNB 2024)?"** → source gives no answer · **"clear cell CS is predominantly metaphyseal"** is false (it is **epiphyseal**) **(TO)**

**"Most important prognostic factor?"** → **histological grade** **(TO)**

**"Poor prognostic factor in pelvic CS?"** → **pelvic location**\
✗ Wrong options: female · negative margins · younger age

**"Recurrence most directly related to?"** → **↑ telomerase activity**

**"Incidental 'possible chondrosarcoma' on knee MRI, no images — next?"** → **orthogonal radiographs**

**"Adult osteochondroma, 14 mm cap, stable?"** → **serial imaging + exam** (cap ≥2 cm = worry)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Chordoma

*Pathology · 11 / 66*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Rank** — **commonest primary malignant spinal tumour in adults** · commonest primary malignant tumour of the **sacrum** **(TO)** · 1–4% of primary bone tumours

**Demographics** — **M:F 3:1** · **>50 yr** (5th–6th decade) · middle-aged Caucasian men

**Site** — **sacrum + coccyx 50%** (S2–S3 predilection) · **spheno-occipital (skull base) 35%** · **mobile spine 15%**

### Pathoanatomy

**Origin** — malignant transformation of **residual notochordal cells** (same lineage as **nucleus pulposus**) → **midline** location

**Behaviour** — slow-growing, locally aggressive, low-grade malignant · diagnosis often delayed

### Genes & lab markers

**Keratin (pan-cytokeratin) +** — separates from chondrosarcoma (keratin −)

**EMA +** **(TO)** · **S-100 weakly +**

## 3 Presentation

**Pain** — insidious **low back / hip pain**, **worse sitting**

**Bowel/bladder** — **constipation**, faecal incontinence, bladder change · UTI symptoms (1/3) · cauda equina 10% **(TO)**

**Motor/sensory deficit** — **rare** (most lesions **S1 or distal**) · S1–S2 lesion may lose L5–S1 function · radiculopathy (sciatic) up to 1/3

**Rectal exam** — **>50% palpable**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — **hard to see** (overlying **bowel gas**) · large lytic lesion **crossing the midline** (>50%) → SI joints, ilium

**CT** — **midline bone destruction + soft-tissue mass** · **calcifications** in the soft-tissue component

**MRI** — **bright on T2** · soft-tissue extension

![Sacral chordoma — T2-bright midline mass](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8032-axialmri.jpg)

### Biopsy & histology

**Biopsy required** — MRI alone insufficient (lymphoma, chondrosarcoma mimic) · **transrectal biopsy contraindicated**

**Gross** — **lobular, gelatinous**

**Histology** — **physaliferous cells**: large, **foamy, vacuolated** (eosinophilic) cytoplasm, vesicular nucleus — **pathognomonic** · cords/lobules in **mucoid matrix** separated by fibrous septa · distinct nodules

![Physaliferous cells](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8032-histology-c-parsons-moved.jpg)

### Differential

| | Sacral lesion, older patient | Keratin + | Similar X-ray | Wide resection alone |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Chordoma** | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| **Chondrosarcoma** | Yes | No | Yes | Yes |
| Metastatic carcinoma | Yes | Yes | Yes | — |
| Lymphoma of bone | Yes | — | Yes | — |
| Myeloma · MFH | Yes | — | — | — |
| Adamantinoma (tibia) | — | Yes | — | Yes |
| Synovial sarcoma · epithelioid sarcoma | — | Yes | — | — |

**Other sacral tumours** — myeloma, **GCT**, ABC, metastasis

## 5 Management

### Treatment & local recurrence

**Standard** — **wide-margin en bloc resection** · **sacrifice sacral roots** to get margins · wide tumour-free margin ∝ tumour-free survival

**Radiation** — **add if margin not achieved** (positive margins) · **inoperable** tumours

**TO answer** — sacral chordoma → **wide resection alone** **(TO)** · chemo- and relatively radio-resistant; RT/SRS for recurrence\
✗ Wrong options: + EBRT · + chemo · curettage + bisphosphonate

**Mobile spine** — **en bloc corpectomy / total vertebrectomy** → best local control · needs anterior + posterior reconstruction

**Not indicated** — chemotherapy (chemoresistant) · debulking (intralesional) · RT alone · observation

### Radiosurgery (CyberKnife)

**Stereotactic radiosurgery** — recurrent/residual **chordoma** and **chondrosarcoma** after failed surgery + fractionated RT **(TO)**

**CyberKnife** — extracranial → **sacral chordoma** ✓ · **Gamma knife** — intracranial only **(TO)**

**Proton–photon beam** — may help recurrence

### Sacral nerve root sparing & function

| Roots preserved / resected | Bowel & bladder |
|---|---|
| **Bilateral S3** preserved | Near-normal (Orthobullets) · **S3 most critical** |
| **Both S2** preserved (at least) | Near-normal (OB) |
| **Unilateral S2, S3, S4 (± S5)** preserved | Near-normal ✓ (OB answer: unilateral S2–S5) |
| **Bilateral S4–S5 resected** | **Near-normal** (S2–S3 splanchnics kept) |
| **Bilateral S2–S5 resected** | **Complete loss** |
| Bilateral S1 / L5 only | Not enough |

**Pelvic splanchnic nerves** — **S2–S4** (S3 predominance) → parasympathetic to pelvic viscera

## 6 Complications

**Local recurrence** — **~50%**, margin dependent → **close surveillance**\
✗ Wrong options: nodal · liver · bone mets · malignant transformation

**Metastasis** — **30–50%**, **late** (lung, rarely bone) · OB: 30–50% already metastatic at diagnosis

**Survival** — **60% 5-yr**, **25% long-term** (25–50%) · local extension may be fatal

**Poor prognosis (TO)** — high histological grade · volume **>10 cm / 200 cm³**

**Post-op** — loss of bowel/bladder function (root sacrifice)

## X vs Y

| | **Chordoma** | **Chondrosarcoma** |
|---|---|---|
| Origin | Notochord, **midline** | Cartilage, often **eccentric** (pelvis) |
| Cells | **Physaliferous** | Atypical chondrocytes in lacunae |
| Keratin / EMA | **+** | **−** |
| S-100 | Weak + | + |
| Treatment | Wide resection ± RT (margin +) | Wide resection |

## How it's asked

**"Sacral lytic lesion, 58-yr-old, lobules with vacuolated physaliferous cells?"** → **chordoma**\
✗ Wrong options: chondrosarcoma · metastatic RCC · CMF · GCT

**"All true of sacral chordoma EXCEPT?"** → **"MRI is sufficient without biopsy"** (✓ notochordal like nucleus pulposus, ✓ mets 30–50%, ✓ motor deficit rare, ✓ resection ± RT)

**"Treatment of sacral chordoma?"** → **wide resection alone** (✗ Wrong options: + EBRT · + chemo) **(TO)** · OB: **wide surgical excision** (✗ Wrong options: chemo · debulking · RT alone)

**"L5 en bloc corpectomy is right for which histology?"** → **chordoma**\
✗ Wrong options: osteomyelitis · myeloma · LCH · lung adenocarcinoma

**"Why surveillance after resection?"** → **high local recurrence**

**"Keratin + and S-100 + sacral mass?"** → **chordoma**

**"Tumour treated by CyberKnife?"** → **chordoma** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: vertebral osteoblastoma · sacral OS · GCT

**"Near-normal bowel/bladder if which roots spared?"** → **unilateral S2–S5** (or both S2 / bilateral S3)

**"Bilateral S4–5 vs bilateral S2–5 resection?"** → **S4–5 patient near-normal**, S2–5 patient loses function

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Conventional Intramedullary Osteosarcoma

*Pathology · 12 / 66*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology & genetics

**Rank** — **most common primary sarcoma of bone** · **commonest non-haematologic primary bone malignancy** · ~20% of primary bone cancers **(TO)**

**Commonest primary malignant tumour of bone overall** — **multiple myeloma** **(TO — FNB 2021)**

**Age** — **bimodal**: 1st peak **2nd decade (10–14)**, **75% <20** · 2nd peak **>65** (secondary: Paget, infarcts, prior osteochondroma/osteoblastoma, **radiation**)

**Sex / race** — **M:F 1.5:1** · ↑ Black, Hispanic

**Site** — **metaphysis** of long bones (10% diaphyseal, <1% epiphyseal) · **distal femur > proximal tibia > proximal humerus > axial** (spine/sacrum) · fastest-growing physes

**Childhood tumour facts (TO)** — commonest tumour of infancy / extracranial / abdominal: **neuroblastoma** · commonest **solid tumour** of childhood: **brain tumour** · commonest **malignancy**: **leukaemia (ALL)** · commonest benign bone lesion: **NOF** · commonest benign bone tumour: **osteochondroma** · commonest malignant bone tumour: **osteosarcoma**

### Genes & lab markers

| Association | Gene / inheritance | Other features |
|---|---|---|
| **Hereditary retinoblastoma** | **RB1** (13q14), germline · **500–1000×** risk **(TO)** | Somatic RB1 loss in **60–70%** of sporadic OS |
| **Li-Fraumeni** | **TP53** (17p13), AD | Breast, brain, adrenocortical cancer · somatic TP53 in 20–50% |
| **Rothmund-Thomson** | **RECQL4**, AR | **Poikiloderma** (sun-sensitive rash), alopecia, juvenile cataracts, dental anomalies, short stature · OS, fibrosarcoma, gastric adenoCa, BCC/SCC |
| **Bloom** | **BLM**, AR | UV facial rash, short stature · OS, leukaemia, lymphoma |
| **Werner** | **WRN**, AR | **"Adult progeria"** — premature ageing, cataracts, osteoporosis |
| Fibrous dysplasia | — | Polyostotic > monostotic |

**Pathogenesis (TO)** — **RB1 + TP53 pathway inactivation nearly universal**, via complex structural variants / **chromothripsis** · **no pathognomonic translocation** · amplification **MDM2, CDK4, MYC** · (✗ Wrong options: MDM2/CDK4 = primary driver of conventional OS (that is **low-grade central / parosteal**) · RECQL4 in sporadic elderly OS)\
✗ Wrong options: like Ewing

**Not OS genes** — EWS-FLI1 (Ewing) · EGFR, IGF-R · VEGF (angiogenesis; overexpression = poor prognosis)

**Labs** — **↑ ALP and ↑ LDH** → aggressive disease, **poor prognosis** · very high LDH may mean mets

**IHC** — **SATB2 +** (all OS subtypes) **(TO)**

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — **pain + swelling**, night/rest pain · often blamed on sports injury ("red herring" fall) · median **4 months** to diagnosis · may have fever, weight loss

**Exam** — mass, tenderness, ↓ ROM, NV compression · pain with axial loading

**Pathologic fracture** — ~10%

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray (AP + lateral, whole bone)** — metaphyseal **medullary + cortical destruction** · **mixed blastic/lytic** (may be purely either) · **"sunburst" / "hair-on-end"** · **Codman triangle** · **cloud-like / fluffy** osteoid mineral · soft-tissue mass · usually **doesn't cross physis**

![Codman triangle and sunburst periosteal reaction](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8014-case-e-femur-shaft-xray-ap-parsons-moved.png)

**MRI (± contrast, entire bone + joint above and below)** — soft-tissue + **NV involvement** · resection level · **skip metastases** (= stage III) · **gold standard** local study

**CT chest** — **required at presentation** (lung mets) \
✗ Wrong options: chest X-ray (less sensitive) · CT abdomen/pelvis (adult unknown-lesion work-up)

**Bone scan (Tc-99m)** — **very hot** · bone mets, skip lesions · **superior to PET in OS** (PET better in Ewing) · PET-CT misses lung nodules <5–9 mm

**FNB 2024 image (Codman triangle, distal femur)** — "least likely appearance?" — answer not given · **onion peel** (Ewing) and **stippled calcification** (chondroid) don't fit OS · sunburst + Codman do **(TO)**

### Staging work-up

**Local** — **MRI of whole involved bone** (e.g. knee + tibia) · **Systemic** — **CT chest + whole-body bone scan**

**Pre-chemo baseline** — CBC, BMP, LFTs, UA · **echocardiogram** (doxorubicin) + **hearing screen** (cisplatin)

**Not needed** — bone marrow biopsy (Ewing) · skeletal survey (myeloma / NAT) · lymph node biopsy · PFTs · contralateral MRI · angiography · reactive-zone biopsy · EXT1/2 testing (MHE)

**Biopsy** — required · by/after discussion with **definitive surgeon** · core needle preferred · sample **peripheral soft-tissue part** · (✗ Wrong options: FNA · biopsy by IR at outside hospital (path fracture → sling, analgesia, stage, refer) · empirical antibiotics without biopsy)\
✗ Wrong options: necrotic centre · Codman triangle = reactive bone

### Histology — diagnostic criteria

**Two criteria** — **tumour cells produce osteoid** + **stromal cells frankly malignant** (atypia, high N:C, abnormal mitoses)

**Hallmark** — **"lacy" osteoid / woven bone** laid down directly by pleomorphic spindle cells

![Malignant cells producing lacy osteoid](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8014-histology-a-moved.png)

| Conventional subtype | Matrix | Mimic |
|---|---|---|
| **Osteoblastic (commonest)** | Abundant **lace-like osteoid**, plasmacytoid cells **(TO)** | — |
| Chondroblastic | Malignant hyaline cartilage + **focal osteoid** | Chondrosarcoma (any osteoid → OS) |
| Fibroblastic | Herringbone/storiform collagen, sparse osteoid | Fibrosarcoma, UPS |
| Giant-cell-rich | Many giant cells | **GCT** (no atypia/osteoid) |

### MSTS staging

**Commonest** — **IIB** (high grade, extracompartmental, no mets) · **90–95% high grade**, penetrate cortex early

**Metastasis at presentation** — **20–25%** (Enneking III) · **lung ~80%** (haematogenous) > **bone 16%** · bone mets without lung mets rare (5%)

**Skip metastases** — intraosseous (same bone, usually proximal) or **trans-articular** · **2–6%** (OB) · **10%** (Orthobullets) · prognosis = lung mets

### Osteosarcoma subtypes

| Location | Grade | Subtype | Key feature |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Intramedullary** | High | **Conventional** (this topic) | Mixed tissues |
| | High | **Telangiectatic** | Purely lytic, **ABC-like** blood lakes · **malignant cells in septa** |
| | High | **Small cell** | OS + Ewing look · **SATB2 +, CD99 − / focal**, no EWSR1 |
| | Low | **Low-grade central** (FD-like, desmoplastic-fibroma-like) | Mimics **fibrous dysplasia** · **MDM2/CDK4** amplification |
| **Surface** | Low | **Parosteal** | **Posterior distal femur**, "stuck-on" · **middle-aged females (3rd–4th decade)** · MDM2/CDK4 |
| | Intermediate | **Periosteal** | **Diaphysis femur/tibia** · **chondroblastic** lobules, sunburst |
| | High | High-grade surface | Like conventional, on surface · medullary involvement common |
| **Intracortical** | — | Rarest bony type | — |
| **Extraskeletal** | High | Soft-tissue OS | <5% · treat like STS |

### Differential

| Lesion | Imaging | Histology |
|---|---|---|
| **Osteosarcoma** | Mixed blastic/lytic, Codman, soft-tissue mass | **Lacy osteoid**, malignant stroma |
| Osteomyelitis | Lags 2 wk, lucency, sequestrum/involucrum | Neutrophils (acute), lymphocytes (chronic) |
| Eosinophilic granuloma | "Great mimicker", vertebra plana | Langerhans cells, eosinophils, no atypia |
| **Ewing** | Permeative, moth-eaten, onion skin | **Small round blue cells**, t(11;22) |
| Dedifferentiated CS | Bimorphic | Cartilage + high-grade spindle |
| Fibrosarcoma | Lytic, >55 yr | **Herringbone** spindle cells |
| Lymphoma of bone | Diaphyseal, moth-eaten, 35–55 yr | Mixed round cells |
| **Osteoblastoma** | Posterior spine, nidus | Benign osteoblasts, giant cells |
| Myositis ossificans | **Mature periphery** | Mature bone, no atypia |
| UPS | — | Spindle cells, **no osteoid** |

## 5 Management

### Treatment — neoadjuvant chemotherapy & wide resection

```
High-grade intramedullary OS (stage IIB)
├─ First → NEOADJUVANT MAP CHEMO (8–12 wk) → RESTAGE
├─ Resectable → WIDE RESECTION (limb salvage) → ADJUVANT CHEMO 6–12 mo
│      └─ Positive margin → ADDITIONAL RESECTION ± RT
├─ Child, distal femur / epiphysis / joint contaminated → ROTATIONPLASTY
└─ Functional + disease-free limb impossible → AMPUTATION
```

**Sequence** — **neoadjuvant chemo → wide resection → adjuvant chemo** · surgery 3–4 wk after last preop dose · chemo restarts within ~2 wk of surgery\
✗ Wrong options: surgery + RT · chemo + RT

**MAP** — **high-dose Methotrexate (leucovorin rescue) + Adriamycin (doxorubicin) + cisPlatin** (US standard) · ± ifosfamide · 3-drug > 2-drug (AP) by ~10% survival despite toxicity **(TO)** · **EURAMOS-1**: adding ifosfamide + etoposide (MAPIE) for poor responders → no benefit\
✗ Wrong options: vincristine/carboplatin regimens (Ewing)

| Agent | Mechanism | Toxicity |
|---|---|---|
| **Methotrexate** | Dihydrofolate reductase inhibitor | Myelosuppression, mucositis |
| **Doxorubicin** | Topoisomerase II inhibitor | **Cardiotoxicity** → echo |
| **Cisplatin** | Covalent DNA binding | **Renal failure, hearing loss**, neurotoxicity → audiogram |
| Ifosfamide | DNA alkylating | Renal failure, **haemorrhagic cystitis** |

**Chemo effectiveness** — same preop or postop (OB)

**Radiation** — **radio-resistant** (NF-κB inhibits JNK-mediated apoptosis) · only palliation, unresectable sites, close margins (pelvis/spine) · **ECRT** — extracorporeal 50 Gy single fraction to sterilise and re-implant bone **(TO)** · **extraskeletal OS radiosensitive** (exception)\
✗ Wrong options: curative for sacral OS **(TO)**

**Pathologic fracture** — limb salvage still possible if stabilised (ex-fix/cast) and good chemo response (healing, ↓ pain/swelling, ↑ ossification, loss of tumour blush on angiogram)

### Limb salvage principles

**Biopsy tract** — **resected en bloc** with specimen, never exposed **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: routine sacrifice of popliteal vessels · keep capsule/pseudocapsule · cut at visible tumour edge

**Bone cut** — **2–3 cm beyond intramedullary extent on T1 MRI** **(TO)** · marrow sampled at cut

**Limb salvage achievable** — **85–90%** · survival **= amputation**

**Contraindications (TO)** — NV bundle encasement · path fracture with massive haematoma contaminating compartments · no clean margin · infection

**Amputation indications** — functional + disease-free limb impossible (only absolute) · NV encasement · growing on chemo next to NV bundle · bad biopsy · infected tumour

**Physeal-sparing** — not when tumour crosses physis (knee OS usually sacrifices articular surface) · transepiphyseal resection only if epiphysis free

### Resection margins (Enneking)

| Margin | Plane |
|---|---|
| **Intralesional** | Through tumour · **tumour at marrow margin** = intralesional |
| **Marginal** | Through **reactive zone / pseudocapsule** |
| **Wide** | Cuff of normal tissue 360° ("orange rind") |
| **Radical** | Whole compartment (e.g. entire femur) |

### Rotationplasty

**Indications** — distal femur / knee / proximal tibia / hip sarcomas in **young children** · **joint contamination** · epiphyseal involvement · failed salvage with intact leg · PFFD · avoids LLD

**Requirement** — **functioning, uninvolved sciatic nerve**

**Technique** — resect segment, **rotate leg 180°**, fix tibia to femur · **ankle → knee** · gastrocsoleus = quadriceps, anterior compartment = hamstrings · keep sural branches

**Winkelmann** — **A** (ankle = knee): **AI distal femur**, AII proximal tibia · **B** (knee = hip): BI proximal femur sparing hip, **BII involving hip**, BIII total femur

**Outcome** — **highest self-reported function, fewest revisions** in pre-adolescents · cosmesis is the drawback\
✗ Wrong options: endoprosthesis (loosening, LLD) · allograft (fracture, infection) · AKA (energy cost)

![Van Nes rotationplasty](../../MCQ/attachments/q3495-illa-rotationplasty-1.jpg)

### Megaprosthesis outcomes

**Modular endoprosthesis** — gold standard reconstruction · rotating hinge (ligaments sacrificed) · cemented if poor bone or early chemo · press-fit for long survivorship

**Henderson failure types (TO)** — 1 soft-tissue · 2 **aseptic loosening** · 3 structural · 4 **infection (5–15%)** · 5 tumour recurrence

**Infection** — **2–10%** (Orthobullets) · 12–19% at 5–10 yr (proximal tibia) · risks **op time ≥8 h**, **drain ≥14 d** · ~50% → amputation

**PARITY trial** — **5-day ≠ better than 1-day** antibiotics · **flaps ↓ infection** · **NPWT → ↑ reoperation, not infection** · longer op time → ↑ complications · **primary malignant tumour → higher short-term reoperation** than benign aggressive

**Aseptic loosening** — ~6% · commonest failure · **tibia** commonest mechanical failure

**Proximal tibial EPR + medial gastrocnemius flap** — good function (flexion 98–110°, lag <10°) but **infection, extensor mechanism failure (3–17%), reoperation** significant · patella alta up to 70%

**Allograft** — fracture at host–graft junction, nonunion (chemo, RT, ECRT risk factors)

### Extraskeletal osteosarcoma

**vs conventional** — <5% of OS · **older (5th–6th decade)** · nearly always **high grade** · **worse survival (~50% vs ~75%)** · chemo unproven · **radiation is part of standard treatment** (wide resection + RT, like STS)

## 6 Complications

### Prognostic factors (necrosis, MDR, margins, metastasis)

**Most important** — **stage / metastasis at diagnosis**

**Chemo response** — **>90% necrosis = good** (5-yr ~85%)\
✗ Wrong options: 60% or 70% necrosis = poor

| Huvos grade | Necrosis | Response |
|---|---|---|
| I | 0–50% | Poor |
| II | 51–90% | Poor (e.g. 88% → continue MAP) |
| III | 91–99% | Good |
| IV | 100% | Complete |

**Poor prognosis** — mets · skip lesions · **<90% necrosis** · **age >40** · male · axial/pelvic site · **large size (≥5 cm)** · **MDR / P-glycoprotein** (25% primary, 50% mets) · **VEGF** · **↑ ALP / ↑ LDH** · vascular involvement · **positive margins** · radiation-induced or Paget OS

**Not poor (TO)** — ✓ **>90% necrosis** is good · urinary heat-shock protein, serum proteoglycan C, unchanged DNA ploidy listed as poor-prognosis distractors

**Local recurrence** — most directly **surgical margins**\
✗ Wrong options: site, size, grade, neoadjuvant chemo

**Radiation-induced OS (TO)** — **worse prognosis** than conventional · **Arlen criteria**: different prior histology, in radiation field, long latency, histological proof

| Situation | 5-yr survival |
|---|---|
| Localised, standard treatment | **~65%** (extremity 65%, **pelvis 55%**) |
| Good chemo response | **85%** |
| Pulmonary mets at diagnosis | **15–20%** (resectable 15–25%) |
| Late (>1 yr) lung mets + thoracotomy | ~30% |
| Bone mets | Incurable · <5% long term |
| Paget OS | <15% long term |

**Pelvic OS** — larger, more mets, local recurrence **up to 44%** despite negative margins

**Metastasectomy** — complete resection of primary + mets → **5×** long-term survival

**Surveillance (NCCN)** — q3 mo yr 1–2 · q4 mo yr 3 · q6 mo yr 4–5 · yearly after · exam, imaging of site + chest, ± bone scan/PET, ALP

## X vs Y

| | **Conventional OS** | **Parosteal OS** | **Periosteal OS** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Grade | **High** | **Low** | Intermediate |
| Age | 10–20 | **3rd–4th decade, F** | Slightly older |
| Site | Metaphysis, medullary | **Posterior distal femur**, surface | **Diaphysis** femur/tibia |
| X-ray | Sunburst, Codman | **Stuck-on**, heavily ossified, central > peripheral | Sunburst on surface, saucerized |
| Histology | Lacy osteoid, malignant stroma | Bland spindle cells, parallel trabeculae | Chondroblastic lobules |
| Treatment | **Chemo + wide resection + chemo** | **Wide resection alone** | Chemo (NCCN) + wide resection |

| | **Osteosarcoma** | **Myositis ossificans** |
|---|---|---|
| Ossification | Dense **centre** | **Mature periphery (zonal)** |
| Histology | Atypia, lacy osteoid | Mature bone, bland fibroblasts |

## How it's asked

**"12-yr-old, distal femoral metaphysis mixed sclerosis/lucency, aggressive periosteal reaction?"** → **high-grade intramedullary OS** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: parosteal · periosteal · telangiectatic

**"Spindle stroma producing lace-like osteoid with malignant osteoblasts — subtype?"** → **osteoblastic OS** **(TO)**

**"Accurate statement on OS genetics?"** → **RB1 + TP53 inactivation nearly universal, structural variants** **(TO)**

**"Gene mutation predisposing to OS?"** → **RB**\
✗ Wrong options: EWS-FLI1 · EGFR · IGF-R · VEGF

**"Rothmund-Thomson predisposes to?"** → **osteosarcoma**

**"Commonest sites in order?"** → **distal femur > proximal tibia > proximal humerus > axial**

**"Commonest metastatic site?"** → **lung** (then bone)

**"Initial staging?"** → **bone scan + MRI of knee AND whole tibia + CT chest**\
✗ Wrong options: chest X-ray · PET alone · reactive-zone biopsy

**"Next test for proximal tibial OS?"** → **MRI of whole tibia incl. knee + ankle**\
✗ Wrong options: skeletal survey · CT CAP · bone marrow biopsy · FNA

**"Additional work-up?"** → **whole-body Tc bone scan**\
✗ Wrong options: CT abdomen/pelvis · angiography

**"Before MAP chemo?"** → **echocardiogram + hearing screen**\
✗ Wrong options: marrow biopsy · PFTs

**"Child with proximal humeral path fracture before transfer — NOT appropriate?"** → **IR biopsy at referring hospital**

**"Febrile teen, aggressive femoral lesion — next?"** → **biopsy**\
✗ Wrong options: IV antibiotics

**"First step in management?"** → **neoadjuvant chemotherapy**\
✗ Wrong options: resection first · AKA · RT · repeat biopsy

**"Protocol maximizing survival?"** → **neoadjuvant chemo → excision → adjuvant chemo**

**"Systemic regimen?"** → **high-dose methotrexate + doxorubicin + cisplatin (MAP)**\
✗ Wrong options: + etoposide · vincristine

**"Which patient gets neoadjuvant chemo + wide resection + adjuvant chemo?"** → **17-yr-old pelvic OS**\
✗ Wrong options: chondrosarcoma · adamantinoma · synovial sarcoma t(X;18) · myxoid liposarcoma t(12;16)

**"Parosteal vs intramedullary OS treatment?"** → **parosteal: wide resection alone · intramedullary: neoadjuvant chemo then resection**

**"Distal femoral OS in an 11-yr-old?"** → **chemo → wide resection distal 1/3 femur + custom (expandable) implant → chemo**\
✗ Wrong options: physeal-sparing intercalary allograft when physis crossed

**"8-yr-old, knee joint contaminated — best local control + function?"** → **rotationplasty**\
✗ Wrong options: through-knee amputation (contaminated margin) · AKA

**"Pre-adolescent, best self-reported outcome + fewest revisions?"** → **Van Nes rotationplasty**

**"Correct rotationplasty for distal femur?"** → **Winkelmann AI**\
✗ Wrong options: BII

**"Most vital limb-salvage principle?"** → **resect biopsy tract en bloc** **(TO)**

**"Tumour at marrow resection margin — margin type?"** → **intralesional**

**"Greatest risk for local recurrence?"** → **surgical margins**

**"Poor prognostic factor?"** → **60% necrosis** / **MDR expression** / **↑ ALP**\
✗ Wrong options: 95% necrosis · stage IA · 4.5 cm · normal LDH

**"All poor prognostic EXCEPT?"** → **>90% tumour necrosis** **(TO)**

**"True statement (all others false)?"** → **radiation-induced OS has worse prognosis than conventional** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: MAP not preferred · curative RT for sacral OS · parosteal in adolescent girls

**"Extraskeletal vs conventional OS?"** → **radiation is part of standard treatment**\
✗ Wrong options: younger · low grade · better survival

**"Megaprosthesis complications — true?"** → **primary malignant tumour → higher short-term reoperation**\
✗ Wrong options: 5-day antibiotics help · NPWT ↑ infection · flap ↑ infection

**"Most common site of parosteal OS?"** → **back of distal femur** **(TO)**

**"Correct subtype correlation?"** → keyed: **periosteal = intermediate-grade surface, chondroid** (stem adds "early metastasis" though the explanation says lower metastatic rate) \
✗ Wrong options: small cell strongly CD99 + · parosteal high grade on tibia · jaw OS more mets **(TO)**

**"Most common tumour in children?"** → **brain tumour** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: neuroblastoma · OS

**"Most common primary malignant bone tumour?"** → **multiple myeloma** **(TO)**

**"45-yr-old, proximal humeral path fracture, moth-eaten?"** → **lymphoma** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: OS · Ewing · chordoma
