# Dermatofibrosarcoma Protuberans

*Pathology · 13 / 66*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **1% of soft-tissue sarcomas** · rare, **low-grade fibrogenic cutaneous** sarcoma arising in the **dermis**

**Demographics** — **20–50 yr** (peak 4th decade) · **male** (3:2) · **African Americans 2:1**

**Site** — **trunk (47%) > proximal extremities > head/neck (14%)**

### Variants

**Bednar tumour** — **pigmented** · **7.5× more common in African Americans** · melanin-containing dendritic cells

**Fibrosarcomatous (DFSP-FS)** — 5–15% · **herringbone** pattern · more aggressive, poor prognosis

### Genes & lab markers

**t(17;22)** — **COL1A1–PDGFB** fusion → PDGF-β overexpression → target for **imatinib**

## 3 Presentation

**Mass** — **slowly enlarging, painless**, firm irregular **nodules** · dark red / blue discoloration · early pink/violet plaques with telangiectatic skin

**Late** — ulceration, attachment to deep structures · symptoms 6 months to 30 years · often misdiagnosed and **excised before sarcoma suspected**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**MRI** — extent + depth · preop for large, atypical, recurrent lesions

**CXR** — pulmonary mets in high-risk cases (recurrence, fibrosarcomatous)

**CT** — bone involvement / mets · **US** — local + nodal monitoring · **PET** — metastatic monitoring

### Histology

**Biopsy required**

**Classic** — **uniform fibroblasts in a storiform pattern** around inconspicuous vessels · diffusely infiltrates dermis/subcutis · **interdigitates with fat ("honeycomb")** at deep margin

![Storiform fibroblasts](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8066-28707.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Treatment — wide excision

**Resectable DFSP** — **wide excision** (with frozen sections) · **2–3 cm margins** (2.5–3 cm skin cuff) **including deep fascia**

**After "lumpectomy"** — **wide tumour bed re-excision**\
✗ Wrong options: observation · marginal resection · RT alone · chemo

**Adjuvant RT (50–70 Gy)** — **positive margins**, large tumours, or when wide excision → major cosmetic/functional deficit

**Imatinib** — PDGF-receptor tyrosine kinase inhibitor · **unresectable, recurrent, metastatic** adult DFSP · **65% response** · **no response without t(17;22)**

**Not indicated** — intralesional excision + RT · neoadjuvant chemo · Mohs referral (OB answer: wide excision)

## 6 Complications

**Local recurrence common** — body **15.7%** · **head/neck 51.8%**

**Distant metastasis rare (<5%)** — **lung** (haematogenous) · usually after **multiple local recurrences**

**Poor prognosis** — regional nodes · **fibrosarcomatous** change · ↑ mitoses, cellularity, aneuploidy, **TP53** overexpression · **age >50**

## X vs Y

| | **Classic DFSP** | **Fibrosarcomatous DFSP** | **Bednar** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Pattern | **Storiform** | **Herringbone** | Storiform + melanin dendritic cells |
| Behaviour | Low grade | Aggressive, can metastasize | Low grade |

## How it's asked

**"45-yr-old, leg mass with skin ulceration, storiform spindle cells?"** → **dermatofibrosarcoma**\
✗ Wrong options: Ewing/PNET · extraskeletal myxoid CS · myxoid liposarcoma · synovial sarcoma (deep, rarely ulcerate)

**"Shoulder skin mass after excisional biopsy shows DFSP — next?"** → **wide tumour-bed resection**

**"Abdominal wall nodular mass, t(17;22), herringbone areas?"** → **wide excision** (± RT)\
✗ Wrong options: Mohs · marginal · neoadjuvant chemo

**"Systemic agent for unresectable DFSP?"** → **imatinib** (only if t(17;22))

**"Tumour treated with chemo + RT (in a list of chemo/RT-resistant tumours)?"** → **DFSP** (vs classic chondrosarcoma resistant to both)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Differential Groups

*Pathology · 14 / 66*

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Workup of incidental lesion

**Principle** — a radiograph suggesting a **potentially malignant process** needs **complete staging before any treatment**, even if it looks like GCT

**Next step** — **cross-sectional imaging (MRI) of the limb** · then chest imaging, labs, ± bone scan → sarcoma centre referral if needed

**Severe pain, insists on treatment today** — **admit for parenteral analgesia + tumour work-up**\
✗ Wrong options: outpatient opioids

**Not appropriate first** — intra-articular steroid injection · physiotherapy + NSAIDs · hip radiograph

**Starting treatment before oncologic work-up** — can be devastating (contamination, lost limb salvage)

```
Aggressive-looking bone lesion on X-ray
├─ First → MRI OF THE LIMB (local staging)
├─ Then → CHEST IMAGING + LABS ± BONE SCAN
└─ Then → BIOPSY AT TREATING CENTRE → DEFINITIVE TREATMENT
```

## How it's asked

**"50-yr-old, obese, knee pain, aggressive proximal tibial lesion, wants treatment today — next?"** → **MRI of the lower extremity**\
✗ Wrong options: steroid injection · opioids · PT + NSAIDs · hip X-ray

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Enchondromas

*Pathology · 15 / 66*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Rank** — **2nd commonest benign cartilage lesion** (osteochondroma 1st)

**Demographics** — **M = F** · **20–50 yr** (3rd–4th decade)

**Site** — **medullary, metaphysis or diaphysis** · **hand 60%** > feet · distal femur 20% > proximal humerus 10% > tibia · metaphyseal when first seen → appears diaphyseal as bone grows

**Hand** — **commonest primary bone tumour of the hand** (42% in small tubular bones) \
✗ Wrong options: periosteal chondroma · subungual exostosis · osteoid osteoma

### "Most common" list (TO)

| Category | Answer |
|---|---|
| **Tumour of the hand (overall)** | **Squamous cell carcinoma** **(TO keyed answer)** |
| Bone tumour of hand bones | **Enchondroma** |
| Malignant bone tumour of hand bones | **Chondrosarcoma** |
| Tumour of bone | **Metastasis** |
| Primary bone tumour | **Myeloma > OS > chondrosarcoma** |
| Benign bone tumour | **Osteochondroma** |
| True benign bone tumour | Osteoid osteoma |
| Lesion of bone | Fibrous cortical defect |
| Malignant bone tumour, 1st decade | Ewing |
| Radiation-induced tumour | **OS > fibrosarcoma > MFH** |
| Most radio-/chemosensitive | **Ewing** |
| Bone tumour of mandible | Ameloblastoma |
| Malignant bone tumour of chest wall | Chondrosarcoma (chest wall) |

### Pathophysiology

**Origin** — **incomplete endochondral ossification** · physeal chondroblasts **escape into the metaphysis** and proliferate\
✗ Wrong options: intramembranous

### Genes & lab markers

**IDH1 / IDH2** — enchondroma, Ollier (and chondrosarcoma)

**Ollier / Maffucci** — **sporadic, no inheritance pattern** **(TO)** · (✗ Wrong options: GNAS (McCune-Albright) · p53 · chromosome 6q13 (CMF) · PGE2/COX (osteoid osteoma))\
✗ Wrong options: AD (that is MHE/EXT)

## 3 Presentation

**Asymptomatic** — **incidental** on films (long bones, foot)

**Pathologic fracture** — typical in the **hand** after minor trauma

**Pain** — uncommon · usually **another cause** (cuff tendinopathy, cervical radiculopathy, ankle sprain, vascular claudication) · **rest/night pain** → think chondrosarcoma

**Syndromes** — shortening, bowing, angular deformity · bluish angiomas (Maffucci)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray (usually diagnostic)** — well-defined, **lucent, central medullary** lesion, **1–10 cm**, calcifies over time · **"popcorn" stippling, rings, arcs, whorls** · endosteal erosion **<50% (<2/3) of cortex** · hands/feet: expansion + thinning, may be purely lytic

![Enchondroma of the distal phalanx](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8018-case-e-distal-phalanx-xray-parsons-moved.png)

**MRI** — not needed for diagnosis · **lobular, T2 bright** (water), **no marrow oedema, no periosteal reaction** · "sled-runner tracks" · **medullary fill >90% → chondrosarcoma**

**Bone scan** — ↑ uptake (remodelling) but **< ASIS** (only 20% > ASIS) · identifies **polyostotic** disease

**CT** — optional, for endosteal scalloping

**Skeletal survey** — if polyostotic suspected (Ollier dysplasia, Maffucci phleboliths)

### Histology

**Gross** — blue-grey lobulated hyaline cartilage, scattered calcification

**Long bones** — **hypocellular, bland mature hyaline cartilage ("blue balls") separated by normal marrow** · encased by lamellar bone · abundant matrix, **no myxoid** component · **small bland chondrocytes in lacunae**, no pleomorphism/mitoses/binucleation

**Hand / small tubular bones / fibula** — **hypercellular, mild atypia** → not malignant

**Biopsy** — rarely helpful · sampling error, can't separate from low-grade CS (SLICE study: poor reliability)

### Enchondroma vs chondrosarcoma

| | **Enchondroma** | **Chondrosarcoma** |
|---|---|---|
| Age | 20–50 | >50 |
| Size | <5 cm | **>5 cm** |
| Pain | None / other cause | **Rest + night pain** |
| Endosteal scalloping | **<50% (<2/3) cortex** | **>2/3 cortex** |
| Cortex | Intact | Thickened, destroyed, periosteal reaction |
| Soft-tissue mass | No | Yes |
| MRI | No oedema | Oedema, >90% medullary fill |
| Bone scan | < ASIS | > ASIS |
| Histology | Hypocellular, lacunar cells, normal marrow between | Hypercellular, plump binucleate nuclei, permeation |

**Bone infarct** — **smoke up the chimney**, **low T2** (vs bright T2 enchondroma)

### Enneking staging

**Enchondroma = Enneking stage 1 (latent)** (with NOF) \
✗ Wrong options: ABC, CMF, chondroblastoma = 2 · GCT = 3

### Cartilage-origin tumours (TO)

**WHO** — osteochondroma, enchondroma, **chondroblastoma, CMF** all cartilage tumours → "all are cartilage tumours EXCEPT" → **none** **(TO)** · WHO 5th ed. moved chondroblastoma + CMF to **benign** (no intermediate cartilage tumour now)

## 5 Management

### Latent lesion — observation vs low-grade chondrosarcoma

```
Enchondroma
├─ Asymptomatic / pain from another cause → OBSERVE, X-rays at 6 + 12 mo (3–6 mo)
├─ Growth / change on serial X-ray, true lesional pain → EXTENDED INTRALESIONAL CURETTAGE + GRAFT
├─ Hand pathologic fracture → CURETTAGE + GRAFT (immediate = delayed)
└─ Aggressive features (scalloping >2/3, mass) → REFER ORTHO ONCOLOGY, treat as chondrosarcoma
```

**Observation** — vast majority · serial radiographs · long-term follow-up for syndromes · no biopsy, MRI, CT, bone scan or CT chest/abdomen (even with prostate cancer history if benign-looking)

**Curettage + graft (or cement) indications** — **change on serial X-rays** · **symptomatic** · **pathologic fracture** · suspicious for low-grade CS · large lesion at risk of re-fracture · local recurrence unusual

**Not indicated** — chemo, RT (radiation-induced sarcoma), RFA, wide resection + arthroplasty (over-treatment), amputation

### Pain from another source

**Treat the real cause** — shoulder pain with overhead activity → **cuff** (PT + observe lesion) · deltoid paraesthesia + elbow flexion weakness → **cervical spine X-rays (C5)** · ankle sprain instability → **functional rehab, proprioception** · walking pain relieved by standing still, ABI 0.8 → **vascular claudication**

### Hand enchondroma & pathologic fracture

**Painful/growing thumb lesion** — **curettage + bone grafting**\
✗ Wrong options: amputation · chemo · RT

**Pathologic fracture** — **curettage + grafting** · **immediate = delayed** (immobilise then curette) for consolidation, DASH, recurrence, complications — only difference is **longer recovery** with delayed · void fillers: autograft, calcium phosphate/sulfate, PMMA, coralline HA

**Hand chondrosarcoma** — wide resection / **ray resection**

## 6 Complications

### Ollier disease & Maffucci syndrome

| | **Ollier** | **Maffucci** |
|---|---|---|
| Lesions | **Multiple enchondromas** (metaphyses + diaphyses, hands) | Multiple enchondromas + **soft-tissue haemangiomas/angiomas** |
| Inheritance | **Sporadic, none** | Sporadic, none |
| Bones | **Dysplastic, short, bowed**, LLD | Marked expansion · **calcified phleboliths** |
| Chondrosarcoma risk | **25–30%** (Orthobullets) · 10–30% · "<30%" · ~25% (OB) | **25–30%** (Orthobullets) · **>50%** (OB) |
| Other malignancy | — | **Visceral + CNS** (astrocytoma, GI adenocarcinoma) → any malignancy **up to 100%** |

**Solitary enchondroma** — malignant transformation **<1%** (~1%)\
✗ Wrong options: 5–8%

**Secondary chondrosarcoma** from enchondroma — usually **low grade** **(TO)**

![Ollier disease — multiple hand enchondromas](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8018-olliers-hand.jpg)

## X vs Y

| | **Enchondroma** | **Osteochondroma** | **Periosteal chondroma** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Location | **Medullary** | **Surface**, exophytic, cortical/medullary continuity | Surface, shallow cortical defect |
| Origin | Escaped physeal cartilage | Zone of Ranvier (EXT1/2 in MHE) | Periosteum |
| Treatment | Observe | Observe / marginal excision | Marginal excision |

| | **Enchondroma** | **Bone infarct** |
|---|---|---|
| X-ray | Rings and arcs throughout | Serpiginous sclerotic rim |
| T2 MRI | **Bright** | Low / central fat |

## How it's asked

**"70-yr-old, incidental proximal femoral cartilage lesion after fall?"** → **observation**

**"Prostate-cancer history, incidental benign-looking tibial chondroid lesion?"** → **reassure + repeat X-ray 3–6 mo**\
✗ Wrong options: biopsy · MRI · CT CAP + referral

**"Shoulder pain with overhead activity + calcified humeral lesion?"** → **observation** (treat cuff)\
✗ Wrong options: biopsy · curettage

**"Incidental lesion grew + new vague pain at 6 mo?"** → **extended intralesional curettage + grafting**\
✗ Wrong options: RFA · chemo · RT + wide resection

**"Night + rest pain, failed PT, humeral enchondroma?"** → **curettage**\
✗ Wrong options: CT CAP + bone scan · resection + TSA

**"Painful growing thumb enchondroma?"** → **curettage + bone grafting**

**"Pathologic fracture through 5th metacarpal enchondroma?"** → **curettage + bone grafting**\
✗ Wrong options: core biopsy · CT · wide excision

**"Immediate vs delayed curettage for phalangeal fracture?"** → **no difference in outcomes**

**"True of a hand enchondroma?"** → **curettage + grafting if pathologic fracture**\
✗ Wrong options: 5–8% malignancy · MRI needed · intramembranous · Enneking 2

**"Hand lesion where curettage + graft is definitive?"** → **distal phalanx enchondroma**\
✗ Wrong options: Ewing · subungual exostosis · chondrosarcoma · osteochondroma

**"Commonest bone tumour in the hand?"** → **enchondroma**

**"Most common tumour of the hand (FNB)?"** → **squamous cell carcinoma** **(TO)**

**"Hypocellular mature hyaline cartilage, bland lacunar cells — which X-ray?"** → **central lucent medullary lesion with rings/arcs**

**"Enchondromas + haemangiomas?"** → **Maffucci**\
✗ Wrong options: Ollier · Trevor = epiphyseal osteochondroma · Hunter

**">50% risk of malignancy?"** → **Maffucci**\
✗ Wrong options: Ollier · MHE · FD · McCune-Albright

**"Multiple enchondromas, bowing — inheritance?"** → **no known inheritance pattern**

**"Ollier — underlying mechanism?"** → **failure of normal endochondral ossification**\
✗ Wrong options: EXT · p53 · 6q13 · PGE2

**"Ollier — associated with?"** → **10–30% chondrosarcoma risk**\
✗ Wrong options: GNAS · angiomas · visceral malignancy

**"FALSE about chondroid lesions?"** → **"Ollier is autosomal dominant"** (✓ grade 1 appendicular → extended curettage, ✓ mesenchymal needs chemo, ✓ secondary CS low grade) **(TO)**

**"Enneking stage 1 lesion?"** → **enchondroma**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Eosinophilic Granuloma

*Pathology · 16 / 66*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Age** — **80% <20 yr** · commonly <10 · **M:F 2:1**

**Site** — skull, ribs, clavicle, scapula, mandible, pelvis · **spine (thoracic commonest)** · **diaphysis** of long bones

**Nature** — **self-limiting**, benign histiocytic lesion (Langerhans cell histiocytosis, LCH) · reticuloendothelial system

### LCH spectrum

| Form | Age | Features | Prognosis |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Eosinophilic granuloma** | Children/adolescents | **Single**, self-limited bone lesion | Excellent · spine lesions resolve spontaneously |
| **Hand-Schüller-Christian** | **>3 yr** | Chronic disseminated · **triad: multiple lytic skull lesions + diabetes insipidus + exophthalmos** · visceral (lung, liver, spleen, skin, nodes) → abdominal pain | Depends on chemo response · worse with more extraskeletal disease |
| **Letterer-Siwe** | **<3 yr** | Fulminant · skin rash from birth, multiple lesions | **Fatal** in early childhood |

### Genes & lab markers

**Genetics** — no clear inheritance or locus

**CD1a +** Langerhans cells · **Birbeck granules** on EM

## 3 Presentation

**Bone** — pain + swelling · **limp** (pelvis / lower limb)

**Spine** — local or diffuse back/neck pain · progressive **kyphosis** · radiculopathy if aggressive

**HSC** — **thirst / polydipsia** (DI) · **exophthalmos** · weight loss, skin lesions, abdominal pain

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**"Great mimicker"** — DD osteomyelitis, leukaemia, lymphoma, fibrous dysplasia, **Ewing**

**Diaphysis** — **well-defined intramedullary lytic "punched-out"** lesion · cortex thinned/expanded/destroyed · **periosteal reaction** possible

**Metaphysis** — up to, **not through, the physis** · less central

**Spine** — **vertebra plana** (flattened vertebral body) · ↑ kyphosis

![Vertebra plana](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8041-case-b-vertebra-plan-xray-parsons-moved.png)

**Skull** — **multiple punched-out lytic lesions**

**MRI** — adjacent soft-tissue mass · **Bone scan** — ↑ uptake · **Whole-body PET/CT** — staging if multifocal · skeletal survey finds extra lesions

### Vertebra plana — differential (TO)

| Cause | Vertebra plana? |
|---|---|
| **Eosinophilic granuloma / LCH** | ✓ **Commonest** |
| Ewing sarcoma | Yes |
| Lymphoma / leukaemia | Yes |
| Gaucher disease | Yes |
| ABC | Yes |
| Infection (spondylitis) | Yes |
| Metastasis (neuroblastoma) | ✓ occasionally |
| **Lyme disease** | No |

### Histology

**Langerhans cells** — mononuclear **histiocyte-like** cells, **oval nuclei** with **prominent nuclear groove ("coffee-bean")**, eosinophilic cytoplasm

**Stain** — **CD1a +**

**EM** — **Birbeck granules** — **tennis-racquet** shaped cytoplasmic organelles, diagnostic\
✗ Wrong options: Langerhans cell itself · plasma cell · Kupffer cell

![Birbeck granules on electron microscopy](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8041-birbeck-body-parsons-moved.jpg)

**Background** — **eosinophils** (bilobed nuclei), mixed inflammatory cells, giant cells

**No nuclear atypia, no atypical mitoses** — separates from Ewing, lymphoma, metastatic neuroblastoma

### Differential

| | Destructive lesion, young | Multiple lesions, young | Observation alone |
|---|---|---|---|
| **EG** | ✓ | — (HSC ✓) | ✓ (self-limited) |
| Osteomyelitis · OS · Ewing · desmoplastic fibroma | ✓ | — | — |
| Lymphoma · leukaemia | ✓ | ✓ | — |
| FD · Ollier/Maffucci · MHE · NOF/Jaffe-Campanacci | — | ✓ | ✓ |
| Haemangioendothelioma | — | ✓ | — |

## 5 Management

```
Eosinophilic granuloma
├─ Isolated, stable → OBSERVATION ± protected weight bearing, repeat X-ray 6 wk
│      └─ Symptomatic → CORTICOSTEROID INJECTION (± after curettage)
├─ Spine, no neuro deficit → BRACE / COLLAR + OBSERVE
│      ├─ Instability / neuro compromise → LOW-DOSE RT (600–800 cGy)
│      └─ Progressive deformity despite brace (~10%) → SURGICAL CORRECTION
├─ Impending fracture / articular surface at risk → CURETTAGE + GRAFT
└─ Disseminated HSC → CHEMOTHERAPY
```

### Treatment — observation

**Observation** — self-limited · heals spontaneously or after biopsy · femoral diaphysis: **crutches + repeat X-ray in 6 wk**\
✗ Wrong options: wide excision · chemo · RT alone · curettage first

**Cervical spine EG, no neuro deficit** — **cervical collar + observation**\
✗ Wrong options: antibiotics · chemo/RT · resection

**Bracing** — prevents kyphosis · corrects deformity **90%** · vertebrae regain ~**50%** height

**Low-dose RT 600–800 cGy** — spinal instability / neurology · lesions not amenable to injection/surgery

**Chemotherapy** — **only diffuse/disseminated HSC**

**Surgical decompression** — rare cord compression

## X vs Y

| | **EG / LCH** | **Ewing** | **Osteomyelitis** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Cells | Langerhans cells, coffee-bean nuclei, eosinophils | Small round blue cells, t(11;22) | Neutrophils |
| Marker | **CD1a**, Birbeck | CD99 | Culture |
| Atypia | None | Malignant | None |
| Treatment | Observe | Chemo + local control | Antibiotics ± debridement |

## How it's asked

**"5-yr-old, skin lesions, limp, weight loss, lytic femoral lesion with histiocytes?"** → **eosinophilic granuloma**\
✗ Wrong options: neuroblastoma · Ewing · ALL · fifth disease

**"2-yr-old, thirst, exophthalmos, lytic skull lesions?"** → **LCH (Hand-Schüller-Christian)**\
✗ Wrong options: abuse · NF · metastatic neuroblastoma · adamantinoma

**"6-yr-old, limp, skull + pelvic lesions, thirst, abdominal pain?"** → **Hand-Schüller-Christian**\
✗ Wrong options: McCune-Albright · Maffucci · Ollier

**"10-month-old, multiple skeletal lesions + rash since birth, CD1a +?"** → **LCH**\
✗ Wrong options: osteomyelitis · Ewing · lymphoma · neuroblastoma

**"Vertebra plana — EM structure (tennis racquet)?"** → **Birbeck granule**

**"7-yr-old, femoral diaphyseal EG?"** → **crutches + repeat X-ray in 6 weeks**

**"7-yr-old, cervical EG, no neuro deficit?"** → **cervical collar + observation**

**"Causes of vertebra plana — all EXCEPT?"** → **Lyme disease** (✓ EG, Ewing, lymphoma) **(TO)**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Epithelioid Sarcoma

*Pathology · 17 / 66*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Rank** — **commonest soft-tissue sarcoma of the hand and wrist** (others in hand: MFH, synovial sarcoma, clear cell sarcoma)\
✗ Wrong options: chondrosarcoma · rhabdomyosarcoma · myxoid liposarcoma · haemangiopericytoma

**Demographics** — adolescents + young adults **10–35** (10–40) · **M:F 2:1**

**Site** — hand/wrist, fingers · forearm, buttock/thigh, knee, foot

**Depth** — superficial → **ulcerates**, mimics **skin carcinoma, rheumatoid nodule, granuloma** · deep → attached to **tendons, tendon sheaths, fascia**

### Spread

**Regional lymph nodes** — **common** · unique, more frequent than other sarcomas (lungs still the commonest overall site)

**Spreads along** lymphatics and **fascial planes** · **30% metastasized at presentation**

**Lymphatic sarcomas — "RACES"** — **R**habdomyosarcoma · **A**ngiosarcoma · **C**lear cell sarcoma · **E**pithelioid sarcoma · **S**ynovial sarcoma\
✗ Wrong options: liposarcoma · fibrosarcoma · leiomyosarcoma · UPS

### Genes & lab markers

**Genetics** — unknown

**IHC** — **keratin +**, **EMA +**, vimentin +, **CD34 +**

## 3 Presentation

**Mass** — **small (3–6 cm), firm, painless, slow-growing** nodule · upper extremity · may **ulcerate**

**History** — often a **previous "benign" excision → recurrence** (misdiagnosed)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — **calcification 10–20%** · adjacent **bone erosion**

**MRI** — indeterminate · **T1 dark, T2 bright** · tendon-sheath nodule

### Histology

**Pattern** — **nodular with central necrosis** in granulomatous-looking areas → mimics **necrotizing granuloma** · chronic inflammatory cells at margins

**Cells** — epithelioid **ovoid/polygonal cells, eosinophilic cytoplasm** + spindle cells · dense hyalinized collagen · pleomorphism rare

![Nodules with central necrosis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8076-epithelioid-sarcoma-histology-low-power.jpg)

### Differential

| Lesion | Distinguishing feature |
|---|---|
| **Epithelioid sarcoma** | Young adult, nodules with **central necrosis**, keratin + |
| Rheumatoid nodule | Similar histology · older, RA |
| Squamous cell carcinoma | **Keratin pearls** |
| Angiosarcoma (soft tissue) | Well-formed **vascular channels** |
| Epidermal inclusion cyst | Benign appearance, low recurrence |
| Clear cell sarcoma / carcinoma | **Clear cells** |
| BPOP (Nora lesion) | Reactive HO, hands/feet, 3rd decade |

## 5 Management

**Wide excision + adjuvant radiotherapy** — all operable tumours

**Sentinel lymph node biopsy** — at excision → regional nodal staging

**Inadequate excision** — high rate of **multiple recurrences** → **re-excision of tumour bed**

**Amputation** — multiple recurrences, to stop spread

### Unplanned excision — re-resection

**Unplanned ("whoops") marginal resection** — residual tumour in **50–60%** → **repeat staging + re-resection of tumour bed** (± pre/post-op RT)\
✗ Wrong options: PT · AFO · tendon transfer · nerve exploration first

## 6 Complications

**Prognosis** — **extremely poor** · lymph node + lung metastasis · recurrence

## How it's asked

**"28-yr-old, recurrent ulcerating thumb mass, nodules with necrosis?"** → **epithelioid sarcoma**\
✗ Wrong options: rheumatoid nodule · SCC (keratin pearls) · angiosarcoma · inclusion cyst

**"Slowly growing finger nodule, nodular necrosis, no clear cells?"** → **epithelioid sarcoma**\
✗ Wrong options: clear cell sarcoma/carcinoma · BPOP

**"Commonest soft-tissue sarcoma of the hand?"** → **epithelioid sarcoma**

**"Sarcoma most likely to spread via lymphatics?"** → **epithelioid sarcoma** (RACES)

**"Site of metastasis more common than in other sarcomas?"** → **regional lymph nodes** → **sentinel node biopsy**

**"Foot drop after outside marginal excision of popliteal epithelioid sarcoma?"** → **staging + re-resection of tumour bed**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Ewing's Sarcoma

*Pathology · 18 / 66*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Rank** — **2nd commonest primary malignant bone tumour in children** (OS 1st) · 3% of paediatric malignancies, 10% of primary malignant bone tumours · ~3/million · commonest malignant bone tumour of the **1st decade** · rhabdomyosarcoma = commonest paediatric soft-tissue sarcoma

**Age** — **5–25** · 80–90% <20 · peak **10–15**

**Sex / race** — **M:F 1.5:1** · **rare in African Americans** and Asians

**Site** — even split axial vs appendicular · **metadiaphysis of long bones ~50%** (femur, tibia, humerus) · **pelvis ~25%** · scapula, ribs · soft tissue rare

**Diaphysis myth (TO)** — only **~50% diaphyseal**, 50% metaphyseal → "predominantly diaphyseal" is false

**Origin** — unknown, probably **neuroectodermal** (PNET family) · cell of origin likely in **marrow**

### Genes & lab markers

**t(11;22)(q24;q12)** — **EWS–FLI1** fusion (aberrant transcription factor: EWS activation domain + FLI ETS DNA-binding domain) · **85–95%** · detect by **PCR / FISH**

**Variants** — **t(21;22) EWS–ERG** (10–15%) · t(7;22)

**t(11;22) presence** — does **not** alter clinical course **(TO)**

| Translocation / fusion | Tumour |
|---|---|
| **t(11;22) EWS–FLI1** | ✓ **Ewing** |
| t(X;18) SYT–SSX | Synovial sarcoma |
| t(12;16) TLS–CHOP | Myxoid liposarcoma |
| t(12;22) EWS–ATF1 | Clear cell sarcoma |
| t(9;22) EWS–CHN | Extraskeletal myxoid chondrosarcoma |
| t(2;13) PAX3–FKHR | Alveolar rhabdomyosarcoma |
| t(X;17) ASPL–TFE3 | Alveolar soft part sarcoma |
| RB, p53 | Osteosarcoma (✗ Wrong options: Ewing) |

**Labs** — **↑ ESR, ↑ WBC, anaemia, ↑ LDH** → mimics infection, marks extensive disease

**Immunostains** — **CD99 / MIC2 + (95%)** · vimentin + · **PAS +** (glycogen) · NSE +, S-100 +, Leu7 (CD57) + · **cytokeratin −**, **reticulin −** (+ in lymphoma), neurofilament − (+ neuroblastoma), myoglobin − (+ embryonal rhabdo)

**CD45 (LCA)** — listed **+** in Orthobullets chart · TO/OB: **lymphoma is LCA +**, Ewing LCA − (OB calls LCA "CD34") — read the options

## 3 Presentation

**Pain** — commonest, worse at night · **>50% symptomatic >6 months** (delay worse in pelvis/axial)

**Infection mimic** — **swelling, erythema, warmth, fever (~20–25%)**, weight loss, ↑ WBC/ESR

**Mass** — may be impalpable until large · limp, ↓ ROM

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — large destructive **diaphyseal/metadiaphyseal** lesion · ill-defined **permeative, moth-eaten** · lytic ± reactive bone · **"onion skin"** (lamellated) or **"sunburst" / "hair-on-end"** periosteal reaction · Codman triangle · **soft-tissue mass >80%**

![Onion-skin periosteal reaction, tibial diaphysis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8047-case-b-tibia-xray-a-parsons-moved.png)

**MRI** — **next step after suspicious X-ray** · soft-tissue extent, marrow, NV relation · T1 low–intermediate, **T2 high heterogeneous**, heterogeneous enhancement · monitors chemo response

**Bone scan** — very hot · skip/distant mets (10%) · **PET better than bone scan for Ewing mets** (vs OS)

**CT chest** — required for lung mets

### Work-up (MRI, bone marrow biopsy)

**Bone marrow biopsy** — **required for Ewing** (marrow mets), **not routine for OS**\
✗ Wrong options: MRI · CT · bone scan · protein electrophoresis (done for both or neither)

**Order** — X-ray → **MRI** → staging (CT chest, bone scan, **bone marrow biopsy**) → **biopsy by treating surgeon**

**Not appropriate** — no further work-up · arthrogram · pelvic US · chiropractor · cast / rest and repeat X-ray · IV antibiotics / I&D when ESR/CRP normal

### Histology

**Gross** — grey-white, necrosis, haemorrhage · **liquid, "pus-like"**

**Micro** — **sheets of monotonous small round blue cells**, **high N:C ratio**, scant cytoplasm · **pseudorosettes (Homer-Wright)** · no stroma

![Small round blue cells](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8047-sa-histo-moved.jpg)

### Small round blue cell tumours — by age

| Age | Likeliest |
|---|---|
| **<5 yr** | **Metastatic neuroblastoma**, leukaemia |
| 5–10 yr | Eosinophilic granuloma |
| **5–30 yr (peak 5–15)** | **Ewing** |
| >30 (15–40) | **Lymphoma** |
| >40–50 | **Myeloma**, metastatic small cell lung Ca |
| 2nd–3rd decade (rare) | Small-cell OS, mesenchymal chondrosarcoma |

**Round-cell tumours** — Ewing, PNET, neuroblastoma, embryonal rhabdomyosarcoma, EG, lymphoma, myeloma, round-cell liposarcoma · not **leiomyosarcoma = spindle cells, cigar nuclei**

**TB** — small round cells (lymphocytes) but periarticular, joint

### Staging

**MSTS** — almost all **IIB or III** · **26–28% macrometastases at presentation** · sites **lung 50%, bone 25%, marrow 20%**

### Differential

| | Destructive, young | Small round cells | Wide resection + chemo |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Ewing** | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |
| Osteosarcoma | ✓ | — | ✓ |
| Lymphoma · leukaemia · EG | ✓ | ✓ | — |
| Osteomyelitis · desmoplastic fibroma | ✓ | — | — |
| Neuroblastoma · myeloma | — | ✓ | — |
| Dedifferentiated CS | — | — | ✓ |

**Osteomyelitis vs Ewing** — mixed inflammatory infiltrate (neutrophils, plasma cells, lymphocytes, histiocytes) → **debridement + antibiotics** · Ewing = uniform round cells, no inflammation

## 5 Management

### Treatment — chemotherapy with surgery or radiation

```
Ewing sarcoma (chemo needed in ALL — presume micromets)
├─ Resectable / expendable site → NEOADJUVANT CHEMO → WIDE RESECTION → ADJUVANT CHEMO
│      └─ Positive margin / poor necrosis / mets → + ADJUVANT RT
├─ Unresectable (spine, pelvis) or unacceptable morbidity → CHEMO + RT
└─ Huge tumour on NV structures, displaced path fracture, foot/ankle → AMPUTATION
```

**Chemo** — **VAC: vincristine, Adriamycin (doxorubicin), cyclophosphamide** (± dactinomycin) **alternating with ifosfamide + etoposide (IE)** · neoadjuvant **8–12 wk** → adjuvant **6–12 mo**\
✗ Wrong options: methotrexate-based = OS

**Surgery** — preferred local control when wide margins achievable · **negative margins → 5-yr survival ↑ >10%** · positive margin → re-resect ± RT · recurrence **5–10% with surgery** vs **35% RT alone**

**Reconstruction** — vascularized/non-vascularized autograft (fibula, scapula, iliac crest, rib, clavicle), allograft, APC, endoprosthesis, rotationplasty

**Radiation** — **radiosensitive** (most radio- + chemosensitive bone tumour) but trend **away** from RT · field = tumour + **2–3 cm**, **56–60 Gy** · standard = hyperfractionated · indications: **unresectable**, positive margins, poor chemo response, **all pulmonary mets**

**Extensive pelvic disease + lung mets** — **chemo + radiation**

**Spine with neuro deficit, resection too morbid** — local control by **radiation**

**Outcomes** — 5-yr **82% (no mets) vs 39% (mets)** · 10-yr 63% vs 32%

### Extraskeletal Ewing sarcoma (TO)

**Histology same as skeletal** ✓ · adolescents/young adults (✗ Wrong options: <10) · **paravertebral muscles, chest wall** (✗ Wrong options: thigh) · t(11;22), MIC2 + · **chemosensitive** (✗ Wrong options: resistant) · same treatment · better prognosis than osseous (OB)

## 6 Complications

**Radiation complications (>60%)** — **LLD**, joint contracture, muscle atrophy, **pathologic fracture**, **secondary sarcoma**

### Radiation-induced sarcoma

**Secondary bone sarcoma** — ~**20% at 20 yr** · in radiation field · dose dependent (**<60 Gy → <5%**) · e.g. lytic lesion 20 yr after RT at age 10\
✗ Wrong options: recurrence · infarct · adenocarcinoma

**AML / myelodysplasia** — **1–2%**, 2–5 yr after chemo (dose-intensive regimens)

### DVT risk

**↑ VTE in paediatric sarcoma** (Ewing) — tumour activates **factor X → Xa** · no consensus on prophylaxis\
✗ Wrong options: haemangioma · MSSA osteomyelitis (MRSA ↑) · NOF · PVNS

**Recurrence** — ~20% localized, >60% metastatic · after recurrence **<10% 5-yr**

**Mets cure** — lung only **30%** · bone only 20% · lung + bone <15%

### Prognostic factors

**Most important** — **metastasis at diagnosis** · lung mets better than bone/marrow · skip mets better than distant

| Poor prognosis | Threshold |
|---|---|
| **Age** | **>10** (TO) · **>14** (Orthobullets) |
| **Size** | **>8 cm** / ≥200 mL |
| **Site** | **Pelvis/spine (worst)** > proximal > distal (best) |
| Chemo response | **<90% necrosis** · surgically treated: necrosis = most important (TO) |
| Labs | **↑ LDH (>200 IU/L)**, ↑ WBC, ↑ ESR, anaemia |
| Clinical | **Fever**, male sex |
| Marrow | Amount of marrow involvement |
| Molecular | **p53 mutation** + t(11;22), Ki-67, HER-2/neu |

**Not prognostic (TO)** — **histological grade** (all Ewing high grade) · t(11;22) presence · female sex is not a bad sign

**Good prognostic sign (TO)** — **age 7 yr**\
✗ Wrong options: 12 cm · fever · TLC 14,000

## X vs Y

| | **Ewing** | **Osteosarcoma** | **Lymphoma of bone** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Age | 5–25 | 10–20, >65 | 35–55 |
| Site | Diaphysis/metadiaphysis, pelvis | Metaphysis | Diaphysis |
| X-ray | Permeative, **onion skin** | Blastic, sunburst, Codman | Moth-eaten, little periosteal reaction |
| Histology | Round blue cells | Lacy osteoid | Mixed round cells |
| Markers | **CD99, PAS +, reticulin −** | SATB2 | **LCA (CD45) +, reticulin +, PAS −** |
| Marrow biopsy | **Yes** | No | Yes |
| Chemo | **VAC/IE** | **MAP** | CHOP ± rituximab |
| RT | Radiosensitive | Radioresistant | Radiosensitive |

## How it's asked

**"13-yr-old, mid-femoral lesion, large soft-tissue mass, onion skin?"** → **Ewing sarcoma** **(TO)**

**"8-yr-old, night thigh pain, ↑ WBC + ESR, onion-skin, round blue cells?"** → **Ewing**\
✗ Wrong options: osteomyelitis · OS

**"Round cells EXCEPT?"** → **leiomyosarcoma** (spindle cells)

**"15-yr-old, thoracic small round blue cell tumour, RT for local control considered?"** → **Ewing**\
✗ Wrong options: chordoma · OS · chondrosarcoma (RT inadequate)

**"Immunostain confirming diagnosis?"** → **MIC-2 (CD99) +**\
✗ Wrong options: cytokeratin + · PAS − · reticulin + · vimentin −

**"9-yr-old, monotonous small round blue cells?"** → **Ewing**\
✗ Wrong options: neuroblastoma <5 · lymphoma · small cell lung Ca

**"t(11;22) on FISH — histology?"** → **sheets of small round blue cells, CD99 +**

**"Translocation?"** → **t(11;22)**\
✗ Wrong options: 9;22 · 12;16 · X;18 · 12;22 · 2;13

**"Fusion protein?"** → **EWS–FLI1**\
✗ Wrong options: SYT-SSX · ASPL-TFE3 · EWS-ATF1 · TLS-CHOP

**"Neoplastic properties due to?"** → **chromosomal translocation**

**"Test required for Ewing but not OS?"** → **bone marrow biopsy**

**"Which patient needs marrow biopsy in staging?"** → **5-yr-old, lytic diaphyseal femoral lesion, EWS-FLI1 +**

**"Teen runner, pelvic lesion after failed PT?"** → **MRI of the pelvis**

**"3-yr-old, febrile, normal ESR/CRP, permeative femur?"** → **MRI of the femur**\
✗ Wrong options: biopsy + culture first · antibiotics

**"Stage IIB proximal tibia Ewing?"** → **neoadjuvant chemo → wide resection → adjuvant chemo**\
✗ Wrong options: chemo + RT without surgery

**"Fibula Ewing?"** → **chemo + surgical resection**\
✗ Wrong options: resection alone · RT alone

**"Pelvic Ewing with sciatic symptoms + lung mets?"** → **chemo + radiation**

**"Best-prognosis regimen?"** → **VAC (+ dactinomycin) + wide resection ± RT**\
✗ Wrong options: methotrexate-based

**"Distal fibula Ewing, no other lesions?"** → **surgery + chemo ± RT** — chemo required in all cases

**"14-yr-old, fever, ↑ WBC/ESR, mixed inflammatory infiltrate?"** → **debridement + antibiotics** (osteomyelitis, not Ewing)

**"Lytic lesion 20 yr after RT for childhood Ewing?"** → **radiation-induced sarcoma**

**"Second commonest primary bone malignancy in children?"** → **Ewing**

**"Paediatric condition with ↑ DVT risk?"** → **bone sarcoma with t(11;22)**

**"NOT a bad prognostic sign?"** → **high grade** (all Ewing high grade) (✓ fever, ✓ ↑ LDH, ✓ age >10–12) **(TO)**

**"Good prognostic sign?"** → **age 7 yr** **(TO)**

**"True of Ewing in a 10-yr-old?"** → **raised ESR = worse prognosis** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: predominantly diaphyseal · grade prognostic · appendicular worse

**"True of extraskeletal Ewing?"** → **histology same as skeletal** **(TO)**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Extra-abdominal Desmoid Tumor

*Pathology · 19 / 66*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Nature** — benign but **most locally invasive benign soft-tissue tumour** (aggressive fibromatosis) · **no metastasis**, no malignant change (unless radiation-related)

**Demographics** — **25–35 yr** (15–40) · **F:M 3:1** · ~900/yr in US

**Site** — **shoulder (20%) > chest wall/back (17%) > thigh (13%)** · >50% extra-abdominal · **sites of previous surgery/scars** · multiple lesions in same limb → **next one appears more proximally**

### Association with FAP

**Familial adenomatous polyposis (FAP) / Gardner syndrome** — desmoid in **4–30%** (prevalence ~21%) · **10,000× risk** \
✗ Wrong options: Sinding-Larsen-Johansson · Ollier · Fanconi · McCune-Albright

**Gardner features** — colonic polyps + **jaw osteomas** + **epidermoid cysts**

**FAP** — **autosomal dominant**, **APC** tumour-suppressor gene · **~100% lifetime colon cancer** → **screen for colon cancer**\
✗ Wrong options: lung, breast, thyroid, renal

**Other associations** — **Dupuytren** contracture · **Ledderhose** disease

### Genes & lab markers

**APC mutation** — 15% of tumours (APC controls **β-catenin**)

**β-catenin ↑** — **all** tumours

**Trisomy 8 or 20**

**IHC +** — **oestrogen receptor-β (100%)**, somatostatin, cathepsin D, Ki-67, c-Kit · **IHC −** — ER-α, PR, HER2

### Cortical desmoid

**Cortical desmoid** — self-limiting benign fibro-osseous lesion of **active adolescents (10–15)** · **chronic tendon traction** · **posteromedial distal femur** at **medial gastrocnemius / adductor magnus** insertion · also humerus (pectoralis, deltoid insertions)

**Imaging** — geographic central lucency, sclerotic margin, **"bite-like" cortical irregularity** · MRI T1 low–intermediate, T2 high, heterogeneous enhancement

**Treatment** — **activity modification + PT** (self-limiting)

## 3 Presentation

**Mass** — enlarging, painless or painful · **"rock hard"**, fixed, poorly circumscribed · limits joint motion

**Nerve compression** — numbness, tingling, weakness, stabbing pain

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**MRI — best means to diagnose** · excludes multicentric lesions · **T1 low**, **T2 low–medium** (collagen) · gadolinium enhances · infiltrates muscle · 5–10 cm · may erode bone

**X-ray** — minimal role · bone erosion, **"frond-like" periosteal spicules** into tumour

### Histology

**Gross** — **white, gritty**, poorly encapsulated

**Micro** — **well-differentiated fibroblasts/myofibroblasts**, **uniform spindle cells with elongated nuclei**, occasional mitoses, **abundant collagen**, infiltrates adjacent tissue

![Uniform spindle cells in abundant collagen](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8062-extra-abdominal-desmoid-tumor.jpg)

| Histology | Diagnosis |
|---|---|
| **Uniform spindle fibroblasts, elongated nuclei, collagen** | ✓ **Desmoid** |
| Fibroblasts + Schwann cells, mast cells, wavy nuclei | **Neurofibroma** |
| Stromal cells, foam cells, haemosiderin giant cells | PVNS |
| Small round blue cells | Ewing |
| Storiform fibroblasts, lipophages, giant cells | NOF |

## 5 Management

```
Extra-abdominal desmoid
├─ Asymptomatic / minimally symptomatic → ACTIVE SURVEILLANCE, SERIAL MRI (first line)
├─ Progressive, inoperable, or can't tolerate chemo → TAMOXIFEN / LOW-DOSE CHEMO
└─ Symptomatic / recurrent / failed surveillance → WIDE RESECTION + RT (50–60 Gy)
```

**Active surveillance ("watch and wait")** — primary strategy (Desmoid Tumor Working Group) · may stay stable or **regress spontaneously**

**Tamoxifen** — **oestrogen-receptor blockade** · moderate short-term success with less morbidity · for inoperable lesions / poor chemo candidates · works as lesions commoner + more aggressive in (premenopausal) women

**Wide resection + radiotherapy** — symptomatic or recurrent lesions · **50–60 Gy** EBRT · abdominal wall desmoid → surgery if surveillance fails

## 6 Complications

### Recurrence

| Margin | Surgery alone | Surgery + RT |
|---|---|---|
| **Negative** | **28%** | **6%** |
| **Positive** | **39%** | **25%** |

**Highest local recurrence** of soft-tissue tumours after wide resection without adjuvant

**Prognosis** — unpredictable · occasional spontaneous regression · **no metastasis**

## How it's asked

**"20-yr-old woman, rock-hard calf mass, ER-β +?"** → **extra-abdominal fibromatosis (desmoid)**\
✗ Wrong options: OS · leiomyosarcoma · angiosarcoma

**"Shoulder mass, family with colonic polyps, jaw osteomas, epidermoid cysts — histology?"** → **uniform spindle fibroblasts with elongated nuclei**\
✗ Wrong options: neurofibroma · PVNS · Ewing · NOF

**"Adolescent lacrosse player, posteromedial distal femoral cortical lesion from tendon traction?"** → **cortical desmoid** → activity modification + PT

**"48-yr-old, 5 cm thigh mass, biopsy → active surveillance with serial MRI?"** → **extra-abdominal desmoid**\
✗ Wrong options: lipoma · GCT · osteoid osteoma

**"Inherited condition associated with desmoids?"** → **FAP**

**"Desmoid patient — screen for?"** → **colon cancer**

**"Tumour treated by oestrogen-receptor blockade?"** → **desmoid** (tamoxifen)\
✗ Wrong options: Ewing · synovial sarcoma · myeloma · GCT

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Fibrous Dysplasia

*Pathology · 20 / 66*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — 1 in 5,000–10,000 · ~2.5% of bone lesions, 5% of benign bone lesions

**Age** — **75% onset <30** (5–30) · monostotic 10–30 · polyostotic earlier (<10; 60% symptomatic)

**Sex** — **M = F**

**Site** — any bone · **proximal femur commonest** · monostotic: **ribs 28%**, **proximal femur 23%**, skull/craniofacial, tibia, humerus · polyostotic: often **unilateral**, femur, skull/face, pelvis, spine · craniofacial: zygomatic-maxillary complex · skull + face generalized = **"leontiasis ossea"** **(TO)**

**Location in bone** — **medullary** (metaphysis/diaphysis) — not cortical (NOF, OFD, adamantinoma are cortical) **(TO)**

### Pathophysiology

**Defect** — primitive woven bone **fails to remodel to lamellar bone** → immature trabeculae in dysplastic fibrous tissue

**Natural history** — active in childhood → **inactive at skeletal maturity** (monostotic grows with skeleton; polyostotic with disease severity)

### Genes & lab markers — GNAS / Gs-alpha

**Mutation** — **activating missense GNAS (GNAS1)** → **Gs-α subunit (20q13)** · **somatic mosaicism**, spontaneous · **no vertical transmission** · timing → extent

**Pathway** — loss of intrinsic GTPase → **constitutive adenylate cyclase → ↑ cAMP** → c-fos ↑ · ↑ **WNT/β-catenin** → osteoprogenitors fail to mature · ↑ NF-κB, IL-6 → resorption

**FGF-23 ↑** — **renal phosphate wasting → hypophosphataemia → osteomalacia** (Looser zones)

**cAMP pathway question** — ✓ **FD**\
✗ Wrong options: achondroplasia FGFR3 · hypochondroplasia · MHE EXT · NF1 · enchondroma

| Gene / defect | Condition |
|---|---|
| **GNAS / Gs-α (↑ cAMP)** | ✓ **Fibrous dysplasia, McCune-Albright** |
| DTD (sulfate transporter) | Diastrophic dysplasia |
| CBFA1 / RUNX2 | Cleidocranial dysostosis |
| COL1A1 / COL1A2 | Osteogenesis imperfecta |
| FGFR3 | Achondroplasia |
| Ring chromosome 12 (MDM2) | Low-grade osteosarcoma |
| t(11;22) | Ewing |

**Labs** — monostotic: none · polyostotic: **thyroid panel**, **IGF-1, GH, prolactin** (MAS screen) · **ALP** to monitor bisphosphonate; ↑ ALP with malignant change

### Associated syndromes

| Syndrome | Components |
|---|---|
| **McCune-Albright** | **Polyostotic FD + café-au-lait ("coast of Maine", jagged, don't cross midline, trunk) + endocrinopathy** (**precocious puberty** — 85% of girls; thyroid 66%; GH excess; ovarian cysts; neonatal hypercortisolism) · **unilateral** |
| **Mazabraud** | Polyostotic FD + **intramuscular myxomas** |
| **Jaffe-Lichtenstein** | Polyostotic FD + café-au-lait, **no endocrinopathy** |
| Jaffe-Campanacci | Multiple **NOFs** + café-au-lait, no neurofibromas |
| NF1 | Café-au-lait **"coast of California"** (smooth) + neurofibromas, 17q11.2 |

## 3 Presentation

**Usually asymptomatic** — incidental

**Pain** — high-stress sites · ↑ in **pregnancy / menses** (oestrogen receptors)

**Deformity** — **LLD (commonest skeletal deformity)** · **shepherd's crook** (coxa vara, lateral bowing, shortening) · **saber shin** (anterior tibial bowing) · **scoliosis**

**Craniofacial** — cosmetic deformity, blindness, hearing loss, airway obstruction, proptosis, sinus occlusion

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — **expansile, central intramedullary**, well-defined, smooth cortices · lytic, sclerotic or mixed → **"ground glass"** · cortical thinning, endosteal scalloping · **no periosteal reaction** · fills bone over length

![Ground-glass lesion of the proximal femur](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8038-prfm1-1-moved.jpg)

**"Rind sign"** — punched-out / ground-glass lesion with **thick sclerotic rim**

**Looser zones** — pseudofractures (osteomalacia)

**Shepherd's crook** — AP + lateral hip

**Spine X-rays (AP/lateral)** — in **polyostotic / MAS** (60% spinal involvement, posterior elements; scoliosis 40%) → **next step after hot spinal lesions on bone scan**\
✗ Wrong options: CT · pelvis X-ray · bisphosphonates · endocrine screen

**CT** — ground glass 56%, sclerosis 23%, cystic 21% · enhances

**MRI** — **T1 dark**, T2 variable, moderate heterogeneous enhancement · separates from malignancy

**Bone scan** — warm until adulthood · screens for **polyostotic** disease / MAS in children

**Diagnosis** — history + exam + X-ray usually enough · biopsy if polyostotic/MAS doubt or malignancy

### Histology

**"Chinese letters" / "alphabet soup"** — curvilinear irregular **woven bone trabeculae** in **fibrous stroma** (fibroblast proliferation)

**No osteoblastic rimming** (✓ rimming = osteofibrous dysplasia)

**Gross** — yellow/tan-white, **gritty/sandy** · cartilage in ~10% · cysts in older lesions

![Woven bone without osteoblastic rimming in fibrous stroma](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8038-1c-moved.jpg)

### Classification

| Type | Definition | Share |
|---|---|---|
| **Monostotic** | Single bone | **70%** |
| **Polyostotic** | Multiple bones | **30%** |

### "Touch-me-not" lesions (TO)

**Definition** — incidental, X-ray-diagnostic, need **no biopsy or intervention** · myositis ossificans, cortical desmoid, enchondroma, osteochondroma, **FD**, NOF, Paget, fibrous cortical defect, intraosseous lipoma, bone island, bone infarct · not **osteoblastoma** (painful, needs treatment)

### Differential

| | Multiple lesions, young | Observation alone | Bisphosphonates help |
|---|---|---|---|
| **FD** | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |
| EG | ✓ | ✓ | — |
| Ollier/Maffucci · MHE · NOF/Jaffe-Campanacci | ✓ | ✓ | — |
| Lymphoma · leukaemia · haemangioendothelioma | ✓ | — | — |
| Paget | — | ✓ | ✓ (>50 yr, coarse trabeculae) |
| Metastasis · myeloma | — | — | ✓ |

**Osteofibrous dysplasia** — **cortical**, anterior tibia, **osteoblastic rimming**

**Adamantinoma** — anterior tibial cortex, **epithelial nests**

**Not seen in FD (TO)** — **Harrison groove** (rickets) · ✓ rind sign, Chinese letters, café-au-lait

## 5 Management

```
Fibrous dysplasia
├─ Asymptomatic, low fracture risk → OBSERVE (6-monthly exam + X-ray to maturity)
├─ Symptomatic pain, polyostotic, low fracture risk → BISPHOSPHONATE (IV, off-label)
├─ Fracture within acceptable limits (UE, tibia, child) → IMMOBILISE ± CLOSED REDUCTION
├─ Impending / pathologic fracture, weight-bearing → LONG INTRAMEDULLARY NAIL ± OSTEOTOMY
│      └─ IM nail contraindicated → TITANIUM LOCKING PLATE (whole bone) + graft
└─ Coxa vara / shepherd's crook → VALGUS (intertrochanteric) OSTEOTOMY + FIXATION
```

### Treatment (pathologic fracture, shepherd's crook, bisphosphonates)

**IM nail** — **gold standard** (load-sharing) · **long** nail to protect whole bone · elastic nails if canal small · for recalcitrant pain, impending/actual fracture, progressive deformity

**Plate** — **titanium** (modulus closer to bone) **locking**, **longest plate**, maximum screws · IM device > plate in lower limb

**Graft choice** — **calcium phosphate cement** (longest resorption) · **cortical allograft** > cancellous (slower resorption) · not **autograft** → resorbed, **converts to dysplastic bone**

**Isolated curettage + graft** — poor (resorption, fracture, reoperation) · only low-risk monostotic upper-limb lesions

**Polyostotic + femoral neck path fracture (>2/3 diameter)** — **biopsy, curettage, bone grafting + lesion-spanning internal fixation**\
✗ Wrong options: observation · RT · percutaneous screws (soft lateral cortex)

**5-yr-old, painful coxa vara** — **valgus osteotomy + internal fixation**\
✗ Wrong options: curettage + allograft · observation · spica

**Symptomatic adult, no fracture risk** — **bisphosphonate**\
✗ Wrong options: RFA · HIFU (osteoid osteoma) · steroid injection (UBC) · curettage + autograft

**Craniofacial** — recontouring / curettage · **radiotherapy contraindicated** (malignant transformation) · calcitonin unsuccessful

## 6 Complications

**Pathologic fracture** — **50% of humeral lesions ≥1 fracture** · 50% of monostotic patients · humerus risk: **>50% cortical involvement, >30 mm, cystic degeneration** · femur: large neck/trochanteric lesion

### Coxa vara classification

| Type | Region | Neck-shaft angle | Share |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | Neck + trochanter | Normal 120–140° | 24% |
| **2** | Neck + trochanter | Valgus >140° | 6% |
| **3** | Neck + trochanter | **Varus <120°** | 7% |
| **4** | **Lateral bowing** of proximal shaft | Normal | 20% |
| **5** | Lateral bowing | Valgus | 14% |
| **6** | Lateral bowing | **Varus** | **29%** |

**Course** — **types 1–2 stable**, **types 3–6 progress** · goal: NSA 120–140°, correct bowing, apex-anterior + rotation, total-bone load-sharing fixation

### Malignant transformation

**Risk ~1%** — higher in **polyostotic** (MAS, Mazabraud), after **radiotherapy**

**Tumours** — **osteosarcoma (commonest)** > fibrosarcoma > MFH · usually **diaphysis**, decades later

**Clinical** — new pain, swelling, mass, **↑ ALP** · **>50% mortality** (worse than primary sarcoma)

**Scoliosis** — 40% in polyostotic

## X vs Y

| | **Fibrous dysplasia** | **Osteofibrous dysplasia** | **Adamantinoma** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Location | **Medullary**, proximal femur | **Cortical, anterior tibia** | Cortical, anterior mid-tibia |
| Age | <30 | <10 | 20–40 |
| Histology | Woven bone **without rimming** | Woven bone **with osteoblastic rimming** | Epithelial nests, keratin + |
| Treatment | Observe / bisphosphonate / IM nail | Observe / brace | Wide resection |

| | **McCune-Albright café-au-lait** | **NF1 café-au-lait** |
|---|---|---|
| Border | **Jagged — "coast of Maine"** | **Smooth — "coast of California"** |
| Midline | Don't cross | — |

## How it's asked

**"Genetic abnormality in cAMP signalling?"** → **fibrous dysplasia** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: achondroplasia · MHE · enchondroma

**"Gs-α point mutation — which X-ray?"** → **polyostotic FD with ground-glass proximal femur**\
✗ Wrong options: adamantinoma · enchondroma · GCT · melorheostosis

**"Gs-α (↑ cAMP) mutation causes?"** → **FD**\
✗ Wrong options: diastrophic dysplasia · cleidocranial · OI · achondroplasia

**"Aetiology of ground-glass lesion + Chinese-letter histology?"** → **Gs-α mutation → constitutive cAMP**\
✗ Wrong options: ring chromosome 12 · parathyroid · t(11;22)

**"GNAS mutation associated with?"** → **FD**\
✗ Wrong options: osteofibrous dysplasia · chondroblastoma

**"McCune-Albright EXCEPT?"** → **multiple neurofibromas**

**"Jagged café-au-lait + hip lesion in a 6-yr-old?"** → **McCune-Albright**\
✗ Wrong options: NF · Holt-Oram · Ellis-van Creveld

**"MAS, 3 hot spinal lesions — next?"** → **AP/lateral spine radiographs**

**"Polyostotic FD, serrated café-au-lait, endocrine, unilateral?"** → **McCune-Albright**\
✗ Wrong options: Jaffe-Campanacci

**"Coxa vara, ground glass, jagged trunk pigmentation — risk of?"** → **other bones + precocious puberty**

**"NOT seen in FD?"** → **Harrison groove** **(TO)**

**"Lesion NOT primarily cortical?"** → **fibrous dysplasia** (✓ NOF, OFD, adamantinoma cortical) **(TO)**

**"Touch-me-not lesions EXCEPT?"** → **osteoblastoma** **(TO)**

**"Incidental ground-glass proximal femur / humerus?"** → **FD**\
✗ Wrong options: enchondroma (rings/arcs) · UBC · infarct

**"Woven bone without osteoblastic rimming in bland stroma?"** → **FD**\
✗ Wrong options: ossifying fibroma · low-grade OS

**"16-yr-old MAS, undisplaced femoral neck path fracture?"** → **biopsy, curettage, grafting + internal fixation**

**"5-yr-old, painful coxa vara with ground glass?"** → **valgus osteotomy + internal fixation**

**"38-yr-old, symptomatic distal femoral FD, biopsy-proven?"** → **bisphosphonate therapy**

**"Polyostotic FD, new pain at 39?"** → **osteosarcoma** (malignant transformation)

**"14-yr-old, huge craniofacial ground-glass lesion on CT?"** → **maxillary fibrous dysplasia** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: EG · plasmacytoma · spina ventosa

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Focal Fibrocartilaginous Dysplasia

*Pathology · 21 / 66*

## 1 Basics

**Nature** — rare, benign tumour-like bone dysplasia · aetiology unknown

**Age** — **infants and toddlers**

**Site** — **tibia** commonest · also humerus, ulna/forearm, phalanx, femur

**Association** — a variant of **infantile tibia vara** that can **resolve without surgery** (vs Blount)

## 3 Presentation

**Unilateral tibia vara** — painless bowing in a walking toddler

**Gait** — knee hyperextension with **lateral thrust**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray (pathognomonic)** — **abrupt varus at the metaphyseal–diaphyseal junction** · **medial cortical sclerosis** · **lucency in the medial cortex** just proximal to it (fibrocartilaginous tissue)

![Focal fibrocartilaginous dysplasia of the tibia](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8079-focal-fibrocartilaginous-dysplasia-xray.jpg)

**Bone scan** — shows deformity, **not "hot"** (not aggressive)

**MRI** — only in ambiguous cases

### Histology

**Dense bland fibrous tissue** — layers of collagenous fibrous tissue + thick dense fibrocartilage · no malignant features

## 5 Management

```
Focal fibrocartilaginous dysplasia
├─ Most (tibia) → OBSERVATION (± orthosis) — spontaneous correction with physeal growth
└─ Persistent / progressive deformity, joint function compromised → CORRECTIVE OSTEOTOMY (or curettage)
```

**Resolution** — **~half of tibial cases** resolve spontaneously · much less often in femur and humerus

**Osteotomy risks** — secondary tibia valgus, neurologic injury

## X vs Y

| | **FFCD** | **Infantile Blount** |
|---|---|---|
| Side | **Unilateral** | Often bilateral |
| Apex | **Metaphyseal–diaphyseal junction**, medial cortical lucency + sclerosis | Proximal medial physis / metaphyseal beak |
| Natural history | **Often resolves** | Often progresses |

## How it's asked

**"18-month-old, unilateral bowing, medial metaphyseal cortical lucency, bland fibrous histology?"** → **focal fibrocartilaginous dysplasia**\
✗ Wrong options: NF · toddler's fracture · NOF · ABC

**"Treatment?"** → **observation** · osteotomy only if persistent/progressive

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Giant Cell Tumor

*Pathology · 22 / 66*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Nature** — **benign but locally aggressive** · 3–5% of primary bone tumours · 1.7/million

**Demographics** — **females** (unlike most bone tumours) · **30–50** (Orthobullets) · 20–40 / 22–40 (OB, TO) · **skeletally mature, closed physes** (immature → metaphyseal)

**Multicentric** — **1–2%** **(TO)**

### Anatomic distribution

**Order** — **distal femur > proximal tibia > distal radius > sacral ala** · **50% around the knee**

**Axial** — **sacrum** commonest axial site (10% sacrum + spine) · spine → **vertebral body**\
✗ Wrong options: **posterior elements**

**Others** — proximal humerus · **phalanges of hand** · apophysis (like chondroblastoma) · proximal fibula

**Not typical** — **diaphysis** (e.g. tibial shaft)

**"Extra-articular GCT" (TO)** — **GCT of tendon sheath** (giant cell synovioma) → **extremities** (hand/wrist flexor tendons) · firm painless nodule · excision

### Cells

| Cell | Identity |
|---|---|
| **Type I — mononuclear stromal cell** | **Neoplastic cell** · fibroblast-like, mesenchymal stem-cell features · **overexpresses RANKL** · carries **H3F3A (H3.3 G34W)** mutation |
| Type II — monocyte/macrophage | Recruited from blood · **giant-cell precursor** |
| **Type III — multinucleated giant cell** | **Hallmark** · osteoclast-like (TRAP, carbonic anhydrase II, cathepsin K, vacuolar ATPase) · **non-neoplastic bystander** · express **RANK** |

### Genes & lab markers

**H3F3A (H3.3 G34W)** — driver mutation in stromal cells **(TO)**

**RANKL overexpression** by stromal cells — **key pathogenic pathway** → denosumab target

**c-myc / p53** alterations — in **metastatic** lesions (lung mets overexpress c-myc)

**Telomeric associations** — 80% · IGF-I/II activity in type II/III cells

**Brown tumour** — must check **serum calcium / PTH** (hyperparathyroidism, multiple lesions)

## 3 Presentation

**Pain** — insidious, activity/night/rest · joint-referred · difficulty walking

**Signs** — mass, tenderness, swelling, **effusion** (periarticular), ↓ ROM, antalgic gait

**Pathologic fracture** — common (subchondral collapse)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — **eccentric, purely lytic, epiphyseal–metaphyseal**, extends to **subchondral bone** · **no sclerotic rim** (or partial) · poorly defined transition · expands/breaks cortex · "neocortex" · no matrix · intra-articular extension rare · **soap-bubble** appearance **(TO — FNB 2021)**

![Eccentric lytic subchondral lesion](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8046-case-j-ankle-xray-parsons-moved.jpg)

**MRI** — extent · **dark T1 (clear demarcation from fatty marrow)**, bright T2, avid enhancement · intermediate T2 signal (like lymphoma, Ewing) · fluid levels = **secondary ABC**

**Bone scan** — variable / hot

**Chest X-ray or CT chest** — **mandatory** staging (lung mets)\
✗ Wrong options: bone scan · skeletal survey · SPEP/UPEP · MRI brain/spine · mammogram

**Biopsy first** — even when imaging looks classic → **incisional biopsy** before reconstruction (malignancy can mimic)

### Histology

**Diagnostic** — numerous **multinucleated giant cells (40–60 nuclei)** in a sea of mononuclear stromal cells · **giant-cell nuclei identical to stromal nuclei** · storiform areas, reactive bone, foam cells, haemosiderin

![Giant cells with nuclei identical to stromal cells](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8046-histology-b-parsons-moved.png)

**Secondary ABC** — 10–14% (Orthobullets/OB) · 20% (TO) · GCT has **enhancing solid component** (primary ABC doesn't)

### Campanacci grading (TO)

| Grade | Behaviour | Radiographic features |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | Latent | Well-marginated, **thin rim of mature bone**, cortex intact or slightly thinned |
| **II** | Active | Well-defined, **no radiopaque rim**, cortex thinned + **moderately expanded** but present · (II + fracture graded separately) |
| **III** | Aggressive | **Fuzzy borders**, permeative · **soft-tissue mass** not contained by bone shell |

**Enneking (benign)** — most GCT **stage 2**; aggressive = **3**

### Differential

| | Epiphyseal | Usually curettage + graft |
|---|---|---|
| **GCT** | ✓ | ✓ |
| Chondroblastoma (epiphyseal) | ✓ | ✓ |
| ABC · osteoblastoma · CMF | — | ✓ |

**Chondroblastoma** — **open physes**, chicken-wire calcification, marked oedema, S-100

**Brown tumour** — multiple lesions, abnormal calcium

**Giant-cell-rich / telangiectatic / fibroblastic OS** — **atypia + osteoid**

**Chordoma** — sacral **midline**, physaliferous cells

**ABC** — fluid levels, USP6 · **UBC** — no cortical destruction

## 5 Management

```
Giant cell tumour of bone
├─ Joint salvageable (most; Campanacci I–II, many III) → EXTENDED CURETTAGE + BURR + ADJUVANT + PMMA/GRAFT ± FIXATION
│      (pathologic fracture is NOT a contraindication)
├─ Structural loss / collapsed subchondral bone → EN BLOC RESECTION + RECONSTRUCTION
│      ├─ Expendable bone (proximal fibula, distal ulna) → RESECTION ± LCL/biceps repair
│      └─ Vertebral body → TOTAL EN BLOC SPONDYLECTOMY
├─ Unresectable (sacrum, spine, pelvis) / multiply recurrent → DENOSUMAB ± EMBOLIZATION (RT last resort)
└─ Hand with cortical breakthrough → INTERCALARY RESECTION or RAY AMPUTATION
```

### Treatment — extended curettage with adjuvants

**Preferred treatment** — **extended curettage + adjuvants + reconstruction** (TO FNB 2024 — key not given; sources' standard)\
✗ Wrong options: curettage alone · curettage + graft · primary RT

**Technique** — **large cortical window (≥ lesion size)** — no curetting under overhanging ridges · head lamp, dental mirror, angled curettes · **high-speed burr 1–2 cm** in all directions · pulsatile lavage · don't perforate subchondral plate

**Most important step** — **aggressive curettage with high-speed burr** (no adjuvant proven superior)

**Adjuvants** — **phenol**, **liquid nitrogen (cryo)**, **hydrogen peroxide**, **argon beam**, electrocautery, ethanol (= phenol) · burr alone recurrence **12%**

**Filling** — **PMMA** (thermal necrosis; recurrence seen as **lucency at bone–cement interface**) · graft · ± internal fixation · not DBM + marrow aspirate (higher recurrence)

| Treatment | Local recurrence |
|---|---|
| Curettage + graft alone | **20–40%** (OB: 15–25%) |
| + adjuvant | **3–10%** |
| High-speed burr only | 12% |
| Overall | ~20% |

**Distal radius, cortices + joint intact** — **curettage + liquid nitrogen + PMMA**\
✗ Wrong options: en bloc + allograft · fusion with fibula · RT

**Hand** — no breakthrough → **curettage + cementing** · breakthrough → **intercalary resection** (free fibula) **or amputation** (lowest recurrence)

### En bloc resection

**Lateral condyle with collapsed subchondral bone + fracture** — **condylar resection + osteoarticular allograft**\
✗ Wrong options: cement injection · chemo · RT · amputation

**Proximal fibula** — **en bloc resection + repair LCL + biceps femoris to lateral tibia** → lowest recurrence, low instability\
✗ Wrong options: curettage · structural allograft · denosumab (high recurrence) · observation

**Knee** — hemicondylar osteoarticular allograft or rotating-hinge endoprosthesis · **distal radius** aggressive → resection + proximal fibular autograft (arthroplasty or arthrodesis) · distal ulna/clavicle → resection alone

**Spine** — total en bloc spondylectomy (± staged anterior/posterior, cage/cement) → 10-yr disease-free up to 100%

### Denosumab

**Drug** — **fully human IgG2 monoclonal antibody against RANKL (receptor activator of nuclear factor kappa-B ligand)** (mimics OPG) · FDA approved for unresectable GCT / when resection → severe morbidity, adults + skeletally mature

| Option | Identity |
|---|---|
| **Denosumab** | ✓ **Human mAb → RANKL** |
| Osteoprotegerin | Decoy receptor → RANKL |
| Infliximab | Chimeric mAb → TNF-α |
| Rituximab | → CD20 |
| Trastuzumab | → HER2 |
| (none) | mAb or decoy → RANK itself |

**Mechanism (TO)** — kills/prevents **osteoclast-like giant cells** · **stromal (neoplastic) H3F3A clone survives**, quiescent\
✗ Wrong options: directly kills stromal cells · halts stromal proliferation · OPG antagonist

**Effect** — **85–90% giant-cell destruction** · sclerosis, cortical reconstitution, pain relief

**Histology after denosumab (TO)** — **loss of giant cells + dense fibro-osseous tissue, new woven bone, stromal spindling** (mimics low-grade fibro-osseous lesion)

**Dosing (TO)** — **120 mg SC days 1, 8, 15, 29** → **every 4 weeks** · neoadjuvant 3–6 mo · **calcium + vitamin D** mandatory

**Neoadjuvant downside (TO)** — **sclerotic, ossified margin obscures tumour boundary → ↑ recurrence after curettage** (20–30% vs 10–15%)

**Stopping (TO)** — **rebound RANKL surge** → rapid recurrence + **severe rebound hypercalcaemia** (3–9 mo) · recurrence 20–60% · mitigate with **zoledronic acid**, tapering, calcium monitoring

**Long-term (TO)** — **1–6% malignant transformation**, ↑ with prolonged therapy · ONJ 1–5% · atypical femoral fracture (>2 yr)

**Side effects** — hypocalcaemia (contraindication: severe hypocalcaemia), nasopharyngitis, arthralgia

**Bisphosphonates** — osteoclast inhibitors · postop → **↓ recurrence 25–30%** **(TO: used to prevent recurrence)** · zoledronic acid stabilises inoperable disease · denosumab > bisphosphonate\
✗ Wrong options: calcitonin · teriparatide · methotrexate

### Radiation & embolization

**Radiation** — only inoperable / multiply recurrent (sacrum, large vertebral) · **~15% malignant transformation** (megavoltage)

**Embolization** — unresectable sacral/pelvic GCT, monthly until palliation · preop to ↓ blood loss

**Not used** — chemotherapy · RFA

## 6 Complications

### Lung metastasis & chest CT

**"Benign" metastasizing tumour** — lung mets **histologically identical** to primary (also chondroblastoma)

| Source | Lung metastasis rate |
|---|---|
| Orthobullets | **2–4%** (1–6% on chest CT) |
| OB | **~2% overall, 6% if recurrent → counsel ~4%** · 1–9% · ~5% |
| TO | **~3%** |
| Spine GCT series | 13.7% |

**Risk factors** — **local recurrence** · **aggressive / stage 3 (Campanacci III)** **(TO)** · **wrist/hand, distal radius**, proximal femur, sacrum/spine · younger · immunocompromised

**Timing** — 18–24 mo, most within 3 yr

**Treatment** — **resect (metastasectomy)** → ~76–80% disease-free · denosumab · RT

**Mortality with lung mets** — **~15% (TO)** · 17% (Orthobullets) · 5-yr disease-free 76%

**Recurrence** — usually **benign GCT again** (identical histology) · diagnose with CT-guided biopsy · lucency at cement interface

### Malignant transformation

**Rates** — <1% (Orthobullets) · **1.4–6.6%** (OB) · <5% overall malignancy · radiation → ~15%

**Primary vs secondary malignant GCT** — secondary after **radiation** or multiple resections · latency **9 yr after RT**, **19 yr spontaneous**

**Late lytic change years after curettage + biopsy shows high-grade sarcoma** — **wide resection + reconstruction** (+ medical oncology)\
✗ Wrong options: repeat curettage · denosumab

**Pathologic fracture** — periarticular lesions · **cryotherapy (liquid nitrogen)** → fracture + **vascular injury**, wound/nerve problems (phenol too)

### Chemotherapy response grading (Huvos)

*(osteosarcoma necrosis grading, filed under this topic's TO set)*

| Huvos grade | Tumour necrosis | Response |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | 0–50% | Poor (little/none) |
| **II** | 51–90% | **Poor** (partial) |
| **III** | 91–99% | **Good** |
| **IV** | 100% | Complete |

**88% necrosis** → **Huvos II, poor responder** → continue standard MAP (EURAMOS-1: changing regimen doesn't help) **(TO)**

## X vs Y

| | **GCT** | **Chondroblastoma** | **Brown tumour** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Age | 20–50, closed physis | 10–25, open physis | >50 |
| Site | Epiphysis–metaphysis, subchondral | Epiphysis | Multiple |
| Giant cells | Nuclei = stromal nuclei | Scattered + chondroblasts | Haemosiderin, osteoclasts |
| Labs | Normal | Normal | **↑ Ca, ↑ PTH** |
| Treatment | Curettage + adjuvant / denosumab | Curettage + graft / RFA | Treat hyperparathyroidism |

## How it's asked

**"30-yr-old woman, wrist lesion, giant cells in mononuclear stroma?"** → **GCT** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: OS · myeloma · Ewing

**"Soap-bubble appearance on X-ray (FNB)?"** → **GCT** **(TO)**

**"Distal radius lesion, homogeneous stromal + giant-cell nuclei?"** → **GCT**\
✗ Wrong options: telangiectatic OS (malignant cells in blood spaces)

**"Sacral lesion in a 25–29-yr-old with giant cells + haemosiderin?"** → **GCT**\
✗ Wrong options: chordoma (physaliferous) · lymphoma (Reed-Sternberg) · Ewing

**"L3 vertebral body expansile lesion with giant cells, 55 yr?"** → **GCT**\
✗ Wrong options: osteoblastoma (posterior elements)

**"Recurrence 2 yr after curettage — histology?"** → **benign GCT**\
✗ Wrong options: malignant GCT · ABC

**"MCP subchondral expansile lytic lesion in a 26-yr-old?"** → **GCT**

**"Mononuclear cells overexpressing RANKL?"** → **GCT**\
✗ Wrong options: ABC (USP6)

**"Common sites EXCEPT?"** → **posterior elements of spine** / **tibial diaphysis**

**"Sites in order?"** → **distal femur > proximal tibia > distal radius > sacral ala**

**"Common site of extra-articular GCT?"** → **extremities** (GCT tendon sheath) **(TO)**

**"Treatment of GCT — all true EXCEPT?"** → **"pathologic fracture contraindicates curettage + cement"** (✓ bisphosphonates ↓ recurrence, ✓ denosumab inoperable, ✓ cryo → fracture) **(TO)**

**"Distal radius GCT — FALSE statement (FNB 2024)?"** → key not given · **"primidone reduces tumour size preoperatively"** fits (denosumab does) · ✓ Campanacci I → curettage, ✓ sandwich technique for periarticular III, ✓ extended curettage when joint salvageable **(TO)**

**"Best treatment of knee GCT?"** → **intralesional (extended) curettage + local adjuvant**\
✗ Wrong options: chemo · RT · wide resection · RFA

**"Which X-ray is best treated by burr curettage + cement?"** → **GCT**\
✗ Wrong options: adamantinoma · FD · osteochondroma · parosteal OS

**"Lateral condyle GCT with subchondral collapse + fracture?"** → **condylar resection + osteoarticular allograft**

**"Proximal fibula GCT with peroneal palsy?"** → **en bloc resection + LCL/biceps repair to tibia**

**"Late lytic recurrence, biopsy high-grade sarcoma?"** → **wide resection + reconstruction**

**"Medication with most benefit?"** → **denosumab**\
✗ Wrong options: alendronate · calcitonin · teriparatide

**"Denosumab — classification + mechanism?"** → **human mAb against RANKL**\
✗ Wrong options: against RANK · decoy receptor = OPG

**"Accurate statement on denosumab in GCT?"** → **binds RANKL, eliminates giant cells, stromal cells persist** **(TO)**

**"Histology after 6 months of denosumab?"** → **giant cells gone, fibro-osseous tissue, woven bone, stromal spindling** **(TO)**

**"Neoadjuvant denosumab before curettage — main drawback?"** → **sclerotic margin obscures tumour → ↑ recurrence** **(TO)**

**"After stopping long-term denosumab?"** → **rebound RANKL surge → recurrence + hypercalcaemia** **(TO)**

**"Long-term denosumab and malignancy?"** → **1–6% transformation, ↑ with prolonged use** **(TO)**

**"True about GCT (FNB 2021)?"** → **radiographic stage 3 → higher risk of lung mets** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: 1% mets · 5% mortality · 5% multicentric

**"Benign bone tumour most likely to metastasize to lung?"** → **GCT**\
✗ Wrong options: NOF · ABC · osteoid osteoma · haemangioma

**"Staging for suspected metastasis?"** → **CT chest** / **chest radiograph**

**"Risk of lung mets after intralesional surgery?"** → **~4%**

**"Spine GCT metastasizes to?"** → **lung**

**"Imaging shows classic GCT of the wrist — first surgery?"** → **incisional biopsy**

**"88% necrosis after MAP — Huvos?"** → **grade II, poor responder, continue MAP** **(TO)**

# Glomus Tumor

*Pathology · 23 / 66*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Nature** — rare **benign** tumour (hamartoma) of the **glomus body** · formerly "paraganglioma" · soft tissue and/or bone · malignant cases rare

**Age / sex** — **20–40** (3rd–4th decades) · females

**Site** — **hand 75%** · **subungual 50%** · palm, wrist, forearm, foot less often

**Bone** — **erosion of distal phalanx in 50%** · primary intraosseous rare

### Anatomy

**Glomus body** — **perivascular temperature-regulating** structure (arteriovenous shunt) at **digit tip / beneath nail**

## 3 Presentation

**Classic triad** — **paroxysmal pain** · **exquisite point tenderness** · **cold intolerance** (cold water worsens pain)

**Signs** — small **bluish nodule** (often invisible subungually) · **nail ridging / discoloration** · may show no mass or colour change

**Delay** — diagnosis often delayed for years

### Clinical tests

| Test | Method | Accuracy |
|---|---|---|
| **Love test** | **Pinhead pressure on the spot → excruciating pain**, none just beside it | **100% sensitive**, 78% accurate |
| **Hildreth test** | **Tourniquet inflation → pain + Love-test tenderness abolished** | **92% sensitive, 91% specific** |
| Cold sensitivity | Cold water exacerbates pain | — |

**Love test diagnostic of** — **glomus tumour** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: trochanteric bursitis · hydatid cyst · SLE

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — **pressure erosion** of distal phalanx, cortical deformity · intraosseous → cystic erosive change · often **normal**

**MRI** — establishes diagnosis · **T1 low, T2 high**, small enhancing subungual nodule

![Glomus erosion into distal phalanx](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8075-glomus-erosion-into-distal-phalanx.jpg)

### Histology

**Well-defined**, **no atypia or mitoses** · uniform **small round cells with dark nuclei** and pink cytoplasm (epithelioid glomus cells) surrounding **small vascular spaces** in hyaline/myxoid stroma · gland-like nests

![Glomus cells around vascular spaces](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8075-glomus-tumor-high-power.jpg)

### Differential

| Lesion | Distinguishing |
|---|---|
| **Glomus tumour** | Triad, MRI nodule, glomus cells |
| Raynaud phenomenon | Cold sensitivity, **no mass** |
| Epidermal inclusion cyst | Prior trauma, less point tenderness / cold pain |
| Osteomyelitis | Labs, systemic, X-ray changes |
| Osteoid osteoma | Cortical nidus + sclerosis |
| Melanoma · SCC · synovial / epithelioid sarcoma | Different histology |

## 5 Management

**Marginal excision** — **curative** · for symptoms affecting quality of life · complete pain relief

**Nail bed reconstruction** — autologous **fat graft** for large defects

**Locally aggressive / intraosseous destructive** — aggressive resection, even **amputation** suggested

## 6 Complications

**Recurrence** — **~20%** (Orthobullets) · "uncommon" (benign) — usually incomplete excision

## How it's asked

**"20-yr-old, painful ecchymotic ring fingertip, eroded phalanx, dark-nucleated round cells?"** → **glomus tumour**\
✗ Wrong options: melanoma · SCC · synovial/epithelioid sarcoma

**"43-yr-old, cold-sensitive fingertip pain, ulnar nail-bed tenderness, normal X-ray, MRI nodule?"** → **glomus tumour**\
✗ Wrong options: Raynaud · inclusion cyst · osteomyelitis · osteoid osteoma

**"Little-finger tip pain worse with cold — histology vascular spaces with epithelioid cells?"** → **glomus tumour**\
✗ Wrong options: schwannoma · scleroderma · paronychia

**"Love test is diagnostic of?"** → **glomus tumour** **(TO)**

**"Treatment?"** → **marginal excision**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Hemangioma

*Pathology · 24 / 66*

## 1 Basics

**Nature** — **benign vascular tumour of bone**

**Site** — **vertebral bodies** and **craniofacial bones** · pelvis (ilium) · may be **multifocal**

## 3 Presentation

**Usually asymptomatic** — incidental

**Symptomatic** — pain or **pathologic fracture**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings — vertebral haemangioma

| Modality | Sign |
|---|---|
| **Plain X-ray** | **"Jailhouse" / "jail-bar" / "honeycomb"** — lytic body with **vertical striations** (thickened vertical trabeculae) **(TO)** |
| **Sagittal / coronal CT** | **"Corduroy"** appearance **(TO)** |
| **Axial CT** | **"Polka dot"** (salt-and-pepper) — white dots on black fatty background **(TO)** |
| **MRI** | **"Mottled"** — **high T1** (fat) + **high T2** (vascular), black punctate/linear signal voids · retains signal on STIR/fat-sat (unlike fatty marrow) **(TO)** |
| Bone scan | Warm to hot |

![Vertical striations — jailhouse vertebra](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8033-case-c-spine-xray-parsons-moved.jpg)

**Biopsy** — almost **never required** (imaging characteristic) **(TO)**

**Why these signs** — normal cancellous bone replaced by **thickened vertical trabeculae** surrounded by **fat marrow / vascular lacunae**

### Histology

**Cavernous lesion** with numerous **thin-walled blood vessels** · small vascular channels lined by **single layer of endothelium** · no giant cells (vs ABC, Paget), no granulomas (vs sarcoid)

![Cavernous thin-walled vessels](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8033-histology-c-hig-parsons-moved.jpg)

### Differential of the spine/ilium lesion

| Lesion | Distinguishing |
|---|---|
| **Haemangioma** | **Organised vertical striations**, polka dots |
| Paget disease | Square, **enlarged** vertebra, thick cortex ("picture frame"), disorganised mineral |
| GCT | Lytic, destructive |
| Metastasis · myeloma | Lytic/sclerotic, no organisation |
| Chordoma (sacral) | Sacrum, midline, physaliferous cells |
| ABC | Fluid levels, giant cells |
| Sarcoid | Granulomas |

### Soft-tissue haemangioma (forearm)

**Phleboliths** on X-ray + intramuscular mass, **↑ T1 (fat)**, heterogeneous T2 + enhancement → **soft-tissue haemangioma**\
✗ Wrong options: synovial sarcoma · liposarcoma · rhabdomyosarcoma

## 5 Management

```
Haemangioma of bone
├─ Asymptomatic → OBSERVATION
├─ Symptomatic + accessible → CURETTAGE + BONE GRAFT (intralesional excision)
└─ Symptomatic + inaccessible → LOW-DOSE RADIATION 25–40 Gy
```

## How it's asked

**"Polka dot sign on CT?"** → **vertebral haemangioma** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: clavicle osteomyelitis · cortical OS · lymphoma

**"Jailhouse appearance is seen on?"** → **plain X-ray** of vertebral haemangioma **(TO)**

**"Corduroy appearance is seen on?"** → **sagittal CT** of vertebral haemangioma **(TO)**

**"Mottled appearance on MRI?"** → **vertebral haemangioma** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: marrow oedema · CML · metastasis

**"Axial CT with mottled/polka-dot vertebra?"** → **vertebral haemangioma** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: myeloma · metastasis · LCH

**"D12 corduroy on CT (FNB 2024)?"** → key not given · **vertebral haemangioma** per the sign **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: blastic mets · myeloma · Paget

**"L3 vertical striations on sagittal CT?"** → **haemangioma**\
✗ Wrong options: Paget · GCT · metastasis · chordoma

**"30-yr-old, iliac lytic lesion with coarse trabeculae, thin-walled endothelial channels?"** → **haemangioma of bone**\
✗ Wrong options: ABC · sarcoid · Paget
