**"7-yr-old, forearm mass with phleboliths, fat on T1?"** → **haemangioma**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Hemangioma of Soft Tissue

*Pathology · 25 / 66*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Nature** — benign vascular neoplasm · **error in new formation of a vascular segment** · hormones modulate · **20% trauma-associated**

**Demographics** — **<30 yr** · M = F

**Site** — **cutaneous, subcutaneous, intramuscular** (deep, lower limb) · **hand** common · usually solitary · **diffuse haemangioma** = rare childhood form involving a whole limb

**Types** — **capillary (superficial)** vs **cavernous (deep)**

**Tissue of origin** — **vascular**\
✗ Wrong options: cartilage, bone, smooth or skeletal muscle

## 3 Presentation

**Pain varies with activity** — engorgement → **aching, heaviness, swelling** · worse when **dependent**, standing, exercising, swimming · relieved by rest, elevation, ice

**Mass size varies** — fills after minutes of standing → examine **supine and standing** · may be warm · birthmark that enlarges

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — **phleboliths** (calcified thrombi in vessels) · bone erosion · may be normal

![Phleboliths in a cavernous haemangioma](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8069-benign-cavernous-hemangioma-x-ray.jpg)

**MRI with gadolinium** — **diagnostic study** · separates from **AVM and angiosarcoma** · **T1 iso- to hyperintense (fat)** · **T2 hyperintense** · low-signal foci = flow voids / calcification · heterogeneous vessels + **fatty infiltration → "bag of worms"** · no necrosis, no rim enhancement, no mass effect

![Intramuscular haemangioma on MRI](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8069-hemangioma-mri-forearm.jpg)

**Fat on T1 + calcification on X-ray** → diagnostic of haemangioma

### Histology

**No malignant cells**, no pleomorphism · multiple **dilated endothelial-lined vascular channels filled with erythrocytes** · **intramuscular type: lumens infiltrated by (striated) muscle fibres** · cavernous: large vessels + fat

**Gross** — red to tan to yellow

### Differential

| Lesion | Distinguishing |
|---|---|
| **Haemangioma** | Activity-related pain/size, phleboliths, fat on T1 |
| **Low-flow venous malformation** | Congenital, **multifocal, infiltrative**, adolescence (hormonal), compressible, phleboliths, interspersed fat |
| **AVM** | High flow · diagnose by exam + imaging (phleboliths, Doppler flow) · **multidisciplinary / IR** |
| Synovial sarcoma | Painless, calcification, different T1 |
| Lipoma | Homogeneous bright T1, suppresses |
| Desmoid | Dark T1 + T2, no fat |
| PVNS | Similar imaging, different pain · haemosiderin giant cells |
| Parosteal OS | Stuck on posterior distal femur |
| Muscle tear haematoma | Should resolve over 3 months |

## 5 Management

```
Soft-tissue haemangioma / vascular malformation
├─ First line (incl. children, incidental) → OBSERVE + NSAIDs + COMPRESSION STOCKINGS + ACTIVITY MODIFICATION
├─ Large, painful, failed conservative → IR: SCLEROTHERAPY (ethanol, US-guided) or EMBOLIZATION
├─ Small hand lesion failing conservative → MARGINAL EXCISION
└─ Recalcitrant, large → WIDE RESECTION (high local recurrence)
```

**AVM failing conservative care** — **consult interventional radiology** (coil embolization / sclerotherapy)\
✗ Wrong options: CT CAP staging · biopsy · chemo · RT

**Not used** — chemotherapy · radiation

**Recurrence after excision** — high · deep lesions abut NV structures → often intralesional

## 6 Complications

**Kasabach-Merritt syndrome** — **platelet trapping → consumptive coagulopathy**, potentially fatal

**Infantile haemangiomas** — **involute by age 7**

**Malignant transformation** — **none**

## How it's asked

**"30-yr-old, painful posterior knee mass enlarging with activity, calcification + fat on T1?"** → **haemangioma**\
✗ Wrong options: synovial sarcoma · lipoma · parosteal OS · PVNS

**"17-yr-old, enlarging hand birthmark, pain when dependent/swimming, phleboliths?"** → **benign haemangioma**\
✗ Wrong options: angiosarcoma · epithelioid sarcoma · GCT tendon sheath

**"Shoulder mass painful + warm after activity, normal X-ray, fat on MRI?"** → **intramuscular haemangioma**\
✗ Wrong options: synovial sarcoma · metastasis · haematoma · abscess

**"Tissue of origin?"** → **vascular**

**"Multiple infiltrative 'bag of worms' masses with fat in a teen?"** → **low-flow venous malformation**\
✗ Wrong options: desmoid · lipoma · MPNST · schwannoma

**"Calf mass enlarging at end of day — histology + treatment?"** → **vascular dilations with erythrocytes + striated muscle · observation**

**"Foot AVM failing stockings + activity change?"** → **consult interventional radiology**

**"Incidental intramuscular gastrocnemius haemangioma?"** → **observation**\
✗ Wrong options: wide/marginal resection · RT · chemo

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Heterotopic Ossification

*Pathology · 26 / 66*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Definition** — bone in atypical **extraskeletal** soft tissue · after **local trauma**, **neurologic injury** (within **2 months** of brain/spinal cord injury), **THA/TKA**, or spontaneous

**Demographics** — **M:F 2:1** · men with **hypertrophic OA**, women >65

**Commonest location** — **between muscle and joint capsule**

| Injury | Order of joints |
|---|---|
| **TBI / stroke** | **Hip > elbow > shoulder > knee** · **spastic side** · elbow HO commoner after brain injury · knee rare |
| **Spinal cord injury** | **Hip > knee > elbow > shoulder** · hip flexors + abductors · medial knee |

**Subtypes** — neurogenic HO · traumatic myositis ossificans · **fibrodysplasia ossificans progressiva** (Münchmeyer)

### Pathophysiology

**Mechanism** — activation/proliferation of **mesenchymal stem cells** → osteoprogenitors · genetic predisposition · experimental HO linked to **BMP** expression

### Risk factors

| Risk factor | Detail | Incidence |
|---|---|---|
| **Injury Severity Score** | **Highest direct correlation** with severe HO after knee dislocation (✗ Wrong options: GCS · timing · number of ligaments · open reconstruction) | 11% |
| **TBI** | Spastic limbs · prolonged coma (20–30 yr), **prolonged ventilation** | 11% |
| **Spinal cord injury** | **Complete > incomplete** | 20% |
| **Decubitus ulcers** + SCI/TBI | — | **70%** |
| **Burns** | Local and remote | — |
| **Amputation through zone of injury** | **Blast** mechanism worst | 63% |
| **Antegrade femoral nail** | **Piriformis** entry | 25% |
| **Distal femoral traction pin** | Large Steinmann pin → **quadriceps HO** (rare) (✗ Wrong options: proximal tibia, calcaneus, radius, metacarpal) | Rare |
| **Acetabular approach** | **Extended iliofemoral > Kocher-Langenbeck > ilioinguinal** · combined approaches → HO is the commonest complication | 25% |
| **THA** | **Psoas tenotomy**, **cementless** (debris, marrow spillage) · **Smith-Petersen and Hardinge > transtrochanteric > posterior (lowest)** · DA "**bikini**" incision ↓ high-grade HO (4% vs 12%) | 53% (significant 5%) · symptomatic ~7% |
| **TKA** | **Anterior femoral notching**, quadriceps trauma, periosteal stripping, **MUA**, high lumbar BMD · **ankylosing spondylitis** (~20%) | — |
| **Other** | **DISH**, hypertrophic OA, previous HO | — |
| **Not proven** | **Open fractures** · **fluorosis** **(TO)** | — |

**THA risk groups (TO)** — **high**: previous HO, hypertrophic post-traumatic arthritis, **men with hypertrophic OA** · **moderate**: AS, DISH, Paget, Parkinson, RA, unilateral hypertrophic OA

### Genes & lab markers

| Marker | Use |
|---|---|
| **ALP >250 IU/L** | ↑ early, nonspecific · **↑ at 12 wk post-op predicts** HO · **cannot determine maturity** (OB) · normalization used in timing (Orthobullets) |
| **CRP** | Tracks inflammatory activity better than ESR · **normalization ≈ maturity** |
| **ESR >35 mm/h** | At 12 wk after THA predicts HO |
| **CK** | Extent of muscle involvement |

## 3 Presentation

**Painless loss of ROM** → ADL interference · ankylosis

**Early** — **warm, painful, swollen** joint, effusion, fever (mimics infection/DVT) · CRPS association

**Complications** — **nerve impingement** (ulnar neuropathy at elbow) · contractures → **decubitus ulcers** · skin maceration · pathologic fracture of osteoporotic bone · TKA: quadriceps snapping, patellar instability

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Modality | Role |
|---|---|
| **Triphasic bone scan** | **Best for early diagnosis**, most used · decreasing activity = maturing |
| **Ultrasound** | Early hip HO — echogenic surface + acoustic shadow |
| **X-ray** | Not early (calcium appears **7–10 days**; may be normal ~3 wk) · **maturity = cortex + sharp demarcation + trabeculae** · **Judet views** for hip |
| **CT (3D)** | **Preoperative planning** · characterize synostosis |

**Histology** — mature trabecular (lamellar) bone + fatty marrow

### Brooker classification (HO after THA)

| Class | Description |
|---|---|
| **1** | **Islands of bone** in soft tissue |
| **2** | Spurs from pelvis/femur, **≥1 cm** between opposing surfaces |
| **3** | Spurs, gap **<1 cm** |
| **4** | Apparent **bony ankylosis** |

**Brooker 1–2** — don't affect THA outcome · **3–4** → pain, ↓ ROM

![Brooker classification](../../MCQ/attachments/q947-illc-booker-classification.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Prophylaxis (radiation, indomethacin)

**Indomethacin** — **75 mg/day for 10 days to 6 weeks** · proven for **distal biceps repair** (radioulnar synostosis)\
✗ Wrong options: 7.5 mg

**Radiation** — single **700 cGy** · **4 h preop or within 72 h postop** · **<550 cGy ineffective** · blocks mesenchymal → osteoprogenitor differentiation\
✗ Wrong options: 500 cGy · 2 wk preop · fractionated after

**Bisphosphonates** — not effective: only delay mineralization of osteoid · HO forms when stopped

**Distal biceps** — **single-incision** technique → lower synostosis rate than two-incision

**Not prophylaxis** — PTH 1-34, VAC chemo

### Timing of excision & maturity

```
Established, symptomatic HO
├─ Immature (<6 mo, bone scan active, ALP/CRP raised) → OBSERVE, PT, re-evaluate
├─ Mature + severe ROM loss / function → WIDE EXPOSURE + EXCISION (CT 3D first)
│      └─ Post-op → INDOMETHACIN (5 d) and/or RT + EARLY GENTLE ROM
└─ HO after THA/TKA, Brooker 3–4 → EXCISION (± revision)
```

| Setting | Wait before excision |
|---|---|
| **General trauma** | **6 months** |
| **Spinal cord injury** | **1 year** |
| **TBI** | **1.5 years** |
| THA | 6–18 months to mature |

**Maturity** — **radiographs: sharp cortical margins** ✓ · marked ↓ bone scan activity + normal ALP (Orthobullets) · (✗ Wrong options: time alone · bone scan always negative · ALP (OB)) · some data: early = late results

**Elbow HO 2 months after TBI with mild ulnar symptoms** — **continue, re-evaluate in 3 months**\
✗ Wrong options: excision ± transposition · RT · isolated release

**Terrible triad, synostosis at 3 months** — **await maturation → CT 3D in 3 months**\
✗ Wrong options: indomethacin/RT now · radial head excision

**Revision THA, 18 months, painful restricted hip** — **excision**\
✗ Wrong options: PT · alendronate · indomethacin · RT (none treat established HO)

**Observation case** — **HO 4 months after THA** (still maturing)\
✗ Wrong options: mature lesions with functional loss

## 6 Complications

**Surgical** — haematoma/bleeding · **infection** (↑ in arthroplasty with HO) · fracture of osteoporotic bone · **AVN** with extensive stripping

**Recurrence** — ∝ severity of neurological injury

## How it's asked

**"Highest correlation with severe HO after knee dislocation?"** → **Injury Severity Score**

**"Amputee at highest HO risk?"** → **through the zone of injury after blast** (propane explosion, transfemoral)

**"Traction pin causing HO?"** → **distal femur** (quadriceps)

**"NOT shown to increase HO?"** → **open fractures** (✓ ventilation, SCI, blast amputation, burns)

**"NOT a risk factor after THR?"** → **fluorosis** (✓ previous HO, hypertrophic OA, DISH) **(TO)**

**"Commonest complication of combined-approach acetabular fixation?"** → **HO**

**"Ankylosing spondylitis TKA — likely complication?"** → **HO**

**"DA THA 'bikini' vs longitudinal incision?"** → **lower HO**\
✗ Wrong options: less LFCN injury · more wound problems

**"What could have prevented hip HO after head injury + fracture fixation?"** → **700 cGy within 72 h of surgery**

**"Prevent radioulnar synostosis after distal biceps repair?"** → **indomethacin 75 mg daily × 6 wk**

**"True about elbow HO 4 months post-injury?"** → **radiographs assess maturity**\
✗ Wrong options: early excision ↓ recurrence · ALP for maturity

**"Painful Brooker 3–4 HO 18 months after revision THA?"** → **excision**

**"Elbow HO 2 months after TBI with mild ulnar paraesthesia?"** → **re-evaluate in 3 months**

**"Terrible triad, forearm stuck at 3 months?"** → **allow maturation, CT 3D in 3 months**

**"Which HO patient can simply be observed?"** → **4 months after THA**

**"Brooker classification is for?"** → **HO around hip after THR** **(TO)**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Impending Fracture & Prophylactic Fixation

*Pathology · 27 / 66*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology & prognosis

**Commonest site of all bone metastases** — **spine**

**Commonest site of pathologic fracture from metastasis** — **proximal femur** (femur = commonest long bone; stress risers)\
✗ Wrong options: distal femur · humerus · thoracic spine

**Upper limb** — 20% of skeletal mets · **50% of those in the humerus**

**Shortest survival after pathologic fracture** — **lung** (and melanoma): median **<6 months**, 5-yr <5%\
✗ Wrong options: thyroid · prostate · breast · renal

### Why fix prophylactically

**Prophylactic > fixing an actual fracture** — shorter operation · less morbidity · quicker recovery

**Fracture-risk predictors** — significant **functional pain** · **>50% cortical destruction** · formal systems: **Harrington** and **Mirels**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Mirels scoring

**Purpose** — **quantify risk of impending pathologic fracture** in a neoplastic bone lesion **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: spinal operability · Scheuermann

| Score | 1 | 2 | 3 |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Site** | Upper limb | Lower limb | **Peritrochanteric** |
| **Pain** | Mild | Moderate | **Functional** |
| **Lesion** | Blastic | Mixed | **Lytic** |
| **Size** (fraction of cortical thickness) | <1/3 | 1/3–2/3 | **>2/3** |

**Threshold** — **>8 → prophylactic fixation** (Orthobullets, TO) · "**8 or higher**" (OB) · one OB explanation says ">9" — read the options · **≤8 painful → radiation**

**Worked examples**

| Case | Score | Action |
|---|---|---|
| Breast mets, proximal humerus, moderate pain, blastic, 1/3–2/3 | **1 + 2 + 1 + 2 = 6** | **Radiation** |
| RCC, humerus, severe (functional) pain, lytic, >2/3 | **1 + 3 + 3 + 3 = 10** | Fix |
| Myeloma, peritrochanteric, functional pain, lytic, >2/3 | **3 + 3 + 3 + 3 = 12** | **Prophylactic fixation + RT** |

![Mirels scoring system](../../MCQ/attachments/q212984-illb-mirel.jpg)

### Harrington criteria

**Use** — prophylactic fixation of impending pathologic fracture of the **proximal femur** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: scoliosis · Blount stapling · proximal humeral fracture

| Criterion |
|---|
| **>50% destruction of diaphyseal cortices** |
| **>50–75% destruction of metaphysis (>2.5 cm)** |
| **Permeative destruction of subtrochanteric femur** |
| **Persistent pain after irradiation** |

**Limitation (TO)** — not validated · proximal femur only

## 5 Management

### Treatment algorithm

```
Impending pathologic fracture
├─ No proven primary with biopsy-proven bone mets → BIOPSY FIRST
│      └─ Primary bone sarcoma → CLOSE, REFER TO SARCOMA CENTRE (no nailing)
├─ Mirels ≤8, painful → RADIATION
└─ Mirels >8 → PROPHYLACTIC FIXATION (protect whole bone) → POST-OP RADIATION
```

**Biopsy** — may need a separate incision from nail incision · nailing a sarcoma **contaminates the whole bone** → loses limb salvage

**Goals of fixation** — **immediate mobilization + weight bearing** · **protect the entire bone** · implant matched to prognosis

### Prophylactic fixation by site

| Site | Fixation |
|---|---|
| **Proximal humerus (Bickel I)** | **Endoprosthesis** (✗ Wrong options: IM nail (poor hold in metaphyseal flare) · plate (thin cortex)) |
| **Humeral diaphysis (Bickel II)** | **IM nail** · resection + intercalary spacer · plate + screws (less preferred) |
| **Distal humerus (Bickel III)** | **Flexible nails** · elbow replacement |
| **Femoral neck / head** | **Cemented hemiarthroplasty** (fails by dislocation, infection) |
| **Peri-/subtrochanteric** | **Cephalomedullary (IM) nail** (fails by nonunion, AVN) (✗ Wrong options: proximal femoral replacement (dissection, weakness, infection, dislocation) · curettage alone (stress riser)) |

**Post-operative radiation** — ↓ pain, slows progression, treats residual tumour (✓ for myeloma lesion after fixation)

## How it's asked

**"Mirels criteria are used for?"** → **quantifying impending fracture risk in a neoplastic lesion** **(TO)**

**"Breast mets, proximal humerus, moderate pain, blastic, 1/3–2/3 — Mirels + next step?"** → **6 → radiation**\
✗ Wrong options: plate at 8 · nail at 7 · replacement

**"RCC humeral lytic lesion >2/3, severe pain — Mirels?"** → **10**

**"Myeloma subtrochanteric lytic lesion, functional pain?"** → **prophylactic internal fixation + radiation** (Mirels 12)\
✗ Wrong options: RT alone · curettage · proximal femoral replacement

**"Commonest site of pathologic fracture from lung mets?"** → **proximal femur**

**"Primary with shortest survival after pathologic fracture?"** → **lung**

**"Harrington criteria are used for?"** → **prophylactic fixation of impending proximal femoral fracture** **(TO)**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Intramuscular Myxomas

*Pathology · 28 / 66*

## 1 Basics

**Nature** — **benign** soft-tissue tumour (mesenchymal) · **no malignant potential** · slow-growing, deep, **confined within skeletal muscle**

**Demographics** — **40–60** (40–70) · slight **female** predilection

**Site** — **thigh**, shoulder, buttock, upper arm, calf

**Origin** — primitive mesenchymal cells → benign fibroblasts that **lose the capacity to secrete collagen**

### Associations

**Mazabraud syndrome** — **multiple intramuscular myxomas + (monostotic or polyostotic) fibrous dysplasia**

**Myxoid liposarcoma** — main differential

## 3 Presentation

**Slowly growing mass** · painful or painless · firm, mobile · deep to fascia

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**MRI** — **homogeneous** · **T1 dark**, **T2 bright** (myxoid) · **intramuscular** location · well circumscribed, no oedema or NV encasement · **rind of fat** at ≥1 pole (bright T1)

![Homogeneous intramuscular myxoma on MRI](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8080-mri-intramuscular-myxoma.jpg)

### Histology

**Bland, hypocellular myxoid stroma** · undifferentiated **stellate cells** with thin collagen fibres · **no atypia**, low N:C ratio, **no atypical mitoses**

### Differential

| Lesion | Distinguishing |
|---|---|
| **Intramuscular myxoma** | **Intra**muscular, homogeneous, fat rind, little enhancement |
| **Myxoid liposarcoma** | **Inter**muscular · **intense diffuse enhancement** · "**pulmonary oedema**" pattern, lipoblasts, chicken-wire vessels |
| **Neurofibroma** | Follows nerve — **entering-and-exiting nerve sign** · ovoid/spindle cells in myxoid stroma |
| Ganglion | Dense fibrous wall around myxoid material, joint origin |
| Metastasis | Soft-tissue mets unusual |

## 5 Management

**Observation** — asymptomatic

**Marginal excision** — **symptomatic** lesions · **no neoadjuvant chemo/RT** · recurrence + metastasis uncommon\
✗ Wrong options: observation · PT when symptomatic

### Excision margin terms

| Margin | Plane | Example |
|---|---|---|
| **Intralesional** | Within pseudocapsule — highest recurrence | Select benign lesions |
| **Marginal** | Through **reactive zone / capsule** | ✓ **Intramuscular myxoma** |
| **Wide** | Cuff of normal tissue | Sarcomas (**myxofibrosarcoma**), **solitary fibrous tumour** |
| **Radical** | Whole compartment | Highly aggressive, no limb-sparing margin |

**Not marginal excision** — ABC (curettage + adjuvant) · desmoid (often non-operative, infiltrative)

## How it's asked

**"40-yr-old, hip/thigh pain, homogeneous intramuscular T2-bright mass, bland myxoid histology?"** → **surgical (marginal) excision**\
✗ Wrong options: observation · neoadjuvant chemo · adjuvant RT

**"56-yr-old, slowly enlarging medial thigh mass, history of thyroid cancer?"** → **intramuscular myxoma**\
✗ Wrong options: myxoid liposarcoma · neurofibroma · ganglion · metastasis

**"Benign tumour treated with marginal excision?"** → **intramuscular myxoma**\
✗ Wrong options: desmoid · myxofibrosarcoma · solitary fibrous tumour

**"Multiple myxomas + fibrous dysplasia?"** → **Mazabraud syndrome**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Leiomyosarcoma

*Pathology · 29 / 66*

## 1 Basics

**Nature** — aggressive sarcoma from **smooth muscle cells lining small blood vessels** ("leio-" = smooth muscle)

**Demographics** — **5th–6th decades**

**Bone** — <100 reported cases (extra-facial) · **metaphysis of long bones** (also diaphysis) · femur, tibia, **ilium**, humerus

### Genes & lab markers

**Immunostains** — **smooth muscle actin +**, **vimentin +**

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — bone pain, palpable firm mass · ~6 months before diagnosis · weight loss

**Pelvic masses** — hard to see on inspection

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — **purely osteolytic**, ill-defined, **moth-eaten / permeative** · intramedullary ± soft-tissue extension

**MRI** — **T1 dark (like muscle)** · T2 heterogeneous bright · diffuse enhancement

**Staging** — **chest X-ray + chest CT** (lung mets) · bone scan uptake

### Histology

**Spindle cell neoplasm** (osseous = soft tissue form) · **cigar-shaped (blunt-ended) nuclei** · **interlacing fascicles** with parallel myofibrils · high N:C, mitoses

![Spindle cells in fascicles with cigar-shaped nuclei](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8058-histolgy-1.jpg)

**Round cells?** — no: leiomyosarcoma is the **spindle-cell** exception among round-cell tumour lists

## 5 Management

```
Leiomyosarcoma
├─ Localized → EARLY WIDE RESECTION + RECONSTRUCTION
│      └─ Wide margin not achieved / contaminated bed → + ADJUVANT RADIATION
├─ Unplanned excision, margins unknown/positive → RE-EXCISION OF TUMOUR BED (± RT)
└─ Diffuse lung mets / unresectable pelvic mass on NV structures → CHEMOTHERAPY
```

**Chemo/RT** — variable response · chemo + surgery better than surgery alone · neoadjuvant/adjuvant role controversial

**Unplanned excision of 3 cm superficial LMS** — **repeat wide excision of tumour bed** · residual disease in up to **35%** · local recurrence **22% vs 7%**\
✗ Wrong options: observation · RT alone · chemo

## 6 Complications

**Prognosis (bone LMS)** — **25% recurrence**, **25% metastasis** · **75% survival at 3 yr**

## How it's asked

**"35-yr-old, aggressive lytic pelvic lesion, smooth muscle actin +?"** → **leiomyosarcoma** histology — interlacing spindle cells\
✗ Wrong options: GCT · myeloma · OS · hyaline cartilage

**"Outside simple excision of 3 cm thigh mass = leiomyosarcoma, margins unknown?"** → **repeat wide excision of the tumour bed**

**"Neoplasms with round cells EXCEPT?"** → **leiomyosarcoma** (spindle cells, cigar nuclei)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Leukemia

*Pathology · 30 / 66*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — malignant proliferation of lymphocytes / precursors (or myeloid cells)

**Commonest malignancy of childhood** — **leukaemia (ALL)**

### Forms

| Type | Cell | Age | Key features |
|---|---|---|---|
| **ALL** | Lymphoblasts | **Peak 4 yr** · **80%** of (childhood) leukaemia | **TdT +** · bone demineralization, **periostitis**, lytic lesions · **T-ALL** teens · **B-ALL**: **t(12;21) children**, **t(9;22) adults** |
| **AML** | Myeloblasts | **Older adults (5th–6th decade)** | **Auer rods** · marrow failure · **APL t(15;17)** disrupts retinoic acid receptor → **all-trans-retinoic acid** · **megakaryoblastic** → **Down syndrome**, <5 yr · **monocytic** → **gum infiltration** |
| **CML** | Mature granulocytes | 5th–6th decade | **t(9;22) Philadelphia chromosome → BCR-ABL tyrosine kinase** (↑ division, ↓ apoptosis) · smear: immature granulocytes, basophilia, eosinophilia · **imatinib** |
| **CLL** | Naive B cells | 5th–6th decade | Insidious · **smudge cells** · lymphadenopathy |

### Genes & lab markers

| Marker | Leukaemia |
|---|---|
| **TdT +** | ALL |
| t(12;21) | B-ALL, children |
| t(9;22) BCR-ABL | CML · B-ALL adults |
| t(15;17) RARα | APL (AML) |
| Auer rods | AML |
| Smudge cells | CLL |

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — **recurrent infections**, **bleeding**, **fatigue**, low-grade fever, **bone pain / limp**

**Signs** — **hepatosplenomegaly**, **lymphadenopathy** (commonest in CLL)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — diffuse osteopenia · **metaphyseal lucent bands** · periosteal reaction · lytic / mixed sclerotic-lytic · permeative destruction

**MRI** — scattered ill-defined medullary lesions · mimics **LCH and chronic osteomyelitis**

**Vertebral fractures** — **thoracic** commonest, then lumbar **(TO)**

**Diagnosis** — **peripheral smear + bone marrow biopsy** (lymphoblasts)

### Differential

| Lesion | Smear / histology |
|---|---|
| **ALL** | **Lymphoblasts** |
| AML | Myeloblasts + Auer rods, older |
| Chronic osteomyelitis | Neutrophilia, left shift |
| LCH | Eosinophilia, **CD1a, CD207 (langerin)**, Birbeck granules |

## 5 Management

**Chemotherapy** — ALL (T and B) · may predispose to **pathologic fracture**

**Targeted** — **ATRA** for APL · **imatinib** for CML

## How it's asked

**"4-yr-old, fatigue, fevers, recurrent ear infections, limp, ill-defined medullary lesions, lymphoblasts on smear?"** → **ALL**\
✗ Wrong options: AML · CML · chronic osteomyelitis · LCH

**"Most common location of vertebral fracture in leukaemia?"** → **thoracic** **(TO)**

**"Philadelphia chromosome?"** → **t(9;22) BCR-ABL — CML** (imatinib)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Lipomas

*Pathology · 31 / 66*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Common** benign tumour of **mature fat** · ~50% of soft-tissue neoplasms (OB)

**Demographics** — **40–60** · slightly **male** · sedentary individuals

**Site** — **subcutaneous** (upper back, thighs, buttocks, shoulders, arms) · deep/intramuscular (thigh, shoulder, arm) · **dorsum of foot** · **multiple in 5–10%**

### Variants

| Variant | Key features | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **Spindle cell lipoma** | Men **45–65**, neck/back/shoulder · mature fat + bland spindle cells, collagen, mast cells, myxoid · **CD34 +, S-100 −** | **Marginal excision** · rarely recurs |
| **Pleomorphic lipoma** | Middle age · lipocytes, spindle cells, **atypical giant cells** → mistaken for liposarcoma | Marginal excision |
| **Angiolipoma** | **Painful on palpation** · small nodules, upper limb · fat + nests of small arborizing vessels | Excision |
| **Intramuscular lipoma** | Often symptomatic · **lipoblasts + muscle infiltration** · may have infiltrative margins (✗ Wrong options: not malignancy) · small risk → liposarcoma | **Marginal resection** |
| **Hibernoma** | **Brown fat** · younger (**20–40**) · reddish-brown (vascular, mitochondria) | Excision |

### Genes & lab markers

**Spindle cell lipoma** — **CD34 +, S-100 −**

## 3 Presentation

**Painless, mobile, soft mass**, long-standing, size plateaus · symptoms from pressure (sitting, shoe wear, tool use) · **angiolipoma painful**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**MRI (diagnostic)** — well-demarcated, **homogeneous, signal = subcutaneous fat on ALL sequences** · **T1 bright**, T2 bright, **fully suppressed on STIR / fat-sat** · **no enhancement**

![Lipoma — homogeneous fat signal on T1](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8067-lipoma-t1-axial-moved.jpg)

**X-ray** — radiolucent soft-tissue shadow · **mineralization → think synovial sarcoma**

**CT** — well-demarcated, like subcutaneous fat

**Biopsy** — usually **unnecessary** · core/incisional prone to **sampling error** in fatty tumours

**Ganglion** — fluid-bright on T2 (not fat signal) · commonest soft-tissue foot/ankle lesion

### Histology

**Mature adipocytes**, eccentric nuclei, **bland acellular stroma**, no atypia · gross soft, lobular, yellow/white, ± encapsulated

### Atypical lipomatous tumour

**Deep (subfascial) or retroperitoneal lipomas** — higher chance of being **atypical lipomatous tumour** · retroperitoneum = **well-differentiated liposarcoma** · extremity = **atypical lipoma**

**MRI iso-intense with fat on all sequences** → lipoma / ALT / low-grade liposarcoma → **no biopsy, marginal excision** · recurrence 25–50% at 10 yr · dedifferentiation rare

### Intraosseous (calcaneal) lipoma

**"Cockade sign"** — well-defined lytic **calcaneal** lesion with **central calcification** → **pathognomonic** of intraosseous lipoma **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: chondroblastoma · NOF · EG

**Site** — **calcaneus ~30%** (commonest) · subtrochanteric femur · around knee · ~40 yr **(TO)**

**MRI** — **high T1 and T2**, suppressed on fat-sat **(TO)**

**DD** — SBC/UBC, ABC, GCT, osteoblastoma, calcaneal pseudotumour **(TO)**

| Milgram stage | Histology |
|---|---|
| **I** | Viable mature lipocytes |
| **II** | Necrotic foci, foamy macrophages, reactive bone |
| **III** | Complete necrosis, **calcification**, cysts (infarct-like) |

## 5 Management

```
Fatty soft-tissue mass, MRI = fat on all sequences
├─ Painless, determinate benign → OBSERVATION
└─ Symptomatic, rapidly growing, deep/retroperitoneal → MARGINAL EXCISION (no biopsy needed)
```

**Not indicated** — core / incisional biopsy · wide/radical resection · RT · chemo · amputation

## 6 Complications

**Local recurrence** — **<5%** (Orthobullets) · 15–60% (OB review, lipomatous masses) · 25–50% at 10 yr for ALT

## X vs Y

| | **Lipoma** | **Well-diff. liposarcoma / ALT** | **Myxoid liposarcoma** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Depth | Subcutaneous mostly | **Deep**, retroperitoneal | Deep, intermuscular |
| MRI | = fat all sequences | Fat + thick septa/nodules | T2 bright, myxoid, enhances |
| Histology | Mature fat, no atypia | Atypical stromal cells, lipoblasts | Lipoblasts, chicken-wire vessels |
| Treatment | Observe / marginal | Marginal excision (extremity) | Wide resection + RT |

## How it's asked

**"55-yr-old, posterior thigh fullness worse sitting, gluteal mass T1 bright, STIR dark, lipoblasts + muscle?"** → **intramuscular lipoma**\
✗ Wrong options: dedifferentiated liposarcoma · fibrosarcoma

**"Forearm mass, T1 = fat, STIR not bright — histology?"** → **lipoma** (mature fat)\
✗ Wrong options: myxoid liposarcoma · chondrosarcoma · Ewing

**"Symptomatic intramuscular arm lipoma, CT CAP negative?"** → **surgical excision**\
✗ Wrong options: core biopsy · RT

**"Painful dorsal foot fatty mass, no enhancement?"** → **lipoma, marginal excision**\
✗ Wrong options: wide resection

**"Enlarging painful forearm intramuscular lipoma?"** → **marginal resection**\
✗ Wrong options: core/incisional biopsy

**"Large symptomatic axillary/chest wall mass iso-intense to fat?"** → **marginal excision**\
✗ Wrong options: biopsy · wide + RT

**"Shoulder mass, CD34 + spindle cells in fat?"** → **spindle cell lipoma → marginal excision**

**"Cockade sign?"** → **calcaneal (intraosseous) lipoma** **(TO)**

**"Heel pain, lucent calcaneal lesion with central calcification, T1 + T2 bright?"** → **intraosseous lipoma** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: ABC · SBC · osteoblastoma

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Liposarcoma

*Pathology · 32 / 66*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Rank** — **2nd commonest soft-tissue sarcoma in adults** (UPS 1st) · ~20% of adult STS

**Demographics** — **50–80** · myxoid in younger adults (30–60, peak 5th decade) · **male** > female

**Site** — **deep to fascia** (almost never subcutaneous) · **75% extremities** (lower > upper) · **retroperitoneum**

**Lipomas do not predispose** to liposarcoma

### Hallmark cell

**Lipoblast** — **signet-ring** cell, indented nucleus, vacuolated cytoplasm · distinguishes from lipoma and UPS

### Subtypes & grade

| Subtype | Grade | Share | Metastasis |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Well-differentiated / ALT** | **Low** | 40–45% (OB) | **<1%** (ALT ~0%) |
| **Myxoid** | Low–**intermediate** | **~50% — commonest** (Orthobullets/OB) | **10–30%** |
| **Round cell** | High | (spectrum with myxoid) | >50% |
| **Pleomorphic** | High | **<5%**, most aggressive | >50% |
| **Dedifferentiated** | High | <10% · "commonest" per one OB explanation | **~50%** (highest) |

### Genes & lab markers

| Marker | Subtype |
|---|---|
| **MDM2 + CDK4 amplification** (12q13–15, **ring / giant marker chromosome 12**) | **ALT / WDL** and **dedifferentiated** · CDK4 sens 86%/spec 89%, MDM2 86%/74% |
| **t(12;16) FUS(TLS)–DDIT3(CHOP)** | **Myxoid** (± round cell) · rarely t(12;22) |
| **p53** mutation, complex karyotype | **Pleomorphic** |

**Best test to separate ALT from lipoma** — **FISH for MDM2 amplification**\
✗ Wrong options: MRI · S-100 (MPNST/melanoma) · CD138 (myeloma) · CD20 (lymphoma)

**MDM2** — negative regulator of p53

| Genetic finding | Tumour (distractors) |
|---|---|
| **MDM2/CDK4** | ✓ ALT/WDL |
| **t(12;16)** | ✓ Myxoid liposarcoma |
| t(2;13) | Alveolar rhabdomyosarcoma |
| t(X;18) SYT–SSX | Synovial sarcoma |
| ETV6–NTRK3 | Infantile fibrosarcoma |
| t(12;22) | Clear cell sarcoma |
| t(11;22) | Ewing |

## 3 Presentation

**Slow-growing, painless, deep mass** · often 10–20 cm · incidental · mass effect (sciatic paraesthesia, retroperitoneal)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**MRI with contrast** — best characterization (sens 100%, spec 71%, PPV 100%)

| Subtype | MRI |
|---|---|
| **ALT / WDL** | Like lipoma — fat signal (Orthobullets: **bright T1, dark T2**) · **thick (>2 mm), irregular septa / stranding**, nodules, mild enhancement, oedema → dedifferentiation focus |
| **Myxoid** | Heterogeneous, T2 bright, little fat (~10%) · **intense diffuse enhancement** (vs myxoma) |
| **High grade** | Like other sarcomas — **T1 dark, T2 bright** (only ~10% fat) |

![Myxoid liposarcoma histology](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8068-myxoid-liposarcoma.jpg)

**X-ray** — soft-tissue mass · calcification/ossification in WDL

**Biopsy** — areas **not consistent with fat** on MRI must be biopsied

### Histology

| Subtype | Histology |
|---|---|
| **ALT / WDL** | Mature fat with **atypical stromal cells**, scattered lipoblasts, minimally cellular · lipoma-like, sclerotic, inflammatory variants |
| **Myxoid** | Proliferating lipoblasts (signet ring at lobule edges) in **myxoid stroma** + **delicate plexiform "chicken-wire" capillaries** · "**pulmonary oedema**" pattern |
| **Round cell** | Small round blue cells |
| **Pleomorphic** | **Giant lipoblasts, bizarre nuclei**, eosinophilic giant cells (UPS-like + lipoblasts) |
| **Dedifferentiated** | ALT/WDL transformed to high-grade sarcoma |

### Staging

**Myxoid liposarcoma — extrapulmonary spread** — lungs + **abdomen, retroperitoneum, spine**, other extremities' soft tissue, bone marrow

**Myxoid staging/surveillance** — **CT chest/abdomen/pelvis + whole-spine MRI** + MRI of primary site\
✗ Wrong options: chest-only · PET (misses spine) · brain MRI · sentinel node · marrow biopsy

**Other STS** — chest CT

## 5 Management

```
Liposarcoma
├─ ALT (extremity), MDM2/CDK4 + → MARGINAL RESECTION, NO RADIATION
├─ Myxoid (intermediate) → WIDE RESECTION + RT
│      └─ >5% round cells (OB: ≥25%) or margins inadequate → + CHEMO (ifosfamide-based)
└─ Round cell / pleomorphic / dedifferentiated (high grade) → WIDE RESECTION + RT (pre-op 50 Gy)
```

**ALT/WDL** — **marginal resection without RT** (OB) · Orthobullets lists WDL for wide resection + RT (vs ALT marginal) — read the options ·\
✗ Wrong options: amputation · chemo · RT alone · wide resection for extremity ALT

**Myxoid** — **most chemo- and radiosensitive** liposarcoma

**Radiation** — **50 Gy preop**, **66 Gy postop** standard for STS · ↓ local recurrence

**Technique** — tourniquet without exsanguination · frozen section margins · NV resection only if bypass possible and morbidity acceptable

### Soft tissue sarcoma treatment (margins, radiation, staging)

**Most important factor preventing local recurrence** — **adequacy of surgical margin**\
✗ Wrong options: chemo · RT alone

**Large (>5 cm), deep, high-grade STS** — **wide excision + radiation** (pre- or post-op)

**Pleomorphic liposarcoma, no mets** — **neoadjuvant RT 50 Gy → wide resection**\
✗ Wrong options: RFA · resection alone · chemo-based · disarticulation

## 6 Complications

**Mass effect** — NV bundles, retroperitoneum, peritoneum

| Subtype | Local recurrence | Dedifferentiation | Survival |
|---|---|---|---|
| **ALT** | **10–50% over 10 yr** | **3–7%** (2% extremity) | ~100% |
| **WDL** (central) | High | **20%** | ~80% 5-yr |
| Intermediate/high | ~20% | — | 50–80% 5-yr |

## X vs Y

| | **ALT** | **Lipoma** |
|---|---|---|
| MDM2/CDK4 | **+** | − |
| Septa | Thick (>2 mm), nodular | Thin |
| Depth | Deep | Usually subcutaneous |
| Treatment | Marginal excision | Observe / marginal |

| | **Myxoid liposarcoma** | **Intramuscular myxoma** |
|---|---|---|
| Location | **Inter**muscular | **Intra**muscular |
| Cellularity | Cellular, **lipoblasts**, chicken-wire vessels | **Hypocellular**, bland |
| Enhancement | Intense | Little |

## How it's asked

**"Thigh fatty tumour, MDM2 + CDK4 +?"** → **atypical lipomatous tumour**\
✗ Wrong options: lipoma · RMS · LMS · myxofibrosarcoma

**"Genetic basis + treatment of thigh WDL?"** → **MDM2 amplification; marginal resection without radiation**

**"Gluteal ALT, MDM2 +?"** → **marginal resection**\
✗ Wrong options: wide · RT · chemo

**"Best test to separate ALT from lipoma?"** → **FISH for MDM2**

**"Feature allowing marginal excision alone?"** → **MDM2 + CDK4 amplification**\
✗ Wrong options: t(12;16) · t(2;13) · ETV6-NTRK3 · SYT-SSX

**"Lipomatous tumour with highest metastatic rate?"** → **dedifferentiated liposarcoma**

**"Risk of ALT dedifferentiating?"** → **3–7%**

**"Thigh mass, signet-ring lipoblasts in myxoid stroma — translocation?"** → **myxoid liposarcoma, t(12;16)**\
✗ Wrong options: alveolar RMS · synovial t(X;18)

**"33-yr-old, thigh mass, 12;16 translocation?"** → **myxoid liposarcoma**\
✗ Wrong options: WDL · round cell · fibrolipomatous hamartoma

**"Posterior thigh mass with sciatic symptoms, chicken-wire vessels?"** → **myxoid liposarcoma**\
✗ Wrong options: synovial sarcoma · MFH · fibrosarcoma

**"Myxoid liposarcoma after resection — extra staging?"** → **whole-spine MRI**

**"Myxoid liposarcoma surveillance?"** → **MRI of thigh + CT chest/abdomen/pelvis**

**"Slide warranting marginal resection alone?"** → **well-differentiated liposarcoma / ALT**\
✗ Wrong options: myxoid · dedifferentiated · UPS · pleomorphic

**"Thigh mass following fat with stranding + enhancement?"** → **liposarcoma**\
✗ Wrong options: synovial sarcoma (cystic, no fat)

**"Most important factor against local recurrence in UPS?"** → **adequate surgical margin**

**"Large, deep, high-grade dedifferentiated liposarcoma?"** → **wide excision + radiation**

**"15 cm pleomorphic liposarcoma, p53 +, t(12;16) −?"** → **neoadjuvant RT (50 Gy) → wide resection**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Lymphoma

*Pathology · 33 / 66*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Primary lymphoma of bone (PBL)** — **very rare** · 2–3% of primary bone malignancies (OB) · 3–7% (TO, osteosarcoma chapter)

**Extranodal** — 10–35% of NHL patients

**Demographics** — **males** · all ages, **35–55** commonest (median 45–60)

**Site** — bones with **persistent red marrow**: **pelvis, spine, ribs** · knee (distal femur, proximal tibia), **proximal femur**, shoulder girdle · **diaphysis** of long bones

**Risk factors** — immunodeficiency (HIV, hepatitis) · viral/bacterial infection

### Forms

| Form | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Primary lymphoma of bone** | Solitary site, no other disease for **6 months** · mostly **non-Hodgkin B-cell** · **better prognosis** than secondary bone involvement |
| Multiple bony sites | No visceral disease |
| Bone + soft tissue | — |

**Subtype** — **diffuse large B-cell lymphoma (DLBCL)** = **70–80%** of bone lymphomas

### Genes & lab markers

**Immunostains +** — **CD20**, **CD45 (leukocyte common antigen)**, CD79a, BCL-6 (B cell) · **CD3 −**

**Negative** — **CD99 −**, **no t(11;22)** (vs Ewing) · reticulin + (vs Ewing −)

| Stain | Tumour |
|---|---|
| **CD20 + CD45** | ✓ **Lymphoma** |
| CD99 + FLI-1 (EWSR1) | Ewing |
| CD138 | Myeloma |
| CD31 + CD34 | Vascular tumours |
| Desmin + MyoD1 (myogenin) | **Rhabdomyosarcoma** |

## 3 Presentation

**Pain unrelieved by rest** · **B symptoms** — fever, **night sweats**, weight loss, fatigue

**Pathologic fracture** — **25%**

**Mass** — warm, swollen, large **soft-tissue mass** · spinal cord compression

**Labs** — may mimic infection (mild ↑ ESR/CRP, lymphocytosis)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — **large ill-defined, diffuse, permeative/moth-eaten lytic** lesion, subtle mottling · **diaphysis** · cortical thickening 25% · may be **sclerotic** · **"ivory" vertebra** · multiple sites · often **subtle/unremarkable**

![Diffuse lytic mottled lesion of the femur](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8025-case-a-femur-xray-parsons-moved.jpg)

**MRI** — **extensive marrow involvement + large soft-tissue mass out of proportion to bone destruction** · T1 dark, T2 bright

**Bone scan** — intensely positive · **PET/CT** — staging + follow-up

**CT chest/abdomen/pelvis** — staging

**Bone marrow aspiration + biopsy** — **required for staging** · **before any definitive bony stabilization** when no known primary\
✗ Wrong options: chemo first · resection · prophylactic nail first

**Moth-eaten (type 2) margins (TO)** — myeloma, mets, infection, EG, OS, chondrosarcoma, **lymphoma** · 45-yr-old, proximal humeral path fracture, moth-eaten → **lymphoma**

### Histology

**Mixed small round blue cells** of **different sizes and shapes** · **diffuse infiltration** of trabeculae (not nodular) · little stroma

**Needle biopsy** — often non-diagnostic (**crush artefact**)

![Mixed small round blue cell infiltrate](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8025-histology-b-high-parsons-moved.jpg)

### Differential

| | Destructive, young | Malignant, older | Small cells | Chemo alone |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Lymphoma** | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |
| Leukaemia · Ewing | ✓ | — | ✓ | — |
| OS · EG · osteomyelitis · desmoplastic fibroma | ✓ | — | — | — |
| Metastasis | — | ✓ | ✓ | — |
| **Myeloma** | — | ✓ | — | ✓ |
| Chondrosarcoma · MFH · secondary sarcoma | — | ✓ | — | — |
| RMS · neuroblastoma | — | — | ✓ | — |

**Ewing vs lymphoma** — Ewing younger, CD99 +, t(11;22) · lymphoma older, CD20/CD45 + · TB → caseating granulomas · osteomyelitis → mixed inflammatory cells

## 5 Management

### Treatment — chemotherapy (R-CHOP) & fixation

```
Primary lymphoma of bone
├─ First → BONE MARROW BIOPSY + PET/CT (staging)
├─ All → MULTI-AGENT CHEMO (R-CHOP) ± LOCAL RT (persistent disease)
└─ Impending / actual pathologic fracture → STABILISE (IM nail / hemiarthroplasty) then chemo ± RT
```

**Chemo** — **CHOP: cyclophosphamide, doxorubicin (hydroxydaunorubicin), vincristine (Oncovin), prednisone** **± rituximab (R-CHOP)** · chemo alone effective for most · **70% 5-yr survival** in disseminated disease

**Radiation** — adjunct for local control of persistent disease · RT alone insufficient · routine RT in R-CHOP era uncertain

**Surgery** — **only to stabilise** pathologic / impending fractures (prophylactic IM nail) · surgery mustn't delay chemo

**Femoral neck lesion found at hemiarthroplasty (frozen section = lymphoma)** — **complete hemiarthroplasty, then staging + chemoradiotherapy**\
✗ Wrong options: close wound + chemo · radical resection + megaprosthesis · marrow transplant · disarticulation

**Not indicated** — wide resection / limb salvage · amputation · hemipelvectomy · marrow transplant (relapse only) · neoadjuvant chemo + resection (OS/Ewing protocol)

## 6 Complications

**Prognosis** — **primary bone lymphoma better** than secondary bone involvement

## X vs Y

| | **Lymphoma of bone** | **Ewing** | **Myeloma** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Age | 35–55 | 5–25 | >50 |
| X-ray | Permeative, mottled, ± sclerotic, big soft-tissue mass | Permeative, onion skin | **Punched-out**, cold bone scan |
| Marker | **CD20, CD45** | **CD99** | **CD138** |
| Treatment | **R-CHOP ± RT** | VAC/IE + surgery ± RT | Chemo ± RT |

## How it's asked

**"45-yr-old, destructive lesion + soft-tissue mass, round blue cells, CD99 −?"** → **lymphoma of bone**\
✗ Wrong options: Ewing · chondrosarcoma · adenocarcinoma · OS

**"32-yr-old distal femur, sheets of blue cells, no t(11;22)?"** → **lymphoma**\
✗ Wrong options: Ewing · bacterial/TB osteomyelitis · OS

**"50-yr-old, tibial lesion, B symptoms, large soft-tissue mass — stains?"** → **CD20 + CD45**\
✗ Wrong options: CD31/CD34 · CD99/FLI-1 · CD138 · desmin/MyoD1

**"48-yr-old, fevers, weight loss, CD20/CD45 — histology?"** → **small round blue cells**

**"76-yr-old proximal femur, PET shows single site, prophylactic fixation + chemo — supporting pathology?"** → **large atypical lymphoid cells CD20 +, CD79a +, BCL-6 +, CD3 −**

**"Pathologic femoral neck fracture through lymphoma at hemiarthroplasty?"** → **hemiarthroplasty, then staging + chemoradiotherapy**

**"Severe thigh pain, extensive femoral lymphoma?"** → **IM nail + multi-agent chemo ± RT**\
✗ Wrong options: AKA · wide resection + prosthesis

**"Knee pain, failed antibiotics, biopsy = lymphoma?"** → **multi-agent chemo ± local RT**

**"Iliac DLBCL, PET no other lesions?"** → **CHOP chemotherapy**\
✗ Wrong options: marrow transplant · CT CAP for primary · hemipelvectomy · RT alone

**"40-yr-old, distal thigh lytic lesions, weight loss, no known primary — next?"** → **bone marrow aspiration + biopsy**\
✗ Wrong options: chemo · resection · nail first

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Malignant Peripheral Nerve Sheath Tumor

*Pathology · 34 / 66*

## 1 Basics

**Synonyms** — **neurofibrosarcoma**, malignant schwannoma · high-grade STS from a **nerve or pre-existing neurofibroma**

**Age** — solitary **30–55** · with **NF1 20–40**

### Diagnosis & NF1 association

**NF1** — most cases · **4% of NF1 patients** develop MPNST · **autosomal dominant** (17q11.2)\
✗ Wrong options: AR · X-linked

### Genes & lab markers

**S-100 +** (diffuse) · **keratin −**

**S-100 positive tumours** — **melanoma, nerve sheath tumours, clear cell sarcoma** (± synovial sarcoma) · chondroblastoma, chondroid lesions

## 3 Presentation

**Mass** on a **large nerve** — **sciatic, brachial plexus**, femoral

**Neurologic** — motor + sensory deficit, radicular pain · **pressure → dysaesthesia** distally

**NF1 red flag** — a neurofibroma that **enlarges** and becomes **constantly painful**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — normal / non-specific soft-tissue mass

**MRI** — **T1 low, T2 high** (like other high-grade sarcomas) · traced to a major nerve · oedema, **rim enhancement (necrosis)** · serial MRI: enlargement of a benign nerve sheath lesion = malignant change

**PET** — **SUV >2.0** → malignant

![MPNST arising from the sciatic nerve](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8055-axial-mri-demonstrating-mpnst-arising-from-sciatic-nerve.jpg)

**Bone scan** — mildly positive

### Histology

**Atypical pleomorphic spindle cells with wavy nuclei** — resembles **fibrosarcoma** · hypocellular stroma areas

| | **MPNST** | **Schwannoma** | **Neurofibroma** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Cells | **Pleomorphic, atypical** spindle cells | Bland, Antoni A/B | Bland, wavy nuclei, mast cells |
| MRI | Necrosis, oedema, rim enhancement | **Target sign** | Entering-exiting nerve, target sign |
| S-100 | + | + | + |

## 5 Management

**Wide resection including the entire affected nerve** + **preoperative (neoadjuvant) radiation** — treated as high-grade sarcoma

**Chemotherapy** — not useful

## 6 Complications

**5-yr survival** — **solitary 75%** · **NF1-associated 30%** (Orthobullets) · one OB-cited series: NF1 not independently worse

**Poor prognosis** — size, mitotic rate, need for amputation

## How it's asked

**"45-yr-old, radicular buttock pain, S-100 + pleomorphic spindle cells?"** → **MPNST**\
✗ Wrong options: schwannoma (bland, target sign) · liposarcoma · GCT tendon sheath

**"NF patient, enlarging painful posterior thigh mass on sciatic nerve, rim enhancement, PET SUV >2?"** → **MPNST**\
✗ Wrong options: benign PNST · MFH · liposarcoma · synovial sarcoma

**"S-100 + spindle-cell tumour of femoral nerve — associated condition's inheritance?"** → **autosomal dominant** (NF1)

**"Treatment?"** → **wide resection of tumour + entire nerve + preop RT**\
✗ Wrong options: chemo

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Melanoma

*Pathology · 35 / 66*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Nature** — aggressive skin malignancy of **melanocytes** (from **neural crest**, deepest epidermal layer)

**Demographics** — **50–70** · **M:F 1.2:1**

**Hand/foot sites** — **thumb > great toe > index finger** · sun-exposed areas

**Subtype** — **acral lentiginous melanoma** (palms, subungual) · **subungual melanoma** = ALM subtype

### Risk factors

**Sun exposure** (UV suppresses immunity, DNA damage) · **family history** · fair skin, blue eyes, freckling · **xeroderma pigmentosum** · FAMM syndrome · multiple / **dysplastic naevi (precursor)** · immunosuppression

**Prevention** — sunscreen, avoid sun

### Genes & lab markers

**S-100 +** (melanoma)

**LDH ↑** — predicts poor prognosis · **AST/ALT** — liver mets · CBC

## 3 Presentation

**Pigmented lesion with recent change in shape or size** · itching, bleeding, ulceration

**ABCDE** — **A**symmetry · **B**order irregularity · **C**olour variation · **D**iameter (>6 mm suspicious; <6 mm benign) · **E**levation / enlargement

**Subungual** — dystrophic nail prone to trauma → often blamed on **injury**

**Hutchinson sign** — brown-black pigment extends from nail bed/plate onto **cuticle and nail folds** ± nail lifting

**Longitudinal melanonychia — malignant features** — band **>6 mm**, **single nail**, wider proximally, colour heterogeneity, blurry borders, pigment into nail fold

![Subungual melanoma with Hutchinson sign](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6093-toe-subungual-melanoma.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**CXR** — lungs often first metastatic site · **US** — nodes · **PET/CT** — metastases

**Biopsy** — **full-thickness biopsy of the nail matrix** after nail plate removal\
✗ Wrong options: trephination · antibiotics · no work-up

### Histology

**Melanocytes with marked atypia + dermal invasion** · vacuolated cytoplasm · hyperchromatic nuclei, prominent nucleoli

### Breslow thickness

| Stage | Thickness |
|---|---|
| 1 | **≤0.75 mm** |
| 2 | 0.76–1.5 mm |
| 3 | 1.51–4 mm |
| 4 | **>4 mm** |

### Clark level

| Level | Invasion |
|---|---|
| **I** | Epidermis only (in situ) |
| **II** | Papillary dermis |
| **III** | Papillary dermis to papillary–reticular interface |
| **IV** | Reticular dermis |
| **V** | Subcutaneous tissue |

### Differential

**General** — naevi · seborrhoeic keratosis · basal cell carcinoma

**Subungual** — **trauma / subungual haematoma** (contained under plate, needs trauma) · **onychomycosis** (thickening, yellow-brown) · psoriasis (pitting) · SCC (no pigment) · splinter haemorrhages (endocarditis → echo) · glomus tumour (painful, blue) · DFSP (trunk)

## 5 Management

### Margins by thickness

| Lesion | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **<1 mm** | Local resection, **1 cm** margin |
| **1–2 mm** | Resection **1–2 cm** + **sentinel node biopsy** → radical node dissection if positive |
| Thick | **>3 cm** margin (OB) |
| Metastatic | Resection + node dissection + chemo |

### Subungual melanoma

**Amputation** — **thumb: IP joint disarticulation / through proximal phalanx** · finger: PIP level · **+ sentinel node biopsy**

**Distal = proximal** — distal amputation with adequate margins has **same recurrence and survival** as MCP/ray/CMC amputation → preserves function

**Not used** — neoadjuvant chemo to downsize · 1–2 **mm** margin · Mohs (controversial; for SCC/BCC) · isolated limb perfusion routinely

## 6 Complications

**Recurrence** — usually **regional lymph nodes**

**Prognosis** — **depth = most important factor** · <0.7 mm → **96%** survival · >4 mm → **47%**

**Poor prognosis** — deep lesion · **male** · **neck/scalp** · positive nodes/mets · **ulceration** · ↑ LDH

**Subungual** — **5-yr survival 40–60%** (late diagnosis, thicker, ulcerated)

**Bone metastasis** — melanoma + lung: worst survival after pathologic fracture (<6 months)

## How it's asked

**"62-yr-old, enlarging brown nail streak on ring finger — biopsy to exclude?"** → **melanoma**\
✗ Wrong options: DFSP · endocarditis · glomus · epithelioid sarcoma

**"Persistent thumbnail pigment after hammer blow, Hutchinson sign — next step?"** → **full-thickness nail matrix biopsy**\
✗ Wrong options: trephination · antibiotics · IPJ disarticulation first

**"65-yr-old, thumbnail discoloration onto folds, no trauma?"** → **melanoma**\
✗ Wrong options: SCC · onychomycosis · haematoma · psoriasis

**"Thin (0.6 mm) subungual thumb melanoma — best cure with function?"** → **IP joint disarticulation + sentinel node biopsy**\
✗ Wrong options: MCP disarticulation · ray amputation + limb perfusion · Mohs · chemo + 1–2 mm margin

**"Most important prognostic factor?"** → **depth (thickness)**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Melorheostosis

*Pathology · 36 / 66*

## 1 Basics

**Nature** — rare, **benign, painful** developmental dysplasia of cortical bone · formation of **periosteal (or endosteal) new bone** — "flowing hyperostosis"

**Demographics** — **before age 40** · **no sex predilection**

**Genetics** — **non-hereditary**

**Site** — **lower extremities** commoner · any bone (long bones, ulna, hand, foot/metatarsals)

### Genes & lab markers

**ALP** — often **elevated**

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — **pain**, **reduced ROM**, **joint contractures**, swelling

**Skin** — **fibrosis with induration and erythema** over the lesion

**Incidental** — may be found on unrelated imaging (asymptomatic)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray (diagnostic)** — **"dripping candle wax"** — dense **cortical hyperostosis flowing along the cortex** · may **cross joints** → mechanical block

![Dripping candle-wax hyperostosis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8043-x-ray-of-hand-melorheostosis.jpg)

**CT** — non-aggressive sclerosis, may **obliterate the medullary canal**

**Bone scan** — **hot** (↑ osteoblastic activity) — not a sign of malignancy here

### Histology

**Normal Haversian systems**, enlarged trabeculae · bland osteoid · **no atypia, no mitoses**

### Differential

| Lesion | Distinguishing |
|---|---|
| **Melorheostosis** | Flowing candle-wax cortical hyperostosis, bland bone |
| Metastatic prostate cancer | Blastic lesions, carcinoma on histology (↑ PSA is a distractor) |
| Periosteal OS | Surface sunburst, malignant osteoid |
| Spindle cell sarcoma | Malignant spindle cells |
| Stress fracture | Fracture line, callus |

**Association** — melorheostosis can underlie **secondary osteosarcoma** (rare)

## 5 Management

```
Melorheostosis
├─ Asymptomatic / incidental → OBSERVATION (X-ray diagnostic, no biopsy)
├─ Mild symptoms, adequate motion → SYMPTOMATIC CARE + BISPHOSPHONATES (zoledronate)
└─ Severe contracture, immobility, pain → HYPEROSTOTIC BONE RESECTION + CONTRACTURE RELEASE (± osteotomy)
```

**Bisphosphonates** — help **pain and swelling**\
✗ Wrong options: calcitonin · rituximab · tamoxifen (desmoid) · stereotactic radiation

## How it's asked

**"55-yr-old, foot pain, sclerotic 2nd metatarsal, hot scan, bland bone, ↑ PSA?"** → **melorheostosis**\
✗ Wrong options: metastatic prostate · periosteal OS · spindle cell sarcoma · stress fracture

**"21-yr-old, incidental flowing hyperostosis of proximal femur?"** → **observation**\
✗ Wrong options: needle/incisional biopsy · curettage · resection

**"Elbow pain, swelling, ROM 30–90°, candle-wax hyperostosis — beneficial drug?"** → **zoledronate**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Metastatic Disease of Extremity

*Pathology · 37 / 66*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Commonest cause of a destructive bone lesion in adults** · commonest tumour of bone · aggressive lytic lesion >40 yr: **metastasis > myeloma > lymphoma > Paget sarcoma > post-radiation sarcoma**

**Solitary bone lesion >40 yr** — **500×** more likely metastatic carcinoma than primary bone tumour

**Bone = 3rd commonest metastatic site** (after lung, liver)

**Age** — >50

**Primaries (BLT + Kosher Pickle)** — **Breast** (commonest in women; overall), **Lung**, **Thyroid**, **Kidney**, **Prostate** (commonest in men) · overall **breast > prostate > lung** **(TO)** · men: prostate > lung · women: breast > lung · children: **neuroblastoma**

**Breast** — bone is the first metastatic site in 40–75% **(TO)**

### Sites

**Commonest site of bone metastasis** — **spine (thoracic)** **(TO)** > pelvis > proximal femur > humerus · then ribs, skull · red marrow

**Pathologic fracture** — **proximal femur** commonest (then proximal humerus) · femoral neck 50%, subtrochanteric 30%, peritrochanteric 20% (OB: 50% neck, 30% subtroch, 20% intertroch) · TO: femoral neck commonest, transverse lines

**Acral (distal to elbow/knee)** — rare · **lung** commonest (then kidney) → **arterial tree** spread · **tibia** commonest bone **(TO)** · poor prognosis

**Occult primary** — biopsy shows carcinoma but CT CAP + bone scan negative → **lung**\
✗ Wrong options: colon, kidney, prostate, breast

**Batson vertebral plexus** — **valveless** venous plexus → lung, kidney, breast, prostate, thyroid to **vertebral bodies, pelvis, skull, proximal girdles** · breast → azygos → Batson in **thoracic** spine

**Spine** — vertebral body involved before pedicle, but **pedicle destruction ("winking owl")** is commonest first plain-film sign **(TO)**

### Biology of metastasis (integrins, MMPs, RANKL, endothelin)

| Step | Molecule |
|---|---|
| Release from primary | **↓ E-cadherin** (cell adhesion lost) · **PDGF** → migration |
| Intravasation, avoid immune surveillance | — |
| **Homing to bone** | **CXCL12** (marrow stromal chemokine) |
| **Attachment to endothelium / bone** | **Integrin** (tumour cell CAM) |
| **Extravasation, ECM degradation** | **MMPs** (collagenases, gelatinases, stromelysins) |
| Angiogenesis | **VEGF** |
| Survival | Genomic instability, **↓ apoptosis** · **thrombospondin inhibits** tumour growth |

**Necessary steps** — intravasation, immune evasion, target localization, angiogenesis \
✗ Wrong options: **direct osteoclast stimulation** (indirect, via RANKL) · **increased apoptosis**

**Lytic "vicious cycle"** — tumour **PTHrP** (+ IL-6) → **osteoblasts release RANKL** → RANK on osteoclast precursors → osteolysis → **TGF-β, IGF-1, Ca²⁺** released → more PTHrP · blocked by bisphosphonates, denosumab, OPG

**Blastic** — **endothelin-1** → ET-A receptor on osteoblasts, ↓ **DKK-1** → WNT ↑ · also TGF-β, BMPs **(TO)**

### Lytic vs blastic

| Pattern | Primaries |
|---|---|
| **Lytic** | **Kidney, thyroid, lung** (also GI, melanoma) |
| **Blastic** | **Prostate (90%)**, seminoma, carcinoid, medulloblastoma · breast 60% (OB) |
| **Mixed** | **Breast** — lytic, blastic or mixed ("all of the above") **(TO)** · commoner lytic (TO) |

**Pulsatile metastases** — **follicular thyroid + RCC** (most pulsatile tumours are mets > ABC > GCT) **(TO)**

**Bone-to-bone metastasis** — Ewing > osteosarcoma **(TO)**

### Genes & lab markers

| Test | Looks for |
|---|---|
| CBC, ESR, BMP, LFTs, PT/PTT, Ca, phosphate | Baseline, hypercalcaemia |
| **SPEP / UPEP** | **Myeloma** |
| **PSA** | Prostate |
| **LDH** | Lymphoma of bone |
| ALP | Paget, bone turnover |
| Urinalysis | Renal cell |
| Acid phosphatase · urine hydroxyproline | Prostate · massive destruction **(TO)** |

**Histology IHC** — **keratin**, **CK7** (breast, lung), **TTF-1** (lung) · RCC: **clear cell change, pseudoalveolar** pattern

**Breast receptor status** — **ER, PR, HER2/neu** guide therapy (tamoxifen/letrozole, trastuzumab)\
✗ Wrong options: RANKL · BRCA · CA-125 · t(11;22)

## 3 Presentation

**Pain** — dull, gradual; **night pain** (tumoral) or **weight-bearing** (mechanical) · weight loss, mass

**Pathologic fracture** — **8–30% at presentation** · **90% need surgery** (rarely heal)

**Atraumatic lesser trochanter avulsion in an adult** — **malignancy until proven otherwise**

**Metastatic hypercalcaemia (emergency)** — confusion, weakness, **polyuria/polydipsia**, N/V, dehydration → **IV fluids**, loop diuretic, **bisphosphonate**

**Complications (Coleman)** — hypercalcaemia 19%, pathologic fracture 19%, cord compression 10%, marrow failure 9%

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Workup of solitary lytic lesion — staging & biopsy before fixation

```
New painful/destructive bone lesion, >40 yr
├─ 1. History + exam (breast, thyroid, prostate) + LABS (SPEP/UPEP, PSA…)
├─ 2. X-ray whole bone + CXR → CT CHEST/ABDOMEN/PELVIS + WHOLE-BODY BONE SCAN (± MRI lesion)
├─ 3. Primary found + multiple lesions → biopsy most accessible lesion if needed
└─ 4. Solitary lesion (even with remote/known cancer) → BIOPSY (treating team, intramuscular tract)
       └─ Carcinoma confirmed → FIX (whole bone) + RT · Sarcoma → wide resection protocol
```

**Never treat a bone lesion without a tissue diagnosis** \
✗ Wrong options: IM nail with **reamings sent as biopsy** (contaminates femur, abductors, skin) · empirical chemo/RT · ORIF

**Remote cancer + solitary lesion** — **biopsy** (could be primary sarcoma) — breast cancer 10–26 yr earlier, lung cancer resected, colon cancer, Paget patient

**Nailed "metastasis" proves high-grade chondrosarcoma** — whole femur contaminated → **wide resection incl. hip disarticulation** (or total femoral resection)

**Open biopsy** — **intramuscular** interval\
✗ Wrong options: intermuscular

**Lab + CT + MRI before biopsy** for atraumatic lesser trochanter fracture\
✗ Wrong options: suture anchor repair · IM nail first

**Pathologic humeral shaft fracture, no cancer history** — **coaptation splint, then bone scan + CT CAP** (then biopsy)\
✗ Wrong options: immediate nail with reamings · ORIF + biopsy first

**Painful TKA with new tibial osteolysis, normal infection labs** — **CT CAP / PET-CT staging**\
✗ Wrong options: revision

**Primary identified** — **~85%** by history + exam + X-ray + CT CAP (OB: CT CAP **90%**, >90% sensitive) · bone scan + CT ~50%

### Key radiology findings

| Modality | Fact |
|---|---|
| **X-ray** | Two planes, joint above + below · lytic / mixed / blastic · **sensitivity ~50%** — needs **30–50% mineral loss**, 1–2 cm · lung → **cortical** mets |
| **CT CAP** | **Find primary in all >40 yr with unknown lytic lesion** · CT of lesion for cortical containment · sens 71–100% |
| **MRI** | **Most sensitive (95%) + specific (90%)** · marrow change before blastic change · T1 low, T2 high · local staging |
| **Bone scan** | Other skeletal lesions · **cold in myeloma + thyroid** → **skeletal survey** · **investigation of choice for occult blastic mets** **(TO)** |
| **PET-CT** | Investigation of choice for **occult lytic** mets **(TO)** · PET alone 30% specificity |
| Indium scan | Infection (✗ Wrong options: tumour) |

![Acral metastasis to the hand from lung carcinoma](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8045-case-d-hand-lung-xray-parsons-moved.png)

### Histology

**Epithelial cells in clumps / glands in fibrous stroma** · RCC: **clear cells, pseudoalveolar** · metastatic gastric adenocarcinoma: glands + bone

![Metastatic carcinoma — epithelial glands in stroma](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8045-histology-a-parsons-moved.png)

**Fix-without-wide-resection histology** — **metastatic carcinoma (breast)**\
✗ Wrong options: OS lacy osteoid · dedifferentiated CS · fibrosarcoma herringbone · chordoma physaliferous cells

### Differential

| | Malignant, older | Multiple, older | Epithelial glands | Bisphosphonates help | Wide resection + RT |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Metastasis** | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | (RCC solitary) |
| Myeloma | ✓ | ✓ | — | ✓ | — |
| Lymphoma of bone | ✓ | ✓ | — | — | — |
| Chondrosarcoma · MFH · secondary sarcoma | ✓ | — | — | — | — |
| Paget | — | ✓ | — | ✓ | — |
| FD | — | — | — | ✓ | — |
| Synovial sarcoma · glomus | — | — | ✓ | — | — |
| Hyperparathyroidism | — | ✓ | — | — | — |

### Mirels criteria

| Score | Site | Size (of shaft diameter) | Appearance | Pain |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | Upper limb | <1/3 | Blastic | Mild |
| **2** | Lower limb | 1/3–2/3 | Mixed | Moderate |
| **3** | **Peritrochanteric** | **>2/3** | **Lytic** | **Functional (weight-bearing)** |

**Recommendation (Orthobullets)** — **<8 RT + observe** · **8 judgement** · **>8 prophylactic fixation** · OB explanations vary: "≥8", "≥9", ">9" — read the options

| Mirels | Fracture risk (6 mo) |
|---|---|
| 4–6 | 0% |
| 7 | 4% |
| 8 | 15% |
| **9** | **33%** |
| 10 | 72% |
| 11 | 96% |
| 12 | 100% |

**Sensitivity 80–90%, specificity 30–35%**

**Harrington (prophylactic fixation)** — **>50% diaphyseal cortex** destruction · **>50–75% metaphysis** · **permeative subtrochanteric** destruction · **persistent pain after RT** · pain with weight bearing · (OB: proximal femur lesion >2.5 cm, avulsion of lesser trochanter)

### Spinal metastasis prognostic scores (Tokuhashi, Tomita)

**Tokuhashi** — **prognosis after spinal metastasis** to guide treatment **(TO)** · ✓ includes **general condition** · **≤8 → <6 months** (conservative) · **9–11** individual · **≥12 → >1 yr** (excisional)

| Modified Tokuhashi parameter | Scores |
|---|---|
| General condition (KPS) | Poor 10–40% = 0 · moderate 50–70% = 1 · good 80–100% = 2 |
| Extraspinal bone mets | >2 = 0 · 1–2 = 1 · 0 = 2 |
| Vertebral body mets (original score) | 0–2 |
| Visceral (major organ) mets | Non-removable 0 · removable 1 · none 2 |
| Primary | **Lung, OS, stomach, bladder, oesophagus, pancreas 0** · liver, gallbladder, unidentified 1 · other 2 · **kidney, uterus 3** · rectum 4 · **thyroid, breast, prostate, carcinoid 5** |
| Palsy | Frankel A–B 0 · C–D 1 · E 2 |

**Tomita (2001)** — **3 parameters**: primary growth, visceral mets, bone mets \
✗ Wrong options: **general condition** (that's Tokuhashi) **(TO)**

| Tomita parameter | Score |
|---|---|
| Primary: slow (breast, thyroid) / moderate (kidney, uterus) / rapid (lung, stomach) | 1 / 2 / 4 |
| Visceral mets: none / treatable / untreatable | 0 / 2 / 4 |
| Bone mets: solitary / multiple | 1 / 2 |

**Tomita action** — **2–4 aggressive surgery** · **5–7 palliative surgery** · **8–10 non-operative / supportive**

**Others** — Bauer, Linden, Rades, Douglas, Katagiri scores **(TO)**

## 5 Management

### General principles

**Goals** — pain control, independence, prevent fracture · **implant should outlive the patient**, allow **immediate weight bearing**, **protect the whole bone**, not rely on healing

**Nonoperative** — asymptomatic · non-displaced fractures of humeral shaft, pelvis, scapula · non-weight-bearing bones · **short life expectancy** · systemically sensitive tumours

**Life expectancy for surgery** — lower-limb fixation if ≥6–12 weeks (OB) · arthroplasty if >3 months (OB) · **>6 months = best predictor of fracture healing** (Orthobullets) · RT alone for pain if <6 months (OB)

**Bisphosphonates** — lytic, blastic, mixed · ↓ skeletal-related events + hypercalcaemia · oral clodronate, IV pamidronate/**zoledronic acid** · not for acute impending fracture · complication **ONJ**

**Denosumab** — solid-tumour mets + myeloma · **superior to zoledronic acid** for SRE prevention · SC · ONJ

**Radiation** — palliation + local control · **pain ↓ in 70% at 2 wk**, complete relief ~30% · **RCC + thyroid radioresistant** (✓ breast, prostate, lymphoma, myeloma radiosensitive) · post-RT bone weakens first → fracture risk ~1 yr · radiating a high-risk lesion first ↑ fracture risk

**Other** — RFA (periosteal nerves), **cementoplasty** (pain, not structure), cryoablation, HIFU · radionuclide therapy (TO)

### Postoperative radiation

**All patients** after fixation (unless death imminent or previously irradiated) · start **2–3 weeks post-op** · **field covers entire implant** (e.g. whole femur after nailing)\
✗ Wrong options: tumour site only

**Preoperative RT** — ↑ wound complications

### Femoral lesions — cephalomedullary nailing & radiation

| Location | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **Femoral head / neck** | **Arthroplasty (cemented)** · hemiarthroplasty if acetabulum free · **THA if acetabular lesions** / OA (✗ Wrong options: screws · DHS · pinning (high failure)) |
| **Intertrochanteric** | Calcar-replacing hemi/THA **or** cephalomedullary nail |
| **Peri-/subtrochanteric** | **Long, statically locked cephalomedullary nail** ± PMMA · or proximal femoral replacement (✗ Wrong options: **DHS / sliding hip screw** · plate · dynamic locking (shortens) · standard antegrade nail · Enders + cement) |
| **Femoral diaphysis** | **Statically locked cephalomedullary nail** |

**Arthroplasty vs nail/ORIF** — arthroplasty **lower failure** · THA more dislocation than hemi · arthroplasty more infection than nails

**Cemented stems** — immediate stability, **RT inhibits ingrowth** · intra-op metastatic surprise during THA → **convert to cemented stem + cup**

**PMMA** — fills defects, immediate structural support\
✗ Wrong options: autograft (unreliable)

**Post-op rehab** — **full weight bearing** with cane (lung mets, Mirels 12) · no axillary crutches with axillary nodes

### Periarticular lesions — arthroplasty & endoprosthesis

| Site | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **Proximal humerus** | Arthroplasty / **endoprosthesis** (ORIF option) · RCC → **proximal humerus replacement** (✗ Wrong options: locking plate · nail · standard reverse) |
| **Humeral diaphysis** | **IM nail** |
| Distal humerus | Flexible nails / elbow replacement |
| **Tibia** | **IM nail** (pathologic fractures progress, don't heal in cast/brace) |

### Solitary renal cell metastasis — embolisation & wide resection

**Preoperative embolization** — **RCC + thyroid** (hypervascular, VEGF) → ↓ blood loss, no effect on healing · palliative pain control

**Solitary RCC metastasis** — **wide (en bloc) resection + endoprosthetic reconstruction** (cemented long stem) → ↓ revision, ↑ survival (median **1.3 → 4.8 yr** with complete resection), ↓ complications, ↓ local recurrence \
✗ Wrong options: IM nail / plate (progression, hardware failure)

**RCC = strongest predictor of local progression after nailing**

**Polymetastatic RCC** — en bloc resection only gives **lower local recurrence**\
✗ Wrong options: survival benefit · cheaper · less blood loss, reoperation equivalent

**RCC features** — lytic, **acral** possible, **radio- and chemo-resistant**, hypervascular, high complication rate with intralesional treatment, survival variable (6 mo – 4–5 yr) · fracture healing **44%** · IL-2 immunotherapy response 18–35%\
✗ Wrong options: sensitive

**Thyroid met (subtrochanteric)** — **curettage + IM nailing** · long survival → stabilise even tibial fracture

## 6 Complications

**Pathologic fracture healing** — **65% nonunion** overall · prostate **42%**, RCC **44%** (comparable across carcinomas)

**Prostate mets** — **blastic (90%)**, **radiosensitive**, **fewer SREs**, **better survival**, comparable fracture healing

**1-yr survival after surgery for metastatic disease** — ~30% (prostate series)

### Prognosis

| Primary | Median survival with bone mets |
|---|---|
| **Thyroid** | **48 months** |
| **Prostate** | **40 months** (Coleman 53) |
| **Breast** | **24 months** (Coleman 20) |
| Kidney | Variable: **6 months – 4–5 years** |
| Myeloma | 2–3 years |
| **Lung** | **6–7 months** (worst) · <5% 5-yr |

## How it's asked

**"Commonest cause of an aggressive lytic lesion >40 yr?"** → **metastatic bone disease**

**"Solitary supracondylar humeral lesion, breast cancer 10 yr ago, staging negative?"** → **biopsy**\
✗ Wrong options: TEA · curettage · cement · RFA

**"Paget patient with THA, lytic pelvic lesion?"** → **bone scan + CT CAP**\
✗ Wrong options: revision · ORIF

**"Knee pain, clear cells, pseudoalveolar pattern?"** → **metastatic RCC**\
✗ Wrong options: chordoma

**"IM nail placed through 'metastasis' that is high-grade chondrosarcoma?"** → **wide resection incl. hip disarticulation**

**"Heavy smoker, new painful femoral diaphyseal lytic lesion?"** → **biopsy, then treat by result**\
✗ Wrong options: nail + reamings

**"Lung cancer history, new femoral lesion, no previous biopsy-proven bone mets?"** → **biopsy**\
✗ Wrong options: nail + reamings

**"Solitary lesion after remote colon cancer + MoM THA?"** → **percutaneous biopsy**

**"45-yr-old, knee lesion, no cancer history?"** → **CT CAP then biopsy**

**"68-yr-old smoker, atraumatic lesser trochanter fracture?"** → **labs (CBC, chemistry, SPEP/UPEP) + CT + MRI**

**"59-yr-old, thigh pain, unknown primary?"** → **CT chest/abdomen/pelvis** before biopsy

**"64-yr-old, 3 mo groin pain, lytic proximal femur?"** → **bone scan + CT CAP + labs**

**"Lytic humeral lesion, hot bone scan, 55 yr?"** → **MRI of lesion + CT CAP**\
✗ Wrong options: indium · skeletal survey · CT needle biopsy first · LDH

**"Pathologic humeral shaft fracture, no history?"** → **coaptation splint, then bone scan + CT CAP**

**"Post-TKA tibial osteolysis, aspirate negative?"** → **CT CAP**

**"Smoker, solitary femoral lesion, staging negative — biopsy how?"** → **open biopsy via intramuscular interval**

**"Isolated femoral carcinoma, primary not found?"** → **lung**

**"Humeral pathologic fracture, CT CAP negative — next?"** → **core needle biopsy**

**"NOT a necessary step in bony metastasis?"** → **direct osteoclast stimulation**

**"Endothelin-1 is involved in?"** → **osteoblastic metastases**

**"NOT a step in becoming metastatic?"** → **increased apoptosis**

**"Tumour cells attach to host endothelium via?"** → **integrin**\
✗ Wrong options: E-cadherin · MMP · VEGF · CXCL12

**"PTHrP + breast cancer cells cause osteolysis via?"** → **osteoblastic RANKL production**

**"ECM degradation in metastasis?"** → **MMPs**\
✗ Wrong options: thrombospondin (inhibits) · VEGF · PDGF

**"Role of MMPs in a pathologic fracture?"** → **ECM degradation for invasion/metastasis**

**"Osteoblastic spinal mets in a 65-yr-old man — primary?"** → **prostate** **(TO)**

**"Commonest site of bony metastasis?"** → **thoracic spine** **(TO)**

**"Lytic spinal secondaries from?"** → **renal carcinoma** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: medulloblastoma, prostate, carcinoid (blastic)

**"Breast metastases are?"** → **lytic, blastic, or mixed — all of the above** **(TO)**

**"Distal to elbow/knee / acral mets from?"** → **lung**

**"Breast cancer pathology guiding medical therapy?"** → **HER2/neu** (ER, PR)

**"Least radiosensitive metastasis?"** → **renal**\
✗ Wrong options: breast, prostate, lymphoma, myeloma

**"Prostate mets vs others?"** → **comparable fracture healing**\
✗ Wrong options: radioresistant · lytic · more SREs · worse survival

**"RCC — LEAST associated?"** → **sensitivity to radiation + cytotoxic chemo**

**"Median survival, lung with bone mets?"** → **~7 months**

**"Subtrochanteric pathologic fracture, lung cancer?"** → **statically locked cephalomedullary nail**\
✗ Wrong options: THA · hemi · SHS · dynamic locking

**"Esophageal cancer, <6 mo life expectancy, subtrochanteric lesion?"** → **cephalomedullary nail**\
✗ Wrong options: SHS · plate · PFR

**"Breast mets, pathologic femoral fracture, biopsy-proven?"** → **IM nail + RT to entire femur + chemo**

**"Breast mets, diaphyseal femur lesion needing cane?"** → **closed IM nail + post-op RT**

**"Lung mets, Mirels 12 peritrochanteric, active 12–18 mo prognosis?"** → **prophylactic IM nail + full weight bearing with cane**

**"Subtrochanteric impending fracture, breast cancer?"** → **long cephalomedullary nail**

**"Thigh pain despite chemo + RT, multiple mets?"** → **prophylactic rodding**\
✗ Wrong options: bisphosphonates acutely · megaprosthesis

**"Lung history, peritrochanteric lytic lesion, Mirels 11?"** → **cephalomedullary nail**\
✗ Wrong options: cementoplasty · uncemented PFR

**"Thyroid papillary mets, peritrochanteric, functional pain?"** → **Mirels 12 → IM nail**

**"Myeloma, femoral head/neck lesion, 1-yr life expectancy?"** → **cemented hemiarthroplasty + RT**\
✗ Wrong options: DHS · APC · RT alone

**"Thyroid mets, tibial pathologic fracture, 1-yr survival?"** → **surgery** (IM nail)\
✗ Wrong options: brace · cast · RT alone

**"Breast mets, peritrochanteric lesion 40% circumference, non-ambulatory?"** → **Mirels 11 → hemiarthroplasty** (nail or THA also acceptable)

**"Solitary RCC metastasis proximal femur, 5 yr after nephrectomy?"** → **proximal femur replacement**\
✗ Wrong options: nail after curettage · chemo/RT

**"Breast carcinoma found intra-op after neck cut in THA?"** → **convert to cemented stem + cup**

**"RCC pathologic proximal humerus fracture?"** → **proximal humerus replacement**

**"Breast mets, femoral neck fracture + acetabular lesions, good prognosis?"** → **THA**

**"RCC femoral lesion — preoperative step?"** → **embolization**

**"Isolated RCC proximal humerus metastasis 7 yr after nephrectomy?"** → **wide resection + prosthetic reconstruction**

**"Why EPR over intralesional nailing for solitary RCC?"** → **lower revision + longer survival + fewer complications**

**"Procedure improving survival in solitary RCC femoral met?"** → **wide resection + proximal femoral replacement**

**"Best candidate for en bloc resection?"** → **solitary RCC proximal femoral lesion**\
✗ Wrong options: multifocal breast spine · thyroid (curettage + nail) · myeloma · lymphoma

**"Polymetastatic RCC — en bloc vs nailing benefit?"** → **lower local recurrence**

**"Highest risk of local progression after IM nail?"** → **solitary RCC metastasis**

**"Histology treatable by fixation without wide resection?"** → **metastatic breast carcinoma**

**"Histology benefiting from preop embolization?"** → **RCC (clear cells)**\
✗ Wrong options: myeloma · chondrosarcoma · chordoma · prostate

**"Tokuhashi score is for?"** → **prognosis after spinal metastasis** **(TO)**

**"NOT a Tomita component?"** → **general condition** (✓ visceral mets, ✓ number of bone mets, ✓ primary type) **(TO)**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Metastatic Disease of Spine

*Pathology · 38 / 66*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Commonest destructive bone lesion in adults** — metastasis · bone = **3rd** site (after lung, liver) · >40 yr

**Spine = commonest site of bone metastasis** — **thoracic** commonest (symptomatic: thoracic **68–70%**, lumbosacral 16–22%, cervical 8–15%) · spine > proximal femur > humerus

**Spinal disease** — up to **90%** of metastatic cancer patients, only **10–20% symptomatic** · **first sign of malignancy in 20%** · >90% of spinal tumours are metastatic

### Primary tumours metastasising to spine

| Primary | % of patients developing spine mets |
|---|---|
| **Breast** | **16–37%** |
| **Lung** | 12–15% |
| **Prostate** | 9–15% |
| **Kidney** | 3–6% |
| **Thyroid** | 4% |
