**Order of spinal secondary spread** — breast, prostate, lung, kidney ≈ **80%** · plus thyroid, myeloma (~75% of bone mets)

**Rarely to spine** — **colon** · hepatic, uterine, skin, "osseous" are distractors · haemangioma doesn't metastasize\
✗ Wrong options: breast, lung, renal, thyroid

**Risk clues** — **BRCA1** (breast) · smoking (lung) · **von Hippel–Lindau / VHL** (renal) · hypercalcaemia \
✗ Wrong options: **APC + positive FOBT** (colon — least likely)

### Mechanism of metastasis

**Theories** — **seed and soil** (compatible end-organ) · **circulation** (blood flow from primary)

**Steps** — ↓ **E-cadherin** → release · immune evasion · **integrin** → endothelial attachment · extravasation · **VEGF** angiogenesis · genomic instability · ↓ apoptosis\
✗ Wrong options: osteocalcin

**Batson plexus** — **valveless** venous plexus → vertebral bodies, pelvis, skull, proximal girdles

### Osteoclast-mediated bone resorption

**Lysis** — **tumour-induced activation of osteoclasts** · tumour releases TNF-α, IL-6, IL-11, **PTHrP**, TGF-β → **osteoblasts secrete RANKL** → RANK on osteoclasts · **OPG** binds RANKL, blocks resorption\
✗ Wrong options: direct resorption by tumour cells (only osteoclasts resorb bone) · macrophages · necrosis · angiogenesis

**Sclerosis** — prostate (± breast) → **endothelin-1** → ET-A receptor on osteoblasts, ↓ **DKK-1** → WNT ↑\
✗ Wrong options: ET-1 causes lysis

### Genes & lab markers

**Labs (unknown primary)** — CBC + diff, **ESR**, BMP, LFTs, Ca, phosphate, ALP, **SPEP/UPEP**, TFTs, **PTH**, **PSA**, urinalysis (± CEA)

**Red flags → first tests** — CBC, ESR, urinalysis (+ PSA, CEA with cancer history)

**IHC** — keratin · **CK7** (breast, lung) · **TTF-1** (lung) · **vimentin** (renal) · **CDX2** (colon)

**Associated** — **hypercalcaemia 10–30%** (lung, breast) → IV fluids, loop diuretic, bisphosphonate · anaemia · hypercoagulability / VTE

## 3 Presentation

**Pain** — **axial night pain** / rest pain · mechanical pain (instability) · **pain worse supine**, relieved standing

**Red flags (tumour)** — age >50, cancer history (most significant), weight loss, night pain, pain lying down

**Neurologic** — cord compression → **UMN signs: hyperreflexia**, clonus, gait ataxia, weakness, bowel/bladder · distinguish **root vs cord**

**Chemo-related** — peripheral neuropathy

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray (AP + lateral)** — **"winking owl" sign** = **unilateral absent pedicle** (pedicle destruction) · lytic or mixed · **up to 40% invisible** on X-ray · discs preserved (vs infection)

![Winking owl sign — absent pedicle](../../MCQ/attachments/q3776-illa-winking-owl-labeled.jpg)

**MRI with contrast** — **next step** for winking owl / suspected mets · dedicated region + **limited sagittal whole spine** · soft-tissue mass, ↑ T2, cortical permeation, **cord compression** · **98%** sensitive + specific

**CT** — sensitivity 66%, specificity 99% · **CT myelography if MRI contraindicated** · **CT CAP** for unknown primary

**Bone scan** — extent · **cold in myeloma + thyroid** → **skeletal survey**

**Blastic pelvis/spine in older man** → **prostate** (→ **prostate biopsy** confirms) · lung blastic only 25–30%

### Workup & diagnosis

**Known cancer, no prior mets / no cancer history** — evaluate before treating · history, exam, X-ray, labs, **CT CAP + bone scan** → identifies primary in **85%** · biopsy adds **8%**

**Staging before biopsy**\
✗ Wrong options: CT-guided biopsy first · excision · RFA · NSAIDs + PT

**Winking owl in IV drug user, normal WBC/ESR/CRP** — **CT CAP**\
✗ Wrong options: cervical MRI · antibiotics · kyphoplasty

**Multiple spinal lesions + very high PSA** — staging + diagnostic tests together (prostate biopsy, CT CAP, bone scan, CT-guided spine biopsy) \
✗ Wrong options: **bone marrow biopsy** (prostate rarely to marrow)

**Known metastatic disease** — biopsy not required

**Biopsy technique** — **posterior transpedicular**, wide-gauge **Jamshidi**, coring needle, image guidance · posterior (cervical) → vertebral artery risk · occult adenocarcinoma → **small lung primary**

### Histology

**Epithelial cells in clumps/glands in fibrous stroma**

### Tokuhashi (revised) score

**Six elements** — general condition · extraspinal bone mets · number of vertebral mets · visceral mets · primary tumour (0–5) · palsy · max **15**

| Total | Survival / approach |
|---|---|
| **0–8** | **<6 months** — conservative / palliative |
| **9–11 (9–12)** | **>6 months** — individualise |
| **≥12 (12–15)** | **>1 year** — excisional surgery |

**Worked example (OB)** — good health 2 + no extraspinal bone mets 2 + 2 spinal lesions 1 + removable visceral 1 + **rectal primary 4** + incomplete palsy 1 = **11** → surgery

**Tomita** — primary growth + visceral mets + bone mets \
✗ Wrong options: **general condition** **(TO)**

### NOMS framework

**NOMS** — **N**eurologic (ESCC grade) · **O**ncologic (**radiosensitivity**) · **M**echanical instability (**SINS**) · **S**ystemic illness (disease burden, comorbidity, ECOG) · most comprehensive current decision framework (FNB 2024 key not given — NOMS fits; ✗ Wrong options: Bigliani · DeWald; Tomita is a score) **(TO)**

**ESCC (epidural cord compression)** — **0–1 low grade**, **2–3 high grade**

### SINS (Spinal Instability Neoplastic Score)

| Element | Scores |
|---|---|
| **Location** | **Junctional** (occiput–C2, C7–T2, T11–L1, L5–S1) **3** · mobile (C3–C6, L2–L4) 2 · semi-rigid (T3–T10) 1 · rigid (S2–S5) 0 |
| **Pain** (mechanical — relief lying down / pain on loading) | Yes **3** · occasional non-mechanical 1 · none 0 |
| **Lesion** | **Lytic 2** · mixed 1 · blastic 0 |
| **Alignment** | **Subluxation/translation 4** · de novo deformity (kyphosis/scoliosis) 2 · normal 0 |
| **Body collapse** | >50% 3 · <50% 2 · none but >50% body involved 1 · none 0 |
| **Posterolateral involvement** (facet, pedicle, CV joint) | Bilateral 3 · unilateral 1 · none 0 |

| SINS | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **0–6** | **Stable** — no surgical consult |
| **7–12** | **Potentially unstable** → surgical referral |
| **13–18** | **Unstable** → surgical referral |

**Worked example (OB)** — T6 (1) + occasional non-mechanical pain (1) + mixed (1) + **focal kyphosis (2)** + 30% collapse (2) + no posterolateral (0) = **7** → surgical referral\
✗ Wrong options: normal-alignment combinations scoring 4–6

## 5 Management

```
Spinal metastasis (NOMS)
├─ Stable (SINS ≤6), no deficit, life <6 mo → PAIN CONTROL ± BRACE, EBRT
├─ No/stable fracture, no compression or deficit → RT / SRS ± KYPHOPLASTY / ABLATION
├─ Neuro deficit or high-grade ESCC, life >6 mo → DECOMPRESSION + STABILISATION → POST-OP RT
│      ├─ Anterior (vertebral body) compression → CORPECTOMY + ANTERIOR COLUMN RECON + POSTERIOR FUSION
│      └─ RCC / thyroid → PRE-OP EMBOLIZATION (same morning)
└─ Radiosensitive tumour (myeloma, lymphoma) → RT may suffice
```

### Nonoperative & radiation

**Pain + brace** — stable fracture, no deficit, **life <6 months** (Tokuhashi)

**External beam RT** — painful lesions **without deficit or cord compression**\
✗ Wrong options: embolization · laminectomy · corpectomy

**SRS** — **88% local control** if no instability or compression

**Kyphoplasty / vertebroplasty / RFA** — no or stable fracture, **no compression, deficit, or instability** · transpedicular, fluoroscopy · kyphoplasty = balloon cavity then PMMA (partial reduction) · not with **posterior cortex disruption** or neurologic impairment

### Surgery — decompression, stabilisation & radiation

**Patchell RCT** — **direct decompression + post-op RT > RT alone** for metastatic cord compression → best for **regaining/maintaining ambulation**

**Indications** — neurologic deficit + life **>6 months** · intractable pain · instability

**Sequence** — **surgery first, then radiation** · chemo too slow for acute deficit\
✗ Wrong options: RT before surgery → **3× wound complications**

**Anterior compression (vertebral body)** — **corpectomy + anterior column reconstruction + posterior instrumented fusion** · posterior-only via **costotransversectomy / transpedicular** with expandable cage\
✗ Wrong options: **laminectomy alone** → doesn't decompress, progressive **kyphosis** · **posterior fusion alone** → continued compression

**Approach** — posterior for thoracic, lumbar, sacral · cervical anterior or posterior · cement augments screws

**Separation surgery** — decompress + stabilise to create space for **SRS** · no en bloc resection for metastases\
✗ Wrong options: goal of total resection; margin usually intralesional

**Outcomes** — **95% local control** with surgery + RT · **16%** of ASIA E with high-grade ESCC decline even with surgery \
✗ Wrong options: laminectomy + steroids alone

**Breast mets, failed chemo, T9 ventral compression, hyperreflexia** — **thoracic corpectomy + instrumented fusion + post-op RT**\
✗ Wrong options: CHOP · posterior decompression after kyphoplasty · RT alone

### Preoperative embolisation (renal, thyroid)

**Hypervascular** — **renal + thyroid** → **preop embolization** (✗ Wrong options: breast, colon, prostate, lung, liver) · ideally **morning of surgery** (✗ Wrong options: post-op) · target the **tumour capillary bed**, not main feeder (collaterals) · agents: glue, alcohol, PVA particles, Gelfoam

**RCC spine with deficit** — **embolization → decompression + stabilization → RT** · RCC radioresistant → RT alone won't relieve compression \
✗ Wrong options: kyphoplasty · steroids · cryotherapy

**Colon cancer** — no embolization needed

### Bisphosphonates

**Zoledronic acid** — **↓ skeletal-related events 30–40%** (fracture, need for RT/surgery, cord compression, hypercalcaemia) · **pain ↓ in ≥50%** · **ONJ ~2.5%** · **renal toxicity** → dose by creatinine clearance · **zoledronic acid > pamidronate**\
✗ Wrong options: 15% · 25%

## 6 Complications

**Recurrence** · **hardware failure / instability** (RT, chemo, frailty; **>6 levels** → instrumentation failure) · nonunion

### Revision surgery — soft tissue reconstruction

**Wound complications** — risk: smoking, **prior RT**, instrumentation, revision · **immediate soft-tissue reconstruction** → better outcomes (delayed = **2×** wound complications)\
✗ Wrong options: preop RT · tobacco · posterior approach

### Prognosis

**Timing** — cancer → spinal met **32 months** · spinal met → fracture **27 months**

**1-yr survival after spine-met surgery** — **50%** · epidural disease worsens

**Survival factors** — histology + prior treatment, function + neurology, age, extent, nutrition

**Median survival** — thyroid **48 mo** · prostate **40** · breast **24** · kidney variable (≥6) · **lung 6**

## How it's asked

**"Smoker, osteoblastic pelvic/lumbar lesions — confirm diagnosis?"** → **prostate biopsy**\
✗ Wrong options: UPEP · thyroid biopsy · renal US

**"Winking owl — LEAST likely?"** → **APC mutation + positive FOBT (colon)** (✓ BRCA1, smoking, VHL, hypercalcaemia)

**"Thoracic lesion on MRI, weight loss, no history — next?"** → **CT CAP + whole-body bone scan**\
✗ Wrong options: CT biopsy first · excision · RFA

**"Multiple spinal mets + ↑ PSA — NOT in initial work-up?"** → **bone marrow biopsy**

**"Arrow at absent pedicle?"** → **metastatic disease**\
✗ Wrong options: haemangioma · infection

**"Back pain worse lying down, weight loss, prostate cancer history, normal X-ray?"** → **CBC, ESR, urinalysis, PSA, CEA**\
✗ Wrong options: PT · manipulation

**"68-yr-old, thoracic pain, winking owl?"** → **MRI with gadolinium**

**"Diffuse hot bone scan + blastic CT lesions, 85-yr-old man?"** → **metastatic prostate carcinoma**\
✗ Wrong options: myeloma · RCC · thyroid (lytic)

**"Colon mets with cord compression, incontinence — true?"** → **hyperreflexic patellar reflexes**\
✗ Wrong options: osteocalcin binding · ET-1 lysis · RT before surgery · embolization

**"IV drug user, winking owl, normal inflammatory markers?"** → **CT CAP**

**"Isolated T9 breast met, ventral compression, worsening gait?"** → **corpectomy + instrumented fusion + post-op RT**

**"L1 destroyed, breast mets, leg weakness — best function?"** → **decompression + instrumentation + post-op RT**

**"ASIA C, single thoracic met from rectal primary, Tokuhashi 11?"** → **decompression + stabilisation → RT**\
✗ Wrong options: RT first (3× wound problems) · RT alone · chemo

**"Lung mets, can't walk 48 h, life 6–12 mo?"** → **circumferential decompression + stabilisation + post-op RT**\
✗ Wrong options: RT alone · laminectomy + steroids · en bloc

**"Breast mets, anterior compression, life >3 yr on targeted therapy?"** → **corpectomy + anterior column reconstruction + posterior fusion**\
✗ Wrong options: laminectomy + PSF · kyphoplasty

**"RCC thoracic met with compression — true?"** → **posterior fusion alone leaves cord compressed**\
✗ Wrong options: laminectomy → lordosis · kyphoplasty

**"T6 met — which features warrant surgical referral?"** → **mixed lesion + 30% collapse + focal kyphosis (SINS 7)**

**"RCC spinal met with clonus — before surgery?"** → **arterial embolization**

**"Metastases needing preop embolization?"** → **renal + thyroid**

**"Highly vascular tumour?"** → **thyroid**

**"Isolated RCC met, knee-extension weakness?"** → **embolization → decompression/stabilization → RT**

**"RCC spinal biopsy, failed nonoperative care?"** → **embolization the morning of corpectomy + stabilization**

**"Zoledronic acid in breast mets — true?"** → **↓ fractures, RT, surgery, cord compression, hypercalcaemia (SRE ↓ 30–40%)**\
✗ Wrong options: 25% ONJ · no pain benefit · less effective than pamidronate · renal function irrelevant

**"Tumours most likely causing spinal mets?"** → **breast, renal, prostate, lung** (+ thyroid)

**"Rarely metastasizes to spine?"** → **colon**

**"Lytic T12 lesion resorption mediated by?"** → **tumour-induced osteoclast activation**

**"Painful lumbar prostate mets, no deficit?"** → **external beam radiation**

**"Improves outcome in revision metastatic spine surgery?"** → **immediate soft-tissue reconstruction**

**"Tokuhashi score is used for?"** → **prognosis after spinal metastasis** **(TO)**

**"Not a Tomita component?"** → **general condition** **(TO)**

**"Most comprehensive current decision framework for spinal mets (FNB 2024)?"** → key not given · **NOMS** fits **(TO)**

# Multiple Myeloma

*Pathology · 39 / 66*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology & eponym (Kahler disease)

**Kahler disease** = multiple myeloma **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: OS · MFH · benign fibrous histiocytoma

**Most common primary (malignant) bone tumour** **(TO)** · 3–4 per 100,000 · 1% of all malignancies

**Demographics** — **>40 yr (median 60; 65 in OB)** · **male** · **2× African Americans**

### Forms

| Form | Key features |
|---|---|
| **Multiple myeloma** | Commonest · marrow + multiple lesions · CRAB |
| **Solitary plasmacytoma** | Single skeletal site, negative marrow · **radiosensitive** · **>50% progress to MM** · MRI + FDG-PET find more lesions in 33% · **best prognosis** |
| **Osteosclerotic myeloma (POEMS)** | **P**olyneuropathy (symmetric, distal → proximal, doesn't improve) · **O**rganomegaly · **E**ndocrinopathy · **M** protein · **S**kin changes (trunk) · **sclerotic** lesions |
| MGUS / asymptomatic myeloma | Annual surveillance |

### Pathophysiology (RANKL, osteoblast suppression)

**Osteolysis** — myeloma cells bind marrow stromal cells → **↑ RANKL**, M-CSF, **IL-6, IL-11**, MIP-1α → osteoclast activation · **OPG ↓** (decoy receptor for RANKL)

**Osteoblast suppression** — **TNF** and **DKK-1** ↓ osteoblast function → **no sclerotic rim**, **cold bone scan**

**Lytic lesions (mets + myeloma)** — **RANK ligand acting on osteoclasts** (RANKL secreted by osteoblasts → RANK receptor; OPG = decoy)\
✗ Wrong options: RANKL on neoplastic cells · OPG on osteoclasts

### Genes & lab markers

| Lab | Finding |
|---|---|
| **SPEP** | **M spike** · **IgG ~50%, IgA ~25%** (heavy chains IgG 52%, IgA 21%, IgM 12%) |
| **UPEP** | **Bence Jones proteins** = **monoclonal light chains (κ, λ)** · proteinuria |
| Blood | **Anaemia**, ↑ **creatinine**, **hypercalcaemia (30%)**, ↑ ESR |
| **β2-microglobulin** | Tumour burden, **prognosis** (ISS) |
| **IHC** | **CD138 +** |

**Poor prognosis genetics** — **del 13**, **t(4;14)**, t(14;16)

## 3 Presentation

**Localized bone pain** (**spine, ribs**) · **pathologic fracture** (low-energy vertebral compression) · **fatigue** (anaemia, renal failure, hypercalcaemia) · radiculopathy, fevers

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — **multiple "punched-out" lytic lesions**, **no sclerotic border** · visible only after **>50% destruction** · diffuse osteopenia, moth-eaten · marrow-packing process

![Punched-out lytic lesions](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8024-case-b-femur-xray-parsons-moved.jpg)

**Skeletal survey** — **preferred screen** · **bone scan cold in ~30%** (30–50%) — no osteoblastic activity\
✗ Wrong options: bone scan ideal

**MRI** — more sensitive than X-ray (spine, pelvis) · T1 dark, T2 bright · marrow replacement · focal lesion ≥5 mm = MDE

**FDG-PET** — **93% sensitive** (> X-ray) · finds more lesions in "solitary" plasmacytoma

### Diagnostic criteria (IMWG)

**Marrow** — **clonal plasma cells ≥10%** (or biopsy-proven plasmacytoma) **+ ≥1 CRAB feature or MDE**

| CRAB | Threshold |
|---|---|
| **C**alcium | >0.25 mmol/L (>1 mg/dL) above ULN or **>2.75 mmol/L (>11 mg/dL)** |
| **R**enal | CrCl **<40 mL/min** or creatinine **>177 µmol/L (>2 mg/dL)** |
| **A**naemia | Hb >20 g/L below LLN or **<100 g/L** |
| **B**one | **≥1 lytic lesion** (X-ray, CT, PET-CT) · if marrow <10%, >1 lesion needed |

| Myeloma-defining event (MDE) |
|---|
| **≥60%** clonal marrow plasma cells |
| Involved:uninvolved **free light chain ratio ≥100** (involved ≥100 mg/L) |
| **≥1 focal MRI lesion ≥5 mm** |

**Plasma cell % — conflict** — IMWG/Orthobullets/TO: **≥10%** · OB: **plasmacytoma 10–30% vs myeloma >30%** (keyed **35%** as minimum) — read the options · normal marrow <2%

**Next test with anaemia + serum M protein** — **bone marrow aspiration + biopsy** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: UPEP (already have serum M protein) · CT head · EMG · lumbar puncture · vertebral CT biopsy

### International Staging System (ISS)

| Stage | Criteria | Median survival |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | **β2-M <3.5 mg/L** + albumin ≥3.5 g/dL | 62 months |
| **II** | β2-M 3.5–5.4 mg/L | 44 months |
| **III** | **β2-M ≥5.5 mg/L** | 29 months |

### Histology

**Plasma cells** — round, **eccentric nucleus**, **"clock-face" chromatin**, prominent nucleolus, basophilic cytoplasm · **perinuclear clear zone (Hof)** = Golgi (immunoglobulin production)

![Plasma cells with eccentric clock-face nuclei](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8024-histology-c-parsons-moved.png)

### Differential

| | Older patient | Multiple lesions | Bisphosphonates help | Chemo + RT |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Myeloma** | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | (chemo ± RT) |
| Metastasis | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | — |
| **Lymphoma** | ✓ | ✓ | — | ✓ |
| Paget | — | ✓ | ✓ | — |
| Hyperparathyroidism | — | ✓ | — | — |

## 5 Management

```
Plasma cell disorder
├─ MGUS / asymptomatic → ANNUAL SURVEILLANCE
├─ Solitary plasmacytoma → EBRT 45–50 Gy (fix if fracture/impending)
├─ Osteosclerotic (POEMS) → CHEMO + RT + PLASMAPHERESIS
└─ Multiple myeloma → MULTIAGENT CHEMO ± STEM-CELL TRANSPLANT + BISPHOSPHONATES
       ├─ Impending / complete fracture, life >3 mo → STABILISE (whole bone, cement) + EBRT
       └─ Painful VCF / instability or compression resistant to RT → KYPHOPLASTY
```

### Treatment (fixation, radiation, bisphosphonates)

**Chemo** — non-transplant (>65, comorbid): **melphalan + prednisone + thalidomide/bortezomib**, lenalidomide/thalidomide + dexamethasone · transplant candidates: **bortezomib/lenalidomide + dexamethasone** (± thalidomide) → autologous SCT (not curative, +2–3 yr DFS)

**Very radio- and chemosensitive** — RT may be primary for impending lesions · surgery shouldn't delay induction / transplant

**Bisphosphonates** — ↓ skeletal events

**Surgery** — complete or impending fracture, **life expectancy >3 months** · whole-bone protection, immediate stability · **post-op RT**

| Lesion | Fixation |
|---|---|
| **Femoral neck fracture** | **Cemented hemiarthroplasty or THA** (✗ Wrong options: IM nail · percutaneous screws · DHS · radical resection) |
| Proximal femur (stem) | **Full-length cemented** stem (✗ Wrong options: press-fit (RT/chemo impair ingrowth)) |
| Peritrochanteric, Mirels 12 | **Stabilisation + RT + bisphosphonates** (✗ Wrong options: wide resection (never for myeloma) · hormone therapy · curettage + graft) |
| Distal humerus path fracture | **Internal fixation without resection** (IM rod) → RT (✗ Wrong options: megaprosthesis · chemo first) |
| Periacetabular, intact subchondral plate | **Curettage + PMMA + modified Harrington pins** |
| Periacetabular, subchondral plate violated | **Complex THA + acetabular cage** |
| Painful VCF | **Kyphoplasty/vertebroplasty** — restores height, corrects sagittal deformity |

**Prophylactic fixation** — indicated before fracture so **systemic therapy isn't delayed** (bed rest, immunocompromise)

## 6 Complications

**Fracture healing** — myeloma has **among the best healing (67%)** of metastatic-type lesions · MM vertebral fractures heal poorly, progressive height loss

**Prognosis** — **median 3 yr** · 5-yr **30%**, 10-yr 11% · **renal failure → shortest survival**

**Poor prognosis** — **del 13 / t(4;14) / t(14;16)** · circulating plasma cells · **↑ β2-microglobulin** · **↓ albumin** · ↑ marrow microvessels

## X vs Y

| | **Myeloma** | **Metastatic carcinoma** |
|---|---|---|
| X-ray | **Punched-out**, no sclerotic rim | Lytic / blastic / mixed |
| Bone scan | **Cold ~30%** → skeletal survey | Hot (except thyroid, RCC lytic) |
| Labs | M spike, Bence Jones, CRAB | Primary-specific (PSA…) |
| Marker | **CD138** | Keratin, CK7, TTF-1 |
| Wide resection | **Never** | Solitary RCC |

## How it's asked

**"Anaemia + serum monoclonal protein, radiculopathy — next investigation?"** → **bone marrow biopsy** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: UPEP · CT vertebral biopsy · LP

**"Spinal biopsy plasma cells — UPEP shows?"** → **monoclonal light chains**\
✗ Wrong options: polyclonal · heavy chains

**"Cell type in T6 biopsy (asterisk)?"** → **plasma cells**

**"Lumbar/thoracic tenderness, L5 weakness, anaemia + serum M protein?"** → **bone marrow aspiration + biopsy**\
✗ Wrong options: urine electrophoresis · CT head · EMG

**"Chronic anaemia, moth-eaten femora, marrow-packing MRI?"** → **multiple myeloma**\
✗ Wrong options: FD · osteoporosis · infarcts

**"Minimum marrow plasmacytosis for myeloma (options 5/10/35/50/75%)?"** → **35%** (OB: >30%) · IMWG uses ≥10%

**"Low-energy VCF + punched-out lesions + M protein — histology?"** → **sheets of clock-face plasma cells**

**"Ca 12, creatinine 2.1, Hb 9.3 — SPEP/UPEP?"** → **IgG 50%, IgA 25%, M spike; κ and λ light chains**

**"Mechanism of lytic lesions in mets/myeloma?"** → **RANK ligand acting on osteoclasts**

**"True of myeloma bone turnover?"** → **TNF suppresses osteoblasts**\
✗ Wrong options: DKK-1 promotes osteoclasts · OPG ↑ · IL-6/IL-11 counteract · bone scan reliable

**"Myeloma hip lesion, IgG/IgA ↑ — osseous care?"** → **surgical stabilization + radiation + bisphosphonates**

**"Myeloma femoral neck lesion?"** → **cemented hemiarthroplasty or THA**

**"Distal humeral pathologic fracture, myeloma?"** → **internal fixation without resection**

**"Myeloma femoral neck fracture after debating prophylaxis — true?"** → **prophylactic fixation was indicated to avoid delaying systemic therapy**\
✗ Wrong options: press-fit stem · radio/chemo-insensitive · poor healing · bone scan ideal

**"Kahler disease?"** → **multiple myeloma** **(TO)**

**"Most common primary malignant bone tumour?"** → **multiple myeloma** **(TO)**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Myositis Ossificans

*Pathology · 40 / 66*

## 1 Basics

### Diagnosis & traumatic aetiology

**Nature** — **reactive, benign** soft-tissue bone formation (heterotopic ossification) · aka **hematoma ossificans**

**Cause** — **direct trauma / contusion** → **intramuscular haematoma** · near diaphysis of long bones · ⅓ idiopathic · also paraplegia, GBS, AIDS encephalopathy, head injury, burns, coma\
✗ Wrong options: surgery · parathyroid tumour · bisphosphonates · steroids

**Demographics** — **young active males 15–35**

**Muscles** — **quadriceps, brachialis, glutei** · elbow = commonest joint · **massage / vigorous passive physio after elbow injury provokes it** **(TO)**

**Genetics** — post-traumatic, not genetic\
✗ Wrong options: COMP · type X collagen · t(9;22) · p53

### Genes & lab markers

**Early** — ↑ CRP, ↑ **CPK** (inflammation, muscle)

**Mature phase** — ↑ **ALP** · falling ALP + mature bone on X-ray = maturity **(TO)**

**FOP** — **ACVR1** mutation (see below)

## 3 Presentation

**Phases (TO)** — **acute** (days–weeks): pain, swelling, joint stiffness · **pseudotumoral** (2–3 wk): painless hard mass · **resolving** (3–6 mo)

**Mass** — grows over months to **3–6 cm**, then firm · palpable · ↓ ROM (e.g. TKA stiffness after thigh blow)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — initially **normal** · mineralization at **2–3 weeks**, matures **3–6 months** · **peripheral ossification with central lucency** ("dotted veil") · ossified, smooth · **next to but not stuck on / not sharing the cortex**

![Myositis ossificans — peripheral ossification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8042-13-moved.jpg)

**CT** — **"eggshell"** peripheral rim

**MRI with gadolinium** — **rim enhancement in first 3 weeks** · intramuscular · surrounding oedema early

**Zonation phenomenon (Ackerman)** — matures **periphery → centre** (✓ MO) vs osteosarcoma **centre → periphery**

**Variants (TO)** — stalked (peduncle to bone) · sessile (broad periosteal base) · muscle-only

### Histology — zonal pattern

| Zone | Histology |
|---|---|
| **Periphery** | **Mature lamellar + woven bone trabeculae** (osteoblastic rimming) · **mature fibroblasts** → calcified on X-ray |
| **Centre** | **Immature, proliferating fibroblasts** (mitoses, **atypia may be seen**), ± cartilage · not calcified |

**TO key** — **mature fibroblasts at periphery, proliferating at centre** **(TO)** · Orthobullets: no cellular atypia · biopsy may mimic sarcoma → **clinical + radiographic correlation critical**\
✗ Wrong options: reverse

### Differential

| Lesion | Distinguishing |
|---|---|
| **Myositis ossificans** | **Peripheral** maturation, separate from cortex, trauma history |
| **Extraskeletal OS** | **Older**, central ossification, no zonation |
| **Parosteal OS** | **Stuck on** posterior distal femur, linear radiolucent gap, 30–40 F |
| Osteochondroma (sessile) | **Cortex + medulla continuous** with bone |
| Synovial sarcoma | Wispy irregular calcification within mass |
| Periosteal chondrosarcoma | On the bone, cartilage mineral |
| Ossifying lipoma · ossifying mets · extraskeletal chondrosarcoma | Different histology |

## 5 Management

### Management — observation

```
Myositis ossificans
├─ Acute → REST, ICE, NSAIDs, COMPRESSION, SPLINT, ACTIVE ROM (no passive stretching)
├─ Asymptomatic / maturing → OBSERVE + SERIAL X-RAYS (no biopsy if imaging diagnostic)
└─ Symptomatic after maturity (≥6 mo; ~1 yr) → SURGICAL EXCISION
```

**Passive stretching / massage** — **contraindicated** (worsens) · PT for active ROM

**Excision** — only if still a problem **after maturation** · **wait ≥6 months** · excision at **6–12 months** → recurrence (Orthobullets) · maturation ~1 yr (TO)

**Asymptomatic 13-yr-old with peripheral mature ossification after injury** — **observation**\
✗ Wrong options: NSAIDs · rheumatology · biopsy · resection

## 6 Complications

**Prognosis** — self-limiting · mass shrinks **after ~1 year**

### Fibrodysplasia ossificans progressiva (ACVR1)

**Gene** — **ACVR1** (activin A type I receptor = **BMP type-1 receptor**) · **R206H** gain-of-function → uninhibited SMAD signalling (Activin A, **BMP 2/4**/6/7) · sporadic or **AD**

**Hallmarks** — **progressive HO** of muscles, fascia, tendons, ligaments, capsules after minor trauma ("**Stone Man**") + **congenital bilateral monophalangeal hallux valgus** (pathognomonic) · cervical fusions, short broad femoral necks, osteochondromas · stooped posture, trismus, recurrent "bony prominences"

**Treatment** — conservative · surgery only for refractory pain, motion block, nerve compression (excision provokes more HO)

| Gene / protein | Condition (distractors) |
|---|---|
| **ACVR1** | ✓ **FOP** |
| LMX1B | Nail-patella |
| GNAS (Gs-α) | Fibrous dysplasia |
| DTDST (sulfate transporter) | Diastrophic dysplasia |
| RUNX2 | Cleidocranial dysplasia |
| EXT1/2/3 | MHE |
| HLA-B27 | Ankylosing spondylitis |

## How it's asked

**"Groin mass a year after helmet blow, dense ossification adjacent to bone, peripheral enhancement?"** → **myositis ossificans**\
✗ Wrong options: ossifying lipoma · synovial sarcoma · periosteal chondrosarcoma

**"Stiff TKA 6 mo after baseball hit to thigh, prostate cancer history?"** → **myositis ossificans**\
✗ Wrong options: ossifying mets · parosteal OS · sessile osteochondroma

**"Most likely cause?"** → **contusion**

**"Medial arm mass after arm wrestling, peripheral mineral on CT?"** → **myositis ossificans** (zonation of Ackerman)\
✗ Wrong options: extraskeletal OS · parosteal OS

**"Thigh mass after jogging?"** → **myositis ossificans**\
✗ Wrong options: sarcoma · pseudoaneurysm · abscess

**"Quadriceps ossified mass in footballer — linked to?"** → **history of thigh trauma**

**"Asymptomatic ossified hip 'knot' 4 mo after injury?"** → **observation**

**"Stone man, trismus, bony prominences, sister affected?"** → **ACVR1 mutation**\
✗ Wrong options: EXT · HLA-B27

**"Thigh deformity since youth, ACVR1 mutation?"** → **FOP**\
✗ Wrong options: melorheostosis · Paget · FD · MHE

**"Congenital bilateral hallux valgus + stiffness — mutated protein?"** → **activin receptor type 1A**

**"True about myositis ossificans?"** → **mature fibroblasts at periphery, proliferating at centre** **(TO)**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Neurilemmoma

*Pathology · 41 / 66*

## 1 Basics

**Synonym** — **schwannoma** · benign, **encapsulated** nerve sheath tumour of **Schwann cells** on the **surface (epineurium) of a peripheral nerve** · motor or sensory nerves

**Age / sex** — **3rd–6th decades** (tested ages 40, 45; 20–50) · M = F

**Site** — **flexor surfaces** of extremities, head and neck · large ones in pelvis · tibial nerve (posterior ankle, tarsal tunnel), median, superficial peroneal

**Malignant transformation** — **extremely rare** (vs neurofibroma)

### Genes & lab markers

**NF2** gene mutations\
✗ Wrong options: NF1 (neurofibroma)

**S-100** — **strong, uniform/diffuse** staining

## 3 Presentation

**Usually asymptomatic** · slow-growing mass · **paraesthesia** in nerve distribution · discomfort with shoe wear

**Percussion (Tinel) sign** — **radiating pain distally on tapping** = most reliable sign · sensory deficit possible, **motor weakness rare** · **rest pain = worrying for MPNST**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**MRI** — **T1 dark**, **T2 bright**, **diffuse gadolinium enhancement** · fusiform, homogeneous, in continuity with a named nerve · **eccentric** to nerve

**String sign** — nerve tapering into tumour above and below · **target sign** — central low signal

![String sign of a schwannoma](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8053-string-sign.jpg)

**Benign vs MPNST on MRI** — **well-defined capsule** favours benign\
✗ Wrong options: >2 cm peritumoral oedema · lack of enhancement · ≥2 deep lobulations · limb location irrelevant

**Hard to separate** from neurofibroma on MRI

### Histology

**Gross** — grey, **well encapsulated** in nerve sheath

| Feature | Description |
|---|---|
| **Antoni A** | **Dense, cellular** spindle cells in intersecting bundles ("spindle cell battle formation") |
| **Antoni B** | **Hypocellular, loose myxoid** areas |
| **Verocay bodies** | **Two rows of palisading nuclei** — **pathognomonic** |

![Verocay bodies — palisading nuclei](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8053-verocay-histo.jpg)

### Differential

| | **Schwannoma** | **Neurofibroma** | **MPNST** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Relation to nerve | **Eccentric**, on surface, encapsulated | **Within** nerve, fusiform | From nerve/neurofibroma |
| Gene | **NF2** | **NF1** | NF1 |
| Histology | Antoni A/B, Verocay | Wavy nuclei, mast cells | Pleomorphic spindle cells |
| Excision | **Marginal, shell out, nerve preserved** | Requires nerve sacrifice | Wide + nerve |
| Recurrence / malignancy | Rare / extremely rare | Higher / can transform | — |

**Others** — ganglion (mucin-filled synovial-lined sac, near joint) · nodular fasciitis (deep fascia) · lipoma (bright T1 + T2) · haemangioma · synovial sarcoma (periarticular, heterogeneous)

## 5 Management

### Treatment — marginal excision

**Observation** — asymptomatic

**Marginal (intracapsular) excision** — symptomatic / affecting QoL · **excise parallel to nerve fascicles** → tumour extrudes, nerve preserved · recurrence **rare (<1%)** · small risk of sensory deficit

**Not indicated** — wide resection / nerve reconstruction · radiation · chemotherapy · RFA

### Fibrolipomatous hamartoma

**Intraneural lipoma** — congenital, benign · **median nerve** commonest · MRI: **"coaxial cable"** (axial) / **"spaghetti"** (longitudinal) — fat between thickened fascicles (T1 bright) · asymptomatic → observe · symptomatic → **carpal tunnel decompression ± epineurolysis**

## How it's asked

**"Posterior ankle mass, percussion radiates into foot — true?"** → **Verocay bodies on histology**\
✗ Wrong options: NF1 · mucin sac (ganglion) · MPNST transformation · high recurrence

**"Tender shoulder mass, Antoni A/B, uniform S-100?"** → **schwannoma**\
✗ Wrong options: MFH · lymphoma · synovial sarcoma · neurofibroma

**"String + target signs, palisading nuclei?"** → **schwannoma**\
✗ Wrong options: nodular fasciitis · lipoma · haemangioma

**"Medial ankle mass, Tinel over tarsal tunnel?"** → **neurilemmoma**\
✗ Wrong options: neurofibroma · ganglion

**"MRI feature favouring benign nerve sheath tumour?"** → **well-defined capsule**

**"Symptomatic arm schwannoma — treatment?"** → **marginal resection only**\
✗ Wrong options: + RT · wide + chemo · RFA

**"Which patient needs only marginal resection?"** → **62-yr-old with nerve-sheath mass + Tinel sign**\
✗ Wrong options: Ewing · OS · liposarcoma · impending fracture

**"Median-nerve schwannoma, frozen section Verocay bodies?"** → **marginal resection parallel to fascicles**

**"Superficial peroneal schwannoma with paraesthesia?"** → **marginal excision**\
✗ Wrong options: observation · wide resection

**"Fibrolipomatous hamartoma — commonest nerve?"** → **median**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Neuroblastoma

*Pathology · 42 / 66*

## 1 Basics

**Nature** — malignant tumour of **sympathetic neural tissue** (neural crest) · near **adrenal gland** or spinal cord (neck, chest, abdomen, pelvis)

**Incidence** — ~1/100,000 children · slight male predominance · majority **<2 yr**

**Rank** — **commonest (malignant) solid tumour of childhood** (Orthobullets, OB) · TO: commonest **extracranial / abdominal / infancy** tumour; commonest **solid** tumour overall = **brain tumour** — read the options · leukaemia = commonest childhood malignancy (not solid)

**Small round blue cell tumour by age** — **<5 yr → metastatic neuroblastoma** (also leukaemia) · bone mets common in children

## 3 Presentation

**Systemic** — fever, malaise, weight loss, diarrhoea · **tachycardia** (adrenal)

**Exam** — **abdominal mass**, hepatosplenomegaly

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — bone mets: **permeative, lytic**, anywhere · may cause vertebra plana

**CT chest/abdomen/pelvis** — initial study, visceral extent · **MRI** — anatomy · **bone scan** — bony mets

![Metastatic neuroblastoma](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8081-neuroblastoma-xray.jpg)

**Biopsy** — needle or open incisional required

### Histology

**Small round blue cells forming rosettes** (Homer-Wright) · neurofilament + (vs Ewing −)

**Other small round blue cell tumours of childhood** — rhabdomyosarcoma, non-Hodgkin lymphoma, Ewing/PNET, blastemic Wilms

## 5 Management

```
Neuroblastoma
├─ Very young infant, low risk → OBSERVE (spontaneous regression)
├─ Low grade, resectable with wide margin → SURGICAL EXCISION
├─ High grade / metastatic, unresectable → CHEMO (cisplatin, ifosfamide) + STEM-CELL TRANSPLANT
└─ High grade near NV structures → CHEMO + SCT + EXCISION
```

## 6 Complications

**Prognosis** — **majority cured** · **bone metastasis = poor prognosis** · spontaneous regression in very young

## How it's asked

**"Most common malignant solid tumour of childhood?"** → **neuroblastoma**\
✗ Wrong options: leukaemia (not solid) · lymphoma · Ewing · EG

**"Most common tumour in children (FNB)?"** → **brain tumour** (neuroblastoma = commonest extracranial) **(TO, OS chapter)**

**"Small round blue cell tumour in a child <5?"** → **metastatic neuroblastoma**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Neurofibroma

*Pathology · 43 / 66*

## 1 Basics

**Nature** — benign peripheral nerve sheath tumour · mostly with **neurofibromatosis (NF1)**

**Age** — sporadic **20–40** · NF1 **<20**

**Site** — sporadic: peripheral nerves of extremities · NF1: near **spinal cord/brain**, unusual sites (parotid, tongue)

**Cells** — **fibroblasts predominate** + **non-myelinating Schwann cells (cell of origin)** + perineural cells, mast cells (vs schwannoma = Schwann cells only)

### Genes & lab markers

**NF1** — **autosomal dominant**, 17q11.2 · **biallelic inactivation** of NF1 tumour-suppressor in non-myelinating Schwann cells → loss of **neurofibromin** → **↑ RAS** (RAS-MAPK) activity

**MPNST transformation** — + loss of **CDKN2A or TP53**

**S-100** — **variable** (vs uniform in schwannoma)

| Schwann cell | Axons | Disease |
|---|---|---|
| Myelinating | Large-diameter, myelin | — |
| **Non-myelinating** | **Small-diameter**, cytoplasmic processes | **Neurofibromatosis** |

### Subtypes

| | **Dermal** | **Plexiform** |
|---|---|---|
| Origin | **Single** nerve | **Multiple** nerve bundles |
| Look | **Fusiform** swelling | **"Bag of worms"** |
| Association | 90% sporadic, 10% NF1 | **Pathognomonic of NF1** |
| Onset | Puberty | Early childhood |
| Malignancy | Never | **10% → MPNST** |

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — asymptomatic · stinging, itching, pain, disfigurement · **exquisite tenderness** (benign nerve sheath tumour hallmark) · reduced sensation

**Cutaneous** — "**button-holes**" through skin defect with finger pressure

### NF1 diagnostic criteria (NIH — 2 of 7)

| Criterion |
|---|
| **≥2 neurofibromas or 1 plexiform** |
| **Axillary/groin freckling** (Crowe sign) |
| **≥6 café-au-lait** spots ("coast of California") — >5 mm prepubertal, >15 mm postpubertal |
| **Sphenoid wing dysplasia** or long-bone cortical thinning (**anterolateral tibial bowing**) |
| **≥2 Lisch nodules** (iris hamartomas, slit lamp) |
| **Optic glioma** |
| **First-degree relative** with NF1 |

**NF1 associations** — **dystrophic kyphoscoliosis**, **rib penciling**, intraspinal **dumbbell** neurofibromas, **dural ectasia**, meningiomas · cancer risk ~4×

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**MRI ± contrast** — T1 low, T2 very high (myxoid-type)

| Sign | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Nerve continuity (entering-exiting)** | Fusiform tumour in continuity with NV bundle |
| **Split fat** | Thin rim of fat around mass in NV bundle |
| **Target** | High-signal periphery, low centre (STIR) |

**Neurofibroma vs schwannoma** — neurofibroma **central** to nerve (fascicles run through it, no capsule) · schwannoma **eccentric**, encapsulated

![Neurofibroma — wavy collagen](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8056-nf-histo.jpg)

**PET-CT** — **distinguishes MPNST from benign neurofibroma** (↑ SUV) in NF1 with multiple nodules\
✗ Wrong options: UPS vs MPNST · infection vs tumour

### Histology

**Hypocellular**, **fibroblasts** + Schwann cells, mast cells, lymphocytes · **elongated, wavy nuclei** · **"wire-like"/rope-like wavy collagen** · may be myxoid · corpuscle-like (Pacini/Meissner) structures

| Histology | Diagnosis |
|---|---|
| **Wavy collagen, elongated cells, hypocellular** | ✓ **Neurofibroma** |
| Herringbone, atypia | Fibrosarcoma |
| Stromal cells, haemosiderin macrophages, giant cells | GCT of tendon sheath |
| Mono/biphasic epithelioid cells | Synovial sarcoma |

### MPNST risk

**MPNST in NF** — **5% (10–25% lifetime)** · usually from **plexiform** · **painful, enlarging** mass · poor prognosis

## 5 Management

**Observe** — asymptomatic

**Surgical excision** — symptomatic · tumour within nerve → **may need nerve sacrifice / grafting** (vs schwannoma shelled out)

## How it's asked

**"Forearm mass 6 yr, episodic sharp pain, fusiform on median nerve?"** → **nerve sheath tumour**\
✗ Wrong options: sarcoma · metastasis · haematoma · abscess

**"Buttock pain sitting, sciatic paraesthesia, wavy collagen?"** → **neurofibroma**\
✗ Wrong options: myxoid liposarcoma · myxoma · MFH · fibromatosis

**"Exquisitely tender long-standing leg mass in tibial nerve, rope-like collagen?"** → **neurofibroma**\
✗ Wrong options: fibrosarcoma · GCT tendon sheath · synovial sarcoma

**"NF1 with multiple nodules — PET-CT distinguishes?"** → **MPNST vs benign neurofibroma**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Nodular Fasciitis

*Pathology · 44 / 66*

## 1 Basics

**Nature** — **commonest fibrous soft-tissue lesion** · **pseudosarcomatous, self-limiting reactive** proliferation of **fibroblasts + myofibroblasts**

**Demographics** — M = F · adults **20–40** · young people

**Site** — **half in upper limb** · **volar forearm**, back, chest wall, head and neck

**Trigger** — often a recalled trauma

## 3 Presentation

**Rapidly enlarging mass over 1–2 weeks** (present only weeks) · **1–2 cm** · painful in half

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — normal

**MRI** — deep-seated, **inhomogeneous** mass · **nodularity extending along fascial planes** · **avid gadolinium enhancement** · usually superficial, also intramuscular · T2 hyperintense, T1 iso/hypo

![Nodular fasciitis on MRI](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8063-46a-moved.jpg)

### Histology

**Plump, immature-looking fibroblasts** (like granulation tissue) in **short, irregular bundles and fascicles** · **dense reticulin network** · small amounts of mature collagen

**Evolution** — short duration → **myxoid** · longer → **hyaline fibrosis**

## 5 Management

**Marginal resection**

| Treated with marginal excision |
|---|
| **Nodular fasciitis** |
| Periosteal chondroma |
| **Neurilemmoma** |
| Epidermal inclusion cyst |
| Glomus tumour |

## How it's asked

**"40-yr-old, rapidly enlarging shoulder mass, short irregular fibroblast fascicles in reticulin?"** → **nodular fasciitis**\
✗ Wrong options: fibrosarcoma · intramuscular haemangioma · schwannoma · rhabdomyoma

**"Deep fascia lesion, homogeneous T2-bright (vs schwannoma)?"** → **nodular fasciitis** arises in deep fascia

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Non-Ossifying Fibroma

*Pathology · 45 / 66*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Commonest benign bone lesion of childhood** (with osteochondroma) · in **30–40% of skeletally immature children** · multiple in **8%**

**Demographics** — **males 2:1** · **5–15 yr**

**Site** — **metaphysis** of long bones · **80% lower limb**: **distal femur > proximal tibia > distal tibia** · uncommon proximal femur/humerus

**Synonyms** — metaphyseal fibrous defect, non-osteogenic fibroma, **fibrous cortical defect** (<2 cm), fibroxanthoma

### Pathophysiology

**Benign fibrogenic lesion** from **dysfunctional ossification** — abnormal subperiosteal osteoclastic resorption during metaphyseal remodelling

**Genetics** — somatic **RAS-MAPK activating mutations** → considered a neoplasm (RASopathy family) · mutation in a subset of cells; histiocytes + giant cells reactive

### Associated conditions

**Jaffe-Campanacci syndrome** — **multiple NOFs + café-au-lait + mental retardation/cognitive delay + heart, eye, gonadal defects + giant cell lesions of the mandible**\
✗ Wrong options: Jaffe-Lichtenstein (FD + café-au-lait) · Maffucci · Mazabraud · McCune-Albright

**Others** — **neurofibromatosis** · familial multifocal NOF · ABC

### Genes & lab markers

**RAS-MAPK** somatic mutations · no lab abnormality

## 3 Presentation

**Asymptomatic, incidental** — found after ankle/knee sprain or fall · pain from the injury, not the lesion

**Pathologic fracture** — ± stress fractures with cortical involvement

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray (diagnostic)** — **eccentric, metaphyseal, cortically based "bubbly"/scalloped lytic lesion** with **thin sclerotic rim** · narrow zone of transition · cortex may be thinned/expanded · **length > width** · no periosteal reaction, no soft-tissue mass

![Eccentric bubbly metaphyseal lesion with sclerotic rim](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8027-5a-moved.jpg)

**With growth** — migrates to diaphysis, enlarges (1–7 cm) · near maturity → **sclerotic**, reossifies

**Quantitative CT** — predicts **fracture risk**

**MRI** — T1 low · T2 varies (higher when developing) · thin enhancing rim · rarely needed

**Mimic** — **adductor magnus avulsion / cortical desmoid** at posteromedial distal femur

**No biopsy, MRI, bone scan, staging** for classic lesions

### Histology

**Fibroblastic spindle cells in whorled / storiform pattern** ("helicopters in a wheat field") · fibroblastic background · **lipophages (foamy histiocytes)**, **giant cells** · **haemosiderin** · occasional ABC component

![Storiform fibroblasts with giant cells](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8027-histology-a-low-power-parsons-moved.jpg)

### Differential

| | Bubbly lytic X-ray | Haemosiderin | Observation alone |
|---|---|---|---|
| **NOF** | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |
| ABC · UBC | ✓ | — | — |
| PVNS | — | ✓ | — |
| FD · enchondroma · osteochondroma · EG · Paget | — | — | ✓ |

| Lesion | Distinguishing |
|---|---|
| **NOF** | **Eccentric, scalloped, metaphyseal**, sclerotic rim |
| **UBC** | **Central**, expands to physis width, **fallen leaf / fallen fragment** sign |
| **FD** | Central, **ground glass** |
| **GCT** | Painful, skeletally mature, subchondral, no mineral, destructive |
| **Osteofibrous dysplasia** | **Diaphyseal, anterior tibial cortex** |
| **Enchondroma** | Central, mineralized, not sclerotic rim |
| **Osteosarcoma** | Painful, permeative, periosteal reaction, mineralized mass |
| **Chondroblastoma** | Epiphyseal |

### Cortical desmoid (TO)

**Posteromedial** distal femoral metaphysis (**adductor magnus** traction), boys 10–15 · cortical erosion with sclerotic base, no lytic lesion · **no biopsy**, observe · "posterolateral" is wrong **(TO)**

## 5 Management

```
Non-ossifying fibroma
├─ Incidental, asymptomatic → OBSERVE (X-ray 6 + 12 mo, then yearly; or 2–3/3–6 mo check)
├─ Pathologic fracture, non-displaced → CAST / SLING, let fracture heal, then observe
└─ Symptomatic + large (>50–75% cortex / >50% diameter) or high QCT risk → CURETTAGE + GRAFT ± PROPHYLACTIC FIXATION
```

**Observation** — first line · **reossifies spontaneously** in 2nd–3rd decade · non-urgent referral \
✗ Wrong options: biopsy · MRI · bone scan · CT staging · cast · CPS

**Pathologic fracture** — treat the fracture (casting · humerus → **sling + analgesia**) · curettage only after healing unless ORIF needed \
✗ Wrong options: IM nail without diagnosis · aspiration + injection (UBC)

**Curettage + bone grafting** — **symptomatic + large** (>50–75% cortical involvement / just over half diameter in weight-bearing bone) · persistent activity pain → **+ prophylactic internal fixation**\
✗ Wrong options: RFA · doxycycline · steroid injection · sclerotherapy · wide excision

## 6 Complications

### Pathologic fracture

**90% lower limb** · **50% distal tibia**

**Risk factors** — **>50% of transverse diameter** · **>33 mm length** in weight-bearing bones

**Prognosis** — spontaneous resolution · **no malignant or metastatic potential**\
✗ Wrong options: 5–10% (that's MHE)

## How it's asked

**"20-yr-old skier, incidental lateral distal femoral sclerotic-rimmed lesion?"** → **reassurance + WBAT**\
✗ Wrong options: biopsy · resection

**"Distal fibula lesion after sprain, asymptomatic 16-yr-old?"** → **activities as tolerated, X-ray in 3–6 mo**\
✗ Wrong options: staging · cast · curettage

**"6-yr-old, knee abrasion, tibial lesion?"** → **repeat X-ray in 3 months**

**"14-yr-old tibial lesion after ankle injury?"** → **routine follow-up**\
✗ Wrong options: endocrine (McCune-Albright) · GI cancer (Gardner) · open biopsy

**"18-yr-old soccer player — diagnosis + treatment?"** → **NOF, observation**\
✗ Wrong options: enchondroma · ABC curettage

**"Associated with NOF EXCEPT?"** → **fallen leaf sign** (UBC) (✓ stress fractures, ✓ commonest childhood lesion, ✓ Jaffe-Campanacci, ✓ haemosiderin)

**"9-yr-old, mild ankle pain, family doctor calls?"** → **symptom management, non-urgent referral**\
✗ Wrong options: urgent CT/labs · RFA · curettage

**"Incidental distal tibial / proximal tibial lesion?"** → **observation**\
✗ Wrong options: MRI · bone scan · biopsy

**"15-yr-old 'shadow on the bone', eccentric sclerotic metaphyseal?"** → **NOF**\
✗ Wrong options: GCT · infection · OFD (diaphyseal) · enchondroma

**"NOF best characterized as?"** → **eccentric scalloped metaphyseal radiolucency**\
✗ Wrong options: fallen leaf · central fusiform to physis width (UBC) · ground glass (FD)

**"True of NOF?"** → **typically ossifies spontaneously**\
✗ Wrong options: trauma-related · cast first-line · high fracture risk · 5–10% malignancy

**"Trampoline ankle injury, bubbly lesion — histology?"** → **storiform fibroblasts**

**"Pathologic fracture through which lesion (lobulated, eccentric)?"** → **NOF**

**"Humeral pathologic fracture through eccentric soap-bubble lesion?"** → **sling + pain medication**

**"Outside core biopsy, no treatment needed — histology?"** → **whorled/storiform fibroblastic spindle cells**

**"11-yr-old gymnast, limp, NOF >50% diameter, daily pain?"** → **curettage + bone grafting + prophylactic internal fixation**

**"Persistent pain, 4 cm NOF over half the diameter?"** → **curettage + bone grafting**\
✗ Wrong options: observation · cast · sclerotherapy

**"Multiple NOFs, café-au-lait, cognitive delay, eye problems, mandibular lesion?"** → **Jaffe-Campanacci syndrome**

**"Cortical desmoid — all true EXCEPT?"** → **"posterolateral distal femur"** (it is posteromedial) **(TO)**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Osteoblastoma

*Pathology · 46 / 66*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Rare** — **1% of primary bone tumours**, 1–5% (3%) of benign · **10% of osseous spinal neoplasms** · less common than osteoid osteoma

**Demographics** — **M:F 2:1** · **10–30 yr**

**Site** — **posterior elements of spine + sacrum (30–40%; >35–40%)** · long bones (lower > upper), diaphysis/metadiaphysis (femur commonest), never epiphyseal · **mandible ("cementoblastoma")**

**Spinal level (TO)** — **cervical > lumbar** > thoracic > sacrum (17%) — no firm consensus; keyed answer **cervical**

**Posterior element tumours (TO)** — **osteoid osteoma > osteoblastoma > ABC** · GCT → vertebral body

**Nature** — **benign, locally aggressive, progressive**\
✗ Wrong options: self-limiting

### Genes & lab markers

**FOS (chr 14) / FOSB (chr 19) rearrangements**

**Nuclear β-catenin** staining → osteoblastoma · **cytoplasmic/membranous** → osteosarcoma

**Associations** — **secondary ABC 10–40%** · **oncogenic osteomalacia**

## 3 Presentation

**Pain** — slowly progressive **dull ache** · night pain **not typical** · **partial NSAID response** (TO FNB stem: night pain improving with NSAIDs, posterior element, 28 yr → keyed osteoblastoma)

**Spine** — **neurologic symptoms** (root compression, weakness, rarely paraplegia), scoliosis

**Signs** — swelling, muscle atrophy, limp · may be incidental

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — **lytic or mixed lytic-blastic**, **radiolucent nidus 2–10 cm** (>2 cm), intralesional densities · reactive sclerosis · expansile · 66% cortical, 33% medullary · **25% look aggressive** (mistaken for malignancy)

![Osteoblastoma on CT](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8013-osteoblastoma-ct.jpg)

**CT** — full characterization: size, location, cortical breach, soft-tissue component

**MRI** — reactive soft-tissue/marrow **oedema** · T1 low–intermediate, T2 intermediate–high

**Bone scan** — **hot**, intense focal uptake

### Histology

**Like osteoid osteoma, more giant cells** · **sharp demarcation nidus–reactive bone** · nidus of **immature osteoid + plump osteoblasts** (normal nuclei) · irregular osteoid/woven spicules rimmed by osteoblasts in **loose fibrovascular stroma** · mitoses but **no atypical** ones · rich vascularity, extravasated RBCs · **no permeation of host lamellar bone**

![Osteoblastoma histology](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8013-histology-c-parsons-moved.png)

### Osteoid osteoma vs osteoblastoma

| | **Osteoid osteoma** | **Osteoblastoma** |
|---|---|---|
| Incidence | 10% of benign | 3% of benign |
| Size | **<2 cm** (<1.5) | **>2 cm** (3–3.5) |
| Site | >50% long-bone diaphysis (proximal femur > tibia > phalanges > spine) | **>35% posterior spine** |
| Natural history | **Self-limited** | **Progressive** |
| Symptoms | **Night pain, relieved by NSAIDs** · painful scoliosis, lesion on **concave** side | Dull ache, **partial NSAID response**, neuro symptoms |
| Spine management | Nonoperative first → RFA/resection | **Always surgery** |

### Differential

| Lesion | Distinguishing |
|---|---|
| **Osteosarcoma** | Periostitis, soft-tissue mass, **permeates lamellar bone**, malignant cells, cytoplasmic β-catenin |
| **ABC** | Fluid levels, septations, blood-filled sinusoids |
| Osteoid osteoma | <2 cm, thick sclerosis around small nidus |
| GCT | Epiphysis–metaphysis, giant cells with stromal nuclei |
| **Chondroblastoma** | Epiphyseal, chondroid |
| NOF | Eccentric metaphyseal, storiform |
| FD | Ground glass, Chinese letters |
| Osteomyelitis · stress fracture | Sinus/skin changes · no large lucent lesion |

## 5 Management

```
Osteoblastoma (doesn't respond to nonoperative care)
├─ Most → CURETTAGE / MARGINAL EXCISION + BONE GRAFT (± phenol, cryo)
├─ Large, locally aggressive, recurrent, some spinal → EN BLOC RESECTION (lower recurrence)
└─ Selected small lesions → ABLATION (RFA / MRgFUS)
```

**Observation** — rarely (keeps growing)

### Treatment — ablation (MRgFUS vs RFA) (TO)

**MRgFUS** — **less invasive** (needleless, no ionizing radiation), real-time thermal map · does not penetrate deeper (deep lesions → RFA needle reaches better)

**RFA** — CT-guided, ~17G needle, single-day stay

**Both** — equally safe/effective · equal resolution of **bone oedema** (oedema loss = inactive lesion) · post-ablation **"halo/ring"** sign (necrotic centre + sublethal rim) · not "RFA less chondral damage" (claim not supported)

## 6 Complications

**Local recurrence** — **15–25%**, mostly within **2 years**

**Prognosis** — excellent, most disease-free after first surgery

## How it's asked

**"Most common site of osteoblastoma (spinal levels)?"** → **cervical spine** (keyed) **(TO)**

**"28-yr-old, posterior element tumour, night pain improves with NSAIDs, no deficit?"** → **osteoblastoma** (keyed; ✗ Wrong options: ABC · GCT · lymphoma) **(TO)**

**"17-yr-old, distal humeral bone-forming lesion extending beyond cortex, large osteoblasts, no atypia?"** → **osteoblastoma**\
✗ Wrong options: osteoid osteoma (smaller) · OS · chondroblastoma · GCT

**"18-yr-old knee, well-marginated lytic/blastic, benign osteoblasts?"** → **osteoblastoma**\
✗ Wrong options: FD · OS · NOF · osteomyelitis

**"23-yr-old, back pain radiating down leg relieved by NSAIDs, mixed lesion >2 cm?"** → **osteoblastoma**\
✗ Wrong options: OS · GCT · ABC · stress fracture

**"True about osteoblastoma ablation?"** → **MRgFUS less invasive than RFA** **(TO)**

**"Touch-me-not lesions EXCEPT?"** → **osteoblastoma** (painful, needs intervention) **(TO, FD chapter)**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Osteochondroma & Multiple Hereditary Exostosis

*Pathology · 47 / 66*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Commonest benign bone tumour (20–50%)** · commonest benign cartilage tumour · tested ages 9–24 (adolescents, young adults)

**Site** — **bone surface**, metaphysis, often at **tendon insertions** · **knee** (distal femur > proximal tibia) · proximal femur, proximal humerus · **femur 30%**, tibia + humerus 10–20% each · **2nd commonest site: pelvis (iliac crest)** **(TO)** · spine rare (posterior elements of **cervical** spine) · **never facial bones** (intramembranous) · subungual exostosis (hallux)

**Polyostotic conditions (TO)** — FD (McCune-Albright), **MHE** ("diaphyseal aclasis"), enchondroma (Ollier/Maffucci) \
✗ Wrong options: **haemangioma** (non-polyostotic)

### Pathophysiology

**Hamartomatous** bone + cartilage · physeal cartilage escapes through a **perichondral ring (node/zone of Ranvier) defect** and grows by endochondral ossification under the periosteum → **grows away from the joint**

**Secondary causes** — Salter-Harris fracture · surgery · **radiation** (**commonest benign radiation-induced bone tumour**, 6–24%, latency 3–17 yr)

### MHE genetics (EXT1/EXT2, heparan sulfate, AD)

**Inheritance** — **autosomal dominant** · penetrance **96% females, 100% males** (>95%) · child of affected parent ≥50% risk · ~15% of osteochondroma patients

**Genes** — **EXT1 (8q24), EXT2 (11p13)**, EXT3 (19) — **tumour suppressors** (exostosins) → **↓ heparan sulfate** in physeal chondrocytes · affect **prehypertrophic chondrocytes** · dysregulated **Indian hedgehog** signalling

**EXT1 vs EXT2** — EXT1 **more severe**: more exostoses, more malalignment, shorter limbs/height, less forearm + knee ROM, more pelvic/flat-bone lesions, **higher chondrosarcoma rate**\
✗ Wrong options: lower

| Gene | Condition (distractors) |
|---|---|
| **EXT1/2/3** | ✓ **MHE** |
| PHEX | X-linked hypophosphataemic rickets |
| PMP-22 | Charcot-Marie-Tooth |
| SYT-SSX | Synovial sarcoma |
| GNAS | FD / McCune-Albright |
| IDH1 | Ollier / Maffucci |
| DTDST | Diastrophic dysplasia (cauliflower ear, hitchhiker thumb) — AR |
| FGFR2 · FGFR3 | Apert · achondroplasia |
| RUNX2/CBFA1 | Cleidocranial dysplasia |

**Other inheritance contrasts** — Ollier sporadic · fibular hemimelia none · sickle (infarcts) AR

## 3 Presentation

**Solitary** — **painless mass** · mechanical symptoms, irritation (bursitis), NV compression · grows until skeletal maturity

**MHE** — family history · multiple masses (knees, wrists, shoulders)

### Forearm & ankle deformity in MHE

**Deformities** — **short stature**, **LLD**, femoral shortening · **coxa valga** (lesser trochanter lesions) · **knee valgus** + patellar dislocation and **ankle valgus** (short fibula) · **ulnar shortening + radial bowing**, **ulnar deviation of wrist**, **radial head (radiocapitellar) dislocation** · pectoral/pelvic asymmetry · premature OA · no absent thumb

| Masada | Description | Frequency |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | **Distal ulnar exostosis → short ulna + radial bowing, radial head NOT dislocated** | **Commonest (55–61%)** **(TO)** |
| **II** | Short ulna + **radial head dislocated** · IIa proximal radial exostosis · IIb no proximal radial exostosis | 22–33% |
| **III** | **Distal radial** exostosis → relative **radial shortening** | — |

**Masada treatment (TO)** — I: conservative / excise painful lesion · II: wait to ~13 yr, then levelling ± radial head resection or ring-fixator reduction · III: excise painful lesion, ulnar shortening for positive variance

**Forearm rotation loss** — **osteochondroma excision** most effective · deformity → excision + ulnar lengthening + radial closing-wedge osteotomy\
✗ Wrong options: osteotomies · radial head resection

**Ankle valgus in a 7-yr-old** — **medial (distal tibial) hemiepiphysiodesis**\
✗ Wrong options: full epiphysiodesis · cast · curettage · resection alone

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray (diagnostic)** — **sessile (broad) or pedunculated (stalk)** surface lesion · **pedunculated points away from the joint** (in both solitary and MHE) · **cortex continuous** with host · **medullary cavity continuous** with host (**pathognomonic**) · cartilage cap radiolucent, involutes at maturity

![Medullary continuity of osteochondroma on CT](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8020-case-d-dist-tibia-ct-parsons-moved.png)

**Cartilage cap** — **MRI best** (T1 low, T2 high) · US and CT also measure it

| Cap thickness | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Child (immature) | **1–3 cm** normal — thick cap = growth, **not** malignancy |
| Adult (mature) | **2–3 mm** normal |
| **Adult >2 cm** (≥2 cm) | **Secondary chondrosarcoma** concern (Orthobullets, TO) · OB variants: **>1.5 cm**, ">1–2 cm", ">2–3 cm" · **thickening** in adulthood (✗ Wrong options: thinning) |

**Biopsy** — not recommended for diagnosis\
✗ Wrong options: needle biopsy · bone scan · PET

### Histology

**Like a normal physis** — **hyaline cartilage cap** + **well-defined perichondrium** + linear columns of active chondrocytes + normal primary trabeculae · stalk = cortical + cancellous bone

### Differential (bony bumps)

| Lesion | Key feature |
|---|---|
| **Osteochondroma** | **Cortical + medullary continuity** |
| Subungual exostosis (Dupuytren) | Dorsal distal phalanx (hallux), **no medullary continuity** |
| **Trevor disease** (dysplasia epiphysealis hemimelica) | **Epiphyseal** osteochondromas, lower limb, M:F 3:1, no malignancy |
| **Turret exostosis** | Dome on dorsal proximal/middle phalanx, post-traumatic, no continuity |
| **BPOP (Nora lesion)** | Hand phalanges/metacarpals, bone + cartilage + fibrous, high recurrence |
| **Parosteal OS** | Posterior distal femur, dense lobulated, no continuity |
| Juxtacortical chondroma | Saucerized cortex, 20–40 |
| Subperiosteal haematoma | Smooth elliptical, trauma |
| Myositis ossificans | Doesn't share cortex, zonation |

**"Benign bone tumour presenting as a bump"** → **osteochondroma** (turret exostosis + Nora lesion = non-tumours) **(TO)**

**Cartilage tumours (TO)** — chondroblastoma, osteochondroma, CMF all cartilage → "EXCEPT" = **none**

## 5 Management

### Treatment — observation vs marginal excision

```
Osteochondroma
├─ Asymptomatic / minimal (incl. MHE child) → OBSERVATION
├─ Symptomatic, cosmetic, mechanical → MARGINAL RESECTION at stalk base + whole cartilage cap
│      (delay to skeletal maturity if possible → ↓ recurrence)
├─ MHE deformity → excision, hemiepiphysiodesis, osteotomy
└─ Growth/pain after maturity, cap >2 cm → MRI/biopsy → secondary chondrosarcoma → WIDE RESECTION
```

**Marginal resection** — resect at **base of stalk including entire cartilage cap** (↓ regrowth) · graft/fix large defect \
✗ Wrong options: chemo · RT · curettage/liquid N₂ · radical resection

**No further tests** before excising a classic symptomatic lesion (MRI already done)

**Incidental humeral / femoral sessile lesion, asymptomatic** — **observation**\
✗ Wrong options: MRI, CT, bone scan, biopsy, long-leg films if alignment normal

**MHE child** — **observe** · excision for radial head dislocation, loss of rotation, symptoms\
✗ Wrong options: biopsy largest · PET · remove all · bisphosphonates

### Subungual exostosis

**Hallux**, adolescents · reactive metaplasia (microtrauma) · painful, may ulcerate · mistaken for wart · histology: mature trabecular bone capped by **fibrocartilage**

**Treatment** — **nail removal + excision / osteotomy through base**, local wound care (protect nail matrix) · recurrence possible\
✗ Wrong options: observation if painful · amputation · chemo/RT

## 6 Complications

### Malignant transformation to chondrosarcoma

| Setting | Risk |
|---|---|
| **Solitary osteochondroma** | **<1%** |
| **MHE** | **5–10%** (Orthobullets) · 3–10% · 0.57–8.3% · ~10% · "1 in 10,000" (one OB) — read options |

**Risk factors** — **pelvic/central (proximal) location** (highest), **sessile**, MHE, **EXT1**, **older age**, cap >2 cm in adult

**Secondary chondrosarcoma** — **low grade (~90%; 67–85%)** · 3rd–4th decade (tested 50) · **pelvis** commonest · <1% in children · presents as **new pain / growth after maturity** · pelvic mass → urinary retention, perineal numbness

**Treatment** — **wide resection** (same as chondrosarcoma), curative 70–90% · pelvic → multidisciplinary (general + plastic) \
✗ Wrong options: chemo · RT · local excision

### Other complications

**Vascular** — **popliteal artery pseudoaneurysm** (rare), compression, true aneurysm, arterial/venous thrombosis

**Nerve compression (up to 25%)** — sciatic, **common peroneal** (anterior + lateral compartment atrophy), radial

**Tendon** — shoulder: cuff impingement, subscapularis tear, bicipital tendinitis

**Bursa** formation (± metaplastic cartilage nodules) · **recurrence 2–5%** after resection

## How it's asked

**"HME — EXCEPT?"** → **"exostoses grow towards the physis"** (they grow away) (✓ AD, ✓ prehypertrophic chondrocytes, ✓ EXT1-3, ✓ medullary continuity)

**"MHE — EXCEPT?"** → **"grow towards joint in MHE but away in solitary"** (both grow away)

**"NOT associated with MHE?"** → **absence of the thumb**

**"EXT1 vs EXT2 — all EXCEPT?"** → **lower sarcomatous transformation** (EXT1 is higher)

**"AD gene defect with ↑ chondrosarcoma risk?"** → **EXT-1**\
✗ Wrong options: PHEX, PMP-22, SYT-SSX, GNAS-1

**"EXT-1 defect — diagnosis + tumour risk?"** → **MHE, chondrosarcoma**

**"X-ray for EXT1/2/3 mutation?"** → **multiple osteochondromas**\
✗ Wrong options: rickets · cleidocranial · NF bowing · Madelung

**"Which X-ray shows an AD disorder?"** → **MHE**\
✗ Wrong options: infarct/sickle AR · diastrophic AR · fibular hemimelia · Ollier sporadic

**"MHE with EXT — true?"** → **↓ heparan sulfate in physis**\
✗ Wrong options: cap thinning = malignancy · EXT2 worse · 25% malignancy · distal lesions riskier

**"Father with exostoses + hemiepiphysiodesis, child with bumps — gene?"** → **EXT1**

**"Incidental lesion — true in children?"** → **secondary transformation usually low grade, cured by surgery alone**\
✗ Wrong options: cap <1 mm · 20%

**"62-yr-old, enlarging lifelong medial thigh bump, multiple bumps?"** → **EXT; secondary low-grade chondrosarcoma**

**"MHE, pelvic grade 2 chondrosarcoma with urinary retention?"** → **wide resection with multidisciplinary team**

**"Highest risk of malignant transformation?"** → **pelvic location**\
✗ Wrong options: sessile · isolated · young age · 2 mm cap

**"Non-polyostotic tumour?"** → **haemangioma** **(TO)**

**"2nd commonest site of osteochondroma?"** → **pelvis** **(TO)**

**"Benign tumour presenting as bump on long bones?"** → **osteochondroma** **(TO)**

**"Sessile medial distal femoral lesion sharing cortex + medulla?"** → **osteochondroma**\
✗ Wrong options: myositis ossificans · parosteal OS

**"Thoracic transverse-process lesion on trauma CT — aetiology?"** → **aberrant cartilaginous growth**

**"Symptomatic pedunculated tibial osteochondroma?"** → **marginal resection**\
✗ Wrong options: chemo · radical · curettage + liquid N₂

**"14-yr-old, sessile anterior femoral exostosis, painless?"** → **observation**

**"Painful, cosmetic distal femoral osteochondroma, skeletally mature?"** → **marginal resection with complete cap removal**

**"Tests before resecting classic symptomatic lesion?"** → **none**

**"7-yr-old with multiple exostoses?"** → **observation**

**"Most common forearm deformity in HME?"** → **ulnar exostosis with radial bowing (Masada I)** **(TO)**

**"Limited pronation/supination in MHE?"** → **osteochondroma excision**

**"7-yr-old, ankle valgus from fibular exostosis?"** → **medial hemiepiphysiodesis**

**"Painful hallux lesion misdiagnosed as wart?"** → **nail removal + excision** / **osteotomy through base**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Osteofibrous Dysplasia

*Pathology · 48 / 66*

## 1 Basics

**Nature** — rare, **benign fibro-osseous** tumour-like lesion of the **tibial (± fibular) cortex** · aka **ossifying fibroma**

**Age** — **<10 yr** (peak 1–5) · **M > F**

**Site** — **anterior tibial cortex**, diaphysis (± metaphysis)

### Genes & lab markers

**Trisomies 7, 8, 12, 22** reported

**No Gs-α (GNAS) mutation** (vs fibrous dysplasia)

**Keratin −** (vs adamantinoma: keratin + epithelial nests)

## 3 Presentation

**Painless swelling** of the shin · may be tender when bumped

**Deformity** — **anterior / anterolateral tibial bowing (procurvatum)**, limp

**Pseudarthrosis** — **10–30%**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray (AP + lateral)** — **eccentric, intracortical, multiloculated lytic lesion of the anterior tibial cortex** · well-marginated lucencies, sclerotic borders · expands/thins anterior cortex · **no periosteal reaction** · confined to anterior cortex

![Anterior tibial cortical lesion with bowing](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8039-35a-moved.jpg)

**CT** — cortical expansion, no periosteal reaction or matrix · **bone scan** — focally hot

**Biopsy** — needed when **young adult (15–35)** or red flags (growth, change, aggressive features) → exclude **adamantinoma**

### Histology

**Like fibrous dysplasia EXCEPT osteoblastic rimming** — fibroblast proliferation (bland spindle stroma) around **woven bone islands lined by osteoblasts** (lamellar rim) · mitoses common · ± giant cells · **no epithelial nests**

![Osteoblastic rimming around woven bone](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8039-35c-moved.jpg)

### Differential

| | Tibial diaphysis | Observation alone |
|---|---|---|
| **OFD** | Yes | Yes |
| **Adamantinoma** | Yes | No (wide resection) |
| FD · EG · enchondroma · osteochondroma · NOF · Paget | — | Yes |

| | **OFD** | **Adamantinoma** | **Fibrous dysplasia** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Age | **<10** | 20–40 | <30 |
| Location | **Anterior tibial cortex** | Anterior mid-tibial cortex | **Medullary**, femur/ribs/skull |
| Histology | Woven bone **with osteoblastic rimming** | **Epithelial nests, keratin +** | Woven bone **without rimming** |
| Gene | Trisomies | — | **GNAS** |
| Treatment | **Observe** | **Wide resection** | Observe / fix |

**Others** — NOF (doesn't erode cortex, storiform) · UBC/ABC (metaphyseal, femur/humerus) · Ewing (onion skin, round cells) · EG

## 5 Management

```
Osteofibrous dysplasia
├─ Most → OBSERVATION (annual follow-up) — regresses by maturity
├─ Significant deformity interfering with walking → BRACE
├─ Impending pathologic fracture / pain → CURETTAGE + GRAFT
└─ Persistent deformity after skeletal maturity → CORRECTIVE OSTEOTOMY
```

**Avoid surgery before ~15 yr** — **very high local recurrence**

**No curettage + grafting** for routine OFD · (✗ Wrong options: casting · wide resection · chemo)\
✗ Wrong options: "accepted for all EXCEPT OFD" question (see ABC chapter)

## 6 Complications

**Prognosis** — lesions **regress**, no problems in adulthood · link to adamantinoma debated (continuum?)

## How it's asked

**"3-yr-old, bowing + limp, lucent anterior proximal tibial diaphyseal lesion?"** → **osteofibrous dysplasia**\
✗ Wrong options: FD (ground glass) · UBC/ABC (metaphyseal) · NOF

**"12-yr-old, tender bump below knee, anterior cortical lucencies, osteoblastic rimming?"** → **OFD**\
✗ Wrong options: adamantinoma · NOF · ABC · low-grade CS

**"9-yr-old shin mass, trabeculae lined by osteoblasts?"** → **OFD**\
✗ Wrong options: NOF · osteoblastoma · chondroblastoma · Ewing

**"6-yr-old, tender tibial shaft lucency, hot bone scan?"** → **OFD**\
✗ Wrong options: GCT · NOF · FD · EG

**"10-yr-old painless lower-leg deformity, OFD histology — next?"** → **observation**\
✗ Wrong options: brace · cast · curettage · osteotomy

**"7-yr-old, incidental anterior tibial lesion, keratin-negative biopsy?"** → **observation with annual follow-up**\
✗ Wrong options: wide resection (adamantinoma)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Osteoid Osteoma

*Pathology · 49 / 66*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **10–14% of benign** bone tumours · **2–3% of primary** bone tumours · **most common true benign bone tumour (TO)**

**Demographics** — **M:F 2.5:1** (Orthobullets) · **2–4:1** (TO, spine) · **5–25 yr**, **70% before 20** · peak 2nd decade

**Site (Orthobullets)** — lower limb >50%: **proximal femur > tibial diaphysis**, **cortical** · spine 10–15% (thoracolumbar > cervical/sacral, **posterior elements**, **concave side** of scoliosis) · hand 5–10% (**scaphoid, proximal phalanx**) · foot <5% (**talar neck**)

**Site (TO)** — **femur > tibia, cortical, diaphysis** (keyed "femur, cortical diaphysis") · vertebrae **7–20%** · usually cortical, can be intramedullary, subperiosteal, intra-articular

**Spinal OO (TO)** — **lumbar** · **posterior elements 75%** · **pars interarticularis** commonest · CT: lesion at **pedicle–lamina junction**

![CT — nidus at pedicle–lamina junction (TO)](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17747111-1.png)

### Pathophysiology (COX, PGE2)

**Nidus** — central nodule of **woven bone + osteoid with osteoblastic rimming** · **reactive zone** — thickened bone + fibrovascular tissue

**Pain** — ↑ **prostaglandin E2** (+ PGI2) and **COX-1 / COX-2** expression · ↑ number and size of **unmyelinated nerve fibres** in nidus

**NSAIDs** — **cyclooxygenase inhibition** → ↓ PGE2 · ibuprofen/naproxen competitive · aspirin irreversible acetylation\
✗ Wrong options: mu-opioid agonist · 30S · transpeptidase · ACh agonist

**Most important pain mediator** — **PGE2** · first shown by **Makley and Dunn**\
✗ Wrong options: PGI2 (endothelial COX-2, anti-platelet) · IGF-1 · glutamate · ATP

**Associations** — **scoliosis, lesion on concave side** (paravertebral spasm) · **LLD** (epiphyseal → overgrowth) · flexion contractures

## 3 Presentation

**Pain** — constant, progressive, **worse at night** and with **alcohol**, **not activity-related**, **relieved by NSAIDs/aspirin** · near joint → mimics arthritis

**Spine** — **night pain 80–100%** · **painful scoliosis 63–70% (TO)** · 75% (Orthobullets) · backache + paravertebral spasm + scoliosis in adolescent → suspect OO

**Hand** — painless swelling possible

**Exam** — swelling, tenderness, effusion, contracture, limp, muscle atrophy

**Delay** — mean ~**14 months** to diagnosis · often misdiagnosed as **stress fracture** in runners

**Labs** — **ESR, CRP, WBC normal**\
✗ Wrong options: osteomyelitis

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray (orthogonal views)** — intense **reactive sclerosis around a radiolucent nidus** · may be normal early · spine: subtle pedicle sclerosis, typical only in half · **obliques** help (pedicle, facet, pars) **(TO)**

**CT (modality of choice)** — **nidus <1.5 cm** (>1.5 cm → osteoblastoma) · **vascular groove sign** in **80%** (1 mm serpentine cortical lucencies) · bull's-eye calcified centre (50%) · sclerosis may exceed nidus · **fine-cut CT** separates OO from **stress fracture**

![CT — radiolucent nidus with sclerosis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8012-osteoid-osteoma-1.jpg)

**Bone scan** — **most sensitive for localization (TO)** · **double-density sign** — intense nidus uptake + lower surrounding uptake

![Double-density sign](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8012-double-density-sign.jpg)

**MRI** — **not recommended** — marrow **oedema masks features** · useful for intra-articular nidus · not needed for diagnosis

**Diagnosis** — clinical history + NSAID response + X-ray/CT → **no biopsy, no staging, no labs needed**

### Histology

**Distinct demarcation nidus vs reactive bone** · uniform **immature osteoid trabeculae (woven bone)** with **osteoblastic rimming** · plump, regular osteoblasts · vascular stroma · **no infiltration** of host bone · no neutrophils/dead bone\
✗ Wrong options: Brodie

![Nidus — woven bone with osteoblastic rimming](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8012-osteoid-osteoma-histology-moved.jpg)

### Enneking staging of benign lesions

| Stage | Grade | Examples |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Latent** | Enchondroma, NOF |
| 2 | **Active** | **Osteoid osteoma**, UBC, ABC, chondroblastoma, CMF, GCT |
| 3 | **Aggressive** | GCT, ABC |

### Differential

| Lesion | Distinguishing |
|---|---|
| **Brodie's abscess** | Neutrophils, dead bone · similar cortical X-ray |
| **Stress fracture** | Activity-related · **fine-cut CT** separates |
| **Osteoblastoma** | **>2 cm**, intramedullary, aggressive, dull ache |
| **ABC** | Posterior elements, fluid levels |
| Ewing | Permeative, onion skin |
| Low-grade intramedullary OS | Intramedullary sclerosis, ~1% of OS |
| Enchondroma | Intramedullary, painless, cartilage |
| Osteoma | **Dense juxtacortical** bony mass |
| **Chondroblastoma** | **Epiphyseal** — OO is diaphyseal **(TO)** |

## 5 Management

```
Osteoid osteoma
├─ First line (most extremity; painful spine without scoliosis) → NSAIDs + OBSERVATION
│      (50% burn out over several years)
├─ Failed NSAIDs / GI side effects → RFA (standard of care) or MR-HIFU
└─ Near skin / nerve / cord, digit, recurrence after RFA, fixed spinal deformity → SURGICAL EXCISION
```

### NSAIDs & observation

**First line** — NSAIDs + observation \
✗ Wrong options: RFA/HIFU/resection before trial · MRI

**Incidental OO** — unrelated acute symptoms (e.g. contralateral radiculopathy after injury) → **continued observation**

### Radiofrequency ablation & MR-HIFU

**RFA** — **CT-guided** electrode into nidus · **90°C for 4–6 min** → **1 cm** necrosis zone · **90% success in 1–2 sessions** (95% one procedure — OB) · **10–15% recurrence** · least invasive, fastest resolution, least morbidity

**RFA distances** — **>1 cm from skin** · **>1 cm from articular surface** · **1–1.5 cm from neurovascular** structures · spine safe with motor response testing

**RFA after failed NSAIDs** — hip, tibia, femur → **RFA**\
✗ Wrong options: biopsy · CT chest · en bloc · curettage + cement

**MR-HIFU** — non-invasive, **non-ionizing** · thermal ablation of periosteal nerves + nidus · comparable to RFA · for failed medical management (e.g. NSAID GI side effects)

### Surgical excision (spine, hand)

**Indications** — not amenable to RFA (**close to skin, nerve, cord/dura**) · **digital lesions** (RFA → thermal skin necrosis, digital NV injury) · **recurrence after RFA** · **spine with fixed deformity**

**Spine near neural elements** — **surgical resection / en bloc excision of nidus**\
✗ Wrong options: RFA · vertebrectomy/corpectomy · fusion

**Digit** — **curettage with power burr** (<10% local recurrence)\
✗ Wrong options: RFA · wide resection

**Principle** — complete marginal removal of **nidus** (sclerotic bone can stay) · **94% success**

**Spinal deformity** — **resolves if excised within 15 months** of onset · no TLSO for mild curve

## 6 Complications

**RFA** — recurrence 10–15% · cellulitis, thrombophlebitis, **skin necrosis** (<1 cm from skin) · NV damage (<1–1.5 cm)

**LLD** — epiphyseal lesion → limb overgrowth

**Open curettage/en bloc near hip** — late joint morbidity

**Prognosis** — pain resolves ~**3 yr** (2–4 yr, hand) · lesion resolves **5–7 yr** · malignant transformation extremely rare

## X vs Y

| | **Osteoid osteoma** | **Osteoblastoma** |
|---|---|---|
| Incidence | 10% of benign | 3% of benign |
