| Size | **<2 cm (typically <1.5)** | **>2 cm** (3–3.5) |
| Site | >50% long-bone diaphysis · proximal femur > tibia > phalanges > spine | >35% posterior spine |
| Natural history | **Self-limited** | **Progressive** |
| Symptoms | **Night pain, relieved by NSAIDs** · painful scoliosis (concave side) | Dull ache, partial NSAID response, neuro symptoms |
| Spine treatment | Nonoperative first → RFA/resection | **Surgery always** |

### Bone-tumour one-liners (TO)

| Fact | Answer |
|---|---|
| Location | Most metaphyseal · **epiphyseal**: GCT, chondroblastoma · **diaphyseal**: Ewing, OO/osteoblastoma, FD sometimes |
| Age | Most <20 · **40–60**: haemangioma, chondrosarcoma · **20–40**: GCT · **bimodal**: OS (10–20 primary, 40–60 secondary) · Ewing 5–15 |
| Most common tumour of bone | **Metastasis** |
| Most common primary | **Myeloma > OS > chondrosarcoma** |
| Most common benign | **Osteochondroma** · true benign: **OO** · lesion: **fibrous cortical defect** |
| Malignant, 1st decade | **Ewing** |
| Radiation-induced | **OS > fibrosarcoma > MFH** · soft tissue: **MFH** |
| Hand | **Enchondroma** · malignant bone: chondrosarcoma · malignant overall: SCC |
| Chest wall malignant bone | **Chondrosarcoma** |
| Night pain + NSAID response | **OO** |
| Pulsatile | ABC, GCT, telangiectatic OS, **thyroid follicular / renal** mets (renal most) |
| Egg-shell calcification | **GCT** |
| Codman tumour | **Chondroblastoma** |
| OS virus | **FBJ murine virus** |
| Lowest recurrence after extended curettage | **Liquid nitrogen** |
| HME inheritance | **Autosomal dominant** |

| Most common site (TO) | |
|---|---|
| FD | **Femoral neck = craniofacial** |
| UBC · ABC | **Proximal humerus** · **proximal femur** |
| Enchondroma · chondroblastoma | **Hand** · **distal femur** |
| CMF | **Proximal tibial metaphysis** |
| Osteochondroma | Distal femur > proximal tibia |
| OO · osteoblastoma | Femur > tibia (diaphysis) · posterior vertebra > femoral diaphysis |
| Haemangioma | **Spine (T4–L4)** > skull |
| GCT | Distal femur > proximal tibia > distal radius |
| OS · chondrosarcoma · Ewing | Distal femur · proximal femur · femoral diaphysis > flat bones |
| Chordoma · ameloblastoma · adamantinoma | **Sacrum** · **mandible** · **tibia** |
| EG · plasmacytoma/myeloma | **Skull** · **spine** |
| Glomus · synovial sarcoma · PVNS | **Subungual** · **knee** · **knee** |

| Sign / pattern (TO) | Lesion |
|---|---|
| Ground glass, rind sign, shepherd's crook | FD (crook also Paget, OI) |
| Fallen leaf, trap door | UBC |
| Corduroy / jail-house, polka dot | Haemangioma |
| Nidus <1.5 cm / >1.5 cm | OO / osteoblastoma |
| Soap bubble | GCT · also ABC, chondroblastoma, enchondroma, CMF, FD, ameloblastoma, plasmacytoma, chordoma |
| Codman triangle, sunray | OS (Codman also Ewing) |
| Popcorn calcification | **Chondrosarcoma** |
| Onion peel | Ewing |
| Punched-out, double contour (skull) | EG |
| Punched-out, no sclerotic rim | Myeloma |
| Chinese letters · chicken wire | FD · chondroblastoma |
| Storiform · herringbone | MFH · fibrosarcoma |
| Small round cells | Ewing, lymphoma, neuroblastoma, PNETs |
| Biphasic | Synovial sarcoma |

| Marker / syndrome (TO) | |
|---|---|
| Ewing | **t(11;22)** (85%), **CD99, MIC-2** |
| Synovial sarcoma | **t(X;18)** |
| LCH | **S-100, CD1a**, NSE, Birbeck granules |
| McCune-Albright | Polyostotic FD + precocious puberty + café-au-lait |
| Mazabraud | Polyostotic FD + intramuscular myxomas |
| Diaphyseal aclasia | HME · Masada — forearm |
| Ollier · Maffucci | Enchondromas · + haemangiomas |
| Vertebra plana | **EG (commonest)**, Ewing, lymphoma/leukaemia, Gaucher, ABC, infection |

| Metastasis & sarcoma (TO) | |
|---|---|
| Mets primary | Males: prostate > lung · females: breast > lung · children: **neuroblastoma** · overall breast > prostate > lung |
| Site | **Thoracic spine** · pathological fracture: **femoral neck** · distal to knee/elbow → lung primary, tibia |
| Occult mets | **Bone scan** (blastic) · **PET-CT** (lytic) |
| STS adults · young adults · children | **MFH** > liposarcoma · **synovial sarcoma** · **rhabdomyosarcoma** (GU > extremity) |
| STS hand/UL · foot | **Epithelioid sarcoma** · **synovial sarcoma** |
| STS prognosis | **Histological grade** |
| Bone resorption markers | N/C-telopeptides, hydroxyproline, deoxypyridinoline, hydroxylysine glycosides, **TRAP**, bone sialoprotein |
| Bone formation markers | **Bone-specific ALP**, **osteocalcin**, procollagen-1 extension peptides |

**Spinal tumours (TO)** — most common benign primary bony: **vertebral haemangioma** · most common overall: **epidural metastasis**

## How it's asked

**"Axial CT spine, lesion at pedicle–lamina junction — diagnosis?"** → **osteoid osteoma** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: OS · EG · Brodie

**"Most common site of osteoid osteoma?"** → **femur, cortical, diaphysis** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: metaphysis · tibia intramedullary

**"17-yr-old runner, shin pain eased by NSAIDs — confirmatory histology?"** → **woven bone nidus with osteoblastic rimming**\
✗ Wrong options: hyaline cartilage (enchondroma) · physaliphorous (chordoma)

**"17-yr-old, failed scoliosis bracing, normal labs, hot spine lesion?"** → **osteoid osteoma**\
✗ Wrong options: idiopathic scoliosis · osteomyelitis

**"23-yr-old fibular cortical lytic lesion, oedema, plump osteoblasts, no inflammation?"** → **osteoid osteoma**\
✗ Wrong options: Brodie · osteoblastoma (>2 cm)

**"18-yr-old night thigh pain, intracortical nidus, woven bone spicules?"** → **osteoid osteoma**\
✗ Wrong options: low-grade intramedullary OS

**"31-yr-old shin pain, NSAIDs help, intracortical lucent nidus?"** → **osteoid osteoma**\
✗ Wrong options: osteoma (dense juxtacortical)

**"15-yr-old athlete, tibial diaphysis pain worse at night — next step?"** → **fine-cut CT**\
✗ Wrong options: biopsy · referral · chest X-ray

**"Mechanism of the drug that relieves OO pain?"** → **cyclooxygenase inhibition**

**"True of this painful finger lesion?"** → **↑ cyclooxygenase activity**\
✗ Wrong options: needs MRI · no spontaneous resolution · day pain

**"Most important pain mediator?"** → **PGE2**\
✗ Wrong options: PGI2 · glutamate · ATP

**"Failed NSAIDs, normal labs, tibial/hip OO — next?"** → **percutaneous / CT-guided RFA**\
✗ Wrong options: biopsy · en bloc · antibiotics

**"Hip OO — fastest relief, least morbidity?"** → **RFA**\
✗ Wrong options: steroid injection · curettage with hip dislocation

**"Runner with groin pain, ibuprofen helps, CT nidus?"** → **RFA**\
✗ Wrong options: MR arthrogram · intra-articular steroid

**"NSAID GI side effects, anterior tibial OO?"** → **MR-HIFU**\
✗ Wrong options: radical resection · chemo

**"Cervical OO near cord, failed NSAIDs?"** → **surgical resection / en bloc excision**\
✗ Wrong options: RFA · corpectomy · C2–3 fusion

**"Little-finger OO, can't take NSAIDs?"** → **curettage**\
✗ Wrong options: RFA (skin/NV necrosis) · wide resection

**"12-yr-old, mild curve, OO abutting canal, NSAIDs failed?"** → **surgical excision**\
✗ Wrong options: TLSO · phenol

**"Water-skier, right leg pain, left L5 OO on CT?"** → **continued observation** (incidental)

**"Night pain worse with alcohol, nidus on CT, no treatment yet?"** → **NSAID trial + observation**\
✗ Wrong options: MRI · RFA first

**"15-yr-old, epiphyseal lytic soap-bubble lesion?"** → **chondroblastoma** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: OO (diaphyseal) · GCT (20–40)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Paget's Disease

*Pathology · 50 / 66*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Nature** — idiopathic **abnormal bone remodelling** — excessive resorption + abnormal new bone formation (osteitis deformans) · **2nd commonest chronic bone-remodelling disorder** in the US after osteoporosis · **3–4% of US population >40**

**Demographics** — **>40 yr**, peak **5th decade** · **M = F** · **Caucasians** (northern European / **Anglo-Saxon**)

**Distribution** — **monostotic or polyostotic**

**Sites** — **femur > pelvis > tibia > skull > spine** (Orthobullets) · **pelvis > tibia** (TO) · also clavicle · **sacrum** typically involved

### Pathophysiology — osteoclast abnormality

**Primary abnormality** — ↑ **osteoclastic bone resorption** (✗ Wrong options: osteoblast · osteocyte · chondroblast · synoviocyte) → **focally increased remodelling** (✗ Wrong options: ↓ vascularity · sclerotic bone replacing marrow)

**Cause** — **slow virus** (intranuclear nucleocapsid-like inclusions) — **paramyxovirus**, **RSV**

**Osteoclast diseases** — Paget · **osteopetrosis** (defective osteoclastic resorption) · pycnodysostosis — share **nuclear inclusion bodies** \
✗ Wrong options: OI (osteoblast) · osteochondroma (chondrocyte) · myeloma · osteoporosis (uncoupling, not inherited)

**Osteopetrosis genes** — carbonic anhydrase II · vacuolar proton pump · chloride channel 7

### Genes & lab markers

**Inheritance** — mostly spontaneous · familial clusters, **~40% autosomal dominant** · family history in **15%** (OB)

**SQSTM1 (p62/sequestosome 1), 5q35-QTER** — most important · severe disease

**TNFRSF11A** — insertion mutation in the gene encoding **RANK**

**Labs** — ↑ **serum ALP** (most useful test; tracks extent + activity) · ↑ **urinary collagen cross-links** · ↑ **urinary hydroxyproline** · ↑ **urinary N-telopeptide, α-C-telopeptide, deoxypyridinoline** · **calcium + phosphate normal** · PTH normal (↑ in small subset with very active disease)

**α-CTX** — rises more than β-CTX → ↑ α/β CTX ratio **(TO)** · **urinary NTX** — sensitive, monitors treatment response **(TO)**

**Hypercalcaemia** — only with **immobilization** (↑ osteocyte **sclerostin**), fracture, predominant lytic phase, or sarcoma **(TO)** · ambulatory → normal calcium

**vs myeloma** — **ALP ↑ in Paget**, normal in myeloma **(TO)**

### Phases

| Phase | Process | X-ray |
|---|---|---|
| **Lytic** | Intense osteoclastic resorption, hypervascular | Lucent, expanded, thin intact cortex · **blade of grass / flame** advancing edge · skull **osteoporosis circumscripta** |
| **Mixed** | Resorption + compensatory formation (weak brittle bone) | Lysis + sclerosis, coarse trabeculae |
| **Sclerotic (burnt-out)** | Osteoblastic formation dominates · dense avascular **mosaic** bone | **Bone enlargement, cortical thickening** — commonest presentation |

**All three phases can coexist in one bone**

## 3 Presentation

**Asymptomatic** — commonest · found incidentally (↑ ALP, X-ray for another complaint)

**Pain** — commonest presenting symptom · dull ache · stress fractures, hypervascularity, warmth · **cyclic** recurring leg pain

**Deformity** — **long-bone bowing** (femur, **tibia varus**) · kyphosis · short neck (flattened skull base, basilar invagination) · skull enlargement

**Fractures** — brittle bone · **transverse** — **banana / incremental fracture** **(TO)** · subtrochanteric

**Arthritis** — hip + knee OA (83% have a rheumatic syndrome — OB)

**Neuro** — **deafness** (CN VIII compression, otosclerosis, cochlear BMD loss) · impaired vision, facial palsy, trigeminal neuralgia · **spinal stenosis** · **vascular steal** → cerebral ischaemia, spinal claudication **(TO)**

**Cardiac** — **high-output heart failure** — large/multiple lesions + poor cardiac reserve

**Red flag** — **new intense pain + swelling** → **Paget sarcoma**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — **coarsened trabeculae, blastic** appearance · **remodelled cortices** — loss of cortex–medulla distinction · **bone enlargement + cortical thickening** · bowing · fractures · OA

![Coarsened trabeculae, cortical thickening](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8040-paget-s-disease.jpg)

| Sign (TO) | Finding |
|---|---|
| **Picture-frame vertebra** | Peripheral cortical thickening (✗ Wrong options: VDRR · fluorosis · MED) |
| **Ivory vertebra** | Diffusely sclerotic vertebra |
| **Cotton-wool spots** | Fluffy sclerotic skull patches |
| **Osteoporosis circumscripta** | Focal lytic skull lesion, lytic phase |
| **Brim sign** | Sclerotic iliopectineal line |
| **Blade of grass** | V-shaped diaphyseal lucency |
| **Banana fracture** | Transverse pathological fracture of bowed bone |

![Picture frame · brim sign · osteoporosis circumscripta · blade of grass](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17130235-1.png)

| Skull sign (TO) | Disease |
|---|---|
| **Salt and pepper** | Hyperparathyroidism |
| **Bevelled edge, double contour** | Eosinophilic granuloma (LCH) |
| **Osteoporosis circumscripta · cotton wool** | Paget (lytic · sclerotic) |
| **Rain-drop skull** (punched-out) | Myeloma |

| Spine sign (TO) | Disease |
|---|---|
| **Picture frame · ivory** | Paget |
| **Rugger jersey** | Hyperparathyroidism / renal osteodystrophy · osteopetrosis |
| **Cod-fish vertebra** | Osteoporosis (biconcave) |

**Bone scan** — marks disease sites accurately · **intensely hot in lytic + mixed**, less in sclerotic · pagetoid bone hot even without sarcoma

**MRI** — thick trabeculae with **normal marrow signal** · lumbar stenosis

**CT** — cortical thickening, coarse trabeculae

**Sarcoma** — cortical destruction + soft-tissue mass

### Histology

**Mosaic pattern** — woven + lamellar bone, **broad irregular trabeculae**, **disorganized cement lines** · **large osteoclasts, many nuclei (up to 100)**, **virus-like inclusion bodies** · **fibrovascular tissue** between trabeculae · biopsy rarely needed **(TO)**

![Mosaic cement lines](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8040-histo-b-moved.jpg)

### Differential

| | Benign, older (40–80) | Multiple lesions, older | Observation only | Bisphosphonate helps |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Paget's disease** | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |
| Enchondroma (older adult) | ✓ | — | ✓ | — |
| Bone island | ✓ | — | — | — |
| Bone infarct | ✓ | ✓ | — | — |
| Hyperparathyroidism (brown tumour) | ✓ | ✓ | — | — |
| Metastatic bone disease · myeloma | — | ✓ | — | ✓ |
| Lymphoma of bone | — | ✓ | — | — |
| NOF · osteochondroma · EG | — | — | ✓ | — |
| FD | — | — | — | ✓ |

**Distractors** — osteoporosis (compression fractures, neck/IT) · renal osteomalacia (erosions, amyloid) · lymphoma (blastic but no enlargement/coarse trabeculae) · melorheostosis (periosteal new bone) · infarct

## 5 Management

```
Paget's disease
├─ Asymptomatic → OBSERVATION + SUPPORTIVE (physio, orthoses, NSAIDs, analgesics)
│      (treat if active disease at sites where complications likely)
├─ Symptomatic → BISPHOSPHONATES (1st line) → CALCITONIN (2nd line)
├─ Hip/knee OA → THA / TKA after medical pre-treatment
└─ Bowing with fracture / impending fracture / failed brace → METAPHYSEAL OSTEOTOMY + PLATE
```

### Medical treatment — bisphosphonates

**Indications** — **bone pain** · **pre-op** (lower ALP to **50% of pretreatment** → less bleeding) · hearing loss, spinal stenosis, HOHF · prevention of fracture/deformity

**Mechanism** — **osteoclast inhibition**\
✗ Wrong options: TNF-α · IL-1R · ER blockade · COX

| Drug | Notes |
|---|---|
| **Alendronate, risedronate** | Oral |
| **Etidronate** | Old, inhibits osteoclasts **and** osteoblasts · **≤6 months** at a time |
| **Pamidronate, zoledronic acid** | IV, osteoclasts only · **single 5 mg IV zoledronic acid** — most effective, normalizes ALP up to **6.5 yr** |
| **Calcitonin** | **2nd line** (bisphosphonate intolerance) · osteoclasts shrink within minutes · SC/IM |
| **Calcium + vitamin D** | Prevent hypocalcaemia on bisphosphonate/calcitonin · not as treatment |
| **Teriparatide** | **Contraindicated** — secondary OS risk |

**Teriparatide contraindications** — Paget · hypercalcaemic disorders · OS · bone mets · pregnancy · skeletal radiation

**NSAIDs** — pain relief, no change in course \
✗ Wrong options: methotrexate · monoclonal antibody · chemo · radiation · wide resection

**Benign biopsy, lytic sacral lesion in old melanoma patient** — Paget → **bisphosphonate**

### Arthroplasty & osteotomy in Paget disease

| Procedure | Commonest complication |
|---|---|
| **THA** | **Bleeding** (rich blood supply) · loosening ~15% |
| **TKA** | **Malalignment** |

**Before arthroplasty** — **pharmacological pre-treatment** to cut bleeding · activity by **ALP + urine hydroxyproline**

**Bowing + persistent pain after 6 mo brace** — **corrective osteotomy + plate fixation**\
✗ Wrong options: IMN without osteotomy · amputation · knee arthrodesis

## 6 Complications

### Paget sarcoma

**Incidence** — **<1%** · **5–10%** in advanced polyostotic disease **(TO)**

**Type** — **osteosarcoma > fibrosarcoma, chondrosarcoma** (spindle-cell sarcomas) · pleomorphic spindle cells, giant cells, atypia

**Patient** — older (**55–80**, 7th decade) · polyostotic · **pelvis, femur, humerus**, proximal skeleton

**Treatment** — **chemotherapy + wide resection + reconstruction** · palliative RT if too infirm\
✗ Wrong options: surgery alone

**Prognosis** — **worst of bone sarcomas** · metastatic 5-yr survival **<10%** (Orthobullets/OB) · **<5%** (OB2004) · non-metastatic **>50%** · secondary OS in >60 yr 7.5% vs primary 37% (Huvos)

**Premalignant for secondary OS (TO)** — FD · Paget · irradiation · multiple osteochondromatosis · bone infarct

**Others** — fractures · OA · HOHF · deafness · stenosis

## How it's asked

**"Picture-frame vertebrae seen in?"** → **Paget's** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: VDRR · fluorosis · MED

**"NOT true of Paget's?"** → **decreased urinary N-telopeptide** (also false: ↑ Ca/PO₄ in lytic phase — better pick is NTX) **(TO)**

**"60-yr-old, back/hip pain, cotton-wool skull, ↑↑ ALP?"** → **Paget's** (one copy keyed "hyperparathyroidism" but its own explanation says Paget's) **(TO)**

**"Subtrochanteric fracture, ↑ ALP, normal Ca/PO₄?"** → **Paget's**\
✗ Wrong options: osteoporosis · renal osteomalacia

**"↑ urinary hydroxyproline, NTX, α-CTX — which X-ray?"** → **Paget's**\
✗ Wrong options: infarct · myeloma · melorheostosis

**"Paget's — which cell?"** → **osteoclast**

**"Excess resorption + disordered formation?"** → **Paget's**\
✗ Wrong options: osteopetrosis · OI

**"Expected lab?"** → **↑ urinary NTX + α-CTX** · **↑ serum ALP**\
✗ Wrong options: ↓ calcium · ↑ calcium · ↓ cross-links

**"Ambulatory 40-yr-old, macrophage-lineage cell disease — lab?"** → **normal calcium**\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ phosphate · low ALP · normal hydroxyproline

**"Enlarged sclerotic hemipelvis with coarse trabeculae?"** → **Paget's**\
✗ Wrong options: lymphoma

**"Another disease of the same abnormal cell?"** → **osteopetrosis**

**"Paget's = osteoclastic abnormality causing?"** → **focally increased remodelling**

**"Asymptomatic Paget's?"** → **supportive therapy**\
✗ Wrong options: bisphosphonate · calcitonin

**"Symptomatic hip, ↑ ALP, normal Ca?"** → **bisphosphonates**\
✗ Wrong options: antibiotics · chemo

**"Contraindicated drug in Paget's?"** → **teriparatide**

**"Bowed leg, mixed lesion — drug mechanism?"** → **osteoclast inhibition**

**"Benign Paget biopsy, hemipelvis — next?"** → **observation + evaluate for bisphosphonate**

**"Painful pagetoid femur, normal marrow on MRI?"** → **medical management**\
✗ Wrong options: prophylactic fixation · RT

**"Enlarged sclerotic bowed tibia, cyclic pain?"** → **bisphosphonate**\
✗ Wrong options: NSAIDs alone · vit D · MTX

**"Paget sarcoma — EXCEPT?"** → **treated by surgery alone**

**"Humeral Paget sarcoma — treatment?"** → **chemo + wide resection + reconstruction**

**"Commonest malignant transformation?"** → **osteosarcoma**\
✗ Wrong options: fibrosarcoma · myeloma · GCT

**"Paget's predisposes to?"** → **all** — chondrosarcoma, OS, spindle-cell sarcoma

**"Worst-prognosis bone sarcoma?"** → **sarcoma in Paget's**\
✗ Wrong options: MFH · OS · Ewing · clear-cell CS

**"Bowed tibia, brace failed?"** → **corrective osteotomy + plate**

**"Paget THA — commonest complication?"** → **increased blood loss**\
✗ Wrong options: malalignment (TKA)

**"Paget TKA — commonest complication?"** → **malalignment**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Parosteal Osteosarcoma

*Pathology · 51 / 66*

## 1 Basics

**Nature** — **low-grade**, well-differentiated **surface** osteosarcoma · **<5%** (OB7521) / **~5%** (OB8282) of osteosarcomas

**Demographics** — **females > males** · **30–40 yr** (3rd–4th decades) · range in stems 20–42

**Site** — **surface of metaphysis** · **posterior distal femur** > proximal tibia > proximal humerus · **80% femur** (Orthobullets) · posterior distal femur **~75%** (OB7488) / **80%** (OB4407)

**Marrow invasion** — **25%** · does not affect long-term survival

### Genes & lab markers

**MDM2 amplification** + **ring chromosome** — high rate

## 3 Presentation

**Painless mass** · slow-growing, firm, mild/no pain · long history (>3 yr)

**↓ Knee flexion / tightness** — large posterior distal femoral lesion · tenderness on deep posterior palpation · effusion

**Labs** — ESR, CBC normal

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — **heavily ossified, lobulated mass arising from cortex**, **"stuck-on"** the metaphyseal surface over a broad area · **cloud-like** ossification · may be multifocal

![Lobulated ossified mass stuck on posterior distal femur](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8015-case-a-posterior-femur-xray-parsons-moved.png)

**MRI** — **mandatory**, whole bone · soft-tissue/marrow involvement, skip lesions · **very dark** signal (heavy mineralization)

**CT chest** — **mandatory** staging (lung mets)

**Bone scan** — **mandatory** · **always hot**

**Angiography** — finds **hypervascular dedifferentiated areas** → target biopsy

**Next step** — **biopsy** after staging\
✗ Wrong options: arthroscopy · bisphosphonate · chemo/RT first

### Histology

**Low grade** — **regularly arranged normal osseous trabeculae** · **atypical spindle cells between trabeculae** = most prominent feature · bland fibroblastic stroma · some atypia needed for diagnosis

**Pitfall** — mistaken for **fibrous dysplasia** histologically (X-ray differs)

![Spindle cells between regular trabeculae](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8015-parosteal-osteosarcoma-histology.jpg)

### Differential

| | Bone surface lesion | Similar histology | Surgery alone |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Parosteal OS** | ✓ | — | ✓ |
| **Periosteal OS** | ✓ | — | — |
| **Periosteal chondroma** | ✓ | — | ✓ |
| **Osteochondroma / MHE** | ✓ | — | ✓ |
| Developmental defect (adductor magnus insertion) | ✓ | — | — |
| **Myositis ossificans** | ✓ | — | — |
| **Chondrosarcoma** · **adamantinoma** | — | — | ✓ |
| **Chordoma** | — | — | ✓ (controversial) |
| **FD** | — | ✓ | — |

| Lesion | Key distinction |
|---|---|
| **Osteochondroma** | **Medullary continuity** with host bone on cross-section |
| **Myositis ossificans** | Thin **egg-shell peripheral** calcification, often **not touching bone** |
| **Surface chondrosarcoma** | More cortical destruction, **rings and arcs** |
| Dedifferentiated parosteal | Hypervascular areas on angiogram |

## 5 Management

```
Parosteal osteosarcoma
├─ Low grade → WIDE RESECTION ALONE
│      (geometric osteotomy to keep native joint; no chemo or RT)
└─ Dedifferentiated (high-grade component) → NEOADJUVANT CHEMO + WIDE RESECTION
```

### Treatment — wide resection alone

**Only OS subtype treated with wide resection alone**\
✗ Wrong options: conventional · periosteal · telangiectatic · secondary (all chemo + wide + chemo)

**Low-grade intramedullary (central) OS** — also **surgery with wide margins only** · "lacey" osteoid, minimal atypia, long premorbid history

**✗ Wrong options** — curettage (high recurrence) · marginal resection · observation

**Conventional OS for contrast** — pre-op chemo **8–12 weeks**, maintenance **6–12 months** after surgery

## 6 Complications

**Prognosis** — **95% long-term survival** with local control · better than other OS

**Dedifferentiation** — **poor prognostic factor** → chemo

## How it's asked

**"21-yr-old, dense bone 'stuck-on' proximal humerus, mature bone in fibroblastic stroma?"** → **parosteal OS**\
✗ Wrong options: periosteal OS · osteochondroma · MO

**"33-yr-old knee, surface lesion, hypervascular on angiogram, high-grade areas?"** → **dedifferentiated parosteal OS** — neoadjuvant chemo + wide resection

**"34-yr-old woman, ↓ knee flexion, lobulated posterior distal femoral surface mineralization?"** → **parosteal OS**\
✗ Wrong options: chondrosarcoma (rings and arcs) · lymphoma

**"Field hockey player, ↓ knee flexion, posterior femoral surface lesion — next?"** → **surgical biopsy**\
✗ Wrong options: chemo · RT · arthroscopy

**"Low-grade intramedullary OS, no mets — treatment?"** → **surgery only**

**"Parosteal OS (biopsy) — definitive management?"** → **wide surgical resection**\
✗ Wrong options: neoadjuvant chemo (conventional OS)

**"24-yr-old, tight knee, surface OS — plan?"** → **surgical resection only**\
✗ Wrong options: chemo · RT · observation

**"42-yr-old slowly enlarging thigh mass, surface OS?"** → **wide resection**\
✗ Wrong options: curettage (recurrence)

**"Which OS subtype gets wide resection alone?"** → **parosteal**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Periosteal Chondromas

*Pathology · 52 / 66*

## 1 Basics

**Nature** — rare, **benign chondrogenic surface** tumour (under periosteum)

**Age** — **10–20 yr**

**Site** — **proximal humerus (59%)** · distal femur, proximal femur · **hand** (metacarpals, phalanges)

## 3 Presentation

**Pain** — many painful from **irritation of overlying tendons** · night pain possible · palpable, mildly tender mass

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — **well-demarcated, shallow cortical defect** · **saucerization** of underlying cortex · punctate mineralization in **1/3** · cortically based, eroding cortex\
✗ Wrong options: medullary epicentre (enchondroma)

![Saucerized cortical defect, proximal tibia](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8019-case-f-prox-tibia-xray-parsons-moved.png)

**X-ray is the key** — histology can look like **chondrosarcoma**

### Histology

**Like enchondroma, but more cellular** with more malignant-looking cells · **bland hyaline cartilage** · small **chondroid cells in lacunae**

![Bland hyaline cartilage](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8019-histology-a-moved.png)

### Differential

| | Surface lesion | Similar chondroid histology | Marginal excision |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Periosteal chondroma** | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |
| **Osteochondroma / MHE** | ✓ | ✓ | — |
| **Parosteal OS** · **periosteal OS** | ✓ | — | — |
| **Enchondroma / Ollier / Maffucci** | — | ✓ | — |
| **Chondrosarcoma** | — | ✓ | — |
| Neurilemmoma · nodular fasciitis (soft tissue) | — | — | ✓ |
| Epidermal inclusion cyst · **glomus tumour** | — | — | ✓ |

| Lesion | Key distinction |
|---|---|
| **Enchondroma** | Same histology, **central medullary** epicentre |
| **Surface chondrosarcoma** | Malignant histology |
| **CMF** | Fibrous + myxoid elements |
| **Chondroblastoma** | Polyhedral cells, **chicken-wire** calcification |
| **Periosteal OS / CS** | Similar location — malignant |

## 5 Management

```
Periosteal chondroma
├─ Mild symptoms → OBSERVATION
└─ Severe / progressive pain, function impaired → MARGINAL EXCISION INCLUDING UNDERLYING CORTEX
       └─ Large defect → BONE GRAFT
```

**Recurs if cartilage left behind** \
✗ Wrong options: wide excision · RT · chemo

## How it's asked

**"19-yr-old shoulder night pain, surface cartilage lesion on CT + biopsy — treatment?"** → **marginal excision including underlying cortex**\
✗ Wrong options: wide excision · chemo · RT 60 Gy

**"10-yr-old wrist, cortical saucer-shaped defect, benign cartilage?"** → **periosteal chondroma**\
✗ Wrong options: enchondroma (medullary) · surface CS · CMF · chondroblastoma

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Periosteal Osteosarcoma

*Pathology · 53 / 66*

## 1 Basics

**Nature** — extremely rare **intermediate-grade surface** osteosarcoma

**Demographics** — **15–25 yr** · **F > M**

**Site** — **diaphysis** of long bones · **femur, tibia**

### Genetics (p53, RB, Li-Fraumeni)

**p53 germ-line mutation** — **15–20%** of periosteal OS

**Li-Fraumeni** — **AD**, germ-line **p53** inactivation · breast, colon cancers, sarcomas — **50% are osteosarcoma** → **OS = commonest bone tumour with p53**\
✗ Wrong options: chondrosarcoma (p53 = aggressive behaviour) · Ewing (EWSR1–ETS) · myeloma

**Sporadic OS** — p53 mutation in **15–30%**

**Retinoblastoma (RB1)** — hereditary (bilateral) retinoblastoma → OS · RB1 linkage analysis identifies at-risk relatives

### Genes & lab markers

| Finding | Tumour |
|---|---|
| **RB + p53 alterations** | **Conventional high-grade OS** |
| **Supernumerary ring chromosomes** | Parosteal OS |
| **t(11;22)** | Ewing |
| **t(X;18)** | Synovial sarcoma |
| **Telomeric associations** | GCT of bone |

## 3 Presentation

**Pain** — commonest symptom · **pathologic fracture 25%**

**Exam** — regional swelling, tenderness · may present after a minor twist

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — **"sunburst" / "hair-on-end"** periosteal reaction, often in a **saucerized cortical depression** · **no medullary involvement** typically

![Sunburst periosteal reaction](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8016-osteosarcoma-radiograph-sunburst-periosteal-reaction.jpg)

**Staging** — **CT chest** (lung mets) + **bone scan** (very hot) required

### Workup — MRI of entire bone

**Bone-forming lesion, indistinct margins** → **MRI of the entire bone ASAP** before biopsy · biopsy by the treating surgeon\
✗ Wrong options: biopsy first · amputation · observation · NSAIDs

### Histology

**Grossly lobular, cartilaginous** · **osteoid + chondroblastic matrix** · **no osteoid → chondrosarcoma**

![Chondroblastic matrix with osteoid](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8016-histology-d-moved.jpg)

### Surface OS compared

| | **Parosteal OS** | **Periosteal OS** |
|---|---|---|
| Grade | Low | **Intermediate** |
| Age · sex | 30–40 · F | **15–25** · F |
| Site | Posterior distal femoral **metaphysis** | Femur/tibia **diaphysis** |
| X-ray | **Stuck-on**, lobulated, ossified | **Sunburst** in saucerized cortex |
| Histology | Spindle cells between regular trabeculae | **Chondroblastic** + osteoid |
| Genes | **MDM2**, ring chromosome | **p53** (15–20%) |
| Treatment | **Wide resection alone** | **Chemo + wide resection + chemo** |

**Other distractors** — osteochondroma (medullary continuity, not aggressive) · dedifferentiated CS (from prior cartilage tumour) · synovial sarcoma (soft tissue, spindle cells, may calcify)

### Conventional OS distractors (same module)

| Lesion | Histology |
|---|---|
| **Osteosarcoma** | **Osteoid from pleomorphic / malignant spindle cells** · Codman triangle, soft-tissue mass |
| Ewing | Uniform **small blue round cells** (reactive osteoid not prominent) |
| Osteomyelitis | Inflammatory cells, dead bone, no mass |
| EG | Histiocytes + eosinophils, rarely mass |
| MFH of bone | Lytic, **storiform**, no osteoid |
| **Osteoblastoma** | Benign stromal cells |
| Stress fracture | **T1 MRI fracture line**, no mass |

## 5 Management

### Treatment — chemotherapy & resection

**Same as intramedullary OS** — **neoadjuvant multi-agent chemo (8–12 wk) → wide resection (limb salvage) → maintenance chemo (6–12 mo)**

**Limb salvage** — allograft / megaprosthesis · rotationplasty · amputation if needed

**✗ Wrong options** — radiation (OS is not radiosensitive) · chemo alone · surgery alone · resection before chemo

## 6 Complications

**Pulmonary mets** — **20–35%**

**Prognosis** — **intermediate** between parosteal and intramedullary OS

**Good sign** — **90–98% necrosis** after neoadjuvant chemo

**Bad sign** — **MDR gene** (pumps chemo out) · in **25% of primaries, 50% of mets**

## How it's asked

**"47-yr-old knee pain/swelling, surface lesion, osteoid + cartilage?"** → **periosteal OS**\
✗ Wrong options: chondrosarcoma · Ewing · GCT · FD

**"24-yr-old softball twist, sunburst surface ossification?"** → **periosteal OS**\
✗ Wrong options: parosteal (stuck-on) · osteochondroma · dedifferentiated CS · synovial sarcoma

**"16-yr-old distal femoral bone-forming lesion, pleomorphic osteoid?"** → **osteosarcoma**\
✗ Wrong options: Ewing · rhabdomyosarcoma · MFH

**"10-yr-old distal femur, destructive bone-forming, malignant spindle cells?"** → **osteosarcoma**\
✗ Wrong options: osteoblastoma · Ewing

**"10-yr-old distal tibia, Codman triangle, soft-tissue mass?"** → **osteosarcoma**\
✗ Wrong options: EG · osteomyelitis · trauma

**"Li-Fraumeni (p53) — commonest primary bone tumour?"** → **osteosarcoma**

**"9-yr-old, retinoblastoma, mother bilateral RB, humeral OS — treatment?"** → **chemotherapy + wide resection**\
✗ Wrong options: RT

**"16-yr-old conventional OS — genetic feature?"** → **RB + p53 alterations**\
✗ Wrong options: ring chromosomes · t(11;22) · t(X;18) · telomeric

**"13-yr-old high-grade OS — treatment?"** → **neoadjuvant chemo → resection + reconstruction**\
✗ Wrong options: resection then chemo · RT

**"10-yr-old proximal tibial OS?"** → **surgery + chemotherapy**

**"10-yr-old humeral OS?"** → **chemo + limb-sparing surgery with reconstruction**\
✗ Wrong options: amputation alone · RT

**"15-yr-old, indistinct bone-forming knee lesion — next?"** → **MRI of the entire bone**\
✗ Wrong options: biopsy first

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Pigmented Villonodular Synovitis

*Pathology · 54 / 66*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Name** — now **tenosynovial giant cell tumour (TGCT)** · WHO 2013: **diffuse TGCT = PVNS** · **localized TGCT = GCT of tendon sheath** (commonest in **fingers**) **(TO)**

**Nature** — **locally aggressive neoplastic synovial disease** ("not a true neoplasm" — Orthobullets) · neoplastic: **aneuploidy, trisomy 7**, ~20 malignant/metastatic cases **(TO)** · "non-neoplastic inflammatory" (OB7584) · rarely metastasizes

**Incidence** — **9.2 per million/yr** (Orthobullets) · **1.8 per million/yr**, 1 in 250 arthroscopies **(TO)**

**Demographics** — **30–40 yr** (any age; stems 14–58) · **M = F** (Orthobullets) · slight **F** predominance (OB9003)

**Site** — monoarticular · **knee > hip > ankle > shoulder > elbow** · localized: **anterior knee (80%)**, patellofemoral, infrapatellar fat pad

**History of trauma** — 50%

### CSF-1 pathogenesis & pexidartinib

**Driver** — **overexpression of CSF1** → CSF1R-expressing cells cluster → synovial hyperplasia · chromosome **1p13** · **5q33** rearrangement

| Gene distractor | Lesion |
|---|---|
| **CSF1 ↑** | **PVNS / TGCT** |
| USP6 | ABC, nodular fasciitis |
| MDM2 | Parosteal / low-grade OS, ALT |
| APC | Desmoid (FAP) |
| c-myc | Aggressive GCT of bone |
| EXT | MHE |
| RB | OS (up to 35%) |
| p53 | Li-Fraumeni sarcomas, breast |
| FGFR3 · RUNX2 | Achondroplasia · cleidocranial dysplasia |
| Ki-67 · NF1 · NTRK | Ewing · neurofibromatosis · various solid tumours |

### Localized vs diffuse

| | **Localized** | **Diffuse** |
|---|---|---|
| Location | Knee > hip > ankle | Knee (75%) |
| Age | 30–50 | <40 |
| Sex | M = F | F ≥ M |
| Presentation | **Painless**, swollen, long-standing · locking/catching (meniscal mimic) **(TO)** | **Painful**, swollen, tender, ↓ motion |
| X-ray | Erosion from local pressure | Degenerative change **both sides of joint** |
| MRI | **Well-circumscribed** mass | **Ill-defined** mass |
| Recurrence after synovectomy | **8%** | **30%** |
| Treatment | **Nodule excision + partial synovectomy** | **Total synovectomy** (anterior arthroscopic + posterior open) |

## 3 Presentation

**Hallmark** — **recurrent atraumatic haemarthrosis** · insidious pain, stiffness, swelling · boggy effusion, warmth, erythema, joint-line tenderness, ↓ ROM

**Pain** — often not the main complaint **(TO)** · vague for years

**Labs** — **ESR, CRP normal**

**Aspirate** — **grossly bloody / brown** · e.g. >50,000 RBC, 1,100 WBC

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray (AP + lateral)** — soft-tissue swelling · **cystic erosions with sclerotic margins on both sides of the joint** · knee often shows advanced OA; erosions/cysts **commoner in hip + ankle** (tight joints) **(TO)** · **no mineralization**\
✗ Wrong options: synovial chondromatosis

![Cystic erosions with sclerotic margins on both sides](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8050-pvns-cystic-erosion-with-sclerotic-margins-160-on-both-sides.jpg)

**CT** — extent of cystic bone loss

**MRI (most sensitive)** — **low signal on T1 and T2** (haemosiderin) · **blooming artefact** on gradient echo (iron) · fat signal on T1 · effusion, synovial expansion, erosion · **posterior extra-articular extension** · modest enhancement · brighter foci = less haemosiderin **(TO)** · "somewhat bright on T2" (OB7420)

![Low T2 signal of PVNS](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8050-low-intensity-signal-of-pvns-on-t2-mri.jpg)

**Arthroscopy (gold standard + biopsy)** — **brownish/reddish inflamed synovium**, **frond-like papillary projections**

![Brown frond-like synovium](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8050-23-moved.jpg)

### Histology

**Haemosiderin-stained multinucleated giant cells** · mononuclear stromal cells · vascular villi lined by plump hyperplastic synoviocytes · **pigmented foam cells (lipid-laden histiocytes)** · mitoses common (few in OB stems) · gross: yellow-brown mass

![Haemosiderin-laden giant cells](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8050-q4-1d-1-moved.jpg)

| Histology distractor | Lesion |
|---|---|
| Biphasic spindle + epithelial | Synovial sarcoma |
| Lipoblasts, myxoid, signet-ring | Myxoid liposarcoma |
| Antoni A/B | **Neurilemmoma** |
| Plasma cells, lymphoid follicles | RA |
| Caseating granulomas | TB |
| Mature chondrocyte nodules | Synovial chondromatosis |
| Osteoclast-like cells + stroma + osteoid | OS / GCT of bone |

### Differential

| Lesion | Key distinction |
|---|---|
| **Synovial chondromatosis** | Nodules **low T1, high T2**, mineralized loose bodies · no haemorrhagic effusion |
| **Synovial sarcoma** | **Periarticular, not intra-articular** · T2 bright · spindle cells |
| Haemophilia | Recurrent effusions, but MRI not nodular mass |
| RA | Pannus, uniform signal · no haemorrhage |
| Gout | Punched-out erosions, overhanging edges, tophi |
| Tumoral calcinosis | Extracapsular periarticular calcification |
| GCT of bone | Eccentric epiphyseal/metaphyseal lesion in bone |
| Baker's cyst | Fluid outpouching, semimembranosus–medial gastrocnemius |
| ABC · chondroblastoma | Intra-osseous (PVNS is extra-osseous) |

**Behaviour descriptor** — **locally aggressive**\
✗ Wrong options: malignant · inflammatory · normal variant

## 5 Management

```
PVNS / TGCT
├─ Asymptomatic → OBSERVATION
├─ Localized → NODULE EXCISION + PARTIAL SYNOVECTOMY (arthroscopic if anterior)
├─ Diffuse → TOTAL SYNOVECTOMY (arthroscopic anterior + open posterior) ± EBRT
├─ End-stage joint → SYNOVECTOMY + ARTHROPLASTY (ankle → ARTHRODESIS)
└─ Recurrent / unresectable, surgery morbid → PEXIDARTINIB
```

### Surgical treatment & recurrence

**Mainstay** — **synovectomy** (open or arthroscopic) · arthroscopic → less morbidity, better ROM · open → pain, stiffness **(TO)**

**Margin** — synovectomy = **intralesional** (OB417 — only lesion where intralesional is standard) · = **marginal** excision (Orthobullets, OB7568)

**Localized** — **resection of nodule only** · arthroscopic anterior excision + partial synovectomy\
✗ Wrong options: complete synovectomy · RT · FNA (excisional biopsy)

**Diffuse knee** — lowest recurrence: **open anterior + posterior synovectomy** (morbid) · arthroscopic anterior + **open posterior** (between gastrocnemius heads — **popliteal NV bundle at risk**) · **posteromedial portal** for posterior compartment **(TO)**

**Foot/ankle** — **marginal excision** · radioisotopes poor (multi-joint spread, **skin necrosis** if extravasated) \
✗ Wrong options: amputation · wide excision

**EBRT** — **30–35 Gy / 15 fractions** or **50 Gy / 25** · with total synovectomy → recurrence **10–20%** · for recurrent/destructive disease · not RT alone

**Intra-articular Y-90 / radiocolloid** — combined with surgery historically **(TO)** · now removed/abandoned — haematomyelopoietic disease, high complications (OB)

**Not used** — **chemotherapy** **(TO)** · intra-articular steroids · plica excision · TKA in young without end-stage cartilage

### Pexidartinib

**Mechanism** — oral **CSF-1R antagonist** (TKI) · also KIT + FLT3-ITD · **first and only FDA-approved** systemic therapy (**2019**) **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: imatinib · emactuzumab · nilotinib (used, not approved)

**Indication** — **diffuse TGCT not amenable to resection** / severe morbidity from surgery / recurrent recalcitrant \
✗ Wrong options: synovial chondromatosis · GCT of bone · synovial sarcoma **(TO)**

**Efficacy** — once daily 24 wk → ~40% improve (ENLIVEN) · partial responses → surgical downstaging

**Side effects** — **cholestatic hepatotoxicity** (REMS, LFT monitoring) · **hair depigmentation** · dose taper\
✗ Wrong options: alopecia

## 6 Complications

**Recurrence** — **most common** complication · **30–50%** despite complete synovectomy (Orthobullets) · **~50%** (OB219276) · 8% localized / 30% diffuse

**Joint destruction** — early OA → arthroplasty (↑ stiffness, infection) · arthrodesis or amputation

**Radiation** — skin necrosis, **radiation-induced sarcoma**

**Uncommon:** arthrofibrosis, chondrolysis, metastasis

## How it's asked

**"45-yr-old, post-trauma knee nodules, arthroscopy finds far more loose bodies than X-ray showed?"** → key absent; sources point to **synovial chondromatosis** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: PVNS · OCD · OA

**"Recurrent effusion, nodularity, deep-red fronds at arthroscopy?"** → **PVNS**\
✗ Wrong options: synovial sarcoma (not intra-articular) · chondromatosis

**"24-yr-old anterior ankle mass, giant cells + haemosiderin?"** → **PVNS**\
✗ Wrong options: ABC · chondroblastoma (intraosseous)

**"Ankle, low T1 + T2 lobulated synovial mass?"** → **PVNS**\
✗ Wrong options: GCT · gout · haemophilia · tumoral calcinosis

**"Low T1/T2, blooming on gradient echo?"** → **diffuse PVNS**\
✗ Wrong options: synovial chondromatosis (high T2)

**"Commonest site of localized PVNS?"** → **anterior knee**

**"58-yr-old, lobular low-T1 posterior knee lesion into tibia, no mineral?"** → **PVNS**\
✗ Wrong options: synovial osteochondromatosis

**"Best descriptor?"** → **locally aggressive**

**"Recurrent haemorrhagic effusions, nodular low-signal masses?"** → **PVNS**\
✗ Wrong options: RA · chondromatosis

**"Correct histology?"** → **lipid-laden macrophages, haemosiderin, giant cells**\
✗ Wrong options: biphasic · Antoni

**"Same disease that also occurs in joints?"** → **tenosynovial giant cell tumour**\
✗ Wrong options: GCT of bone · Baker's cyst

**"Brown synovium at total synovectomy — histology?"** → **haemosiderin-stained multinucleated giant cells**\
✗ Wrong options: caseating granulomas · chondrocyte nodules

**"NOT part of PVNS knee treatment?"** → **chemotherapy** (✓ arthroscopic synovectomy, ✓ RT, ✓ imatinib) **(TO)**

**"Intralesional resection is standard of care for?"** → **PVNS**

**"Ankle PVNS, bloody aspirate — next?"** → **arthroscopic or open resection**\
✗ Wrong options: chemo + wide · RT + radical

**"Commonest complication after synovectomy?"** → **recurrence**\
✗ Wrong options: arthrofibrosis · chondrolysis · metastasis

**"Brown aspirate, parapatellar tenderness — next?"** → **arthroscopic or open resection**\
✗ Wrong options: plica excision

**"Symptomatic localized knee PVNS?"** → **resection of nodule only**\
✗ Wrong options: complete synovectomy · RT

**"Hindfoot mass from subtalar joint, low T1/T2?"** → **marginal excision**\
✗ Wrong options: radioisotope · amputation

**"Diffuse knee PVNS — lowest recurrence?"** → **open anterior + posterior synovectomy**\
✗ Wrong options: limited arthroscopic · steroid · radioisotope

**"16-yr-old, well-circumscribed anterior knee mass?"** → **arthroscopic excision + partial synovectomy**\
✗ Wrong options: open posterior (diffuse)

**"Indication for pexidartinib?"** → **PVNS** **(TO)**

**"Only FDA-approved medical treatment for TGCT?"** → **pexidartinib** **(TO)**

**"Recurrent effusions, brown synovium — driver?"** → **CSF-1 overexpression**\
✗ Wrong options: USP6 · MDM2

**"New drug for PVNS — mechanism?"** → **CSF-1 receptor antagonist**

**"Knee synovectomy planned — mutated gene?"** → **CSF-1**\
✗ Wrong options: APC · c-myc · EXT · RB

**"Recurrent PVNS after open synovectomy — prevent recurrence?"** → **oral pexidartinib**\
✗ Wrong options: Y-90 · steroid · TKA

**"CSF-1R drug treats?"** → **TGCT**\
✗ Wrong options: gout · haemophilia · RA · chondromatosis

**"Pexidartinib side effect + mechanism?"** → **hair depigmentation from CSF-1R inactivation**\
✗ Wrong options: alopecia · anthracycline cardiotoxicity

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Plantar Fibromatosis (Ledderhose Disease)

*Pathology · 55 / 66*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Nature** — benign **myofibroblast + collagen** proliferation of the plantar fascia · **commonest soft-tissue neoplasm of the foot**

**Demographics** — **males** · **middle-aged / elderly** (any age) · Caucasian

**Bilateral** — **25–50%**

**Risk factors** — male · Caucasian · **epilepsy** · repeated trauma · **diabetes** · **long-term alcohol** · chronic liver disease · other fibrosing conditions

**Associations** — **Dupuytren's** (plantar fibromatosis in **25%** of Dupuytren's) · **Peyronie's**

**Genetics** — basis **unclear** (unlike Dupuytren's)

### Anatomy

**Plantar fascia** — **3 bands** (central, medial, lateral) · **medial band most affected** · origin medial + anterior calcaneus → 5 digital slips at MTPJs → base of proximal phalanges

### Phases

| Phase | Feature |
|---|---|
| **Proliferative** | ↑ Fibroblast activity, cell proliferation |
| **Involutional / active** | **Nodule** formation |
| **Residual / resting** | ↓ Fibroblast activity, collagen maturation, scar/contracture |

## 3 Presentation

**Subcutaneous thickening / nodules**, **medial plantar** foot · usually asymptomatic, may become painful · **digital contractures rare**

**Exam** — look for Dupuytren's, Peyronie's · ankle/hindfoot motion · Achilles/gastrocnemius contracture

![Bilateral medial plantar nodules](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8061-bilateral.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray (AP + lateral)** — usually normal

**MRI (study of choice)** — nodular thickening on **medial** plantar aponeurosis · **low T1** (acellular, high collagen) · **low/medium T2** (high-signal areas if aggressive)

![Low T1 nodule on plantar aponeurosis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8061-t1-mri.jpg)

**US** — less reflective fibroma within bright fascia · **comb sign** — alternating hypo- and iso-echoic linear bands

![Comb sign](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8061-ultrasound-comb-sign.jpg)

### Histology

**Dense fibrocellular tissue** — mature collagen, fibrocytes at varying maturity · **no atypia, no abnormal mitoses** · gross: lobulated firm irregular mass

**Differential** — post-traumatic neuroma · fibrosarcoma · clear-cell sarcoma

## 5 Management

```
Plantar fibromatosis
├─ Small / minimally painful → OBSERVATION + SUPPORTIVE
│      (NSAIDs, orthotics, PT, steroid injection, radiotherapy)
└─ Large, painful, activity-limiting → TOTAL FASCIECTOMY ± RADIATION
```

### Treatment & recurrence

| Excision | Recurrence |
|---|---|
| **Local excision** | **57–100%** |
| Wide excision (2–3 cm margins) | 8–80% |
| **Total fasciectomy (preferred)** | **0–50%** |

**Technique** — avoid incisions directly over nodules · partial fasciectomy → **excise nodule + overlying skin** (± skin graft)

**Surgical complications** — skin necrosis · nerve entrapment (medial plantar nerve) · **loss of arch height / windlass** · wound breakdown

**Radiotherapy** — best evidence: **randomized double-blind sham-controlled trial (2023)**, durable efficacy at ≥1 yr · **post-op RT** for high-grade lesions to cut recurrence

**Evidence grades** — collagenase: insufficient (case reports) · orthotics: expert opinion (Level V) · surgery: no Level I superiority data

## 6 Complications

**Recurrence** — **60% of excised lesions** · recurs **more aggressive** · risk: **bilateral**, **multiple nodules**, **family history** · prevent with adjuvant RT

**Prognosis** — malignant transformation **rare**

## How it's asked

**"Plantar fascial lesion — excisional biopsy's likely complication?"** → **recurrence**\
✗ Wrong options: metastasis · wound breakdown, nerve injury, windlass loss = wide excision problems

**"Bilateral plantar nodules, failed orthotics/NSAIDs — highest-grade evidence?"** → **radiotherapy (RCT, double-blind sham-controlled)**\
✗ Wrong options: collagenase RCTs · surgery RCTs

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Pleomorphic Sarcoma of Bone (Malignant Fibrous Histiocytoma)

*Pathology · 56 / 66*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **<2% of primary malignant bone tumours** · **2nd commonest bone sarcoma in adults** after chondrosarcoma

**Demographics** — **20–80 yr** (peak 2nd–4th decades) · **M:F 3:2** · Caucasian

**Site** — **75% appendicular** · **metaphysis** of long bones: **distal femur, proximal tibia, proximal humerus**

**Secondary (25%)** — from **bone infarct, Paget's, prior radiation**

### Genes & lab markers

**p53** mutations — secondary MFH of bone

**Labs** — ↑ WBC · **eosinophilia** · abnormal LFTs · **hypoglycaemia**

## 3 Presentation

**New, painful mass** · **pathologic fracture** · constitutional (fever, chills, night sweats, weight loss) · swelling, limp, ↓ ROM

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray (AP + lateral)** — **purely lytic, destructive metaphyseal** lesion · **no matrix mineralization** · variable periosteal reaction · cortical destruction + soft-tissue extension · non-specific

![Lytic destructive femoral lesion](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8030-case-b-femur-xray-parsons-moved.jpg)

**MRI** — deep heterogeneous mass · internal haemorrhage · **T1 low, T2 high**

**CT** — **chest CT staging** (lung mets) · bone destruction

**Bone scan** — staging · ↑ uptake in adjacent cortex

### Histology

**Pleomorphic spindle + histiocytic cells in storiform pattern** · malignant multinucleated **giant cells with grooved/indented nuclei** · chronic inflammatory cells · variable collagen · fascicles radiating from hypocellular foci · haemorrhage/necrosis → high grade · **no osteoid** (vs OS)

![Storiform pleomorphic spindle cells](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8030-histology-b-moderate-power-parsons-moved.jpg)

### Differential

| | Malignant destructive, older | Sacral, older | Wide resection + chemo |
|---|---|---|---|
| **MFH / fibrosarcoma** | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |
| **Chondrosarcoma** (conventional) | ✓ | ✓ | — |
| **Dedifferentiated chondrosarcoma** | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |
| Metastatic disease · myeloma | ✓ | ✓ | — |
| **Lymphoma of bone** | ✓ | ✓ | — |
| **Secondary sarcoma** | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |
| **Osteosarcoma** · **Ewing** | — | — | ✓ |
| **Chordoma** (sacral) | — | ✓ | — |
| Rhabdomyosarcoma (soft tissue) | — | — | ✓ |

### Enneking staging (OB)

| Stage | Meaning |
|---|---|
| I | Low grade |
| II | High grade |
| III | Distant metastasis |
| A / B | **Intracompartmental / extracompartmental** |

**Stage IIB spindle-cell sarcoma** → pre-op chemo + wide excision + chemo

## 5 Management

### Diagnosis & treatment

**Treat like osteosarcoma** — **neoadjuvant chemo (8–12 wk) → wide resection → adjuvant chemo (6–12 mo) ± RT**\
✗ Wrong options: wide resection alone · chemo alone · RT alone · observation

**Margin** — **wide excision or amputation** → better 5-yr survival than intralesional/marginal

**RT** — incomplete/questionable margins · adjunct

**Soft-tissue UPS for contrast** — **radiation + wide resection** (painless, slow, T1 dark/T2 bright)

### Reconstruction after resection

**Limb salvage** — endoprosthesis · resection arthrodesis · allograft · **rotationplasty**

| Proximal tibia | **Megaprosthesis (MP)** | **Allograft-prosthetic composite (APC)** |
|---|---|---|
| 10-yr implant survival | **79%** | **94%** |
| Failure rate | No significant difference | No significant difference |
| Function | Similar | Similar · **better extensor mechanism in high-demand patient** |
| Leg length | Both restore | Both restore |

## 6 Complications

**Survival** — **50–60% at 5 yr**

**Lung mets** — up to **30%**

**Prognosis** — **secondary worse than primary** · **>90% necrosis** after chemo → better · **age <40** → better DFS

## X vs Y

| Distractor (soft-tissue thigh mass) | Key feature |
|---|---|
| **UPS** | Painless, slow, deep, T1 dark / T2 bright |
| **Liposarcoma** | Lipoblasts, signet-ring cells |
| Desmoid | Upper torso, **dark T1 + T2**, fibroblasts + collagen |
| **Synovial sarcoma** (periarticular) | Biphasic, **t(X;18)**, SYT-SSX |
| Schwannoma | Homogeneous, **target sign**, Antoni A/B |

**PET** — ↓ glucose uptake = early response predictor (OS, Ewing, GIST)

## How it's asked

**"75-yr-old, slow painless 7 cm thigh mass, T1 dark/T2 bright, pleomorphic?"** → **undifferentiated pleomorphic sarcoma**\
✗ Wrong options: liposarcoma · desmoid · synovial sarcoma · schwannoma

**"60-yr-old, spindle-cell sarcoma of bone — optimal treatment?"** → **neoadjuvant chemo + wide resection + adjuvant chemo**\
✗ Wrong options: wide resection alone

**"12-yr-old, purely lytic distal femoral metaphyseal lesion, storiform malignant spindle cells?"** → **chemotherapy + surgery** (MFH of bone)

**"Proximal tibial resection — APC vs megaprosthesis?"** → **APC better extensor mechanism in high-demand patient**\
✗ Wrong options: MP longer survival · APC higher failure

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Radiation Therapy

*Pathology · 57 / 66*

## 1 Basics

**Mechanisms** — **free radical production** · **direct genetic (DNA) damage**

### Radiosensitive tumours

| Use | Tumours |
|---|---|
| **Definitive control** (primary bone) | **Ewing / PNET** · **primary lymphoma of bone** · **haemangioendothelioma** · **solitary plasmacytoma** |
| **First-line RT alone (spinal mets — OB)** | **Leukaemia, lymphoma, myeloma/plasmacytoma, haemangioendothelioma, Ewing** |
| **Adjuvant to surgery** | **Soft-tissue sarcomas** (pre- or post-op) |
| **Palliation / after fixation** | **Metastatic bone disease** — after fixing impending/pathologic fractures |

![Solitary plasmacytoma — radiosensitive](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8004-38c-moved.jpg)

| Metastasis sensitivity | |
|---|---|
| **Prostate** | **Very radiosensitive** |
| **Breast** | **70% sensitive**, 30% resistant |
| **GI, renal** | **Not radiosensitive** |

**Radioresistant (OB)** — **fibrosarcoma, chondrosarcoma, melanoma, angiosarcoma** · osteosarcoma (not radiosensitive — OS chapters)

## 5 Management

### Technique

**1 rad = 1 cGy** · typical **180–200 cGy/day** in **fractions** · RT is **cumulative** (total = sum of fractions)

| Total dose | Tissue healing |
|---|---|
| **<45 Gy** | Usually uncomplicated |
| **45–55 Gy** | Heals, with problems |
| **>60 Gy** | **Unlikely to heal** |

### Preoperative vs postoperative radiation

| | **Pre-op (neoadjuvant)** | **Post-op (adjuvant)** |
|---|---|---|
| **Wound complications** | **More (up to 40%)** | Less (<20%) |
| **Oedema, fibrosis, stiffness** | **Less (<15%)** | More (>20%, often temporary) |
| Lymphoedema · fracture | **Lower** | Higher |
| Field · dose | **Smaller field**, lower dose | Larger field, higher dose |
| Pseudocapsule | **Forms** → close-margin resection | No |
| Tumour shrinkage | **Yes** | No |
| Local recurrence | **Lower** (Orthobullets) · **no difference** (OB6336) | Higher (Orthobullets) |
| Overall survival | No difference by timing | No difference |

## 6 Complications

### Radiation complications (fracture, secondary sarcoma)

**Early** — **delayed wound healing**, **infection**, **desquamation**

**Late** — **fibrosis**, **joint stiffness**, **secondary sarcoma**, **fractures**

**Post-radiation fracture** — **~25%** after STS resection + EBRT

| Fracture risk factor | Yes / No |
|---|---|
| **Dose ≥59 Gy** | Yes |
| **Weight-bearing bone, esp. femur** | Yes |
| **Female** | Yes |
| Volume of bone irradiated | Yes |
| **Anterior femoral compartment resection** | Yes |
| **Age** | Yes |
| **Periosteal stripping** (→ consider prophylactic fixation) | Yes |
| Osteoporosis | Yes |
| **Method of RT administration** | No |

**Post-radiation sarcoma** — sarcoma in a previously irradiated field · **~13%** · ↑ with prior chemo · **very poor prognosis**

**Criteria** — **≥5 yr** after RT (range **3–50 yr**) · **in the RT field** · **different histology** from the original disease

![Lytic destructive clavicle lesion — radiation-associated sarcoma](../../MCQ/attachments/q6345-a-68a.jpg)

## How it's asked

**"Pre-op vs post-op RT — higher with pre-op?"** → **wound necrosis**\
✗ Wrong options: fibrosis · lymphoedema (post-op) · local recurrence (no difference)

**"Synovial sarcoma (SS18-SSX), pre-op RT (A) vs post-op RT (B)?"** → **A: more wound complications, less fibrosis**

**"NOT a risk factor for post-RT fracture?"** → **method of RT administration** (✓ female, ✓ age, ✓ dose, ✓ periosteal resection)

**"47-yr-old, clavicle lytic lesion, mantle RT 22 yr ago, atypia + bone formation?"** → **radiation-associated sarcoma**\
✗ Wrong options: osteonecrosis · osteomyelitis

**"L4–5 lytic metastases — RT first-line for?"** → **lymphoma**\
✗ Wrong options: fibrosarcoma · chondrosarcoma · melanoma · angiosarcoma

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Rhabdomyosarcoma

*Pathology · 58 / 66*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology & subtypes

**Nature** — malignant tumour of **primitive mesenchyme** (mesoderm, skeletal-muscle precursors)

**Commonest soft-tissue sarcoma in children**\
✗ Wrong options: foot (synovial sarcoma) · calcifies (synovial sarcoma) · Maffucci (haemangiomas) · from myxomas

**Site** — **head/neck, genitourinary, retroperitoneum** · GU > extremities (TO, OO chapter)

| Subtype | Age | Histology | 5-yr survival |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Embryonal** | Infants, young children | **Small round blue cells**, ± cross-striations | **80%** |
| **Alveolar** | **Adolescents, young adults** (15–40) | Poorly differentiated round cells + **multinucleated giant cells**, aggregates in **dense fibrous septae** | **60%** |
| **Botryoid** ("bunch of grapes") | Infants, young children · **vagina** | — | **<30%** ("uniformly fatal") |
| **Pleomorphic** | **40–70 yr** | Multiple cell types, like other pleomorphic sarcomas | **25%** |

### Genetics — t(2;13) PAX3-FKHR

**Alveolar** — **t(2;13) → PAX3-FKHR** · **t(1;13) → PAX7-FKHR** (as named in OB8296; paired the other way round in some sources) · **high metastatic risk**

| Translocation | Tumour |
|---|---|
| **t(2;13)** | **Alveolar RMS** |
| t(11;22) EWS-FLI1 | Ewing |
| t(12;16) | Myxoid liposarcoma |
| t(16;17) | ABC |
| t(9;22) | CML |
| t(X;18) | Synovial sarcoma |
| GNAS · IDH1 | FD · Maffucci/Ollier |
| p53 · RB | Osteosarcoma |

### Genes & lab markers

**IHC** — **MyoD1 +**, **myoglobin +**, **myosin +**, **desmin +**, **vimentin +**

## 3 Presentation

**Rapidly growing painless mass** · ± **enlarged regional nodes** (e.g. axillary)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**MRI** — non-diagnostic but needed for planning · **dark T1, bright T2** (like other STS)

**CT chest** — required for staging

**Bone marrow biopsy** — **required** for staging · marrow mets → worse prognosis

**Nodes** — **sentinel node biopsy** (esp. if nodes enlarged)

![Embryonal RMS — small round blue cells](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8059-51d-moved.jpg)

![Alveolar RMS — fibrous septae](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8059-51c-moved.jpg)

### Lymph-node-metastasizing sarcomas

**"SCARE"** — **synovial sarcoma, clear-cell sarcoma, angiosarcoma, rhabdomyosarcoma, epithelioid sarcoma** \
✗ Wrong options: Ewing · myxoid liposarcoma

### Small round blue cell differential

**RMS · neuroblastoma · lymphoma · leukaemia · Ewing · metastatic disease**

## 5 Management

```
Rhabdomyosarcoma
├─ Paediatric → WIDE EXCISION + CHEMO (+ RT if no radical margin)
│      (vincristine, dactinomycin, cyclophosphamide)
├─ Adult pleomorphic → WIDE EXCISION + RT (chemo not effective in adults)
├─ Unresectable / close or positive margins → RT
└─ Widespread metastases (selected) → CHEMO ALONE
```

**Teenager, isolated limb RMS** — **wide resection + chemo + RT** · chemo has a **substantial survival benefit** · amputation rarely (only if better function) \
✗ Wrong options: observation · marginal excision

## How it's asked

**"True of rhabdomyosarcoma?"** → **commonest soft-tissue sarcoma in a child**\
✗ Wrong options: commonest in foot · calcifies · Maffucci

**"Teenager, lower-limb RMS — management?"** → **wide resection + chemo + RT**\
✗ Wrong options: hemipelvectomy · marginal

**"Alveolar RMS — genetic abnormality?"** → **PAX3-FKHR**\
✗ Wrong options: EWS-FLI1 · p53 · RB

**"9-yr-old, rapid painless arm mass, axillary nodes?"** → **t(2;13)**\
✗ Wrong options: t(12;16) · GNAS · t(11;22) · IDH1

**"Translocation of a sarcoma with lymphatic mets?"** → **t(2;13)**\
✗ Wrong options: t(16;17) (ABC) · t(9;22) (CML)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Soft Tissue Sarcoma

*Pathology · 59 / 66*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **12,000/yr** in US · 4,700 deaths/yr · **M > F** · **85% >15 yr**

**Site** — **60% extremities** · **44% thigh**

**Origin** — mesoderm (connective tissue, muscle, fat, nerve, vessels) · grow **centripetally with a pseudocapsule**, compress neighbours

**Types** — **>50** histologic types (~100 — OB) · same presentation, imaging, differential and treatment · commonest: **UPS/MFH, liposarcoma, leiomyosarcoma, synovial sarcoma**

**Commonest by group (TO)** — adults: **MFH > liposarcoma** · young adults: **synovial** · children: **rhabdomyosarcoma** · hand/UL: **epithelioid** · foot: **synovial**

### Genes & lab markers

| Translocation / association | Sarcoma |
|---|---|
| **t(X;18), SYT-SSX** | **Synovial sarcoma** |
| **t(2;13)** | **Alveolar rhabdomyosarcoma** |
| **t(12;16)(q13;p11)** | **Myxoid liposarcoma** |
| **NF1** | **MPNST** |
| **Stewart-Treves / chronic lymphoedema** | **Angiosarcoma** |
| **S-100 + HMB45** | **Clear-cell sarcoma** (melanoma of soft parts) |

### Sarcomas with lymph node spread

**"RACES"** — **R**habdomyosarcoma · **A**ngiosarcoma · **C**lear-cell sarcoma · **E**pithelioid sarcoma · **S**ynovial sarcoma → consider **sentinel node biopsy**

**Overall** — only **5%** of STS go to nodes \
✗ Wrong options: liposarcoma · desmoid · GCT · Ewing · OS · chondrosarcoma · MFH · MPNST

**Leiomyosarcoma** — can spread via lymphatics but mainly **haematogenous** → lungs

## 3 Presentation

**Slow-growing, painless mass** · trauma draws attention · **rapid growth → higher grade**

**Red flags** — **>5 cm**, **deep to fascia**, **immobile**, rapid growth, pain in a previously painless mass, recurrent swelling

**Mimics** — lipoma, **haematoma** (no trauma, no ecchymosis → suspect sarcoma; mean **7-month delay** when misdiagnosed), desmoid, nerve sheath tumours, abscess, biceps tear

**"Sarcoma until proven otherwise"** — large (>5 cm), deep, heterogeneous mass

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Workup of soft tissue mass (MRI, biopsy)

```
Soft-tissue mass
├─ X-rays (2 planes) → bone involvement, mineralization (synovial sarcoma)
├─ Contrast MRI → diagnostic & benign & symptomatic → EXCISE WITHOUT BIOPSY
│      (lipoma, ganglion, schwannoma, intramuscular myxoma)
└─ MRI indeterminate / sarcoma-like → CORE NEEDLE or OPEN BIOPSY → STAGING CT
```

**MRI (mandatory)** — **T1 low** (iso to muscle), **T2 high**, gadolinium: **peripheral enhancement + non-enhancing necrotic centre** · ~**90%** accurate benign vs malignant (ill-defined, large, heterogeneous)

![Deep adductor sarcoma on MRI](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8049-adductor-sarcoma.jpg)

**CT chest** — staging (lungs) · **myxoid liposarcoma → CT chest/abdomen/pelvis (± bone scan)**

**PET** — evolving · useful for node-spreading sarcomas · ↓ uptake predicts response

**Next step order** — X-ray → **MRI** (✗ Wrong options: biopsy before MRI · FNA · excisional biopsy) → **biopsy** (✗ Wrong options: RT · chemo · resection · observation before tissue diagnosis) → staging

### Biopsy

**Core needle** — modality of choice · correct in **68–93%** (Orthobullets) · **84%** (Skrzynski) · cheaper ($1106 vs $7234)

**Open biopsy** — gold standard · after **non-diagnostic core** (e.g. "only blood" in a presumed haematoma)

**Excisional biopsy** — only small (**<3 cm**) clearly benign lesions

**Who** — at the treating centre, by the definitive surgeon · **2–12× more complications** otherwise

**Technique** — **longitudinal** incision · avoid NV structures/compartments · meticulous haemostasis · **drain distal + in line** with incision · expert pathologist\
✗ Wrong options: transverse

### Histology distractors

| Lesion | Histology |
|---|---|
| **Leiomyosarcoma** | Spindle cells, smooth muscle |
| Myxoma | Paucicellular myxoid stroma, bland spindle cells, macrophages |
| Haematoma | Cystic cavity, necrotic debris, fibrin, clot |
| Neurofibroma (dermal) | Discoid proliferations of peripheral nerves |
| Fibroma | Poorly marginated hypocellular collagen |
| **Fibrosarcoma** | **Herringbone** spindle cells **(TO)** |
| **MFH / UPS** | **Storiform** pleomorphic |
| **Clear-cell sarcoma** | Nests of **clear cells** in fibrocollagenous septa, giant cells |

### AJCC 8th staging (extremity / trunk)

| Stage | Size | Nodes | Mets | Grade |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **IA** | <5 cm | None | None | Low |
| **IB** | >5 cm | None | None | Low |
| **II** | <5 cm | None | None | Intermediate/high |
| **IIIA** | **5–10 cm** | None | None | Intermediate/high |
| **IIIB** | **>10 cm** | None | None | Intermediate/high |
| **IVA** | Any | **Any N+** | None | Any |
| **IVB** | Any | Any | **M+** | Any |

**8th edition changes** — **separates appendicular/truncal from other sites** (head/neck, visceral, retroperitoneum) — widely accepted · **depth removed** (not independent) · new T3 (10–15 cm) + T4 (>15 cm) · **node = stage IV** (controversial, equated with lung mets) \
✗ Wrong options: margins · histologic subtype · hospital type

**Vanderbilt staging** — includes depth · **Enneking** — no size, not for marrow/RES tumours, limited for spine

## 5 Management

```
Soft tissue sarcoma (localized)
├─ Most → WIDE RESECTION + RT (pre- or post-op)
├─ Abutting critical structure → PLANNED CLOSE / MICRO-POSITIVE MARGIN + RT
│      (remove adventitia / perineurium / periosteum)
├─ Encasing critical structure → EN BLOC with the structure (bypass graft if possible)
├─ Can't get negative margin / irreversible nerve loss / comorbid → AMPUTATION (often foot)
└─ Resectable lung mets → WEDGE RESECTION / LOBECTOMY
```

### Surgical margins & resection

**Most important treatment factor** (subcutaneous sarcoma DFS) — **size of operative margin**\
✗ Wrong options: chemo dose · RT · RFA

**High grade** — at least **wide** · not intralesional or marginal · dorsum of foot → **wide resection + fasciocutaneous flap**\
✗ Wrong options: STSG over tendons

**Abutting SFA + sciatic nerve, tumour within 1 mm, ink negative** — **equivalent to wide resection removing the structures** (with RT)

**Periosteal resection >10 cm** → fracture risk → **prophylactic nailing**

**Large midfoot synovial sarcoma** — **transtibial amputation** (best local control, fastest return)

**Resection technique** — elliptical incision **incorporating biopsy + drain sites** · tourniquet **without exsanguination** · frozen section margins

**Lung mets** — first-line **surgical resection** if complete resection possible (wedge/lobectomy) · curative in up to **25%** \
✗ Wrong options: RT · chemo first

**Chemotherapy** — **controversial** (no survival benefit in RCTs for most) · consider high grade · **standard for rhabdomyosarcoma + Ewing** · synovial sarcoma more chemo-sensitive

### Radiation therapy

**Effect** — **↓ local recurrence regardless of margin status** · **no overall survival benefit** (Yang) · transient strength/ROM loss · does not hamper limb salvage

**Dose** — **50 Gy pre-op**, **66 Gy post-op** (Orthobullets, OB7588) · **60 Gy** typical adjuvant (OB1117), range 45–65 Gy · palliative bone mets **~30 Gy** · HO prophylaxis **~6 Gy**

| | **Pre-op** | **Post-op** |
|---|---|---|
| Wound complications | **↑** — 20–30% (Orthobullets) · 30% (O'Sullivan) · **35% vs 17%** (OB7588) | ↓ |
| Dose · volume | **Lower, smaller** | Higher, larger |
| Fibrosis, oedema, stiffness, fracture, lymphoedema | ↓ | **↑** · ↑ radiation-induced sarcoma |
| Function | Trend better | — |
| Local recurrence · survival | **No difference** | No difference |

**RT not used** — chondrosarcoma, osteosarcoma (as adjuvant) · **WD liposarcoma / ALT → excision alone** (recurrence 2%)

### Unplanned excision — tumour bed re-excision

**Commonest mistake** in STS treatment · **74% residual disease**

```
Unplanned ("whoops") excision
├─ First → MRI limb + CT chest/abdomen/pelvis (no RT or surgery before imaging)
├─ Positive margins → TUMOUR BED RE-EXCISION (wide, ≥3 cm along scar) ± RT
└─ Negative margins → OBSERVATION vs ADJUVANT RT
```

**Deep, positive margins (e.g. sartorius)** — **wide tumour bed excision + RT**\
✗ Wrong options: RT alone · amputation · chemo

**High-grade superficial forearm** — **wide resection of tumour bed + coverage**\
✗ Wrong options: marginal to spare NV

**After wide re-excision vs planned** — **↑ plastic reconstruction** · less blood loss · same function · **5-yr LR-free >90%** · no extra chemo

### Specific subtypes (clear cell, alveolar soft part)

**Clear-cell sarcoma** — **malignant melanoma of soft parts** · adolescents/young adults · foot/ankle, bound to tendons · **S-100, HMB45** · **lymph node** mets · chemo + RT resistant → **wide excision / amputation**

**Alveolar soft part sarcoma** — **<1%** of STS · very vascular · lung, bone, **CNS** mets at presentation · LR 11–50% · **wide resection** + metastasectomy

**Fibrosarcoma (TO)** — diagnosis of exclusion · **herringbone** spindle cells · 5–10 cm slow painless deep lower-limb mass, **30–50 yr**

### Synovial sarcoma — histology & prognosis (TO)

**Not synovial in origin** — arises near bursae, tendon sheaths, capsules from multipotent stem cell rests → **biphasic = epithelial + mesenchymal (spindle)** · **lacks synovial differentiation**

**Subtypes** — **biphasic** (epithelial + spindle) · **monophasic** (spindle only) · poorly differentiated

**Genetics** — **t(X;18)(p11;q11)**, SS18 (SYT) on 18 + SSX1/2/4 on X → **SYT-SSX** · in **>90%** / **95%** · **SYT-SSX1 → biphasic** · markers (poorly differentiated): **CD56, CD99, HMW keratin** · BCL-2 under study

**Epidemiology** — **7%** (6–8%) of STS · commonest **foot** STS · commonest **non-RMS paediatric** STS · median **35 yr**, 15–40 · slight **M** · **knee** commonest

**Imaging** — X-ray: heterogeneous well-defined septate cystic mass, **calcification in 1/3** (CT best) · MRI: cystic, sharp, multiloculated → **misread as benign** · PET for mets/recurrence

**Grade** — **all high grade** · FNCLCC grading used

**Treatment** — wide excision (**>4 cm** margin) + RT (all >5 cm: EBRT, brachytherapy, IMRT) ± (neo)adjuvant chemo · amputation for fungating mass, difficult fracture, infection · metastatic → **doxorubicin + ifosfamide**

| Advanced-disease drug | Mechanism |
|---|---|
| **Pazopanib** | **Only approved targeted drug for STS** · TKI vs VEGFR, PDGFR, KIT |
| Trabectedin | Blocks DNA binding of the fusion oncoprotein |
| Tazemetostat | **EZH2 inhibitor** (INI1/SMARCB1-negative) |
| NY-ESO-1 T cells | Antigen in **80%** |
| Isolated limb perfusion | **TNF-α + melphalan**/doxorubicin, hyperthermic · limb preservation **72–96%** |

| Poor prognosis (TO) | Yes / No |
|---|---|
| **Age >50** (younger better) | ✓ |
| **Size ≥5 cm** | ✓ |
| Deep | ✓ |
| **Axial site** (distal extremity best) | ✓ |
| Higher stage · FNCLCC grade II–III · mitoses | ✓ |
| **Monophasic** histology | ✓ |
| CXCR4, IGF-1R nuclear expression | ✓ |
| Inadequate margins | ✓ |
| **Biphasic histology** | **No (favourable)** |

**Metastasis** — most important factor · in **~50%** · lung **80%**, nodes **up to 20%**, bone 9.9%, liver 4.5% · **5-yr OS ~60%** (61–73%)

## 6 Complications

**Metastasis** — **lungs commonest (40–60%)** · then **lymph nodes** (OB6341) · or bone/soft tissue (OB7545) · liver, brain rare

**Local recurrence** — **<10%** with surgery + RT · commonest site for low-grade recurrence = **local** · most within **5 yr**, but follow indefinitely

**Radiation** — pre-op wound problems · post-op fibrosis, fracture, secondary sarcoma

### Prognosis

**Most important factor** — **tumour stage** (Orthobullets) · **metastatic disease at presentation** (OB6333, bone sarcoma) · **histological grade** (TO one-liner)

**Poor** — high grade · mets · **>5 cm** · **deep to fascia** · delayed diagnosis · **unplanned excision**

| Stage | 5-yr survival |
|---|---|
| Low grade | **90%** |
| II–III | **50–75%** |
| IV | **15%** · median 12–18 months |

## How it's asked

**"52-yr-old, large deep thigh mass — initial step?"** → **biopsy**\
✗ Wrong options: marginal excision · RT · chemo

**"Slow-growing thigh mass with ecchymosis, 'haematoma', core yields only blood?"** → **open surgical biopsy**\
✗ Wrong options: RICE · drain · I&D

**"4 cm deep thigh mass, X-rays clear — next?"** → **contrast MRI**\
✗ Wrong options: FNA · core · excisional biopsy

**"64-yr-old fixed posteromedial thigh mass, varicosities, spindle cells?"** → **soft tissue sarcoma** (leiomyosarcoma)\
✗ Wrong options: myxoma · haematoma · fibroma

**"83-yr-old enlarging forearm mass >5 cm?"** → **needle biopsy**\
✗ Wrong options: marginal resection

**"Slow gluteal mass, heterogeneous on MRI?"** → **CT-guided biopsy**\
✗ Wrong options: wide resection first

**"Medial ankle mass after tripping, MRI suggestive of sarcoma?"** → **biopsy**

**"Painless subfascial arm mass after lifting boxes?"** → **CT-guided core needle biopsy**\
✗ Wrong options: ED drainage · biceps repair

**"55-yr-old painless heterogeneous deep thigh mass on MRI?"** → **core-needle biopsy**\
✗ Wrong options: excisional (<3 cm benign)

**"Low-grade STS shelled out, positive margins, no mets?"** → **tumour bed re-excision**

**"Incompletely excised high-grade superficial forearm sarcoma?"** → **wide tumour-bed resection + coverage**

**"'Lipoma' in sartorius → high-grade sarcoma, positive margins?"** → **wide tumour-bed excision + RT**

**"Piecemeal 'lipoma' excision → t(12;16) — next?"** → **imaging** (CT CAP + MRI) before re-excision

**"Wide re-excision after unplanned excision vs planned?"** → **↑ plastic reconstruction**\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ blood loss · worse function · LR >50%

**"Most important treatment factor for subcutaneous sarcoma DFS?"** → **size of operative margin**

**"39-yr-old large midfoot synovial sarcoma — best local control + fastest function?"** → **transtibial amputation**\
✗ Wrong options: wide resection + RT · ray resection

**"28-yr-old 5.7 cm high-grade dorsal foot sarcoma?"** → **wide resection + flap coverage**\
✗ Wrong options: marginal + STSG · BKA

**"Liposarcoma abutting SFA + sciatic nerve, close margin, ink negative?"** → **equivalent to wide resection removing both structures**

**"60 Gy is typical for?"** → **adjuvant RT for STS**\
✗ Wrong options: HO ~6 Gy · bone mets ~30 Gy

**"True about RT in STS?"** → **reduces local recurrence**\
✗ Wrong options: improves survival · pre-op fewer wound problems

**"Wide excision + RT for all EXCEPT?"** → **well-differentiated liposarcoma**\
✗ Wrong options: round cell · fibrosarcoma · synovial · pleomorphic

**"Pre-op vs post-op RT?"** → **higher wound complications**\
✗ Wrong options: higher dose · larger volume · worse survival

**"Most significant factor for long-term survival in bone sarcoma?"** → **metastatic disease at presentation**

**"Commonest site of STS metastasis?"** → **lungs**

**"Solitary lung nodule 9 months after STS resection?"** → **wedge resection**\
✗ Wrong options: pneumonectomy · chemo · RT

**"15 cm deep arm sarcoma on biopsy — next imaging?"** → **chest CT**\
✗ Wrong options: PET · bone scan

**"7 cm high-grade STS, N0 M0 — AJCC?"** → **IIIA**

**"AJCC 8th major change?"** → **appendicular separated from other sites**

**"4 cm synovial sarcoma, groin node +, lungs clear — AJCC?"** → **IVA**

**"High grade, 8 cm, N0 M0?"** → **IIIA**\
✗ Wrong options: 7 cm intermediate = IB (it is IIIA)

**"Five STS that go to nodes?"** → **rhabdomyosarcoma, synovial, epithelioid, clear-cell, angiosarcoma**

**"25-yr-old dorsal wrist clear-cell sarcoma — spreads to?"** → **lymph nodes**

**"6 cm medial thigh mass + inguinal nodes — differential?"** → **clear-cell, synovial, rhabdomyosarcoma**\
✗ Wrong options: GCT · liposarcoma · desmoid

**"Dorsal wrist 'ganglion' excised — nests of clear cells?"** → **clear-cell sarcoma**\
✗ Wrong options: epithelioid · synovial

**"Posterior leg mass, vascular nests — diagnosis + treatment?"** → **wide resection for alveolar soft part sarcoma**

**"Synovial sarcoma — all true EXCEPT?"** → **"biphasic with synovial + epithelial differentiation"** (it lacks synovial differentiation) (✓ knee, ✓ young adults, ✓ t(X;18) >90%) **(TO)**

**"NOT a poor prognostic factor in synovial sarcoma?"** → **biphasic histology** (✓ old age, ✓ large size, ✓ axial) **(TO)**

**"Herringbone pattern?"** → **fibrosarcoma** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: Ewing · synovial · MFH (storiform)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Squamous Cell Carcinoma

*Pathology · 60 / 66*

## 1 Basics

**Nature** — malignant tumour of **keratinocytes** (epidermis) · elderly

**Hand** — **commonest malignancy of the hand** (skin cancers ≫ primary MSK malignancy) · **commonest subungual malignancy** · dorsum of hand + forearm

**Skin overall** — **2nd commonest skin cancer** after BCC (OB212007) · in the hand, **BCC is 2nd** (OB220030) · face/neck > hand

**Risk factors** — **UV exposure** (main) · **actinic keratosis** · Bowen's disease, keratin horns, leukoplakia · **solid-organ transplant** (immunosuppression) · **chronic osteomyelitis**

### Marjolin ulcer

**Definition** — SCC in **chronic draining sinus tracts** (osteomyelitis), **burn scars**, pressure sores

**Rate** — **~1%** malignant change · latency **~30 yr** · many low grade

**Distractors** — Mooren (corneal) · chancroid (genital) · **Curling** (peptic, burns) · **Cushing** (gastric, ↑ ICP)

**High suspicion** — biopsy chronic draining sites

## 3 Presentation

**Indurated, scaly, erythematous** plaque/lesion · pain · subungual mass mistaken for **haematoma**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray (AP + lateral)** — **lytic** lesion when bone involved (e.g. distal phalanx tuft destruction)

![Lytic destruction of distal phalanx](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8073-40a-moved.jpg)

**Biopsy** — **punch, shave or incisional**

### Histology

**Keratin nodules / squamous pearls** · tumour nests penetrating deep fascia

![Keratinizing squamous cells](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob8073-40b-moved.jpg)

| Skin lesion | Feature |
|---|---|
| **SCC** | **Keratin pearls** |
| BCC | Pink/flesh papule, central ulcer · nests of basal cells |
| **Melanoma** | From naevus · ABCD changes |
| Seborrhoeic keratosis | "Stuck-on" waxy lesion · histology like SCC |
| Lentigo | Small pigmented macule |
| M. marinum | Culture 30–32°C |
| Sarcoidosis | Non-caseating granulomas |

## 5 Management

### Hand SCC — diagnosis & treatment

```
Squamous cell carcinoma
├─ Most → WIDE LOCAL RESECTION (6 mm margin to subcutaneous fat) ± SKIN GRAFT
├─ Small lesions → MOHS MICROSURGERY (highest cure rate)
├─ Bone involvement (digit) → AMPUTATION
```
